## VIII *Al-Bustán*, or “The Orchard” / 第八章 果园
===Original===

When Mahmoud’s nephews reappeared before him at the hour of public executions it was in a certain weariness of spirit; for though they knew that the fortunes of their uncle must subsequently be recovered in the narrative (since there he was before them, rolling, or, as the phrase went in Baghdad, “dripping” with it) yet the blows of fate had fallen upon him with such violence in the recent tale that something of his then despair had entered their own souls. They sat down therefore with hanging heads to listen, as they feared, to little better than the further advance of intolerable things.
The old man began in a subdued voice of lamentable recollection:
“I wandered on through the bare uplands, miserable, weak, penniless and in rags. So far had my soul fallen that on the seventh day I came near to omitting my prayer at even﻿ ﻿… but I thank Heaven that this temptation was conquered! I knelt down painfully upon the little carpet which was my last possession and submitted myself to the will of Allah.
“As though in answer to my prayer, and while I still knelt there, I saw afar off the figure of one who moved, as I could see from that distance, with a carriage of leisure, and I hoped﻿—I dared to hope﻿—that in answer to my prayer, I will not say a victim, but, at any rate, some provender was to be afforded me.
“I hastened my steps to catch up the stranger, and as I approached him remarked with pleasure his fine clothes and stately manner. ‘I have here,’ said I to myself, ‘some important man, someone doubtless unused to the base necessities of commerce; simple, noble in mind, straightforward, generous, amply provided: the very companion whom I should desire.’ I turned over in my mind (as I slackened my steps for a moment, so that he should not yet observe my arrival) various schemes whereby I might excuse my intrusion upon his solitary walk. At last I hit on that which seemed to me the most agreeable to his supposed circumstances and to my appearance. I strode up to him and bowing low asked him whether his Greatness could direct a poor wretch to a certain village the name of which I had heard and which lay more or less in the direction I had taken.
“The stranger turned to salute me and with that I felt an added delight. For he was the very thing I had prayed.

===Chinese===

当马哈茂德（Mahmoud）的侄儿们在公开行刑的时辰再次来到他面前时，个个神情萎靡。尽管他们明知叔父的财富在后文的故事里定能失而复得（毕竟他就端坐在眼前，腰缠万贯，或者用巴格达的俗话说，浑身“流金淌银”），可上一回故事中命运对他的打击委实太过惨烈，以致他当年的绝望情绪竟也渗入了侄儿们的心灵。于是他们垂头丧气地坐下，唯恐接下来听到的不过是更多难以忍受的厄运。
老人用低沉而哀怨的追忆语调开了口：
“我漫步在那片光秃的高地上，凄凉、虚弱、身无分文，衣衫褴褛。我的灵魂堕落到了这般地步，以至于在第七天（7），我险些连晚祷都给免了……但我感谢上苍，这一诱惑终究被我战胜了！我艰难地跪倒在仅剩的最后一份家当——那张小地毯上，顺从了真主的旨意。
“仿佛是对我祈祷的应验，就在我依然跪在那里时，远远望见一个人影正悠闲地踱步；从那距离看，他的举止显得颇为从容。我暗自期盼——甚至斗胆期盼——作为对我祈愿的回应，即便谈不上送来一个牺牲品，至少也能赐我一份口粮。
“我快步赶上去追那位陌生人，走近时欣喜地注意到他华美的服饰与尊贵的气度。‘我眼前这位，’我心中暗忖，‘定是个有头有脸的人物，想必未曾受过经商谋生那种下作营生的腌臜气；心性单纯、品格高贵、为人正直、出手阔绰、资财充盈——正是我梦寐以求的同伴。’我暗自盘算（稍稍放慢脚步，免得他过早察觉我的到来），构想着各种名目来为自己打扰他独自散步开脱。最后，我选中了一条自认为最契合他身份与我眼下尊容的由头。我快步走到他跟前，深深鞠了一躬，恳请大人能否为我这样一个可怜虫指点去往某座村庄的道路——我曾听闻过那个村名，大体就在我前行的方向上。
“陌生人转过身来向我致意，这让我心中更添喜色。因为他正是上苍赐给我的最佳人选。

===Original===

Young, simple in manner, courteous, probably, by his dress, independent and wealthy; probably, as our language has it, ‘his own father.’
“He wore rare ornaments; his cloak was of the finest wool and the cord that bound his headdress was interspersed with silver.
“By way of reply to my request, he told me in a pleasant, deep voice, speaking after the fashion of the rich, that he was himself strolling towards it so far as his own house and farm, which lay between, and that there he would put me upon my way. I expressed my gratitude, and my fear lest so bedraggled a companion might be distasteful to him. He smiled and assured me that he loved nothing better than converse. He had visited a neighbour that morning to ask advice on a certain set of pear trees of his which had not been doing well. He had left his servant to follow him with his mount, preferring this hour’s stroll back homewards in the cool of the sunset hour which had now descended.
“As we went we talked of many things and I frankly told him the story of my life; for I have discovered that nothing is more pleasing to men of his station than the account of how another has been reduced from wealth to poverty.
“ ‘I was not always,’ said I, as I strolled by his side, ‘the deplorable figure you now see me. Indeed, but a very few months ago I was the over-manager of a great fruit plantation some hundred miles to the north of this place. I had come with good recommendations from my former employers, planters of the Gulf. I had left these my original masters with the best of characters and the kindest of recommendations, and only because the eldest son of one of the partners had to be put into the business and there was no room for both of us. I had accumulated in some years of useful service a sufficient little capital which my kind masters were so exceedingly generous as to double, and I was able to put the total sum into the new business to which I had been recommended. For it is always better,’ I added, ‘to have some stake in the firm.’
“ ‘You are right,’ said my new friend in hearty approval. ‘There is no greater error than to offer a firm such intangible things as talent, honesty and the rest. Valuable as they are, if they are unaccompanied by metal they are without substance and void.’

===Chinese===

他年轻、举止朴实、谦和有礼，看穿戴想必自立且富有；按咱们的行话说，简直就是‘自己的老子’——自食其力的东家。
“他佩戴着珍稀饰品，斗篷由最上等的羊毛织成，束头巾的发带间还缀着银丝。
“针对我的请求，他用富人特有的悦耳而浑厚的嗓音回答说，他自己也正朝着那个方向散步，中途会经过他自家的宅院与农庄，到时他便可在那里为我指明去路。我忙不迭表达感激，又唯唯诺诺地表示，生怕自己这副邋遢相惹他嫌恶。他微微一笑，宽慰我说他最喜欢与人攀谈。那天早晨他去拜访了一位邻居，为自家几株长势不佳的梨树求教良方。他打发仆人牵着马匹跟在后头，自己则情愿在晚霞落下的凉爽时分，优哉游哉地踱步回家。
“我们一边走一边海阔天空地聊着，我毫不保留地向他倾诉了自己的生平；因为我早已发现，对于他这种阶层的人来说，最受用的莫过于听别人讲述自己如何从大富大贵沦落到一贫如洗。
“‘我从前，’我陪他信步前行时说道，‘可不是您如今瞧见的这副凄惨模样。其实就在短短几个月前，我还在此地以北约一百英里（100）的一座大型果园种植庄园担任总管。我揣着前东家——几位海湾种植园庄主——的热情推荐信来到那里。我离开最初的东家时，赢得了最高的品行赞誉与最温和周到的推荐信，只因其中一位合伙人的长子要进庄园接班，容不下我们两人并存。我在多年勤勉任职中积攒了一笔微薄却可观的本钱，仁慈的东家竟慷慨地为我贴补了一倍，我便把这笔全部款项投入到了那家受荐的新行当里。毕竟，’我又添了一句，‘入伙入股总归是更稳妥的。’
“‘说得极是，’我的新朋友深表赞同，‘天底下最大的谬误，莫过于单凭才干、诚实这类虚无缥缈的东西去跟商号合伙。这玩意儿固然可贵，可若是没有真金白银撑腰，全是一文不值的空架子。’

===Original===

“With an expression of great humility I applauded his reply and told him how flattered I was to find that my judgment had jumped with his. ‘But, alas, Sir!’ I continued deferentially, ‘There is no controlling the current of our destinies! For there is One above﻿—’
“ ‘I know, I know!’ agreed my companion hurriedly, in the tones of one to whom the sentiment was familiar and at the same time doubtful, and I continued:
“ ‘By that Divine Will,’ I went on, ‘was I visited. Heaven saw fit to try its servant. In the course of my management I was sent to negotiate the purchase of a cargo of lime-dressing at the nearest port, for use upon the plantation. On my way I had the misfortune to be robbed at an inn of the pouch of gold that had been confided to me. I ought, of course, to have returned at once and told my partners and employers what had happened, and to have offered, perhaps, to repair out of my own property what might look like the result of my own negligence; but I was afraid lest I should not be believed, and again lest, if I were believed, the loss should prejudice me in their eyes as an incompetent. What I did was to go forward to the port that very day, penniless, and trust to the credit of my firm to complete the transaction I had in hand.
“ ‘But once again I was unfortunate! I carried through the negotiations with success and purchased the cargo upon very reasonable terms. I delayed to the last moment the payment of earnest money and then, when delay would no longer serve, I said carelessly, that full payment would follow by messenger within two days. The merchant’s face darkened. He told me that he had been led on by false pretences, roughly bade me begone and would hear no more of the transaction. He refused to sign, and indeed left me abruptly, saying that he was off to seek another purchaser and telling me at the same time that he was seriously considering whether or no to summon me before the magistrate for having thus lost him a whole day upon a false pretence.
“ ‘He was as good as his word, and I received a summons from the magistrate that very evening to attend his court the next day.
“ ‘It was unfortunate that during the night another theft took place in the inn where I lay. The bundles of those staying at the place were searched. My own alone contained no valuables of any kind.

===Chinese===

“我摆出一副极其谦恭的姿态赞赏他的见解，并表示自己的看法能与他不谋而合，实在深感荣幸。‘然而，唉，大人！’我毕恭毕敬地继续道，‘人的命运之流岂能由自己掌控！冥冥中自有至高主宰——’
“‘懂得，懂得！’我的同伴急忙应和道，语气既像对此类言论耳熟能详，又隐隐带着一丝怀疑。于是我接着说：
“‘正是那神圣旨意，’我说道，‘降临到了我头上。上苍认为该考验考验它的仆人了。在任总管期间，我受派前往最近的港口洽谈采购一批用于庄园的生石灰肥料。途中我不幸在一家小客栈里遭窃，受托携带的一袋金币被洗劫一空。按理说，我当然该立刻返回向合伙人和雇主据实相告，并主动提出自掏腰包弥补这看似因我疏忽招致的损失；但我害怕他们不信，又害怕即便信了，这笔损失也会让他们觉得我办事不力。于是我硬着头皮，当天身无分文地赶到港口，指望全凭商号的信誉来敲定这笔交易。
“‘谁知我又倒了大霉！谈判本极顺利，我以极其合理的条件买下了整船货物。我将定金交付拖延到最后一刻，待到实在拖不下去时，我漫不经心地推说全额货款将在两天（2）内由信差送达。那客商脸色顿时阴沉下来。他指责我用花言巧语欺诈，粗暴地喝令我滚蛋，绝口不提这笔交易。他拒绝画押签字，甚至拂袖而去，临走前扬言要去另觅买主，还警告我说正在慎重考虑要不要把我扭送法官，治我靠虚假借口耽误了他整整一天工夫的罪。
“‘他言出必行，当天傍晚我便收到了法官传票，勒令我次日出庭受审。
“‘更倒霉的是，当夜我落脚的客栈又发生了一起窃案。所有住客的行李都被搜查，唯独我的包裹里没有半点值钱之物。

===Original===

One would have thought that such a circumstance would have spoken in my favour. It was exactly the other way. It was argued that a man who will stay in an inn without the means of paying must be a thief of some sort and that since the sum stolen was not to be found elsewhere it was probably I, thus manifestly suspect of trickery, who was the culprit. In my fright I attempted to escape. I was caught and roughly handled, with the final result that I appeared in the magistrate’s court covered with blood, my garments torn and in such a posture subjected to a double accusation upon the part of the innkeeper and also upon the part of the foreign merchant who appeared upon the original charge.
“ ‘In such distress I had no avenue of escape save a reference to my honoured firm, the name of which, though distant, was familiar to the court. The magistrate expressed his doubt that I had any connection with such important people, and asked me if I would risk the sending of a messenger to my so-called partners. I said I would do so gladly, but during the two days’ interval of the messenger’s absence I was closely confined in the public prison, where I regret to say the foreign merchant had the heartlessness to come and make faces at me through the bars, and where, having no money to give my gaolers, I was treated with the utmost harshness.
“ ‘My misfortunes were not at an end. As luck would have it the firm to which I belonged and of whose books I had the sole management, undertook a surprise audit on the very day of my departure, and discovered a most serious deficit in one item which the partners, in their ignorance, could not account for. Had I been present I could easily have explained what had happened. It was but an advance which I had made to a customer whose transactions with us were of the highest value. As much in my own interests as in those of my partners I was well justified in risking the money. I had acted foolishly perhaps in refusing to take a receipt or to enter the matter in the books, but the thing was only for a week and after so many years of prosperity I could not dream of so small a thing turning out untowardly. However, there it was. My partners hurriedly sent after me and learned to their dismay that I had left the first inn upon the road without payment, and giving no account of my future movements.

===Chinese===

按常理推想，这种情形本该有利于洗清我的嫌疑。事实却恰恰相反。客栈咬定，一个住店却无力付账的人必定是小偷之流；既然失窃的金银在别处搜不出来，那定然是我这个行迹可疑的骗子所为。惊恐之下我拔腿就跑。结果被逮了回来，挨了一顿毒打；最终我浑身是血、衣衫褴褛地被押上了公堂，同时面对客栈掌柜的盗窃指控以及那位外邦客商最初的欺诈指控。
“‘在那般绝境中，我唯一的一线生机就是搬出我所效力的高贵商号；该商号虽远在天边，公堂之上倒也如雷贯耳。法官怀疑我与那些大人物究竟有无瓜葛，问我可敢担风险派信差前去查验我那所谓的合伙人。我欣然应允；然而在信差往返的两天（2）间隙，我被关押在官府大牢严加看管。遗憾的是，那位外邦客商竟铁石心肠地跑到栅栏前冲我做鬼脸；我又无钱打点狱卒，遭到了百般虐待。
“‘然而我的灾厄远未结束。说来真是不巧，就在我动身的那天，由我全权掌管账目的商号突然进行突击查账，查出了一笔数额巨大的严重亏空，那些合伙人见识浅薄，对此茫无头绪。若是我在场，三言两语便能解释清楚：那不过是我垫付给一位极为重要的尊贵大客户的一笔暂借款。无论从我自身利益还是合伙人利益考量，冒点风险垫款都是完全站得住脚的。我或许糊涂在未曾索取收条、亦未将此事记入账册，但这笔款项本只需周转一周，在经历了多年顺风顺水之后，我做梦也没料到这么件小事竟会横生枝节。可是事实已经铸成。合伙人们火急火燎派人沿途追赶我，却惊愕地得知我未结清第一家客栈的账目便溜之大吉，且未留下半点行踪去向。

===Original===

They had sent a man posthaste on a swift horse. He had covered the distance to the port in twelve hours, but (as I was now in prison), could discover nothing of me in the town nor find any cargo I had bought or, indeed, any trace of me. He returned to my partners, as they had instructed him, upon another beast as swift (having sold his spent mount) and it was just as they received this grave news of my apparent absconding, just while my partners grew more and more convinced of my supposed guilt, that the messenger from the magistrate arrived and completed the accusation. They answered, not by coming in person, but by sending a letter of the most violent kind, calling me a notorious thief, expressing their pleasure that I had been laid by the heels and begging that, so far as they were concerned, the magistrate would not spare me in any punishment he might see fit to inflict for my other escapades. Meanwhile (they said) they would not trouble him to enter judgment for the sum I had taken, since they had replaced it out of my capital in the firm, which nearly, or exactly, made good the deficit.
“ ‘You may imagine, my lord, the result of all this! The magistrate read the court a sermon on the justice of the law which spared no man for his rank or commerce, and concluded, “You have before you the sad spectacle of a man of substance fallen through temptation into poverty and disgrace.” The foreign merchant contemptuously waived his action, the innkeeper with equal contempt expressed himself satisfied with the punishment I had already undergone, claiming only my clothes by way of payment, giving me these few rags in exchange. With yet another admonition the magistrate dismissed me. I went out from the court a broken man, wandered aimlessly southward, doing a little work here and there upon the farms, and I am now seeking the next village with the object of offering my services.
“ ‘Such, Sir,’ I concluded, ‘is my tale.﻿ ﻿… Here am I, with every commercial aptitude, and full training in the various transactions of business (but especially in the management of plantations) for no fault of my own unable to exercise these talents, rehabilitate my character, and recover my position in society.’

===Chinese===

他们派出一名快马加鞭的信使，十二小时（12）便跑完了去往港口的全程；然而（由于我当时身陷囹圄），他在城中压根打听不到我的下落，也找不到我采买的货物，连我的蛛丝马迹都没见着。遵照吩咐，他变卖了跑垮的坐骑，换乘另一匹快马赶回向合伙人复命。合伙人们刚得知我似乎畏罪潜逃的严重消息，正越发笃定我有罪时，法官派出的差役恰好赶到，坐实了这桩罪名。合伙人并未亲自前来，而是回了一封言辞极为激烈的信函，痛斥我是臭名昭著的窃贼，对我落网深表快慰，并恳请法官大人不必顾忌他们，对于我犯下的种种劣迹从严惩处。与此同时（信中写道），他们无意麻烦法官判定追讨我卷走的款项，因为他们已将我在商号里的入股本金予以没收充公，正好——或者说几乎完全——弥补了账面亏空。
“‘大人，您尽可想象这一切的下场！法官在公堂上发表了一通宏论，宣扬法律面前人人平等、绝不宽贷任何显赫门第或商贾巨贾的正义，最后总结道：“展现在诸位眼前的，是一个昔日的富足之士因贪欲诱惑而沦落到穷困潦倒、身败名裂的悲惨下场。”外邦客商轻蔑地撤销了起诉；客栈掌柜也带着同样轻蔑的神情，表示对我所受的皮肉之苦感到满意，只扒下了我的衣服作为房钱抵偿，换给我这几片破布烂条。在法官又一通告诫之后，我被逐出了公堂。我走出法庭，成了一个彻底破产之人，漫无目的地一路向南流浪，在各处农庄打零工糊口；眼下正打算前往前方的村庄碰碰运气，看看能否谋个差事。
“‘大人，’我总结道，‘这便是我的一腔血泪……我坐拥一身经商本领，深谙各类交易门道（尤其精通种植园管理），却非因自身过错而无处施展才华，无法洗刷清白，亦无法重获体面的社会地位。’

===Original===

“The rich young man was deeply touched by my story, every word of which, I am glad to say, he seemed to believe; for I was not deceived in my reading of character and I had rightly guessed that a man under thirty, honest-faced and clearly enjoying leisure and wealth would be singularly open to the reception of any romantic tale that might be offered to him.
“ ‘It is indeed fortunate,’ he answered, ‘that you understand plantations. It is a matter in which I am for the moment interested. I have an orchard which is not doing well.’ He had evidently forgotten his first sentence on our meeting which had given me my clue. But rich and generous natures are like that in early youth: hence, also, are they bad players in games of skill.
“ ‘Come with me,’ he continued, ‘and pass the night in my house yonder’ (it already lay before us in the hollow); ‘the conversation on your past life, which is doubtless full of adventures, will entertain me at my meal. Tomorrow I will see that you have occupation upon my farm, and after a short experiment I think we shall get along very well indeed together, particularly as I have recently planted by way of experiment a number of pear trees which﻿—as I think I just told you﻿—are not doing well. I thought myself able from my general knowledge to conduct this orchard, but I regret to say that some of the trees have died, and that the rest are in a poor way. I evidently lack the special experience required. Since plantations are your special line you may be of the greatest service to me in this little matter.’
“Here, my dear nephews, I was in something of a quandary. This, I am told, is a difficulty we men of affairs come across often enough in the conduct of our negotiations. It is our duty, as I need hardly tell you, to add details of a corroborative kind to the statements we have to make in affairs. To omit any detail is to court suspicion. On the other hand, one never knows where the most necessary fictions may lead one. Here I was confronted by the task of bringing to fruition an orchard﻿ ﻿… an orchard of pears﻿ ﻿… and I knew nothing whatever of orchards and of pears far less.
“I replied, therefore, with the greatest enthusiasm that the opportunity was exactly what I should have desired. Orchards were the *one* kind of plantation I had most carefully studied, and of all fruits *pears* were those upon which I had specialised most.

===Chinese===

“那位年轻的富人被我的身世深深打动了。叫我欣喜的是，他似乎对我说的每一个字都深信不疑；因为我相面的眼光从未看走眼，我早就猜准了一个不满三十岁（30）、面相诚实、显然优游闲适且家财万贯的阔少爷，对于任何送到嘴边的传奇故事都会全盘笑纳。
“‘你懂得种植园之道，当真是天大的幸事，’他答道，‘这恰好是我近来正操心的事。我有一座果园，近来长势很是不妙。’他显然把自己初遇我时吐露的那句话忘得一干二净了，而我正是从那句话里摸到的底细。但生性质朴慷慨的富家子弟在年轻时大抵如此：正因如此，他们在智谋博弈中向来是不中用的输家。
“‘跟我走吧，’他继续说道，‘今晚就到我前面的宅子里过夜’（那座宅院已然呈现在我们前方的洼地里）；‘听你聊聊过往那些想必凶险万分的奇遇，正好给我下饭佐餐。明日我便在农庄上为你安排差事。经过一番小小尝试，我想我们定能相处得十分融洽——尤其是，我近来试种了一批梨树，正如我方才告诉你的，长势都很不理想。我本以为凭着普通见识便能管好这座果园，可遗憾的是，有些树已经枯死，剩下的也是奄奄一息。显然我缺乏所需的专门经验。既然种植庄园是你的看家本领，在这件小事上你必定能助我一臂之力。’
“我亲爱的侄儿们，这下我可有点骑虎难下了。据我所知，这是咱们这些商海中人在斡旋事务时屡见不鲜的困境。无需我多言，你们也明白，在谈生意时添枝加叶、补充佐证细节是我们的天职。漏掉哪怕一处细节都会招致猜忌。但另一方面，你永远料不到那些不可或缺的虚构编造会把人引向何方。眼下我面临的差事，竟是让一座果园起死回生……而且还是一座梨树园……可我对果园一窍不通，对梨树更是两眼一抹黑。
“因此，我带着满腔热忱回答道，这桩美差正是我梦寐以求的。果园乃是我毕生钻研最深的一类种植园，而在所有瓜果之中，‘梨树’更是我的专攻之首。

===Original===

Once I had seen the kind of tree my kind host had planted I should certainly be able to tell him what was the matter.
“It was almost dark when we came to his enclosure, but so eager was he on his new idea that he led me at once to the back of the house where the trees were planted. Very sickly indeed did their gaunt twigs look in the gloaming. A good third of them were shrivelled and dead, the rest drooped in various degrees, one only gave a promise of fruit out of some three hundred stems. The rich man surveyed the ruin and gazed at me anxiously while I held my chin in my hand as though meditating upon the best course for him to pursue, but in reality considering my own.
“Then it was, my dear infants, that I received from on High one of those illuminations which have always been, with me, the forerunners of great things. I deliberately kept the rich young man waiting for the space of a long prayer and then said suddenly and with determination, ‘Scrap the lot!﻿ ﻿… Excuse me,’ I added, ‘I have used a phrase current in the barbaric cities of the north, which is somewhat corrupt in speech. My intention was to express to Your Highness my conviction, formed upon this rapid survey in gathering darkness, that the orchard can no longer be saved, for I am sure my judgment will be confirmed when I make a more thorough examination tomorrow morning in broad day. I see also, even in this light, that the type of tree you have planted is wholly unsuited to the climate. May I be so bold as to ask where you purchased the stock?’
“ ‘I was assured,’ answered my new friend a little shamefacedly, ‘that it was stock grown within this very region and peculiarly adapted for our dry climate: for the drought of our position which, as you know, stands too high for waterways.’
“I shook my head. ‘You were deceived,’ said I. ‘Who sold you this unsuitable stock?’
“He told me that it was a sound friend of his who had gone off for a while upon a journey, that he was quite sure he had not intended to deceive. ‘Perhaps there was some error in the consignment shipped to you,’ I answered cheerfully as we turned towards the house. ‘This kind does admirably in the River Lowlands, and I take it your friend’s servants by some mistake sent your consignment to some lowland client and *his* to *you* in these uplands.

===Chinese===

只要容我看一眼我那仁慈的东道主所种的树苗，我必能指出症结所在。
“我们走到他的宅院时天色已近全黑，但他对自己的新想法热切万分，立刻引着我绕到宅后种植果树之处。暮色苍茫中，那些干瘪的枝桠当真显得病入膏肓。足足有三分之一已然枯萎坏死，其余的也不同程度地耷拉着；约三百株（300）树干中，唯独一株略现挂果的生机。富人望着这一片败相，焦灼地凝视着我；我则手托下巴，表面上仿佛在为他苦思最佳对策，心底里实则在盘算自己的退路。
“便是在那时，我亲爱的孩子们，我从至高主宰那里领受了灵光一闪——在我的生平中，这类神启往往是大发横财的前兆。我特意让这位年轻阔少等了足足念完一篇长篇祷词的工夫，随后断然决绝地开口道：‘统统报废！……恕我冒昧，’我又补了一句，‘我方才用了北方蛮荒城邑的粗鄙切口。小人是想向大人表明，在这苍茫暮色中草草一瞥，我便确信这座果园已无药可救；待到明日清晨天光大亮、作一番细致勘验之后，我相信我的论断定会得到证实。即便在眼下这微光中我也能断定，您栽种的树种全然不适宜本地水土。敢问大人，这批树苗是打哪儿采买来的？’
“‘人家向我拍胸脯保证，’我的新朋友略带羞赧地答道，‘说这批苗木正是本乡本土培育的，专门适应我们这种干燥气候——正如你所知，我们这片地势过高，渠水难以灌溉。’
“我摇了摇头。‘您上当了，’我说道，‘是谁把这种不中用的苗木卖给您的？’
“他告诉我，那是他一位十分可靠的朋友，眼下正出门远行，他确信那位朋友绝无欺瞒之意。‘兴许是发货时出了差错，’我们转身朝宅子走去时，我语气轻快地应道，‘这种树在河谷低洼湿地长势绝佳；依我看，八成是您朋友手下的奴仆办事疏忽，误将原本送往低地买主的货错发给了高地上的您，倒把您的苗木发给了别人。

===Original===

Anyhow, the orchard is manifestly doomed, as you can see, and for my part I make no doubt that the trouble has come from the use of a wrong species. Now what you want here,’ I continued rapidly, turning over my chances well in my mind, and plumping for technical terms, ‘is a pear neither palinate nor sublongate, nor, for that matter perforate, but daxullic, or, as we sometimes call it in the trade “retarded”﻿—at any rate in the second and third stirp.’
“ ‘I see; I understand; I apprehend,’ said the rich young man. For thus, have I discovered, do rich young men carry on a conversation which leaves them entirely at sea.
“ ‘I do not,’ I hastened to add, ‘insist, of course, upon the Persian stock, though that is the best. It might be difficult to procure and it is *very* expensive. What I mean is something of the same family. I should advise, as a stock more easily purchased in the local markets, the pear called by the merchants “The Glory of Heaven.”
“ ‘It was introduced some few years ago by my friend Nasredin and is now a favourite stock on the Plateau of Reshed where the climate is very similar to yours. It bears a large, luscious fruit, highly marketable, and maturing early; and it can be purchased at a moderate expense. I will, if you like, go for you to the nearest provider of such things and see what I can do.’
“My host thanked me profusely. He remarked how small the world was and at the same time how manifest were the workings of Providence. He blessed the day when he had met me. For though (he said), the matter of expense did not weigh upon him, he had made a particular point of success in the matter of pear trees, and but for my advice he really did not know what he should have done.
“He was so keen upon the affair that he pressed me to start for the nearest nurseryman the very next morning. There was an excellent nursery plantation, he said, not more than half a day’s ride away to the West. It stood, with the owner’s house in the midst, just outside the gates of the town to which he would direct me, either going himself or sending his bailiff with me. He would also send a wagon for the conveyance of the young shoots. Indeed, as the meal progressed (for we were now dining), he grew more and more enthusiastic on the matter and could hardly bear the delay of the night. I saw which way the land lay and saw fit to increase his keenness.

===Chinese===

不管怎样，如您所见，这座果园显然已经无可挽回，而在我看来，症结无疑在于选错了品种。眼下您这片地所需要的，’我脑筋飞转，一边权衡胜算，一边连珠炮般抛出种种行话切口，‘是一种既非掌状裂变亦非半延展型、更非穿孔透导型的梨树，而必须是达克苏里克属——也就是行内俗称的“迟滞型”——至少得是第二或第三代杂交品系。’
“‘我懂了；我全明白了；我领悟了，’年轻富人连连点头。我早就摸清了，有钱的阔少在被行话绕得如坠五里雾中时，往往都是这般接话的。
“‘当然，’我忙不迭补充道，‘我倒不是硬要您非波斯原种不种，虽说那确实是极品。那品种不仅难以搜求，且价格昂贵得吓人。我的意思是采纳同属近亲。依我之见，在附近市集上更容易采买到的一种砧木，便是被客商们冠以“天之荣耀”美誉的名梨。
“‘此树乃是数年前由家友纳斯拉丁引种，如今在气候与您此地极相仿的雷谢德高原上最受追捧。它结出的果实硕大甜美，销路极畅，且早熟丰产；采办费用也相当公道。大人若首肯，我愿亲自替您走一遭最近的树苗行，看看能办出什么名堂。’
“我的东道主千恩万谢，连连感叹这天下何其之小，同时天意的昭彰又是何其显豁。他盛赞与我相逢的这一天乃是大吉之日。因为（他坦言）开销几何他浑不在意，但他一心要在种梨树上一鸣惊人；若非得我指点，他真不知该如何是好了。
“他对这桩买卖热忱如火，甚至催逼着我次日清晨便启程去寻最近的苗圃行商。他说往西骑马不过半日行程，便有一处极佳的苗木庄园。苗圃就坐落在他将为我指引的那座市镇城门外，主家的大宅居于其中；他要么亲自同去，要么派庄头管事与我同行。他还会拨出一辆大车用于装运幼苗嫩枝。事实上，随着晚宴进行（我们当时正在用膳），他对这事越发如痴如狂，简直连一夜的工夫都耐不住。我摸清了风向，自然顺水推舟，进一步吊起他的胃口。

===Original===

I therefore told him it was quite impossible to act with such speed. ‘The young shoots,’ said I, ‘must not be left to lie untended and unplanted. We must first of all prepare the ground. The old trees must be dug up, the pits enlarged. It is the narrowness of the earthing that has been half your trouble, for the smaller root tendrils which we call “trips” are easily estopped in hard groundings.’ ‘I see!’ said he, sapiently. ‘The ground must be well soaked,’ I continued, ‘and manured with a full dressing of lime, and only when all this has been completed could I think of advising you to plant.’
“I paused to concoct something new, and the amiable youth filled the gap for me by murmuring: ‘Precisely! Exactly! Now I understand.’
“I resumed: ‘Further we must underpin the runners and work up the earth herring-wise. And then there is the daubing.﻿ ﻿… It will be a matter of full three days’ work. On the fourth day I can set out before sunrise. You may take it that I will be back by evening, and we will, if you please, plant the very next morning﻿—that is on the fifth day﻿—lest the stock should suffer; for I have always found it,’ I added profoundly, ‘of invariable service to plant immediately. I have indeed lost in the past one or two most valuable sets of trees﻿—*pear* trees﻿—by delaying at this season of the year so much as twenty-four hours before putting them in the ground.’
“As I thus spoke he nodded frequently, admiring my talent and knowledge of these affairs, and I took occasion, as evening wore on, to ground him yet more deeply in this fascinating subject, which I had already begun to feel was mine.
“The next day with the first of the light we both of us set out to the orchard. He summoned his workmen and our labours engrossed us for many hours during which I fed his enthusiasm with renewed tales of marvels in the way of fruit-growing﻿—and especially of pear trees. That particular pear called ‘The Glory of Heaven’ increased wonderfully as I proceeded until at last it had grown to such a size that each individual fruit was as large as a child’s head, and half a dozen of them would fetch a piece of gold ‘if’ (I was careful to add) ‘if they are properly packed! For I regret to say that, simple as the detail is, the neglect of good packing has been the ruin of most speculators in this line.’

===Chinese===

因此我告诉他，万万不可操之过急。‘那些幼苗嫩芽，’我说道，‘绝不可弃置不顾、延误栽植。我们必须先整饬地力。旧树必须连根掘除，树穴必须拓宽凿深。您原先的大半症结，就在于填土狭隘，行内称为“行须”的微细须根在硬土中极易窒闭阻绝。’‘受教了！’他恍然大悟般称善。‘土壤必须彻底浇透，’我继续说道，‘并施以足量的生石灰底肥；唯有将这桩桩件件齐备妥当，我才敢建言您破土移栽。’
“我略一停顿，暗自编排新花样；那位热诚的年轻人便趁着空隙喃喃接道：‘正是！确凿无误！如今我全明白了。’
“我接着吹嘘道：‘不仅如此，我们还得加固匍匐茎，按鱼骨纹样翻整土壤。随后还得进行糊泥裹封……这一通忙活，足足得费上三天功夫。到了第四天黎明之前，我方能启程。您可以确信我傍晚定能赶回；若是大人应允，咱们就在次日清晨——也就是第五天破晓——立刻下种，免得苗株受损。因为我历来的经验，’我故作深沉地补充道，‘移栽务求神速。往年我在这等时节，就曾因下地耽搁了区区24小时（24），平白折损了一两批最名贵的树苗——而且全都是梨树！’
“听我这般娓娓道来，他不住点头称是，对我经办农事的宏才博识赞叹不已；夜色渐浓，我趁热打铁，将他更深地引向这门引人入胜的学问——此时此刻，我已隐然自居为一代宗师了。
“翌日清晨天光乍破，我们二人便奔赴果园。他召集全庄长工，大伙热火朝天地忙碌了数个时辰；其间我不断向他灌输种种果木种植的奇迹——尤其是梨树的神效——以此滋养他的狂热。那被称为‘天之荣耀’的名梨，随着我口若悬河的铺陈越长越大，到最后竟被我吹得如孩童头颅般硕大，半打便能卖出一枚金币——‘前提是，’我煞有介事地补上一句，‘前提是包装得当！因为说来遗憾，尽管只是区区细枝末节，市面上无数投机行家偏偏就栽在包装粗疏上。’

===Original===

“During the second day of our labours I dilated upon other details of the trade which occurred to me as I went along. I especially insisted upon what I called the ‘maximum point,’ and for this he was all ears.
“ ‘There is a limit,’ I said, ‘to your plantation, after which the expenses of management begin to eat into the profits earned. A first small speculation of 300 trees, such as you have here, is, of course, a mere bagatelle. It would provide you with amusement, but no appreciable income. The most profitable size of orchard is far larger.﻿ ﻿… In such a situation as yours,’ said I, looking round with an air of a connoisseur, ‘and with such soil as this,’ and with that I took up a clod and carefully crumbled it in my fingers, ‘possessing acidulated properties of this type, but corrected by some slow exhaust of porphyritic matter, it would need but a top dressing of bardulm and an occasional picketing of charcoal to make some 3,000 trees produce a regular annual profit of not less than 200 pieces of gold﻿—and that upon an original expenditure less than double the amount. I would estimate your return with care and good fortune at quite fifty percent, but at any rate you could calculate upon thirty percent. But *more* than 3,000 trees,’ said I, musing, ‘would, I fear, be an error; the earnings after that get eaten into by expenses.’
“He interrupted me with the eager words: ‘I should be happy﻿—’ I lifted my hand to check him and said, ‘No! I assure you, that even such a number as 3,500 would be just beyond the line, and as you approach 5,000 you would find the expense absorbing nearly all your profit. It is as great an error to overdo these things as to starve them. Let us fix the number at 3,000 and the capital expenditure at 400 pieces of gold. Then I think you will not be disappointed.’
“The third day I spent overlooking the levelling of the ground and its last preparation, as also in making mysterious marks with little pegs and jotting down notes in a book: all of which excited the owner to the last degree, and left him (as the phrase goes) with his tongue hanging out for the new trees.
“That evening my kind host after some little embarrassment made me an offer. Would I, he asked, share in the profits of the enterprise? I at once refused.

===Chinese===

“劳作的第二天，我一边干活一边随口发挥，大肆敷陈行业门道。我尤其大谈特谈所谓的‘极点效应’，直听得他竖起耳朵、唯恐漏掉一字。
“‘种植庄园的规模，’我高谈阔论道，‘存在一道临界限度；一旦跨过这道界限，管理开支便会蚕食吞噬净利。像您眼前这区区300株（300）树的小打小闹，自然不值一提。权当消遣尚可，断不能带来像样的进项。真正能攫取暴利的果园规模要宏大得多……以您此处的地势，’我说着摆出行家的架势环顾四周，‘加之这般土壤，’我俯身拾起一块土坷垃，煞有介事地在指间捻碎，‘蕴含此类微酸特质，又得斑岩风化物的缓释调和，只需在上层施以巴杜姆覆料，间或插以木炭桩排毒，便足以令大约3,000株（3000）树每年产出不少于200枚（200）金币的稳定净利——而初始本钱还不足这收益的两倍。若悉心照拂外加运气不错，我预估您的回报率足足可达百分之五十（50），保底也能算到百分之三十（30）。然而若是超过3,000株（3000），’我沉吟着，‘恐怕便弄巧成拙了；超过这数目，收益就会全被开销吃光。’
“他迫不及待地打断我：‘我愿意——’我抬手制止了他，说道：‘切莫如此！我可以向您保证，哪怕是3,500株（3500）也会过犹不及；若是逼近5,000株（5000），您会发现开销把利润吞噬殆尽。凡事过火与不足同样致命。依我看，咱们便将苗木定在3,000株（3000），本钱定为400枚（400）金币。这样安排，定教大人心满意足。’
“第三天，我整日监督平整土地与收尾工序，还煞有介事地钉下一排排小木桩做神秘记号，又在小册子上写写画画：这一切将庄园主人的胃口吊到了极点，直教他（正如俗语所言）馋涎欲滴、巴望着新树早日进门。
“当晚，我那位仁慈的东道主略带腼腆地向我提出一桩邀约。他问我是否愿意参与庄园的分红。我当即一口回绝。

===Original===

My decision surprised him: but, as he pressed the project upon me, I told him that gratitude was only a part of my decision. I owed him everything; he had found me﻿—it seemed a month ago indeed, though it was but three days﻿—in rags; he had clothed me, fed me and, what was more, trusted me. His trust, I assured him, would not be deceived. ‘I shall be content,’ I concluded, ‘with the salary proper to my position’ (he at once mentioned a sum, which I halved), ‘but I will go so far as this; if, upon the opening of the fourth year, your profits shall be found to have *exceeded* what I have suggested, if you make in the three years more than 600 pieces of gold, at 200 pieces a year, which I suggest as the probable result, I will accept, though reluctantly, one half of the excess. For I am confident,’ and here I put an especially serious tone into my voice, ‘that we shall do better than I have said. I have ever held it my duty to give a conservative estimate and to avoid the disappointment of those who employ me. To this, among other things, do I ascribe the great success which attended me during my earlier years, and which only failed me through the deplorable accidents I related to you on our first meeting.’
“My host appeared a little confused at my probity, or rather, at my scruples; but he told me that he had always found such errors to be upon the right side, and assured me that I should not lose by the austerity of my temper. Nor did I.﻿ ﻿…
“We spent the rest of the evening looking at the illuminations in his fine library. I expressed myself enthralled by them all. I lingered with especial care over every representation of an orchard in these pictures, and spoke in the most learned manner of the various fruits therein displayed. As luck would have it we came to one particularly fine painting in which were delineated the most enormous pears of a brilliant golden hue interspersed with soft leaves. ‘This,’ I cried delightedly, ‘is the very fruit of which I have been speaking! How interesting! How exciting!’
“ ‘Is that so?’ said my host, transported at the coincidence, ‘Once more I must say it: how small is the world!’
“ ‘Yes,’ said I, ‘it is that pear “Glory of Heaven,” of which I have been speaking and which you may see, by comparison with the insects here portrayed and of the trellis work, to be most enormous fruit.

===Chinese===

我的决断令他大为惊异；但他一再坚持要与我分红，我便对他说，感恩之情不过是我这番决定的缘由之一。我的一切全拜他所赐；他遇见我时——虽说只是三天前的事，却恍若隔了一个月——我衣衫褴褛；他为我更衣，供我饱食，尤为重要的是，还对我深信不疑。我向他保证，他的信任绝不会被辜负。‘只要给我这份差事应得的薪酬，我便心满意足了，’我总结道（他当即开出一个数目，我硬是给砍掉了一半），‘但我可以退这么一步：倘若到了第四年伊始，您的收益被证实超出了我所提的估算——倘若您三年间赚得的利润超过了600枚（600）金币，即按我提议的每年200枚（200）金币这种极可能达成的数字计算，那么多出的盈余部分，我愿意勉为其难收下一半。因为我确信，’说到这里，我的语气格外郑重其事，‘咱们的收成定会比我所说的还要丰厚。我历来认为，给出审慎保守的估算、免教东家失望，乃是我的职责所在。在早年间，我之所以能大获成功，正是归功于这等品性；若非初次相逢时我向您倾诉的那些可悲横祸，我的事业断不至于中道崩殂。’
“我的东道主对我这等廉洁操守——或者说，对我这般死板的顾虑——显得有些不知所措；但他告诉我，在他看来这种谨小慎微向来是有益无害的过失，并向我保证绝不会让我因清廉自律而吃亏。我确实没吃亏……
“那个夜晚余下的时光，我们全在他那典雅精致的书斋里翻赏泥金画卷。我对那些画作赞叹不已。我尤其流连于每一幅描摹果园的图画，以极博学的口吻评判着画中陈列的各类果实。说来真巧，我们翻到了一幅格外精美的细密画，上面绘着金光灿灿、硕大无朋的梨子，掩映在柔嫩的绿叶之间。‘正是这个！’我欣喜若狂地叫嚷起来，‘这正是我适才向您提起的那种神果！何等巧妙！何等令人振奋！’
“‘当真如此？’我的东道主为这等巧合激动万分，‘我不得不再次感叹：这世间何其之小啊！’
“‘正是，’我说道，‘这正是被誉为“天之荣耀”的绝品名梨，您瞧瞧画中衬托的鸣虫与棚架，便知其果实是何等巨硕了。

===Original===

Of its succulence I must leave you to judge when you shall gather your first harvest. Of its highly saleable quality in the markets of the north you will, I trust, soon have satisfactory experience.’
“ ‘I shall indeed!’ said my host, now quite beside himself with the combined emotions of the collector and the man of property. He blessed again and again the day he had the good fortune to meet such a man as myself. Summoning his bailiff he gave orders for the wagon to be prepared over night and the horses to be ready by sunrise. ‘No, no,’ said I, ‘an hour before sunrise, if you please! I am determined, at whatever inconvenience to myself, to have the plants back here, at your house, on the night of the same day. I will risk no failure in this great affair!’ Again he blessed and thanked me, and when his dependents were dismissed took me aside and prepared to count out the money which would be required for my expenditure.
“ ‘You said 400 pieces of gold,’ said he, as he disposed the coins in little heaps of ten upon the table. ‘You had better make it 500, for there may have been fluctuations in the market since you last purchased, and it is good that you should have a margin.’
“I told him I thought the provision a wise one, but that I would account for every penny when he should next see me. And this, curiously enough, was my true intention, though I could not have given him any very exact date for our next meeting. I wrote him out a formal receipt in spite of his protests, remarking that business was business; and so that every formality should be accomplished I signed the document in the name of an old friend of mine, one Daoud-ben-Yacoub. I said I would further have affixed my seal had I possessed one, but placed as I was, no such instrument was available.
“ ‘The ball of your thumb will do,’ said the young man carelessly. His words brought me up rather sharp, and it was not without trepidation that I acceded to this chance request. But once more the inspiration of Heaven served me. I dexterously substituted my middle finger for my thumb as I pressed the wax thereunder. This arrangement,” said the old merchant, as he crossed the two fingers in the presence of his nephews, by way of illustration, “I recommend you upon every occasion of life. It is especially useful in those tyrannical countries where the police take the thumbmarks of innocent wayfarers.

===Chinese===

至于它的多汁甘美，等您迎接收成时自见分晓；而它在北方市集上的抢手畅销，我相信大人也很快便能亲身体验到了。’
“‘定当如此！’我的东道主应道，此刻他兼具藏家与庄园主的狂喜，已然按捺不住激动之情。他一遍又一遍地为自己有幸结识我这般高人而感谢上苍。他唤来庄头管事，吩咐连夜备好大车，骏马在日出时分备妥。‘万万不可，’我说道，‘烦请在日出前一个时辰备妥！无论自己受多大劳苦，我都决意在当天夜里将树苗运抵您的宅邸。在这件大事上，我绝不容许半分纰漏！’他再次对我千恩万谢，屏退左右后，将我拉到一旁，准备清点此行所需的盘缠货款。
“‘你说过需要400枚（400）金币，’他说着，将金币在桌上排成十枚一堆的小叠，‘你不如带上500枚（500）吧，自你上次采买以来行情或许有所涨落，手里留些富余总是好的。’
“我对他说这番考量极有远见，但保证下次相见时定会向他账目分明地核算每一枚小钱。说来奇怪，这确实是我的真心话，只不过下回相见的期日我实在无法给他个准头。尽管他一再推辞，我依然坚持公事公办地为他立下了一张正式收据；为了将各项手续办得滴水不漏，我用一位旧友‘达乌德·本·雅库布’的名字签了字。我表示若我有印章本该盖印为证，但眼下落魄至此，身边自无印章可用。
“‘按个大拇指手印便可，’年轻人漫不经心地说道。这话着实让我心头一紧，对于这突如其来的要求，我是怀着惴惴不安之情照办的。但上苍的神启再次眷顾了我。当我在火漆上按印时，巧妙地用中指掉包了大拇指。这种巧妙的变通，”老商人说着在侄儿们面前交叉起两根手指，现身说法道，“我奉劝诸位在人生的每个关头都多加利用。尤其是在那些残暴不仁的国度，官府差役动辄强行采集无辜过客的大拇指印，这招更是妙用无穷。

===Original===

I have used it a dozen times.﻿ ﻿… But to return to my tale.
“I pattered on to my kind host as I pressed my finger down, and thus distracted his attention from too close a watch on my hand. ‘This thumb mark,’ said I, releasing my middle finger from the wax, ‘this thumb mark is as good as any signature, I think; for Allah has made it the sign manual of all honest men; no two are alike. Remember, pray,’ I added laughingly, ‘that it was the thumb of my *right* hand.’
“ ‘I will,’ said he, laughing in turn. ‘As you say, these are mere formalities. I do not think the less of you for your insistence upon their performance.’
“With these words we parted in the greatest mutual satisfaction. He to dream of this fine new plantation and his coming wealth, but I to pour out my soul in prayer to my Maker and humbly to ask for further guidance.
“Next morning while it was yet dark I rose and mounted, the bailiff at my side, and the slaves taking the wagons behind. Early as was the hour my kind host was astir; he gave me his blessing for the tenth time on my departure and poured out petitions for my safe return. I hung the pouch of gold to my saddle bow, where I securely fastened it; I took the weapon with which he had kindly provided me in case of any misadventure by the road, and left him under the benediction of God. I thought a little sadly, as we rode out in silence through the gate and out on to the bare uplands again, how transitory were all human affections. How short had been this episode of friendship and hospitality! How brief even in the short course of one human life are these passages of complete confidence and brotherly kindness! When should we meet again?
“Of my journey there is little to be related. We plodded on, our pace necessarily determined by that of the slow wagon following us and my mind still turned upon what my future action should be; for, to tell you the truth, my dear nephews, Allah had vouchsafed me no revelation, in spite of my earnest prayers during the night, and I was still considering what turn I should give to the affair when once more that Infinite Mercy which has never failed me (or, at any rate, only for some short few days and even so but to chastise my pride) came to my rescue.

===Chinese===

这招我用过不下十几回……言归正传。
“我一边把手指按下去，一边不停地同我那位仁慈的东道主攀谈，以此分散他的注意，免得他过分紧盯着我的手。‘这拇指印，’我将中指从火漆上移开，说道，‘依我看和任何亲笔签名一样管用；因为真主将其赋予了一切诚实之人，作为独一无二的信记，天下绝无两个相同的指纹。烦请您记好，’我笑着补上一句，‘这可是我右手的大拇指。’
“‘我记下了，’他也跟着笑了起来，‘正如你所言，这些不过是繁文缛节。你执意讲究手续完备，我不仅不怪你，反而更敬重你。’
“说完这番话，我们在极其融洽的彼此欣慰中各自回房。他去梦见那座崭新的壮丽果园和滚滚而来的财富，而我则倾心向造物主虔诚祈祷，谦卑地祈求神明进一步指引迷津。
“次日拂晓前天色漆黑，我便翻身下榻跨上马鞍，庄头管事策马伴我身侧，奴仆们赶着大车尾随其后。尽管时辰尚早，仁慈的东道主却早已起身；临别之际他第十次为我祈福，喋喋不休地祈求我平安归来。我将那一袋沉甸甸的金币系在马鞍桥上扎紧；又带上他为防沿途变故而好心赠予我的防身兵刃，在真主的庇佑下辞别了他。当我们默默穿过大门、重返光秃的高地时，我心中略感凄凉地思忖着：人世间的种种温情是何等短暂！这段友谊与款待的插曲何其匆促！即便在短暂的一生中，这等彻底的信任与手足般的温存又是何其罕见！我们不知何时才能重逢？
“此后路上的行程倒无甚可表。我们缓步前行，步伐不得不被身后尾随的笨重慢车所拖累；我的脑子则一刻不停地盘算着接下来该如何脱身脱险。因为老实跟你们说吧，我亲爱的侄儿们，尽管我整夜苦苦祈求，真主却未曾向我降下半分神示；正当我苦思冥想该如何给这场戏收场时，那从未抛弃过我的无量仁慈（或者说，充其量只抛弃过我区区几天，且那也是为了惩戒我的傲慢）再次赶来搭救我了。

===Original===

“There are, in this country, deep gullies called nullahs, the course of streams which run but rarely on these heights, but which, when they run, dig their channels deeply into the friable soil. The crossing of these gullies by any rolling vehicle is something of a business. As we reached one, therefore (they came at intervals of two or three miles) the bailiff and I were careful to dismount and help the slaves with the wheels.
“It was while thus engaged during our careful drop into the second nullah that the inspiration from on high flashed into my brain. I perceived that the wheels of the wagon were fastened to the hub with wooden pins, one of which, at the off-hind wheel which I was holding back, looked a little loose. The slaves had their backs turned to me, holding back the front wheels, and checking the horses; at the other hind wheel, with the body of the front wagon between us and concealing his view from mine, strained and heaved the bailiff, a fat, elderly rogue, unaccustomed to such work. I pulled out the pin, threw it into the depths of the neighbouring scrub, and as I did so continued to cry, ‘Steady there! Steady! So! Hold hard! That’s better! Woa-oh! Stand by!’ and other words of the sort, which showed my interest in the operation. I could see the wheel wobbling as we crossed the flat bed of the nullah. At the foot of the far rise it was nearly off. The time had come. ‘Now!’ I cried suddenly, ‘*All together!* Whip up the horses and shove!’ The whips cracked, the slaves hauled at the traces, the horses strained, up went the wagon, off came the wheel and the whole collapsed upon one side with a great din and with a sharp cracking, as though something had given way.
“And so it proved to be; for the main axle, though not snapped, had split; so there we were with the wagon out of service for the moment, the axle unsure, a hind wheel off, and the whole contraption on its side.
“The bailiff was seriously disturbed. It seemed that my kind host was a firm master, that he had his moments of sharp temper; and the bailiff bewailed his fate and considered what awaited him on his return. I laughed good-naturedly and reassured him.
“ ‘Come, come,’ said I, ‘it is no great matter! I understand these things. Do you ride on towards the town. I will help the slaves put back the wheel.

===Chinese===

“在那片地界，横亘着不少被称为‘干谷’的深沟险壑，那是高地上罕见的旱溪冲刷出来的河道；一旦山洪暴发，便会在松软的泥土中切出极深的沟渠。驾驶任何带有轮子的车辆穿过这类深壑，都是件极其吃力的难事。因此每当我们来到一处深沟前（大约每隔两三英里便有一处），管事和我都会小心翼翼地下马，协助奴仆们稳住车轮。
“正当我们齐心协力将大车小心翼翼放进第二处干谷的当儿，来自至高主宰的灵感如闪电般劈进了我的脑海。我敏锐地注意到，大车的车轮是用木销钉固定在轮毂上的，而我正在后方把持着的右后轮上，那根销钉似乎略有松动。此时奴仆们背对着我，正拼力扯住前轮、勒紧辕马；在另一侧的后轮边，庞大的车厢将视线阻隔得严严实实，那个未曾干过重活、大腹便便的年迈老滑头管事，正气喘吁吁地弓着身子推车。我趁机一把拔出木销，甩进了路旁的灌木丛深处，嘴里却一刻不停地大声嚷嚷：‘稳住！稳住！好！拉紧！这就对了！驭——！站稳了！’尽是这类显示我对差事何等卖力关切的号子。在穿过平坦的干涸谷底时，我眼睁睁看着那只车轮摇摇晃晃；待到大车行至对岸坡脚处，轮子已经快要脱落了。千钧一发之际到了。‘就是现在！’我冷不丁大吼一声，‘大家一起用力！抽马，使劲推！’皮鞭啪啪作响，奴仆们死命拽动挽绳，挽马引颈发力，大车猛地一冲，车轮应声脱落，整辆大车伴随着一阵轰然巨响和木料断裂的脆响，歪斜坍塌在了一侧。
“果不其然；主轴虽未彻底折断，却也裂开了一道大缝；大车就这么彻底趴了窝，车轴摇摇欲坠，一只后轮滚落一旁，整副家伙歪倒在地。
“管事吓得面如土色。看来我那位仁慈的东道主御下极严，发作起来脾气极为暴烈；管事哭丧着脸哀叹自己的命苦，一想到回去后等待着自己的下场便不寒而栗。我却宽厚地哈哈大笑，出言宽慰他。
“‘得啦，得啦，’我说道，‘这算得了什么大不了的祸事！我最懂这些摆弄家什的门道。你只管先骑马奔市镇去，我留下来帮奴仆们把车轮复位。

===Original===

We will make some sort of jury pin to put into the hub, we will tie a rope round the cracked axle, and all will be well. We are men enough between us to repair the wagon, but you, as I say, ride forward. I shall soon catch you up.’
“The bailiff was relieved at this proof of my efficiency and good will, and delighted to be released from the work to which he was quite unused. He rode on at a moderate pace while the slaves and I heaved the wagon back upright. I fashioned a jury pin out of a piece of the scrub and I was careful to make it too weak for its work. We bound a piece of rope round the axle and when all this was done I bade them go forward carefully lest a further accident should befall. I then rode on smartly to catch up the bailiff, who had by this time got about a mile ahead. As I neared him I looked back from an intervening rise of land. It was as I had anticipated. The jury pin had given way, and the wagon was on its side again: but the lift of land soon hid it from me and down on the further slope I caught up the bailiff, ambling along. ‘The wagon is all right,’ said I, ‘but it will have to go rather slow.’ He gave a great sigh of content. ‘Thanks be!’ said he. ‘Truly you are a genius!’
“ ‘Not at all!’ said I modestly. ‘It is quite a small accident and of a sort to which I am accustomed; but do you ride back now that everything is well, for the slaves are uneducated men’ (this sort of flattery is honey to bailiffs), ‘and will need someone of your standing to moderate their pace and to check their horses and to see that the wagon comes on in good condition. Go very carefully, for the axle is weak. When you shall reach the town we will get a proper pin and have everything put right in an hour or two, meanwhile I will go forward and we will make an appointment at the market gardener’s, which you will reach, I think, some three-quarters of an hour after myself. For we are now, I take it, some couple of hours from the town.’
“ ‘You are right,’ said the bailiff, ‘you have but to follow the track and we will come after you.’ With that he turned back. Once I had seen him disappear behind the rise of the hill I dug my spurs sharply into my poor horse and went at top speed across the *bled*.
“I am a poor rider and had not my saddle been ample and my stirrups weighty I should have fallen. But Providence was with me once again.

===Chinese===

咱们就地削一根临时木销插进轮毂，再用绳索把开裂的车轴紧紧捆牢，万事便齐备了。凭我们这几号人，修好大车绰绰有余；至于您嘛，正如我所说，只管先行策马前去。我随后很快便能追上您。’
“见我如此精明强干又仗义体贴，管事长舒了一口气；能从这辈子从未干过的粗活中脱身，更是让他喜出望外。他便骑上马以不紧不慢的步子先行一步，我和奴仆们则合力将大车重新扶正。我从灌木丛里折了一截枝条削成临时木销，手上却暗中使诈，故意削得细弱不堪用。我们又用绳子把车轴胡乱缠了几圈，待这一切敷衍停当，我便叮嘱奴仆们小心翼翼地慢慢挪动，免得再遭横祸。随后我拍马飞奔，前去追赶管事，此时他已走出去大约一英里路了。行至隆起的高冈时，我勒马回头张望。果不出我所料：那根临时木销早已崩断，大车再次歪倒在路旁；但起伏的地势很快遮挡住了视线。在前方缓坡下，我赶上了信马由缰的管事。‘大车修妥了，’我说道，‘只是眼下走得极慢。’他长长地舒了一口惬意的大气：‘谢天谢地！’他由衷赞叹道，‘您当真是个旷世奇才！’
“‘哪里哪里！’我谦逊地应道，‘区区微末小祸，我平素经历得多了；既然大局已定，您不妨赶快策马折返回去压阵，毕竟那些奴仆全是粗鄙无知之辈’（这类奉承对管事最受用不过），‘必须得有您这般体面有威望的人从旁督导，约束马速，照看挽马，盯紧大车完好无损地运到。务必千万小心，那根车轴可经不起折腾。等诸位到了市镇，咱们寻一根结实的铁销，花上一两个时辰便能彻底修葺完好；眼下我先行一步，去跟苗木行的花匠掌柜定好交货事宜，我想您大概比我晚到大半个时辰——毕竟从这儿到市镇，充其量也就两个时辰的路程。’
“‘您说得极是，’管事点头称善，‘您只管顺着大路往前走，我们随后就到。’说罢他调转马头折返回去。我一见他的身影消失在山坡后头，便将马刺狠狠扎进坐骑两肋，在荒原（bled）上纵马狂奔。
“我骑术极烂，若非马鞍宽大、马镫沉重，我早就被掀翻在地了。但天意再次与我同在。

===Original===

I came through a gap of rocks and saw, immediately before and below me, the white domes and flat roofs of a large city, and just outside the gate a fine plantation of young fruit trees, which I recognized as the nursery gardener’s.
“I had ridden past his house and grounds, and admired the young pear trees especially (a fine collection) when a useful thought occurred to me, and I acted on it at once, eager though I was to save time. I turned back and said to the slave at the gate of the plantation, ‘I have a message for your master. Tell him that if anyone asks for Daoud-ben-Yacoub, he has bidden me say that he went back by a shortcut to help his companions with a broken wagon.’ I then turned again and rode off towards the city walls.
“I approached the town and rode through the gate with dignity in the new fine clothes the young lord had given me. I nodded in a superior manner to the guard and made straight for the opposite entrance to the city. A horse fair was proceeding. I put up mine at an inn, took off the bag of gold (which was heavy, but not too heavy to be carried) walked towards the market and asked where I could best purchase a horse. The name of a horse-seller was given me. I approached him, failed to believe all that he told me with regard to the beast he offered, but said it would be enough for my purpose. I had not an idea whither to fly, yet fly I must, for sooner or later the bailiff or my late master himself must follow. I knew nothing of the country nor the names of its towns nor of the roads. I took refuge in a piece of diplomacy. As I paid for the horse, I said to the seller, that I had to reach my mother’s house in the next city before sundown, and that I hoped my purchase was able to carry me that far in the remaining half-day.
“ ‘Half a day’s riding?’ answered the merchant in astonishment. ‘I know not how you ride! If you mean the town of Taftah it is not more than three hours’ going for any reasonable mount.’
“ ‘Is that so?’ said I in surprise. ‘I am a stranger and I can only believe what I was told. But you know how vague these country people are. I was assured that this was the road to Taftah,’ and here I pointed through the eastern gate.
“ ‘Yes! That is the road,’ he said. ‘You can easily reach your mother’s house before evening upon this beast,’ he said, clapping its crupper.

===Chinese===

我穿过一处石隙，眼前豁然开朗，正下方呈现出一座大城的座座白色穹顶与平顶民居；城门外正坐落着一大片整齐幼嫩的果林，我一眼便认出那正是苗圃花匠的庄园。
“我骑马经过他的宅邸与苗圃，特别驻足观赏了那些幼嫩的梨树（好一批名贵树种！）。此时一个极为有用的念头闪过心头，尽管我急于赶路，还是立刻付诸行动。我拨转马头，对守在苗圃大门口的奴仆说道：‘我有句话交代给你家主人。若有人打听达乌德·本·雅库布（Daoud-ben-Yacoub），便转告他，达乌德抄近路折回去帮同伴修坏掉的大车去了。’说完我再次调转马头，径直朝着城墙奔去。
“我行至城下，身着年轻贵胄赠予我的崭新华服，仪态威严地穿过了城门。我倨傲地朝守卫微微颔首，径直朝城池另一侧的城门走去。城里正举行一场马市。我把马匹寄存在一家客栈，解下沉甸甸的金币褡裢（虽重，随身携带倒也不成问题），踱步走向集市，打听何处能买到最好的良驹。旁人给我指引了一位马贩的名字。我走到他跟前，虽对他吹嘘坐骑的花言巧语一个字也不信，但料想那马已足够应付我的需求。我压根不知该往何处逃遁，但逃是一定要逃的，因为庄头管事或我那位前东家迟早会沿途追来。我对本地的水土、城邑的名称与道路一无所知。我便耍了一记机巧的外交手腕。在付钱买马时，我对马贩说道，我必须在日落前赶到隔壁城池的母亲家中，但愿买下的这匹坐骑能支撑我在剩下半天里跑完全程。
“‘骑马小半天？’马贩惊愕地反问，‘我不知您是怎么个骑法！倘若您指的是塔夫塔（Taftah）镇，任何一匹像样的坐骑跑过去充其量也就三个时辰（3）的路程。’
“‘果真如此？’我故作诧异道，‘我是外乡人，人家怎么说我就怎么信了。不过您也晓得，乡下庄稼汉说话向来没个准数。人家信誓旦言说出东门便是去往塔夫塔的大道，’说着我朝东城门指了指。
“‘对！正是那条道，’他拍了拍马臀说道，‘骑上这匹畜生，您保管能在天黑前轻轻松松赶到尊堂府上。’

===Original===

‘Without doubt you will be there long before the prayer: and God be with you, Hassan!’ For, incidentally, it was as Hassan that I had done business with him.
“I paid him ten pieces of gold for the beast. (It was more than it was worth). I humbly repeated his prayer which I felt did apply with peculiar force to me now, for I was conscious that I was once more under the beneficent guidance of Heaven﻿—who could not be with that heavy pouch now hanging again on his saddle? I rode out therefore confidently, quite careless whether I killed the beast or no in my rapid progress and brought it into Taftah well within three hours in such a state that I was delighted to find a purchaser (to whom I gave my name as Abdurram, and my profession as that of a leather dresser), who offered but five pieces of gold: I was glad to be rid of the horse and him at that.
“Time still pressed. I might be traced. I knew not what accidents had occurred upon the road behind me, whether indeed those poor fools had managed to mend the wagon again, if not, whether the bailiff would have the courage to tell his master or ride on to find me in the first town. If he had so ridden on he might find evidences of my departure, and even (more doubtfully) of my second horse and its purchase.
“But though time pressed it would have been fatal to show any too great speed. I therefore sauntered very gradually in my fine clothes, afoot, bearing my pouch in my hand concealed under the fold of my garment, until I reached a gathering of merchants outside a sort of exchange which this town of Taftah boasted, like others of the neighbourhood, in the vicinity of the governor’s palace.
“It was there that, during a conversation which, for all my anxiety, I took care to make slow and dignified, I learned how *dates* were in great demand in a large city called Laknes, about two weeks’ journey beyond the hills. It was, to a business man like myself, a most fascinating story that I heard! The people of that far country were passionately fond of dates and gave that fruit the briskest market imaginable. Their appetite had grown all the more formidable since the next people immediately beyond them had passed a law prohibiting the culture and sale of all dates on account of the toothache sometimes arising from that fruit. With this reduction of supply Laknes became more of a bidder for dates than ever.

===Chinese===

‘毫无疑问，您在晌礼之前便能赶到；愿真主与你同在，哈桑（Hassan）！’顺便提一句，方才正是借着哈桑的名头，我才同他把买卖办妥的。
“我付给他10枚（10）金币买下这匹牲口（价钱远超这马的实际成色）。我谦恭地回敬了他的祈福，心中深感这句祝福于眼下的我而言有着奇妙的感应；因为我深切地意识到，自己再次蒙受着上苍的洪福庇佑——摸着马鞍旁重新挂着的沉甸甸的金袋，任谁能不作此想呢？于是我豪情满怀地策马出城，一路风驰电掣，浑不顾会不会把这牲口活活累死；不到三个时辰，我便将马催进了塔夫塔（Taftah）镇。此时马匹已累得脱了相，所幸我撞见一个买主（我对这人自称名叫阿卜杜拉姆，是个皮匠），他开价区区5枚（5）金币——能把这匹马连同买主一并打发掉，我已是求之不得了。
“时间依然紧迫。我随时可能暴露行迹。我全然料不到身后的大道上出了何等变故，那些蠢奴仆究竟修没修好大车，若是没修好，管事是敢回府向东家如实招供，还是会一路骑到前头市镇来寻我。倘若他追进市镇，便可能查探到我离去的蛛丝马迹，甚至（虽说不大可能）查出我换乘第二匹马的底细。
“然而时局虽急，若在街头慌张奔逃则是自取灭亡。于是我身着锦袍，手里将金币袋藏在衣褶之下，以从容舒缓的步态信步闲逛，直逛到一处商贾云集的场所——这塔夫塔镇同邻近城镇一样，在总督府邸附近也建有一处规模不小的交易所。
“便是在那里，尽管心中焦虑如焚，我却格外注重维持语调的从容与尊贵。在同众人的攀谈中，我得知隔着重山、约两周路程之外一座名为拉克内斯（Laknes）的巨邑，‘椰枣’竟奇货可居！对于像我这样的生意人来说，听到的消息简直令人心醉神迷！那遥远异邦的子民对椰枣痴迷如狂，形成了难以想象的炽热市面。而尤其刺激他们胃口的，是与他们紧邻的另一个属国刚刚颁布了律法，因牙痛之虞而严禁一切椰枣的种植与贩售。供给的大幅锐减，使拉克内斯市面上对椰枣的争夺达到了前所未有的狂热境地。

===Original===

Great rewards were offered to any taking the fruit to such a market. The last advices, not a month old, quoted thirty dinars the kantar and were rising. The merchants designed to despatch a caravan the day after the morrow.
“With equal leisure and dignity I left them after this little talk and made it my immediate business in the next half-hour to procure with the capital at my disposal a number of camels and a couple of bales of dates for each, together with a few slaves that should conduct the caravan to its destination; I also hired a free man for a leader, as he was acquainted with the road.
“By this time it was nearly dark. I had given orders (in order to conceal my movements) that I should not start till late in the week, but I had also given a child a small coin to come up to me, upon a signal, with a piece of paper folded, upon which indeed nothing was written. Just as the camels were being driven off to their litter, I signalled to the child, who ran up and gave me the note. I opened it before the head man, put on an air of great perturbation, and said, ‘This message changes all my plans! I fear I disturb you, but will you start out tonight?’
“ ‘Willingly,’ said he. ‘We have provisions, and I know a good place on the road where we can purchase more tomorrow. The weather is warm and if your business demands haste, it may be better to march during the cool.’ The slaves (who were not consulted) were no doubt agreeable enough. We set out and all that night went our way.
“It was a monotonous journey through an arid land, with few towns or villages, sufficient watering, but no more. Though I pressed the pace we lost no beasts, and on the twelfth day, with the cool of the evening, we reached Laknes.
“My camels were parked, I took my place in the chief inn of the city (under the name of Ishmaïl-of-Taftah, merchant), and my first act before ordering a meal was, again, from the very bottom of my heart, to thank Allah for the return of his mercies. My capital was, indeed, nearly exhausted. I had but a few pieces of gold left in my garment, and the pouch was empty; but there was my solid row of camels and my fine cargo of dates. I made no doubt I should sell at a good profit next day and that my career was once more launched.

===Chinese===

凡是将椰枣运抵那片市集的人，都会获得重金酬谢。不足一个月前的最新行情报称每坎塔尔（kantar）30第纳尔（30），且仍在看涨。当地客商正盘算着在后天清晨发出一支商队。
“一番简短闲聊之后，我依旧带着十足的悠闲与尊贵辞别众人，并在随后半个时辰内，以手头掌握的本钱迅速采办了一批骆驼，为每峰骆驼配齐两捆椰枣，并雇下几名押运商队前往目的地的奴仆；我还雇佣了一名熟稔道路的自由民充任向导。
“此时天色已近全黑。为了掩人耳目，我原本传下号令本周晚些时候再动身；但我暗中赏给一个孩童一枚小钱，命他看我手势送上一张叠好的纸条，上面其实只字未写。正当骆驼被牵往槽头歇息之际，我向那孩子递了个眼色，那孩子飞奔上前将纸条呈递给我。我当着商队头领的面拆开信笺，装出一副惊惶失措的神情说道：‘此信打乱了我所有的计划！虽恐打扰诸位，不知今夜能否立刻拔营起程？’
“‘十分乐意，’向导答道，‘我们备有干粮，且我知道前路上一处明日便可补给的好去处。天色燥热，若是您的生意催得紧，趁着夜凉行军反倒更好。’至于奴仆们（本就无人征询他们的意见），自然也毫无异议。我们便连夜拔营，披星戴月踏上了征途。
“那是一段穿行于焦渴荒漠的枯燥跋涉，沿途城镇村落寥寥无几，水源勉强够用而已。尽管我一再催促脚程，牲口却未曾折损半头；到了第十二天（12）凉爽的黄昏时分，我们终于抵达了拉克内斯（Laknes）。
“骆驼被安顿在驼场，我（以‘塔夫塔商人伊斯梅尔’的名义）住进了城中首屈一指的大客栈。在吩咐备饭之前，我做的第一件事，便是从心底再次虔诚感谢真主降下的恩慈重新降临。我的盘缠确实已消耗殆尽，衣袍里只剩下区区几枚金币，褡裢已然见底；但眼前有一整排健壮的骆驼和成捆的上等椰枣。我毫不怀疑次日定能卖出巨利，我的远大前程再次扬帆起航了。

===Original===

I took care to speak to all openly of my arrival, to hint at my wealth, to make all familiar with the name of Ishmaïl-of-Taftah, a merchant in dates which it was proposed to offer next day in the market. For there are occasions, my dear nephews, in commerce when it is perfectly advisable to tell the truth and even to spread it abroad.”
With these unexpected words the merchant Mahmoud suddenly ceased his tale, for the shriek of the muezzin was heard rending the air. The nephews rose and bowed. “We trust,” said the eldest, “that when we next appear we shall find you, my dear uncle, climbing from greatness to greatness in the story you still have to unfold.”
“Alas, my children,” answered the venerable sage, “I fear you must hear of other disappointments before the goal is reached!”
At this the youngest boy put forth his lower lip, which trembled, and began screwing up his eyes.
“Stop!” said the merchant testily. “Stop! My little fellow! I have had enough of this!”
“Oh, Uncle,” sobbed the boy, “I cannot bear to think that perhaps all that new wealth will be stolen from you.”
“Stop, I say!” shouted Mahmoud angrily, and half-rising, “I tell you I have had enough of it! I appreciate your motive. I admire your judgment. It is marvellous in so young a child. But I cannot be disturbed with useless tears at things so long past. You show too great a sympathy. You are too sensitive my dear.”
The child saluted, assumed a more equable appearance, and followed his brothers out of the room, while Mahmoud, his equipoise a little disturbed by the incident, set himself right by the simple process of drawing from one sleeve a handful of coins and counting them out slowly into the other: a pastime which never failed to restore him to the best of tempers.

===Chinese===

我处处毫不避讳地高谈自己的到来，隐隐炫耀着自己的财力，让满城百姓都耳熟能详‘塔夫塔商人伊斯梅尔’的名头，以及次日将在市集上发售的极品椰枣。因为，我亲爱的侄儿们，在商场之中，有时候如实相告甚至大张旗鼓地宣扬真相，才是最精明的上上之策。”
老商人马哈茂德这番出人意料的话音刚落，便戛然而止，因为宣礼员高亢刺耳的呼拜声已然划破长空。侄儿们纷纷起立鞠躬。“我们坚信，”长侄说道，“当我们下一回前来拜谒时，在叔父尚未讲完的传奇里，定能得见您在功名利禄的阶梯上从辉煌走向更大的辉煌。”
“唉，我的孩子们，”这位饱经沧桑的长者答道，“我唯恐在达成夙愿之前，你们还得听我经历别的惨淡挫折呢！”
听到这话，最小的男孩撅起下唇，嘴唇微微颤抖，眼圈也红了。
“打住！”商人没好气地喝道，“打住！小家伙！我受够这套了！”
“呜呜，叔父，”男孩抽泣着说，“一想到您新积攒的那些万贯家财可能又全被人偷个精光，我就心疼得受不了。”
“我叫你打住！”马哈茂德怒喝一声，半支起身子，“我跟你说了，我受够这套了！我体谅你的好心，也钦佩你的悟性——这么小的孩子能有这般见识当真了得。但为了陈年旧事流这些毫无用处的眼泪，未免太扫我的兴了。你的同情心太过泛滥，心肠也未免太脆弱了，我的小乖乖。”
孩子行了个礼，神色恢复了平静，跟着哥哥们走出了房间。因这一阵风波而心境稍受扰动的马哈茂德，则熟练地从一只衣袖里摸出一大把金币，慢条斯理地一枚一枚数进另一只衣袖：这桩消遣向来能百试不爽地让他重新喜笑颜开。

## II *Al-Durar*, or “The Pearls” / 第二章 珍珠
===Original===

A week later, at the hour of public executions and beheadings, the seven boys were again assembled cross-legged at the feet of their revered uncle, who, when he had refreshed them with cold water, and himself with a curious concoction of fermented barley, addressed them as follows:
“You will remember, my lads, how I was left cut off from my dear home and from all companions, in a strange country, and with no more than 1,500 dinars with which to face the world. This sum may seem to you large, but I can assure you that to the operations of commerce” (and here the merchant yawned) “it is but a drop in the ocean; and I had already so far advanced during one brief week in my character of financier that I gloomily considered how small a sum that 1,500 was wherewith to meet the cunning, the gluttony, and the avarice of this great world. But a brief sleep (which I took under a baobab tree to save the cost of lodging) refreshed at once my body and my intelligence, and with the next morning I was ready to meet the world.”
Here the merchant coughed slightly, and addressing his nephews said: “You have doubtless been instructed at school upon the nature of the baobab?”
“We have,” replied his nephews, and they recited in chorus the descriptions which they had been taught by heart from the textbooks of their academy.
“I am pleased,” replied their uncle, smiling, “to discover you thus informed. You will appreciate how ample a roof this singular vegetable affords.
“Well, I proceeded under the morning sun through a pleasantly wooded and rising country, considering by what contrivance of usury or deceit I might next increase my capital, when I saw in the distance the groves and white buildings of an unwalled town, to which (since large places, especially if they are not warlike, furnish the best field for the enterprise of a captain of industry) I proceeded;﻿ ﻿… and there, by the Mercy of Allah, there befell me as singular an adventure as perhaps ever has fallen to the lot of man.

===Chinese===

一周之后，正值闹市公开行刑斩首的时辰，七个（7）孩子再次盘腿围坐在备受尊崇的叔父脚边。叔父给他们端上凉水解渴，自己则抿了一口用发酵大麦酿制的奇特饮品，随后开口对他们说道：
“孩子们，想必你们还记得，我当时如何被迫同挚爱的故土以及所有同伴天各一方，流落异国他乡，手里仅攥着区区1,500第纳尔（1500）去闯荡江湖。在你们眼里这笔钱或许像个大数目，但我可以向你们保证，在商海风浪里，”说到这里，商人打了个哈欠，“这不过是沧海一粟；在那短短的一周里，我在金融巨鳄的境界上已经参悟得极深，以致我愁眉不展地思忖着：这点区区1,500第纳尔（1500），拿什么去招架这滚滚红尘中的奸诈、贪婪与饕餮？所幸（为了省下住店脚钱而在猴面包树下打的）一场短短的小憩，同时恢复了我的体力与灵智；到了翌日清晨，我已整装待发，准备与这大千世界见个高下了。”
说到这里，商人轻轻咳嗽了一声，向侄儿们问道：“你们在私塾里想必听先生讲过猴面包树的特性吧？”
“听过的，”侄儿们齐声应道，随即便异口同声地背诵起学院课本里死记硬背下来的树种释义。
“甚好，”叔父微笑着答道，“见你们这般博学，老夫甚感欣慰。你们自当领会，那种奇特的草木能提供何等广阔的一顶遮阴大伞。
“言归正传，在晨光熹微中，我穿行在一片树木葱郁、地势渐起的高原上，脑中不住盘算着下一步究竟该用何等盘剥放贷或欺诈手腕来扩充我的本钱。此时我望见远处掩映在一片树丛中的座座白色房舍，那是一座未筑城墙的市邑。既然大市镇（尤其是缺乏尚武之风的地方）向来是实业巨子大展拳脚的沃土，我便径直奔了过去……在那儿，承蒙真主的仁慈（Mercy of Allah），竟降临到我头上一桩堪称人间罕见的奇遇。

===Original===

“I had not taken up my place in the local caravanserai for more than an hour﻿—I had met no likely fool, and my plans for the future were still vague in my head﻿—when an old gentleman of great dignity, followed by an obsequious officer and no less than six Ethiopian slaves, approached me with deep reverence, and proffering me a leathern pouch, of a foreign kind, the like of which I had never seen before, asked me whether I were not the young man who had inadvertently left it upon a prayer-stone at a shrine outside the city.
“I seized the pouch with an eager air, held it up in transports of joy, and kissing it again and again said, ‘Oh! my benefactor! How can I sufficiently thank you! It is my father’s last gift to me and is all my viaticum as well!’ with which I fell to kissing and fondling it again, pressing it to my heart and so discovered it to be filled with coins﻿—as indeed I had suspected it to be.
“Into so active an emotion had I roused myself that my eyes filled with tears, and the good old man himself was greatly affected. ‘I must warn you, young stranger,’ he said paternally, ‘against this thoughtlessness so common in youth! A great loss indeed had it been for you, if we had not had the good fortune to recover your property.’
“You may imagine my confusion, my dear nephews, at finding that I had been guilty of so intolerable a fault. I blushed with confusion; I most heartily thanked the old gentleman, not for his integrity (which it would have been insulting to mention to one of his great wealth) but for the pains he had taken to seek out a careless young man and to prevent his suffering loss.
“ ‘Nay,’ said that aged gentleman to me with a low and pleasant laugh, ‘you must not thank me. Perhaps had I myself come upon the treasure I might have thought it too insignificant to restore. But you must know that I am the chief magistrate of this city and that last evening my officer noticed from some distance a young man, apparently a stranger to this city, whom he describes as of your height and features, rise from the prayer-stone, but leave behind him some object which, in the gathering dusk, he could not distinguish. On his approach he found it was this purse of yours which some boys had already found and were quarrelling over, when he took it from them.

===Chinese===

“我在当地的驿站大车店落脚还不足一个时辰——既未撞见像样的冤大头，脑海中的后着也尚无定算——此时一位神态极其威严的老爷，在一员阿谀奉承的差官以及足足六名（6）埃塞俄比亚黑奴的簇拥下向我走来。他神情毕恭毕敬，递给我一只我从未见过的异域皮质钱袋，询问我是否就是适才疏忽大意、将钱袋遗落在城外圣祠祈祷石上的那位年轻后生。
“我一把抓过钱袋，脸上洋溢出急不可耐的狂喜，将那钱袋亲了又亲，口中连呼：‘啊！我的大恩人！我该如何重谢您的大恩大德！这可是家父留给我的最后一份薄礼，也是我出门在外的全部盘缠啊！’说着我又是一阵亲吻抚摩，将它紧紧贴在胸口，顺带摸出里面装满了沉甸甸的钱币——正如我暗中所料的那样。
“我的情绪表演得如此逼真投入，眼中甚至泛起了泪花；那位好心的老者见状也深为感动。‘年轻的异乡客，’他以长辈般的慈爱口吻叮嘱道，‘我不得不告诫你切莫这般毛手毛脚，年轻后生向来容易犯这毛病！若不是我们有幸替你寻回失物，你这一趟可就落得倾家荡产了。’
“我亲爱的侄儿们，你们尽可想象我得知自己犯下如此不可饶恕的过失时是何等无地自容。我涨红了脸，局促不安；我发自肺腑地向这位老先生道谢——并非称颂他的清正廉洁（向一位坐拥万贯家财的巨室提廉洁未免是种冒犯），而是感激他不辞辛劳四处寻访一个粗心大意的年轻人，免教我遭受灭顶之灾。
“‘哪里的话，’那位上了年纪的老爷发出一阵低沉而愉悦的笑声，对我说，‘你不必谢我。若是我自个儿拾着了这包银子，说不定还嫌它微不足道懒得奉还呢。不过你得知道，老夫乃是本城的首席法官；昨夜我的差官远远瞧见一位年轻后生从祈祷石上起身，看身形相貌与你一般无二，显然是个外乡人，但他起身时却落下了个物件，暮色昏暗中差官一时辨认不清。待差官走近前去，才发现正是你的这只钱包，几个孩童已然捡着了正在争抢，被差官一把夺了过来。

===Original===

He brought it to me with some description of your person: I thought you might well be at this caravanserai and brought it with me: I had the pleasure of hearing my officer, who now accompanies me, recognize you as we approached.’ That functionary bowed to me and I to him most ceremoniously, and as I did so I was rapidly revolving in my head what I had better do if the real owner should appear. I was torn between two plans: whether to denounce him as a thief before he could speak, or to run off at top speed.
“This preoccupation I dismissed lest the anxiety of it should appear upon my face.
“I again thanked this good old man most warmly and we entered into a familiar conversation. What was my delight at the close of it when he bade me without ceremony accept of his hospitality and come home to take a meal with him in his palace. I was eager for further adventures, and accompanied him with the greatest joy.
“Reclining at table, where there was served (as I need hardly inform my dear nephews) lamb stuffed with pistachio nuts, the old man asked me whence I had come, what was my trade, and whither I was proceeding.
“I answered (as I thought, prudently) that I had come from Aleppo, that I had been entrusted by my father with the sum in the purse he had so kindly restored to me, in order to purchase pearls, and that when the purchase was completed I had instructions to sell them in India in a market where my father was assured that pearls were rare and fetched the highest prices.
“ ‘This is indeed well found!’ exclaimed the old man, with enthusiasm. ‘I am myself seeking for someone to whom I may sell a magnificent collection of pearls inherited from my great-grandmother, an Indian begum. The old woman,’ he added nonchalantly enough, ‘was a miser; she kept the drops higgledy-piggledy in an old cedarwood box, and I confess myself quite ignorant of their value. Moreover, as I have taken a liking to you, I shall let you fix your own price, for I should much like to remember when my time comes that I had helped a friendless man in his first step to fortune; only, I am a little ashamed to appear to be making money out of an heirloom!’

===Chinese===

差官带着钱包来向我禀报，并描摹了你的容貌特征；我料定你十有八九会落脚在这家驿站，便亲自顺道带了过来。能听到你说这钱袋不仅挽救了你的生计，更是你令尊的临终信物，老夫着实欣慰之至。’
“那位法官又向我说了许多抚慰有加的话，甚至纡尊降贵地邀我去他的府邸共进午膳。我一心渴望招揽更多的奇遇，自是欣喜若狂地欣然领命前往。
“围坐在餐桌旁斜倚着用膳时，桌上呈上来的（无需我向亲爱的侄儿们多言）自然是塞满开心果的烤羔羊肉。老者问起我打何处来、做何营生、又意欲前往何方。
“我自以为精明谨慎地答道：我打阿勒颇（Aleppo）来，家父将他适才仁慈归还的那袋银钱托付给我，命我在此地收购一批珍珠；采买齐备之后，我奉命将珍珠运往印度发售，家父确信印度的珍珠奇缺，能卖出顶尖的暴利。
“‘当真是踏破铁鞋无觅处！’老者兴奋万分地赞叹道，‘老夫恰巧正四处物色买主，打算脱手一批祖传的绝品珍珠呢！那是我曾祖母——一位印度贵妇（begum）留下的遗产。那老太太，’他漫不经心地补充道，‘是个守财奴；她把那些珠子乱七八糟地堆在一只旧雪松木匣里，老夫对它们的行情实属一窍不通。既然老夫与你一见投缘，价钱就全由你自个儿定夺吧；待到老夫大限之日，回想起曾拉扯过一个举目无亲的年轻人迈出发家致富的第一步，心里也踏实。唯独变卖祖传之宝换钱这档子事，老夫面子上多少有些挂不住！’

===Original===

“While the old gentleman so spoke I was rapidly revolving in my mind what motive he could have for such an affection of indifference to wealth, when I recollected that he was the chief magistrate of the city, and immediately concluded that these pearls, being the property of local people, and obtained by him for nothing by way of bribes and other legal channels, he would both desire to have them sold at a distance and would let them go cheap.
“ ‘Nay,’ continued he, seeing that I hesitated as these thoughts occurred to me, ‘I will take no denial. For me it is but a mere riddance, and for you a most excellent bargain. Come, I trust your honest face and youthful candour. You shall take them at your own price! And I will even advise you of the city of India where you will find your best market.’
“Put thus, the offer, I will confess, attracted me; but I had already learned the wickedness of mankind (though not as yet, I am glad to say, my dear nephews, at my own expense), and I said that I would at least so far meet him as to take the jewels to a local merchant, invent some tale, as though they were my own, and see what sum might be offered for them. Only when I thus had some measure of their value could I honourably make an offer. I continued at some length in this strain, expressing a humble inability to judge, and the fear lest my capital might not be sufficient (which he pooh-poohed). I stipulated, for a reason you will soon perceive, that a slave of his should accompany me﻿—if only as a matter of routine﻿—for (said I) I was very jealous of my honour. He agreed, though he was good enough to call it a pure formality.
“I left the aged magistrate with many thanks and, accompanied by the slave, proceeded with the pearls to the jewel merchants’ quarter in the bazaar. I stopped before one of the richest and most reputed booths, and spreading the pearls before the merchant told him that I was compelled to sell these under order from authority as the end of a family dispute, to pay the dowry of my sister; that I therefore was in haste to settle and would take the least price he might choose to mention within reason. I was, said I, wholly in his hands. It was urgent for me that the bargain should be quickly completed, but before I could receive his cash I must hear the lowest figure he would name.

===Chinese===

“老者这般夸夸其谈之际，我脑海中飞速盘算着他假意视金钱如粪土的动机；旋即我猛然省起他是本城的首席法官，当即断定这批珍珠必定是搜刮本地百姓得来的赃物，是借由贿赂或巧立名目的司法手段空手套白狼得来的，因此他急于转手倒卖到远方销赃，并且乐得贱卖出手。
“‘别推辞了，’见我若有所思地犹豫不决，他继续催促道，‘老夫决不许你拒绝。对我而言不过是甩掉一件累赘，于你而言却是一桩天大的划算买卖。来吧，我信得过你这副忠厚的面孔和年轻人的赤诚。你开个价全拿去便是！老夫甚至可以指点你印度哪座城池才是脱手的最佳销路。’
“说句实话，这般条件确教我心动不已；然而我早就见识过了人世间的险恶（尽管我欣慰地告诉你们，亲爱的侄儿们，当时我尚未吃过切肤之痛），于是我答复说，我至少可以退一步：先带着这些珠宝去找本地的一位行商，编个由头谎称是自己的家当，打探一番旁人究竟肯出多少银子。唯有摸清了大致底价，我才能问心无愧地向他开价。我顺着这套说辞絮絮叨叨大肆表白了一番，声称自己眼拙不识货，又唯恐本钱微薄难以承接（他对此嗤之以鼻）。出于你们马上便能参透的缘由，我特意提出一条硬性要求：务必派一名府上的奴仆陪我同去——即便权当照章办事也好——因为（我说）我把自个儿的清誉看得比性命还重。他欣然应允，尽管他宽仁地称这纯属多此一举的形式。
“我向年迈的法官千恩万谢，在奴仆的陪同下，携带着那批珍珠奔赴巴扎里的珠宝商街区。我驻足在本城规模最大、声誉最隆的一家宝楼前，将珍珠在掌柜面前一一摊开，声称自己因官府断案解决家族纠纷，被迫限期变卖这些细软给妹妹凑嫁妆；因此我急于变现，只要他开出的价码合乎情理，哪怕再低我也全盘照收。我说，我全凭掌柜做主。我急需速战速决了结这笔买卖，但在我收下现银之前，必须先听听他能给出的最低底价。

===Original===

“While I thus spoke the slave stood respectfully behind me and listened to our conversation. The jewel merchant said that no class of merchandise was more distasteful to him than pearls; there was at this moment no market for them. It was impossible to purchase them save properly set and in regular sizes; and finally it was well known that pearls were the most unlucky of gems. It was quite impossible for him to offer more than 10,000 dinars, and even so he would doubtless be the loser by the transaction.
“When I heard this I rapidly wrote upon a slip of paper the following words:

> “ ‘**My Lord**﻿—The chief merchant in this city estimates your jewels at 10,000 dinars. I cannot, alas, provide that sum, and therefore I cannot honestly make an offer myself as I had hoped; if you desire to have them sold here I will faithfully execute your commission, but if you prefer that I should return them to you send me word. Meanwhile, I will still bargain here awaiting your reply.’

===Chinese===

“我这般巧舌如簧之际，奴仆恭恭敬敬地立在我身后，将我们的一字一句全听在耳中。珠宝掌柜拿腔拿调地表示，全天下的货色里他最瞧不上的便是珍珠；眼下市面上珍珠根本没有销路。除非镶嵌妥当且大小规整，否则断无收购的可能；况且世人皆知，珍珠乃是不祥之物。他满打满算顶多只能出到10,000第纳尔（10000），即便这价钱，这桩买卖他也注定要赔个底朝天。
“一听这话，我迅速在一张纸条上飞快写下如下字句：
> ‘大人——城中首屈一指的珠宝大商对您的宝物估价为10,000第纳尔（10000）。遗憾的是，小人囊中羞涩无力凑齐这笔巨款，因此无法如先前所愿正大光明地向您承购；倘若大人属意在本地发售，小人定当恪尽职守代为经办；若是大人情愿收回宝物，烦请传个口信。小人暂且在此继续讨价还价，恭候回音。’

===Original===

“I sent this note by the slave and begged him to give it to his master and to bring me an answer. The slave went off, and when I judged him to be well out of hearing I turned and said to the merchant, sighing: ‘Well, since you offer no more I must take what you offer; the slave whom you saw me despatch carried the news to my family; I burn when I think of how their scorn will mock my humiliation. I therefore said nothing true of the price. Indeed, I have set it down in that note as something much higher. But I submit, for, as I told you, I am pressed. Come, count me the money, and I will away.’
“The merchant, after I had handed over the pearls, counted me the money into yet another large leathern bag, which I shouldered, and with rapid steps bore out of the bazaar and soon out of the town itself, by a gate called the Bab-el-Jaffur, that is, the gate of innocence.
“Beyond the town walls was a long roll of dusty sloping land set here and there with dusty stunted bushes and having beyond it a high range of desert hills. A track led roughly rising across it, away from the town.
“I followed this track for one hour and then sat down (for my new fortune was heavy) and rested.
“As I thought it probable that my good old friend himself would return speedily with his slave to the bazaar, and as the complication of the affair might embroil me, I hid during the remainder of the day squeezed in a jackal’s earth beneath a bank. Before nightfall I ventured out and gazed about me, leaving my original pouch, my windfall and my big leathern bag of 10,000 dinars in the jackal’s earth while I surveyed the track.
“It was the hour I love above all others.
“The sun had just set beyond the distant ocean towards which my face was turned, and between me and which, upon the plain below﻿—for I had come to the rise of the mountain side﻿—lay the beautiful city I had just left. The fragrant smoke of cedarwood rose from some of its roofs as the evening fell. There was still hanging in the air the coloured dust of evening above the roads of entry, and there came faintly through the distance the cry of the muezzin.
“I was not so entranced by the natural beauty of the scene as to neglect the duty which this sound recalled.

===Chinese===

“我打发奴仆递送这张条子，恳请他务必面呈主子并为我带回回音。奴仆领命而去，待我估摸着他已走出听觉范围，我转身朝掌柜长叹一声道：‘罢了，既然掌柜再不肯加价，那我便依你开出的数目接下了；适才见我遣走的那名奴仆，乃是给家里报信去的；一想到家人会如何冷嘲热讽我的无能与耻辱，我便如坐针毡。因此我在纸条上对真实价码瞒天过海，写了个高得多的虚数。但我认命了，正如我适才所言，我被逼无奈急于用钱。来吧，点清银两，我拿了钱便走。’
“我交割了珍珠之后，掌柜将现银如数点进另一只硕大的皮口袋里。我将沉甸甸的皮袋扛上肩头，迈开大步疾步走出巴扎，不多时便穿过一座名为‘巴卜·贾福尔’（即‘清白之门’）的城门，彻底遁出了城外。
“城墙之外是一道绵延起伏、尘土飞扬的漫坡，零星点缀着蒙尘的低矮灌木，远处横亘着苍茫的荒山。一条崎岖小径蜿蜒攀升，通向远离城池的荒野。
“我沿着小径跋涉了一个时辰，随后坐倒在地（那笔新到手的横财委实太过沉重）歇脚喘息。
“我料想那位好心的老法官八成很快就会带着奴仆赶回巴扎对质，事态一旦败露势必引火烧身，于是在那一天剩下的时间里，我将身子死死挤进岸坡下一处豺狼废弃的土穴里藏身。直到夜幕降临前，我才敢探出身子四下张望；我把原本的小钱袋、那笔飞来横财以及那包装满10,000第纳尔（10000）的大皮袋留在豺穴里，自己则蹑手蹑脚勘察山径。
“那正是我生平最钟爱的时辰。
“夕阳刚刚沉入远方的重洋，我正面向大海，在我与苍海之间、下方的平原上——此刻我已经攀上了山腰的高处——坐落着我刚刚逃离的那座秀美城池。随着夜色降临，缕缕芬芳的雪松木炊烟从座座屋顶袅袅升起。进城的官道上方，暮色浸染的五彩浮尘依然在虚空中回荡；隐约之间，从辽远的天际传来了宣礼员高亢的呼拜声。
“我并未因这如画的美景而忘乎所以，乃至荒废了那声声呼唤所唤醒的天职。

===Original===

I fell immediately upon my knees and was careful to add to the accustomed prayers of that hour my heartfelt thanks for the Guidance and the Grace which had so singularly increased my fortunes in the last few hours.
“As I rose from these devotions I heard upon my right a low wailing sound and was astonished to discover there, seated hopelessly beneath a small shrub and waving his hands in grief, a young man of much my own height and appearance: but I flatter myself that not even in my most careful assumptions of innocence have I ever worn such a booby face.
“He was swaying slowly from side to side, and as he did so moaning a ceaseless plaint, the words of which I caught and which touched me to the heart. Over and over again he recited his irreparable loss. He had but that small sum! It was his patrimony! His sole security! How should he answer for it? who should now support him? or what should he do?
“So he wailed to himself in miserable monotone till I could bear it no longer, for I saw that I had by a singular coincidence come upon that poor young man whose pouch I had been given in error by the magistrate of the city.
“I bowed before him. He noticed me listlessly enough and asked me what I would. I told him I thought I could give him comfort. Was it not he, said I, who had left a certain pouch (I carefully described it) containing sundry coins upon a prayer-stone outside the city at this very same sunset hour of the day before? His despair was succeeded by a startling eagerness. He leapt to his feet, seized my arm, rose feverishly and implored me to tell him further.
“ ‘Alas,’ I said, ‘what I have to tell you is but little! I fear to raise your hopes too high﻿—but at any rate I can put you upon the track of your property.’
“ ‘Sir,’ said he, resuming his hopeless tone for a moment, ‘I have already done my best. I went to the chief magistrate of the city to claim it and was met by an officer of his who told me that the purse had already been delivered to its owner, suspected my claim and bade me return. But how shall I prove that it is mine, or how, indeed, receive it, since the abominable thief who took possession of it must by now be already far away?’
“ ‘You do him an injustice,’ said I. ‘It is precisely of him whom you uncharitably call a thief that I would speak to you.

===Chinese===

我当即双膝跪地，在平日例行的晚祷祷词之外，格外虔诚地倾注了我发自肺腑的感激之情，感谢上苍的指引与恩典在过去短短数个时辰内，以如此不可思议的方式让我的资财翻了数倍。
“当我做完祷告起身之际，忽听右侧传来一阵低沉的抽泣哀鸣。我惊讶地发现，就在一株矮灌木下，瘫坐着一个绝望的年轻人，正痛苦地挥舞着双手；他的身形相貌与我颇为神似——但我敢自负地说，即便在我装出一副最无辜的嘴脸时，也从未露出过这般愚不可及的蠢相。
“他身子前后微微摇晃，嘴里翻来覆去念叨着永无休止的哀怨；我听清了他的只字片语，心中不禁为之一动。他一遍又一遍地哀叹自己遭遇了无法挽回的浩劫：他手里仅有那么一笔微薄的钱财！那是他的全部家底！是他唯一的指望！他回去该如何向家里交代？今后又有谁来抚养他？他该何去何从？
“他就这般用凄凉单调的哭腔哀号着，直到我再也听不下去了；因为我敏锐地意识到，冥冥中的机缘巧合，竟让我撞见了那位真正的失主——那位城中法官阴差阳错把钱袋塞给我的倒霉青年！
“我朝他躬身行礼。他眼神空洞地瞥了我一眼，问我想干什么。我告诉他，我或许能给他带来宽慰。我问道，昨日日落时分，在城外圣祠祈祷石上遗落了一只装着若干银钱的皮钱袋（我细致入微地描摹了那钱袋的模样）的人，莫非正是阁下？他的绝望瞬间化作了惊人的狂喜。他猛地一跃而起，死死抓紧我的臂膀，浑身战栗着苦苦哀求我吐露实情。
“‘唉，’我说道，‘我所知晓的实属微不足道！我唯恐把您的胃口吊得过高——但无论如何，我能为您指明找回失物的线索。’
“‘仁兄，’他眼中的希冀又黯淡下去，重重叹了口气，‘小弟早已竭尽全力了！我先前奔赴首席法官府邸讨要，法官手下的差官却说钱包早被正主领走了，还怀疑小弟是来讹诈的，把我轰了出来。可我该如何证明那是我的？又该如何讨回？那个冒领钱财的千刀万剐的大盗，此刻想必早已远走高飞了！’
“‘你可冤枉好人了，’我说道，‘我正要同你谈起的，恰恰便是那位被你刻薄地斥为大盗的仁兄。

===Original===

You think that he is far away, whereas he is really at your hand whenever you choose to act, for this is the message that I bring you. He awaits you even now, and if you will present yourself to him he will restore your property.’
“ ‘How can you know this?’ said the young man, gazing at me doubtfully. ‘By what coincidence have you any knowledge of the affair?’
“ ‘It is simple enough,’ said I. ‘This person to whom your purse was given, and I, were in the same inn. We fell to talking of our adventures along the road, for he also was a stranger, and he told me the singular tale how he had recovered from the authorities a purse which he honestly thought his own, for it was very like one which he himself possessed; but that on finding his own purse later on in his wallet he was overwhelmed with regret at the thought of the loss he had occasioned; at the same time he made me his confidant, telling me that he intended to restore it this very evening at sunset to the authorities and that anyone claiming it after that hour and proving it was his could recover it at the public offices. But he warned me of one thing: the officers (he told me) were convinced (from what indication I know not, perhaps from the presence of something in the purse, or perhaps from something they had heard) that the owner dealt in *pearls*.’
“Here the young man interrupted me, and assured me he had never bought or sold pearls in his life, nor thought of doing so.
“I answered that no doubt this was so. But that when the authorities had a whim it was well to humour them. He would therefore do well to approach the officer who guarded the gate of the chief magistrate’s house, with the simple words, ‘I am the seller of pearls,’ on hearing which his path would be made smooth for him, and he would receive his belongings.
“The young man thanked me heartily; he even warmly embraced me for the good news I had given him, and felt, I fear, that his purse and his small fortune were already restored to him. It was a gallant sight to see him in the last of the light swinging down the mountain side with a new life in him, and I sincerely regretted from my heart the necessity under which I was to imperil his liberty and life. But you will agree with me, my dear nephews, that I could not possibly afford to have him at large.

===Chinese===

你以为他早已远走高飞，实则只要你愿意动弹，他眼下近在咫尺——因为这正是我替他带来的口信。他此刻正候着你呢，只要你肯去见他，他便会将失物原样奉还。’
“‘你怎会知晓这些？’青年满腹狐疑地凝视着我，‘天底下哪有这般巧事，你怎么会对这桩案子了如指掌？’
