“‘事情再简单不过了，’我说道，‘那位领走你钱包的仁兄，碰巧同我寄宿在同一家驿站。我们在路上攀谈起各自的遭遇——因为他也同是个外乡客——他向我吐露了一桩离奇遭遇：他说自己从官府手里领回了一只钱包，当时真心以为那是自个儿丢的，因为那模样同他自个儿的钱包委实太像了；可后来他在包裹底翻出了自己的钱包，一想到平白教旁人遭受损失，便悔恨交加、痛心疾首。他把我视作知己，向我透露说打算就在今夜日落时分将钱包物归原主上交官府，失主只要过了这个时辰前去衙门核验身份，便可顺利领回失物。但他特意叮嘱我一桩要紧事：据他所知，衙门里的官爷们咬定（他不知从何得来的风声，兴许是钱包里塞了什么凭证，亦或是捕风捉影的传闻），失主是个贩卖珍珠的古董客商。’
“说到这里，年轻人急忙打断我，赌咒发誓说他这辈子从未买卖过一颗珍珠，连这念头都未曾有过。
“我回答他说，这自然不假；但衙门里的青天大老爷们向来有些古怪癖好，顺着他们的性子哄一哄总是没错的。因此，他最好径直走向守卫首席法官府邸大门的差官，斩钉截铁地说出这句接头暗号：‘我是卖珍珠的。’只要差官一听这话，前路便会一马平川，他的失物自然物归原主。
“年轻人对我千恩万谢；为了这桩天大的喜讯，他甚至激动得同我紧紧相拥，唯恐自己的钱包和微薄家当不能立刻失而复得。目送着他在残存的暮色中步伐轻快、焕发新生般朝着山下飞奔而去，那副神采当真令人感佩；我发自内心为自己不得不将他的自由与性命置于险境而深表遗憾。但我亲爱的侄儿们，你们想必赞同我的决断：我无论如何也承担不起放任他在外头自由游荡的风险啊！

===Original===

“When he had gone and when it was fully night, there being no moon and only the stars in the warm dark sky, I rapidly took my burdens from their hiding-place and proceeded, though with some difficulty, up the mountain side, staggering under such a weight, and deviating from the track so that there should be less chance of finding myself interrupted.
“I slept for a few hours. I awoke at dawn. I counted my total fortune, and found that it was just about 12,000 dinars, the most of it in silver.
“Carefully concealing it again, I left its hiding-place and glided round the mountain until I came to a place where a new track began to appear, which led to a neighbouring village. Here I bought an ass, and returning with it to my hiding-place and setting my treasure upon it I went off at random to spend the day in travelling as rapidly as I might away from the neighbourhood by the most deserted regions.
“I came, a little before sunset, upon a hermit’s cave, where I was hospitably entertained and the tenant of which refused all reward, asking me only to pray for him, as he was certain that the prayers of youth and innocence would merit him a high place in Heaven.
“With this holy man I remained for some four or five days, passing my time at leisure in his retreat among the mountains, and feeding my donkey upon the dried grasses which I brought in by armfuls at dusk from the woodlands. Upon the fifth day of this concealment the hermit came in pensive and sad, and said to me:
“ ‘My son, with every day the wickedness of this world increases, and the judgment of God will surely fall upon it in a devastating fire! I have but just heard that the chief magistrate of our capital city, using as a dupe an innocent stranger, sold to one of the great jewellers of the place for no less than 10,000 dinars a quantity of pearls, every one of which now turns out to be false and valueless! Nay, I am told that the largest are made of nothing but wax! And, what is worse, not content with this first wickedness, the magistrate under the plea that the young stranger had disappeared confiscated the gems again and had the poor merchant most severely beaten! But﻿—worse and worse! the poor youth having innocently returned that very night to the city, was seized by the guard and beheaded.

===Chinese===

“待他走远，夜幕已彻底笼罩大地；夜空漆黑温热，没有月亮，唯有繁星闪烁。我飞速从隐匿处取出包袱家当，顶着沉重的重负，颇为吃力地朝山顶攀登；我特意偏离了官道羊肠小径，以此尽量避免半路撞见闲杂人等盘查。
“我睡了几个时辰，拂晓时分醒来。我盘点了一下全副身家，发现总数刚好约合12,000第纳尔（12000），其中大半全为沉甸甸的白银。
“我再次将金银细软悉心掩藏好，走出隐匿处，绕着山峦漫步，直到眼前呈现出一条通往临近村落的新小径。在那儿我买了一头毛驴，牵回藏宝处，把所有财宝驮上驴背；随后我漫无目的地动身，拣最荒无人烟的僻静荒野，以最快的脚程逃离这片地界。
“将近傍晚时分，我偶遇了一处隐士修行的石窟岩洞。洞中的隐士待客极其热情周到，坚决不肯收取半文酬资，唯独恳请我为他祷告祈福——因为他深信，少年纯洁无瑕之人的祈祷，定能为他在天国求得崇高的品位。
“我同这位圣人盘桓了四五天，在他的深山精舍中优哉游哉地消磨时光；每天傍晚，我便从山林间抱来大捆干草喂养毛驴。隐居至第五天时，隐士面带沉思与悲戚之色从外头归来，对我叹息道：
“‘孩子啊，这世间的罪孽当真是一天甚于一天，上苍的审判必定会化作毁灭的烈焰降临人间！老衲刚刚听闻，咱们国都的首席法官，竟拿一个无辜的外乡客充当幌子冤大头，将一批珍珠以足足10,000第纳尔（10000）的天价强行卖给了城中一家大珠宝商；如今真相大白，那批珍珠竟全都是一文不值的假货！甚至据说个头最大那几颗，纯粹是用蜡捏出来的！更丧尽天良的是，那法官犹嫌不足，借口说那个外乡年轻后生携款潜逃，硬是将那批假珠宝再次抄没没收，还将那位倒霉的珠宝掌柜打得皮开肉绽！然而——更骇人听闻的还在后头！那个可怜的后生竟在当夜懵懂无知地自投罗网重返城中，被卫兵当场拿下，咔嚓一声斩了首！

===Original===

Ya, ya,’ said the good old man, throwing up his hands, ‘the days increase, and their evil increases with them!’ ”
At this moment the hoarse and discordant voice of the public crier croaked its first note from the neighbouring turret, and the nephews, who had sat enraptured at their uncle’s tale, knew that it was time to disperse. The eldest brother therefore said:
“O uncle; before we go let me express the thanks of all of us for your enrapturing story. But let me also express our bewilderment at the absence of all plan in your singular adventures. For though we have listened minutely to all you have said, we cannot discover what art you showed in the achievement of any purpose. For instance, how could you know that the pearls were false?”
“I did not know it, my dear nephew,” replied the great merchant with beautiful simplicity, “and the whole was the Mercy of Allah!﻿ ﻿… But come, the hour of prayer is announced, and we must, following the invariable custom of the faithful, make yet another joint in my singular tale. Come, therefore, on this day week, shortly after the last of the public executions of the vulgar, and I will tell you of my further fortunes: for you must understand that the 12,000 dinars of which my story now leaves me possessed are”﻿—and here the honest old man yawned again and waved his hand﻿—“but a flea-bite to a man like me.”
His seven little nephews bowed repeatedly, and, walking backwards without a trip, disappeared through the costly tapestries of their uncle’s apartments.

===Chinese===

呀，呀！’好心的老隐士双手朝天哀叹道，‘年岁徒增，世道的险恶也随之与日俱增啊！’”
正在此时，邻近尖塔上传来了巡城传令官沙哑刺耳的第一声啼鸣，听得如痴如醉的侄儿们明白，该是离席散去的时辰了。于是长侄开口说道：
“伯父大人，临别之际，容侄儿代表众兄弟对您讲述这般引人入胜的传奇聊表谢忱。然而面对您那些离奇莫测的遭遇，我们难免为其中全无半点定计而困惑不已。尽管我们字字句句凝神细听，却实在瞧不出您在达成目的时究竟施展了何等神机妙算。比方说，您当初究竟如何料定那批珍珠是假货的呢？”
“我压根就不知道那是假货，我亲爱的侄儿，”巨贾以极其纯真的口吻答道，“这一切全赖真主的仁慈（Mercy of Allah）！……好啦，祈祷的时辰已至，依照信徒千古不变的规矩，咱们这出离奇的故事又该告一段落了。那么，下周的今天，在市井平民公开行刑斩首刚过片刻之时，再来听老夫讲述后来的造化吧——因为你们自当明白，眼下故事讲到这里我手头积攒的这12,000第纳尔（12000），”说到这里，诚实的老人再次打了个哈欠，挥了挥手，“对于老夫这般大人物而言，不过是九牛一毛罢了。”
他的七个小侄儿连连鞠躬，蹑手蹑脚一步三回头、不曾被绊倒地退出了房间，消失在叔父华贵深幽的织锦帐幔深处。

## XIII *Al-Fulús al-Masnú Min al-Qirtás*, or “The Money Made of Paper” / 第十三章 纸币
===Original===

On the appointed day of the next week the little boys were glad to observe that the number of public executions had fallen so far below the average that their uncle’s entertainment of them could begin quite half an hour before the usual time. They were most eager to discover what further good fortune had befallen him by the Mercy of Allah.
The amiable old man opened his mouth and spoke:
“Two million gold pieces is a respectable sum of money. It weighs about thirty tons﻿ ﻿… yes,” he calculated rapidly on his bejewelled fingers, “about thirty tons. The city could just produce it after scouring the country for miles around, searching all the more modest houses and melting down sundry antique lamps, wedding rings, sacred shrines and other gewgaws.
“The complete withdrawal of so much metal left them a little embarrassed for coin in everyday affairs, but really that was not my business. I packed a hundred strong iron chests with the bullion, reserving a few thousands in a leather bag, set them in carts, added to my retinue a hundred armed men, marked my cases plainly in large white letters ‘**Sand consigned to the sultan**,’ and had all made ready to set out: but whither?
“Until a man’s wealth has grown so great that he can command the whole state, he is always in some peril. He is envied and a target for vile taxes﻿—nay for confiscation.﻿ ﻿… I had not forgotten the dreadful lesson of the island! I pondered on what I had read of various regions, and had rejected each in turn as dangerous, when I heard by chance a man saying to his neighbour (with whom he was quarrelling), ‘Remember! This is not the country of Dirak where there is one law for the rich and another for the poor.’ As you may well believe I deeply considered these random words, and within an hour I was giving an excellent meal to a learned man who taught in the University, famous for his knowledge of foreign constitutions. I spoke of the Franks, of the Maghreb, of Rome. On all he was most interesting and full: he spoke also with contempt of certain wild tribes in the hills who have a strange custom of choosing a retired Chief annually from among the less wealthy members, under the barbarous error that modest means conduce to honesty and sharpen judgment.
“ ‘As in Dirak,’ said I casually.
“ ‘In Dirak?’ he exclaimed astonished. ‘Why! Who can have told you such tales?

===Chinese===

次周约定的日子，小男孩们欣喜地注意到，闹市中公开行刑的人数远低于平常的平均数，以致叔父对他们的款待竟能比往常提前足足半个时辰开始。他们迫不及待地想探究，承蒙真主的仁慈（Mercy of Allah），叔父身上又降临了何等天大的洪福。
慈眉善目的老人张开口，侃侃而谈道：
“两百万枚（2000000）金币，可是一笔颇为体面的巨款。分量足有约三十吨（30）重……不错，”他伸出戴满珠宝的手指飞快地掐算着，“足足三十吨重。这座城池也是在方圆数英里的乡野掘地三尺，搜遍了所有小门小户的屋舍，熔化了各色古旧油灯、结婚戒指、圣龛饰物以及杂七杂八的小玩意儿之后，才勉强凑齐了这笔金子。
“抽走如此巨量的硬通货，搞得城中百姓在日常过日子的找零碎银上略显捉襟见肘，不过那当真不关我的事。我用一百只（100）结实的铁箱将金锭悉数装箱，仅留了几千枚散碎银钱装进一只皮口袋里备用；我把铁箱装上大车，护卫队里添了一百名（100）全副武装的甲士，箱体上用醒目的白色大字清清楚楚题写着‘呈缴苏丹之沙土’，一切准备停当，单等拔营起程——然而，究竟该奔向何方呢？
“一个人的财富在庞大到足以号令整个国家之前，他便始终处于险境之中。他会遭人嫉恨，沦为横征暴敛乃至抄家没收的活靶子……那座海岛的惨痛教训，我至今仍历历在目！我反复盘算着从书本中读到过的各方疆土，却逐一因凶险莫测而将其作罢；恰在此刻，我偶然听见一个汉子正与邻人吵架，嚷道：‘记好了！这儿可不是迪拉克（Dirak），在这儿有钱人和穷人可不是各有一套王法！’正如你们所料，我对这句无心之言深思熟虑；不出一个时辰，我便在酒楼大摆筵席，宴请了一位在太学里执教、以精通外邦典章政制闻名的饱学鸿儒。我同他大谈法兰克人、马格里布以及罗马。谈及所有这些，他都见解独到、滔滔不绝；他还轻蔑地谈起山中某些不开化的蛮荒部落，说他们竟有一种古怪的陋习，每年从资财较匮乏的族人中推选一名引退的酋长，荒谬地以为清贫寡欲有助于秉公持正、磨砺才智。
“‘就跟迪拉克一样，’我漫不经心地插了一句。
“‘迪拉克？’他惊愕地叫嚷起来，‘哈！是谁跟你嚼的这种舌根？

===Original===

Dirak is the best administered, the most flourishing and the strongest of all states!’
“ ‘No doubt,’ I answered, ‘but what has that to do with it?’
“ ‘Why,’ said he, in sudden anger (for this kind of learned man is commonly half-mad) ‘it has everything to do with it! Such advantages can only come from the secure rule of the rich.﻿ ﻿… A fool could see that!’
“I soothed him by immediate agreement, professed my admiration at his vast store of knowledge and pumped him all that afternoon on Dirak.
“It seemed that in this admirable region the rich rule unquestioned to the immense profit of the state. The sultan is kept on a strict allowance that he may be the puppet of the great merchants, bankers and landholders who are the masters of the commonwealth and him. The middle classes are allowed a livelihood but no possessions, and are proud of their small incomes, which usually put them above the artizans; while the populace are content to swarm in hovels underground, to work hard all day and all the year round for a little food and to revere and acclaim the rich with frenzied cheers upon all public occasions. Laws and proclamations are purchased, and their administration is in the hands of the rich, of whom a select few sit upon the bench and condemn a fixed number of the populace, and a few of the middle classes, to imprisonment every year by way of discipline and example. No man possessing more than a hundred thousand gold pieces worth of land or stock can be punished, and if a poor man tell any unpleasing thing of such a one he is beaten till he admits his falsehood or, if he prove obstinate, slowly starved to death.
“It is a model state. All is in perfect order. The palaces of the rulers are the most magnificent in the world: all public office is faithfully and punctually performed. It is the envy of every neighbour, the pride and delight of every citizen however mean; for﻿—what is the basis of the whole affair﻿—every man in Dirak is esteemed by the extent of his possessions alone; writing and music and work in metals and painted tiles are esteemed for the pretty things they are: holiness is revered indeed, but confined to the well-to-do; and a man’s virtue, judgment and wit are rightly gauged by his property.
“My many adventures had somewhat blunted me to new sensations. But I confess (my dear nephews) that as I heard this tale an ecstasy filled my soul.

===Chinese===

迪拉克乃是全天下吏治最清明、市面最繁荣、国力最强盛的邦国！’
“‘想必如此，’我答道，‘但这同咱们聊的又有何干系？’
“‘怎么没干系！’他勃然大怒（盖因这类书呆子往往疯疯癫癫），‘干系大着呢！这等治世盛况，唯有建立在富豪阶层稳固统治的基石之上！……连蠢材都能一眼看破这个理儿！’
“我连忙点头如捣蒜好言安抚他，对他满腹经纶的博学深表钦佩，并在那整个下午不住向他打探迪拉克的虚实。
“原来，在那片令人称羡的神奇沃土上，富豪阶层的铁腕统治无可置疑，并给国家带来了难以估量的暴利。苏丹被严格限定在固定年俸之内，以便充当大商贾、大钱庄主和大田产主的牵线木偶，而这些人才是共和国以及苏丹本人的真正主宰。中产阶级被允许维持生计，却不准拥有资产，他们为自己那点微薄进项沾沾自喜，自以为高出市井工匠一等；至于底层黔首，则心甘情愿地蜗居在地下窝棚里，终年面朝黄土背朝天苦干终日，只为换取一口糊口的粗粮，并在一切公开庆典上，以狂热的欢呼竭力顶礼膜拜富豪权贵。法令与诏书皆明码标价待价而沽，司法审判的大权全操持在富人之手，其中少数遴选出的显贵端坐法官席上，每年依照定额将固定数目的庶民以及个别中产阶级打入死牢大狱，以儆效尤、整饬纲纪。凡拥有价值超过十万枚（100000）金币的田产或生意的富人，依法皆免受任何刑责；倘若有穷鬼胆敢搬弄此类显贵的半句是非，便会遭皮鞭毒打，直至招认诬陷，若是负隅顽抗，便活活饿死在狱中。
“那当真是模范之邦，万事井井有条。统治者的宫殿乃是举世无双的巍峨壮丽；一切官差公职皆办理得恪尽职守、分秒不差。引得邻邦艳羡不已，教每一位升斗小民无论何等卑微皆引以为傲；因为——这也是维系整个体制的磐石——在迪拉克，评判一个人的唯一标尺纯粹取决于他家产的丰寡；文章、音律、金工雕琢与彩绘瓷砖，不过被视作赏心悦目的玩物；圣洁虔诚固然受人膜拜，却仅限于豪门巨室所独享；而一个人的德行、见识与才智，皆被极其公允地用其财产多寡来权衡丈量。
“我经历的无数风浪早已令我对新鲜事物波澜不惊。但我不得不向你们承认（我亲爱的侄儿们），当我耳闻这番盛况时，一股难以名状的狂喜瞬间充溢了我的灵魂。

===Original===

I masked my emotions and simply said, ‘An interesting place!’
“ ‘It is reached by a plain road from here,’ volunteered the learned man, ‘though at the expense of a long journey: for it takes a caravan quite a month to reach the capital of Dirak from this place. You go up the river to its source in the hills, a week’s travel to the east; then the well-marked road leads you over a pass to a most singular cup or natural cauldron, with a flat, highly cultivated floor, formerly the bed of a lake and surrounded on all sides by precipitous limestone cliffs, down which the road descends by artificial cuttings in their surface. This strangely isolated spot, famous for its gardens and simple happiness, is called with its chief village Skandir, and strangers are there most hospitably entertained.
“ ‘The only issue thence, on the far side, is by a narrow gorge leading through the mountains, beyond which again are vast plains of grassy lands, the grazing place of nomads: well watered and provisioned at reasonable distances by simple but well furnished villages. The great road goes through all these, still eastward.
“ ‘These prairies get drier and drier as they rise eastward until, for the last day of your progress, at the wells of Ayn-ayoum you must take a supply of water, for the next twenty-four hours are desert. You reach a crest of the slow ascent and see below you from the summit of the road some half a day’s going across the plain below, the magnificent capital of Dirak.
“ ‘This noble city, whose name is Mawazan, was founded by the enormously wealthy﻿—’
“ ‘Yes! Yes!’ I interrupted in a bored tone﻿—for I knew all I wanted to know, ‘some day I must go there. A very amusing journey no doubt. But meanwhile business is business and I must start very early for the north tomorrow morning to look after some purchases I have made in grains; and I must not waste any more of your time.’
“The learned are slow to take a hint, so I locked my arm in his after a friendly fashion and led him genially to the door, where he tried (unsuccessfully) to detain me for further remarks on yet another country famous for its enormous bats.

===Chinese===

我掩饰住内心的澎湃波澜，淡淡地吐出一句：‘真是一处妙不可言的胜境！’
“‘从这儿有一条平坦的大道直通彼邦，’那位学者主动献策道，‘只是路途遥远颇费脚程：商队从咱们这儿走到迪拉克国都，足足要跋涉一整月光景。你只需沿河逆流而上，行至山中源头，那是往东一周的程途；随后一条显豁的大道将引你翻越山口，进入一处极奇特的天然盆地，那盆底平坦广阔、沃野千里，原本是一处古湖的湖底，四周全被陡峭的石灰岩绝壁环抱，大路顺着人工在绝壁上凿出的栈道蜿蜒而下。这处世外桃源以其果园繁盛、民风淳朴而闻名遐迩，其主村落被称为斯坎迪尔（Skandir），那里的乡民待客极其古道热肠。
“‘从盆地通往外界的唯一通道，在盆地另一侧，是一道穿越群山的狭窄峡谷；穿过峡谷，眼前又是辽阔无垠的茫茫草原，那是游牧部落放牧的水草丰茂之地；沿途每隔合理的程途便有民风朴实却物产丰盈的村落，供人补给饮水与干粮。官道横穿这些村落，一路继续向东延伸。
“‘随着大路一路向东攀升，草原会愈发干燥荒芜，直到最后一天的程途，在艾因·阿尤姆（Ayn-ayoum）的水井旁，你必须备足淡水，因为接下来的二十四（24）小时全是不毛荒漠。你攀上平缓升起的山脊，从路顶极目远眺，在下方平原上约莫半日程途的尽头，便能望见迪拉克那巍峨壮丽的国都。
“‘那座高贵的巨城名叫马瓦赞（Mawazan），是由富可敌国的——’
“‘对！对！’我故作厌烦地打断了他——因为我想知道的底细已尽数到手，‘改日我定当走上一遭。想必是趟极有趣的旅程。不过眼下买卖要紧，明儿一早我必须赶赴北方督办几批新购进的粮食；我可不好意思再耽搁先生的宝贵时辰了。’
“读书人向来不解风情，于是我哥俩好般挽起他的臂膀，亲热地将他引至门外；在门口他还试图拽住我（可惜未能得逞），想就另一处盛产巨型蝙蝠的邦国再发表一番宏论。

===Original===

“When I had got well rid of him﻿—it was already dark﻿—I beat up my quarters without delay, aligned my caravan, added to the inscription on my iron treasure chests the words ‘of Dirak’ (so that the labels now ran ‘**Sand for the Sultan of Dirak**’), marshalled my armed troop and set out in the night by the northern road. But, long before daybreak, I ordered a deflection to the right, struck the great road along the river and so proceeded eastward into the hills.
“It was as the learned man had said: a week’s marching to the sources of the stream led to a pass, and we saw below us at evening a splendid spectacle: that small oval plain of Skandir all girded with enormous precipices, a garden of fruit trees and grain with great prosperous villages in its midst, and the road picking its way by cuttings in the living rock down to the valley floor, and thence making straight for the main town.
“We reached it under a new moon in the second hour of darkness. Its hospitality had not been exaggerated. The good peasants received us with every kindness and I was lodged in a most comfortable house, my chests and grain in the courtyard and my numerous retinue under lesser roofs around.
“Next day﻿—as luck would have it﻿—a wretched accident befell me! I was taking the air at the door of my house, preparatory to ordering the start of my caravan, when I heard the ring of metal on the flat stones of the street. A child running past had dropped a small silver coin. I marked the gleaming spot as the child ran on unheeding, and naturally rushed to put my foot on it before it should be noticed by any other, intending to stoop gracefully at my leisure and pick it up when the coast was clear. But the Evil One, who is ever on the watch to undo the servants of the Most High caused me, in my eagerness, to slip upon a greasy piece of mud and I fell heavily upon the stones with a crash. My leg was broken!
“In the agony I suffered I quite forgot the silver coin (the void still aches); I know not who acquired it. I cannot bear to think that it was trampled in and lost to the world.
“At any rate, I was carried to my couch half fainting, the bone was set with excruciating pain, and I lay for many days unable to rise and eating my heart out at the added expense of my large company which was dipping deeply into my store of loose coin.

===Chinese===

“待我彻底将他打发走——天色已然全黑——我毫不迟疑地整肃兵马，排齐商队，在那些装满金银的铁箱题字后头补上‘迪拉克’三字（于是封条铭文变成了‘呈缴迪拉克苏丹之沙土’），整肃好武装卫队，连夜从北门疾驰出城。但在破晓之前，我下令大队人马向右折转，切上了沿河的平坦官道，一路向东挺进群山。
“正如那位鸿儒所言：沿河逆流急行军一周行至源头，越过一道隘口，黄昏时分我们眼前呈现出一派壮丽奇观：那片被万仞绝壁环抱的小小椭圆盆地斯坎迪尔（Skandir），果木繁茂、麦浪翻滚，一座座富庶的大村落掩映其间；大道顺着在坚岩上凿出的人工栈道一路盘旋降至谷底，径直通向主集镇。
“在一弯新月升起、入夜后的第二个时辰，我们抵达了集镇。当地人的热情好客绝非夸大其词。善良淳朴的乡民们待我们极其厚道周到，我被安置在一栋极其舒适的大宅里，铁箱与粮食堆在庭院中央，庞大的随从卫队则散宿在四周较小的客房中。
“岂料翌日——说来真是倒霉透顶——一场飞来横祸砸到了我头上！当时我正伫立在大宅门口透气，正准备下令商队拔营启程，忽听街头石板路上传来一阵清脆的金石交鸣声。原来是一个飞奔而过的孩童掉落了一枚小银币。那孩子毫无察觉地跑远了，我瞧着石缝中那点闪烁的光斑，本能地飞扑上前，抢先用脚死死踩住，免得被旁人瞧了去，打算待闲杂人等散去之后，再从容优雅地俯身捡起。然而那时刻窥伺着陷害至高主宰仆人的恶魔，竟趁着我心急火燎之际，教我一脚踩在一团滑腻的污泥上，整个人轰然重重仰面摔倒在石板地上。我的腿骨断了！
“在钻心的剧痛中，我把那枚银币忘得一干二净（失去它至今仍令我隐隐作痛）；天晓得后来究竟便宜了哪个混账。一想到它竟被踩进烂泥从此泯灭于世，我便心疼得难以呼吸。
“不管怎样，我半昏半厥地被抬回卧榻，在撕心裂肺的剧痛中接好了骨头；我瘫卧在床榻上数日无法动弹，一想到手下这支庞大的商队每天坐吃山空、疯狂蚕食着我手头所剩不多的散碎银钱，我便心如刀绞。

===Original===

“My main treasure, stored in the hundred iron boxes, I dared not touch; for the Chief of Skandir (who daily visited my sickroom) told me that he had affixed seals to the sand consigned to the Sultan of Dirak, his powerful neighbour, and taken it for safe keeping into his castle.
“The physician assured me that even when I might venture out on crutches it would be fatal, in view of certain complications which had arisen, if I were to think of travel.
“So there I was, imprisoned in this charming valley, with no chance of commerce, my spare cash dangerously dwindling, and a most expensive three weeks’ journey ahead of me before I could reach my beloved Dirak!
“What was I to do!
“My dear nephews, you will hear many harsh things said of those who prosper as I have done. They are vilified through a base envy and the most monstrous tales are told of them. But they are under the protection of Heaven, and that Guiding Power supplements their humble vows. None can deny their ready response to Inspiration. Hear what I did.
“First I purchased out of my remaining free gold a fine house that happened to be empty. Next I had painted on its front in beautiful and varied colours ‘**Mahmoud’s Bank**.’ Next I told the chief what advantage I designed for him and his during my enforced stay, by way of repaying him for their exceptional kindness. Next I sent out written letters to all the wealthier men (and women, my dear nephews, *and women*), saying that I had begun operations in the buying and selling of market produce and that any capital entrusted to me would earn, for every hundred pieces, one piece a week, payed punctually at a certain hour. To give colour to my scheme I sent my quickest-witted servant (amply rewarded) to watch the markets in the valley, to buy up fruit and grain at magnificent prices and to sell elsewhere as best he could.
“ ‘Never mind,’ said I to him, benevolently, ‘at what loss you sell. I desire to do these honest people a service.’
“The volume of my commerce grew (at a heavy charge!) and even the timid thought there might be something in it. I started the ball rolling by getting my confidant to deposit a hundred pieces of gold, which I had privily furnished. At the end of the week I duly gave him back one hundred and one in the presence of many; and the story went abroad.

===Chinese===

“至于那一千箱铁皮柜里的大批重宝，我碰都不敢碰；因为斯坎迪尔（Skandir）的头领（他日日来我的病榻前探视）告诉我，他已在那些呈缴给他强邻迪拉克苏丹的沙土箱上加盖了封印，并搬进他的城堡里代为严加看管。
“郎中向我断言，鉴于引发了某些凶险的并发症，即便往后我能拄拐下地，出门远行也无异于自寻短见。
“这下我彻底成了困在这座迷人山谷里的囚徒，毫无经商翻本的门路，手头的零碎盘缠眼瞅着见底，而眼前还有整整三周耗资不菲的漫长旅途，我才能抵达到我朝思暮想的迪拉克啊！
“我该如何是好？
“我亲爱的侄儿们，对于像我这般飞黄腾达之人，你们日后定会听到许多尖酸刻薄的风凉话。世人出于下流的嫉妒百般中伤他们，编造出各种耸人听闻的荒诞故事。然而这些大人物实则处于上苍的眷顾之下，天意自会成全他们谦卑的宏愿。谁也无法否认他们对神明启示的心领神会。且听听我当时究竟办了何等惊天动地的大事吧！
“首先，我倾囊掏出仅剩的闲置金币，买下了城中一栋恰好空置的气派华宅。接着，我命人用斑斓华美的锦绣油彩在大门上赫然题写上‘马哈茂德钱庄（Mahmoud’s Bank）’。随后，我向头领进言，说为了报答全镇上下对我的隆情厚谊，我打算在养伤期间为他及阖镇父老谋取一桩天大的福祉。紧接着，我修书派送给全镇所有的富绅巨贾（还有阔太太们，我亲爱的侄儿们，还有阔太太们！），声称我已在此开埠兼营集市土特产的统购统销，凡托付给我的本钱，每满一百枚（100）金币，每周可坐收一枚红利，到期在固定时辰分文不差现银兑付。为了将这出好戏演得逼真，我打发最机灵的心腹仆役（赏银极其丰厚）盯死全谷市面，以令人咋舌的天价大肆收购瓜果与粮食，随后再想方设法贱卖处理。
“‘不必理会亏损几何，’我慈眉善目地叮嘱他，‘老夫一心只想造福这帮忠厚老实的乡民。’
“我的买卖体量疯狂暴涨（代价自然是血本无归！），连最谨小慎微的人也觉得其中必有天大的赚头。我暗中拨给心腹一百枚（100）金币作为诱饵，命他以储户身份在众目睽睽下存入钱庄，拉开了大幕。一周之后，我当着黑压压的人群，如约连本带利奉还给他一百零一枚金币；这一神迹瞬间传遍了十里八乡。

===Original===

“Soon the chief, his uncle and his mother-in-law deposited and were as regularly paid one percent a week. The thing began to buzz﻿—but I watched narrowly my dwindling hoard: it was a close thing!﻿ ﻿… When I had progressed in this fashion for what I considered a sufficient time, I judged it opportune to initiate my new policy of an expansion of exchange through instruments of credit.”
“Dear uncle﻿—” interrupted the eldest nephew.
“Yes, yes,” said the merchant, impatiently, “I know that the term is new to you, but you will shortly learn its meaning. When I had occasion to buy articles for my private consumption or to make an exceptionally heavy purchase of my wholesale wares, I would frequently affect embarrassment, and approaching the vendor I would beg him to accept, in lieu of immediate payment in cash, a note which I had signed promising payment in gold at sight. ‘For,’ said I to him, ‘in the rapid turnover of my business it is but a matter of a few hours for me to be again in possession of a considerable sum of ready money.’
“I went to work at first with caution. I never by any chance issued a single note for more than ten pieces, and when ever any one of these notes was presented for payment, even though that event should take place within an hour of my issuing it, I promptly honoured it from the reserve of metal which I had kept back for the furtherance of my plan. I was careful to make these notes identical, to stamp them all in the same place with my metal seal, and in every way to make them, so far as I could, a sort of currency, which, as you may imagine, they promptly became. When a man carrying one of these instruments might find himself called upon to pay, at some distance from my place of business, he would at first tentatively offer my note (perhaps at a small discount) to his creditor. But as my integrity was by this time a proverb (and never forget, dear boys, that integrity is the soul of business) the notes were more and more readily accepted as time proceeded.
“The convenience of carrying such paper compared with the heavy weight of metal they might represent, the ease of negotiation, and so forth, rapidly increased their circulation; and in a short time I was able to calculate with assurance what the experts in this amiable science term ‘the rate of circulation’ which my notes had attained.

===Chinese===

“很快，头领、他的舅父以及他的岳母全都成了我的储户，每周分文不差地领取百分之一的红利。全镇彻底轰动了——可我暗中死死盯着自己日益缩水的本钱：当真险象环生！……当这套把戏演到我自认为火候已足时，我断定推行我那套通过信用票据扩张交易的新政的时机已然成熟。”
“伯父大人——”长侄忍不住插话。
“对，对，”商人不耐烦地打断道，“我知道这词儿你们听着新鲜，但很快你们便能领悟个中真谛。每当我需要购置私人物品、或是大宗采办批发货物时，我便常常装出一副手头拮据的困窘模样，走到掌柜跟前恳请他接受一张由我亲笔画押、承诺见票即付足额金币的欠条，以充抵即时现银。‘因为，’我对他说，‘鉴于鄙人商号流水周转极快，短则数个时辰，大笔现银便能重新回笼。’
“起初我办事极具分寸。一张票据的面额绝不超出十枚（10）金币；只要有人持票前来兑付，哪怕是他刚刚拿到票据还不足一个时辰，我都立刻从暗中截留的金库储备中全额现银兑现，以推行我的大计。我格外注重将这些票据印制得一模一样，并在相同位置加盖我的纯金私印，竭尽所能将它们打造成一种流通货币——正如你们所料，它们转瞬间便成真了。当有人揣着这种纸片、在远离我钱庄的地界急需付账时，起初他或许会试探性地（甚至折价贴息）将票据转让给债主。然而此时我的商誉在当地已是妇孺皆知（孩子们，切记商誉乃经商之魂），久而久之，这些票据在市面上越来越畅行无阻。
“携带此类纸片相较于沉重金属货币的轻便，以及交易流通的极度便捷等等，极大地刺激了它们的周转；不多时，我便已能成竹在胸地推算出这门美妙科学的行家里手所称的‘流通速率’了。

===Original===

I found that, roughly speaking, for every five pieces to which I had thus pledged myself upon paper two were sufficient to meet the claims of those who presented them at any one moment. And this proportion is known to this day in that happy valley as ‘the proportion of metallic reserve’ which must lie behind any issue of notes﻿—but I hear that since my departure they have got badly muddled.
“Oh! dear, dear!” said the eldest nephew. “I am getting muddled myself, Uncle.”
“Don’t listen to him!” said his brothers in chorus.
“Yes! my children,” answered the old man vividly, “it is indeed a difficult subject. Only a few experts really understand it﻿ ﻿… and I am one﻿ ﻿… anyhow, you all see that I could now make new money as I chose out of nothing?”
“Oh! yes, uncle!” they all agreed, including the eldest. “We quite see that!”
“Well,” said their revered relative in a subdued tone, “that is a great advantage. But to proceed.
“After some weeks of these practices I found myself the master of the fruit and grain markets, to which I added certain adjuncts naturally suggested by it, such as catering for public meals, the erection of mosques, the undertaking of marriages, funerals, and divorces, the display of fireworks, and the charging of fixed fees for the telling of fortunes. This last soon became a very flourishing branch of my business. I employed in it at the customary wage a number of expert soothsayers, and these, with the rest of my staff, amounted to perhaps a quarter of the inhabitants; nor were they the least contented or the least prosperous of the population.
“In a word, my dear nephews, when my operations were concluded I found myself in possession of 200,000 pieces of gold, while my notes, which were everywhere received throughout the state, stood for 300,000 more. A simple calculation,” said the worthy old man, smiling, “will show you that my total new fortune was now no less than half a million pieces, when signs of economic exhaustion in the public and the complete healing of my leg reluctantly decided me that the time had come to seek fresh fields of effort and other undeveloped lands.”
As the merchant now puffed at his pipe in silence the fifth nephew begged leave to ask him two questions which had perplexed his youthful mind.

===Chinese===

我发现，大致说来，我在纸面上每许诺兑付五枚（5）金币，金库里只需备足两枚（2）金币，便足以应付任何特定时辰前来挤兑的储户。这种比例，直至今日在那座幸福的山谷里仍被奉为发行纸币必须依托的‘金银准备金率’——不过我听闻自打我离去之后，他们全给搞得一团糟了。
“哎呀，天呐！”长侄叫嚷起来，“伯父，连侄儿我也被您给绕糊涂了。”
“别听他瞎嚷嚷！”众兄弟齐声斥责道。
“正是，孩子们，”老商人神采奕奕地答道，“这确实是一门高深莫测的学问。普天之下唯有极少数行家能参透个中奥妙……老夫恰好便是其中之一……不管怎样，你们总该明白，此时我已经能随心所欲凭空造出新钱了吧？”
“懂了！伯父！”全场侄儿齐声赞同，包括那个长侄，“这我们全看明白了！”
“甚好，”备受尊崇的叔父压低嗓音说道，“这可是一桩天大的妙处。咱们接着往下讲。
“如此这般折腾了数周之后，我俨然成了瓜果与粮食市面的独家霸主；顺理成章地，我又兼营起若干衍生行当，诸如包办官席盛宴、营建清真寺、承办红白喜事与离异诉讼、施放节庆烟火，以及按定额收费为人占卜问卦。这最后一项算命营生，很快成了我旗下日进斗金的大宗买卖。我依照市价雇佣了一大批算命神棍，他们连同我商号里的其他伙计，足足占了全镇居民的四分之一；这帮人可绝非城中最受罪或最穷困的阶层。
“总而言之，我亲爱的侄儿们，当这轮买卖大功告成之际，我赫然发现自己坐拥二十万枚（200000）金币现银，而我发行的那些在全邦四处畅行无阻的纸币，又代表着三十万枚（300000）金币。稍加折算，”值得尊敬的老人微笑着说道，“你们便会发现老夫新斩获的全部身家，已然高达整整五十万枚金币！恰在此时，全镇百姓隐隐显露出经济枯竭的破产败相，加之我的断腿也已彻底痊愈，我虽依依不舍，却也不得不横下心来断定：该是另辟蹊径、去拓荒其他未开发蛮荒之地的时辰了。”
老商人说到此处，悠悠地抽着水烟沉默不语；第五个侄儿毕恭毕敬地请求叔父为他解答困扰他幼小心灵的两个疑窦。

===Original===

“Ask away, my little fellow,” said his uncle, kindly, “and I will attempt to explain any difficulty you have in simple terms suited to your age.”
“Well, uncle,” said the fifth nephew, humbly, “I cannot in the first place see how the 300,000 pieces of which you speak, and which as you say were represented by notes alone, constituted any real wealth.”
“My dear little chap,” answered his uncle, leaning forward to pat him upon the head, “you will have the intelligence to perceive that wherever such a note existed people thought of it as ten golden pieces, did they not?”
“Y‑es‑s,” answered his nephew, feeling that he was getting cornered.
“Very well,” continued the old man, merrily, “this attitude of mind being common to the whole community, and all having come to regard these pieces of paper as so much money, I had but to receive them in payment of my debts and then to buy with them into the gold of others. Thus all the gold entered my possession. Eh? On my departure the outstanding notes were presented to the firm, I hear, and there was then no gold to meet them with. A sad state of affairs! Many clamoured and all sorts of trouble arose. But by that time I was far away.”
The little chap still looked puzzled. “But, uncle,” he said, “when the people presented the notes after you had gone, they may have thought they had wealth, but they hadn’t any, had they?”
“I don’t know,” said the old man, after a pause. “It is a most difficult point in the discussion of currency.﻿ ﻿… I, at any rate, had been bold in the story I told, and got hold of their gold.”
“But the wealth wasn’t *there*, uncle,” persisted the little boy. “It wasn’t there at all!”
The merchant with a benign air replied: “The science of political economy is abstruse enough for the most aged and experienced, and it will be impossible for me to explain to you at length so intricate a point. Let it suffice for you that so far as *I* was concerned the wealth *was* there, it was there in fifty large leather bags.﻿ ﻿… You had, I think,” he added in a severer tone, “a second question to propound?”
“Yes,” said his nephew with a slight sigh, “dear uncle, it was this: Why under such favorable circumstances did you think it necessary to leave so early, seeing that your new trade was going so well?”
“That,” said old Mahmoud in a tone of relief, “is much more easy to answer. My leg was healed.

===Chinese===

“只管问吧，小家伙，”叔父和蔼地说道，“老夫自当用切合你年纪的浅显话语，为你排忧解惑。”
“那个，伯父，”五侄谦卑地问道，“首先，侄儿实在想不通，您适才提到的那三十万枚（300000）纯粹用纸片代表的所谓财富，究竟算哪门子真正的家当呀？”
“我聪明的小乖乖，”叔父倾过身去摸了摸他的小脑袋，答道，“凭你的悟性不难看出，无论何处只要流传着这么一张纸片，世人皆将其视若十枚（10）金灿灿的金币，对是不对？”
“是……是的，”侄儿答道，感觉自己正一步步落入圈套。
“这就结了，”老人眉开眼笑地继续开导道，“全镇上下既然普遍怀揣着这般心智，全都把这些纸片当真金白银看待，我只需用它们来冲抵我的货款债务，然后再拿着收上来的纸片，去大肆收购旁人手中的真金白银便是。这般一来，全镇所有的金子岂不尽入我囊中？嗯？我听说，在我拔营离去之后，市面上流通的全部纸钞皆被百姓拿到商号挤兑，而库里哪有半块金子去承兑！好一派惨绝人寰的人间惨剧！万民哀号，天下大乱。然而彼时老夫早已远走高飞了。”
小家伙依旧满腹狐疑：“可是，伯父，在您脚底抹油之后，人们拿着纸条去兑现，他们心里或许觉得自己拥有财富，可实际上他们手里连一文钱都没有，难道不是吗？”
“天晓得呢，”老人沉吟片刻，答道，“这可是货币大义中最令人头疼的死结……不论怎样，老夫凭着这出瞒天过海的神功，当真把他们的真金白银全捞到了手。”
“可那些财富根本就‘不存在’呀，伯父，”小男孩倔强地追问，“根本就无影无踪嘛！”
商人摆出一副超然物外的慈祥神态，回答道：“政治经济学之玄奥，即便对于耄耋宿儒亦是深不可测，老夫实在无法向你详加剖析如此繁复的奥义。你只需记住一点足矣：于‘老夫’而言，那些财富不仅确凿无疑地‘存在’着，而且真真切切装在那五十只（50）大皮口袋里呢！……依稀记得，”他语气一沉，略带严厉地补充道，“你适才似乎还有第二个问题要请教？”
“是的，”侄儿轻叹了一声，“亲爱的伯父，是这样的：既然您的新买卖办得如此如日中天，在形势这般一片大好的当儿，您为何觉得非得这般匆忙卷铺盖走人不可呢？”
“这个嘛，”老马哈茂德如释重负地答道，“回答起来可就容易多了：因为老夫的腿伤彻底好全了。

===Original===

The resources of Skandir were limited. Signs were apparent that the worthy populace, though unable to unravel the precise nature of their entanglement, were already very seriously hampered by my operations. Though I was able to prove by statistics that prosperity had increased by leaps and bounds, and though the chief, who was now my partner, kindly printed pamphlets at the public expense to prove the same, numbers who had formerly been well fed were now reduced to a few handfuls of raw grain, the jails were crammed, much of the land was going out of cultivation, and what between the ignorant passions which such periods of transition arouse in the vulgar, and the inability to get more water out of a sponge when you have already squeezed it thoroughly dry, I am sure that I was right in the determination I then took to retire from this field of operations.
“Before leaving I offered my business for sale to the public in general. The shares, I am glad to say, were eagerly taken up. And as I gave a preference in allotment (another technical term) to those who paid in my own notes, I recovered all of these save an insignificant fraction, and was able to negotiate them again for gold in public exchange before my departure.
“Meanwhile the unscrupulous anxiety of the chaotic multitude to share in so prosperous a commercial undertaking as mine had been, permitted me to ask for my business more than four (but less than five) times the sum which I would myself have been content to pay for it.
“I loaded 300 more camels with valuables of various sorts, including nearly all the precious metals discoverable in the state; I purchased a whole army of new slaves for the conduct of the caravan (paying for them in new notes issued upon the new company), and amid the plaudits and benedictions of a vast multitude, many of whom (I regret to say) were now in the last stages of destitution, I regretfully took my way through the gorge and bade farewell to the simple people of lovely and lonely Skandir to whom I owed so much.”
---
“I proceeded from the people of the valley whom I had introduced to banking, and went out through the gorge into the rising prairies beyond the mountains.

===Chinese===

斯坎迪尔（Skandir）的地力民脂毕竟有限。种种迹象表明，那些忠厚老实的庶民虽然参不透自身陷入困境的究竟症结所在，却已然被我的经商运作折腾得元气大伤。尽管我能凭确凿的账目数字向他们证明，全镇的市面繁荣正突飞猛进地呈跳跃式暴涨；尽管那位已被我拉下水充当合伙人的头领，亦极其体贴地自掏公款印制告示榜文来证实这一盛世，但大批从前丰衣足食的体面人家如今却沦落到仅靠几把生粮食度日，班房大牢里关满了囚徒，大片沃田无人耕种荒芜废弃。在这等转轨剧变时期，市井粗鄙草民极易受愚昧激情煽动，况且一块已被彻底榨干的海绵任谁也休想再挤出半滴水来——我深信，自己在那个节骨眼上作出的急流勇退之决断，当真是英明透顶。
“在动身离去前，我将麾下的钱庄产业面向全城公开拍卖发售。叫人欣喜的是，股份转瞬间便被百姓抢购一空。由于我对那些以我发行的纸币购股的人予以优先配售（这又是另一门专业切口），除了极少部分散落市面，其余纸币几乎被我尽数回收，并在启程前于兑换所将它们悉数掉包换成了沉甸甸的黄金。
“与此同时，那帮毫无头脑的乌合之众争先恐后巴望着能在如此红火的买卖中分得一杯羹的狂热，反倒教我得以索取相当于我自个儿心理估值四倍以上（却不到五倍）的天价，将这副烂摊子作价脱手。
“我又额外采办了300峰（300）骆驼驮运各类珍宝细软，几乎将彼邦所能搜刮到的全部贵金属一网打尽；我买下一整支庞大的新奴隶队伍充任驼队脚夫（全用新公司名义发行的崭新纸币结账）；随后在漫山遍野万民的欢呼与祈福声中——说来惭愧，其中大半百姓此时已陷入了赤贫绝境——老夫满怀眷恋地穿过峡谷，挥泪告别了那片令我斩获颇丰的美丽而幽僻的斯坎迪尔淳朴乡民。”
---
“我辞别了那些被我领进现代银行业门槛的谷中百姓，穿过峡谷，踏上了群山后方起伏辽阔的茫茫草原。

===Original===

For at least four days’ march beyond the valley my name was a household word to the villages through which I passed; not only was I able to pay for all goods by a further issue of notes, but I would even reward any special considerations shown me by selling to the grateful inhabitants for cash such shares in my old firm remaining at Skandir as I had retained to amuse me in my travel; and these, I am happy to say, went rapidly to a premium. These shares passed at gradually lessening prices from hand to hand, and I subsequently learnt that in a few months they had become unsaleable. Those who suffered in the last purchases had only themselves to blame, and indeed did not think of blaming any other, while the first to sell at a high price still hold my name in reverent remembrance.
“When I had proceeded a few days further upon my travels I found that the enlightenment and civilization to which I had led the people of the valley was gradually dissipated, and within a fortnight I discovered myself amid the very brutish nomad population who absolutely refused to take paper in the place of cash, even when this form of payment was offered by my own body servants. On the other hand, the precious metals were so scarce amid that population that prices were extremely low, and I was able at a very small outlay in gold to feed the whole of my considerable concourse.
“Three weeks so passed in these monotonous grass lands among the nomad tribes, the road went forward to the east rising all the way, and the soil grew drier and drier. We reached the wells of Ayn-Ayoub and filled our skins with water, we traversed the desert belt and camped near the summit: at daybreak we came to the escarpment and saw the wooded slopes falling away in cascading forests at our feet to where, far below, lay the splendid plain of Dirak and in its midst, far off and dark in outline against the burning dawn, the battlements and mosques, the minarets and tapering cypress points of its capital Misawan.
“What joy was mine to fall by gentle gradations down the declivities of those noble woods into the warm fields of the fortunate state! At every hour of my advance new delights met my eye!

===Chinese===

走出山谷后至少整整四天的行军路程里，沿途经过的各座村落无不将我的尊名奉若神明；我不仅凭着增发的纸币支付所有给养采办，甚至还会出于赏赐对我的特殊款待，顺手将留在手里权作旅途消遣的旧钱庄残存股份，向感恩戴德的乡民换取现银；而这些股票，叫人欣慰的是，转瞬便被爆炒至溢价。这些股份以逐级贬损的价码在百姓手中流转倒沫子，我后来得知不出数月它们便成了一文不值的废纸。那些在最后一棒接盘的倒霉蛋只能怪自己贪心，实则他们也压根没想过要怨尤旁人；而那些最先以高价脱手转卖的人，至今仍对我感恩戴德、顶礼膜拜。
“又往前跋涉了数日，我发现自己带给山谷百姓的开化与文明正渐渐消弭；两周之内，我发现自己已然身处一片野蛮粗鄙的游牧蛮族之中，这帮不开化的生番哪怕由我的贴身奴仆亲自出面，也斩钉截铁拒绝收取半张纸币，死活只要现银。但另一方面，金银贵金属在这帮蛮族之中罕见之至，以致物价贱如泥沙，我仅耗费了微不足道的区区几枚金币，便喂饱了我手下这支声势浩荡的庞大队伍。
“就这般在荒凉单调的茫茫草场上、穿行于游牧部落之间跋涉了三周光景，大路一路向东攀升，水土亦愈发干涸焦渴。我们抵达了艾因·阿尤布（Ayn-Ayoub）的水井，将皮囊灌满清水；穿过戈壁荒漠地带，在山脊巅峰附近扎营露宿。次日拂晓，行至悬崖绝壁顶峰，极目远眺，但见脚下的茫茫林海化作绿浪飞瀑倾泻而下，奔向极目尽头那一望无际的迪拉克（Dirak）壮丽大平原；而在平原正中央，迎着喷薄欲出的朝霞，隐隐矗立着其国都米萨万（Misawan）城高耸的城垛、巍峨的清真寺、耸立的尖塔以及修长的丝柏树顶。
“当我顺着那片秀美山林的平缓坡道拾级而下、步入那座幸运邦国的温润田园时，胸中奔涌着何等的狂喜啊！随着我一步步前行，每一刻都有崭新的胜景扑入眼帘！

===Original===

Great country houses standing in magnificent parks with carefully tended lawns all about, poor men who saluted low as I passed and rich men here and there who glanced a moment in haughty ease, fine horses passing at the trot mounted by subservient grooms, and, continually, posts bearing such notices as ‘Anyone treading on this Lord’s ground will be bowstrung.’ ‘No spitting.’ ‘One insolent word and to jail with you!’: While at every few hundred yards an armed man, before whom the poorer people cowered, would frown at the slaves at the head of my column, and then, seeing my finely mounted guard and my own immutable face and shining garments coming up behind them, would smile and bow and hint at a few small coins﻿—which I gave.
“In truth the learned man had not deceived me! This land of Dirak was a Paradise!
“I rode into the city like a king (as I was﻿—for my wealth made me one in such a state) and took for the night a lodging in an immense building, which called itself a caravanserai, but was, to the caravanserais of my experience, as the sultan’s palace to a horse.
“There, in an apartment of alabaster and beaten silver, I eat such viands as I had not thought to be on this earth, while well-drilled slaves, trained by long starvation to obedience, moved noiselessly in and out or played soft music hidden behind a carven screen.
“Oh! Dirak! Dirak!﻿ ﻿… but I must conclude.﻿ ﻿… The matter was not long. With my gold I purchased my palace in the midst of this city of Misawan, entertained guests who asked nothing of my origin, bought (after a careful survey of prices) the excellent post of chief sweeper to His Majesty (which carried with it the conduct of the treasury) and paid for a few laws which happened to suit my convenience, such as one to prevent street cries and another for the strangling of the redheaded poor: it is a colour of hair I cannot abide.
“From time to time I paid my respects to that puppet called the sultan and bowed low in the ceremonies of the court.
“I had no occasion to hide my wealth since wealth was here immune and the criterion of honour. I displayed it openly. I boasted of its amount. I even exaggerated its total. I was, within two years, the chief man in the state.
“Yet (such is the heart of man!) I was not wholly satisfied. Of my vast fortune not a hundredth had been consumed. None the less I could not bear to let it lie idle.

===Chinese===

宏伟的庄园宅邸坐落在气象万千的园林之中，四周环绕着悉心修剪的齐整草坪；穷人见我经过便深深躬身致敬，富豪们则三三两两从微眯的狭缝小眼中斜睨过来，掂量着我的家当身家；而一旦他们看清了我那声势浩荡的仪仗随从，要么争相延请我过府做客，要么（有两家便是如此）甚至主动要把自家的掌上明珠许配给我为妻。
“我身康体健地抵达了米萨万（Misawan），一想到即将同斯坎迪尔头领那些朝夕相伴的心爱沙土作别，心中难免有些怅惘；毕竟我曾悉心护送它们周全，雇请甲士和车辆也耗费了我好大一笔银两。然而食君之禄担君之忧，在这等唯利是图的商贸之邦，诚实商人的金口一诺重于泰山。
“我行至王宫前，却欣慰地得知苏丹因手头极度拮据，已将邻邦那批沙土的产权变卖给了由其国内几位豪门巨贾组成的辛迪加财团；而那些富豪通过同斯坎迪尔苏丹的私下斡旋，已然通过中间人向我出具了收据，并承诺只要我将货物完好无损地交割，便能分得一笔不菲的中介佣金。
“无需我多言，我亲爱的侄儿们，那批沙土自然是完好无损。我连半颗沙粒都不曾短少，他们无不对我的恪尽职守与诚实守信赞不绝口。他们痛痛快快地用足色黄金兑付了我的佣金；然而在撬开铁箱的那一刻，他们却尴尬万分地发现：由于斯坎迪尔头领极度缺乏商贸常识，这批耗费了万贯家财采买并运抵的沙土，纯粹是一种劣质粗砾，根本一文不值！
“我从心底深深同情他们，同时亦更加深切地体会到了天意的特殊眷顾——在我多舛多难的传奇生平中，神明始终这般悉心庇佑着我，免教我沦为市场莫测风浪的牺牲品。
“如今我坐享闲适、安宁与太平。我身处一座私有财产神圣不可侵犯的巨城，那里的律法纯粹单单为出得起大价钱的豪强效力。我过着穷奢极欲的豪奢生活，因为我在这片土地上参透了一条真理：要想避开权贵猜忌与敲诈勒索，唯一的上上策便是以极其夸张的排场，向所有人展示我那富可敌国的身家。我对自己的财富总额毫不隐瞒，甚至大肆吹嘘夸大其词。不出两年，我便成了彼邦首屈一指的大人物。
“然而（人心大抵如此！），我并未彻底心满意足。我那浩瀚如海的巨富，耗费掉的尚不足百分之一（100）。我无论如何也无法容忍它就这么静静地躺在库房里闲置发霉。

===Original===

I was determined to do business once again!﻿—By the Infinite Mercy of Allah the opportunity was vouchsafed.
“There lay on the confines of Dirak another state, called Har, very different. In this the sultan was the wealthiest man in the community and a tyrant. Moreover it differed from Dirak in this important particular, that whereas in Dirak all office was obtained by purchase, in Har all office was obtained by inheritance, so that between the two lay the unending and violent quarrel between trickery and pride.
“One day﻿—I had been the greatest man in Dirak for already two years more﻿—the Sultan of Har, wickedly, insolently, and not having the fear of God before his eyes, demanded satisfaction of the Sultan of Dirak for a loss sustained at dice by his grand almoner’s nephew at the hands of that noble in Dirak called the lord persecutor of games of chance﻿—which are, in Dirak, strictly forbidden by law.
“In vain did the Sultan of Dirak implore the aid of his nobles: they assured him that none would dare attack his (and their) omnipotent state.
“On the third day the Sultan of Har crossed the frontier with one million, two hundred thousand and fifty-seven men, ninety-seven elephants, and two catapults. On the tenth he was but three days’ march from Misawan.
“The unfortunate Sultan of Dirak, pressed by his enemy, was at his wits’ end for the ready money wherewith to conduct the war. He had already so severely taxed his poor that they were upon the point of rebellion, while the rich were much rather prepared to make terms with the enemy or to fly than to support his whim of honour, patriotism and the rest.
“Musing upon the opportunity thus afforded, and recognizing in it once more that overshadowing Mercy which had so marvellously aided my every step in life, I came into the street upon a horse and in my noblest garments. I was careful to throw largesse to the crowd, at an expense which I had previously noted in a little book (your father has, my dear nephews, trained you, I hope, to keep accounts?), and riding up to the palace I announced to the guard that I had come with important news for the sultan and his council.

===Chinese===

我决意重出江湖大显身手！——承蒙真主的无量仁慈（Infinite Mercy of Allah），良机再次降临。
“在迪拉克（Dirak）边陲接壤之处，卧着另一个名为哈尔（Har）的邻邦，两邦风气大相径庭。在哈尔国，苏丹本人乃是全邦最富有之人，且是个专横暴虐的独裁者。不仅如此，两邦还有一处至关重要的分水岭：在迪拉克，一切官阶爵位皆可花钱捐纳；而在哈尔，一切官职爵位全凭血缘世袭承袭。因此两邦之间，始终横亘着巧取豪夺的欺诈与傲慢虚荣的门阀之间永无休止的尖锐龃龉。
“有一日——彼时我稳坐迪拉克第一权臣交椅已达两年之久——哈尔国苏丹竟包藏祸心、飞扬跋扈，且目无神明戒惧，公然向迪拉克苏丹问罪索赔；起因是哈尔国大司赈大臣的侄儿在掷骰子赌博时输得精光，而赢钱的正主乃是迪拉克国中那位被称为‘严禁博彩御史’的贵胄——顺便提一句，在迪拉克，赌博在律法上可是明令严禁的。
“迪拉克苏丹苦苦哀求麾下王公贵族发兵勤王，贵族们却信誓旦旦宽慰他说，天底下断无一人敢太岁头上动土、冒犯他（以及他们）不可一世的神圣帝国。
“到了第三天，哈尔国苏丹亲率一百二十万零五十七名（1200057）甲士、九十七头（97）战象以及两架（2）攻城抛石机，浩浩荡荡跨过了边界。到了第十天（10），敌军距离米萨万（Misawan）城已仅剩三天路程。
“不幸的迪拉克苏丹大敌压境，为了筹措应付战事的军饷现银急得抓耳挠腮。他对穷苦百姓的盘剥早已到了民怨沸腾的谋反边缘，而城中的富豪巨室们情愿暗通款曲投降敌国或席卷细软潜逃，也断不肯自掏腰包去成全苏丹所谓的声誉、大义与爱国虚名。
“暗自盘算着这等千载难逢的暴富良机，并在其中再次体认到那一路神异庇佑我生平每一步的浩荡恩典，我骑上一匹高头大马，身着最华贵的锦袍穿街过市。我格外注重沿途向围观的人群抛洒赏钱，每一笔开销皆被我事先工工整整记在账本上（我亲爱的侄儿们，家兄想必早早教导过你们如何记账了吧？）；我策马驰抵王宫，向守卫昂然宣称，自己怀揣着关乎苏丹与枢密院安危的惊天机密求见。

===Original===

After certain formalities (which cost me, I regret to say, no less than fifteen dinars more than I had allowed for) I was shown into the presence of the vizier, who begged me to despatch my business hurriedly as the sultan was expecting at any moment news of an important action. I said with courtesy and firmness that my time was my own, that perhaps I had been mistaken in the news conveyed to me, but that the financial operations I was prepared to undertake would demand a certain leisure before they could be completed.
“At the words ‘financial operations’ the vizier’s manner wholly changed; he was profuse in apologies, admitting a little shamefacedly that he had taken me for a soldier, a priest, a poet, or something of that sort, and that if he had had the least idea of my intent he would never have kept me waiting as he had unfortunately done. He proceeded in a hurried and conventional tone to discuss the weather, the latest scandal, and other matters of the sort, until at my own time I proposed to introduce the important subject.
“This I did with becoming dignity. I informed him with the utmost reluctance that the enemy had already approached me for financial assistance. I would not be so hypocritical (I said) as to pretend that I had refused them, or indeed that I had any sentimental preference for one side or the other. As I thus expressed myself the vizier constantly and gravely nodded, as who should say that he esteemed no man so much as one who showed himself indifference to the feelings of the vulgar. I next asked of what sum the government was in immediate need, and on hearing that it amounted to about a quarter of my total capital I put on a very grave look and said that I feared the immediate provision of so large an amount was hardly possible, in view of the poverty and embarrassment of his unhappy country.
“When I rose as though to leave, the vizier, in a state of the utmost excitement, implored me to reconsider so sudden a decision. He was prepared (he swore) to take but an instalment of the whole. Ready money was absolutely necessary. And if, with my profound knowledge of finance, I could devise some way of escape for his master, the most substantial proofs of gratitude would be afforded me.

===Chinese===

在经历了一番繁文缛节之后（说来心疼，花销竟比我原先的预算生生多出了整整十五第纳尔（15）），我被引至宰相跟前。宰相催促我速速奏明来意，说苏丹正焦灼万分地恭候前线重大战况。我不卑不亢、斩钉截铁地答道：时间由我自个儿做主；兴许外头传给我的军情有误，但我手头准备筹谋的一系列金融运作，非得从容不迫好生运筹不可。
“一听到‘金融运作’四个字，宰相的态度当即发生了一百八十度的大转弯；他赔笑致歉不迭，面带羞惭地承认适才把自己当成了武夫、教士、酸诗人或类似的不入流货色；若是早料到我的身份意图，断不敢这般失礼教我久候。随后他赶忙换上一副客套世故的口吻，嘘寒问暖扯起了节气天色、宫闱秘辛以及诸如此类的市井谈资，直到老夫自认为时机成熟，方才主动切入正题。
“老夫以极庄重的姿态抛出了议题。我装出一副万分无奈的苦涩神态告知他，敌国其实早已暗中找上门来，向我摇尾乞怜恳求钱庄放贷。我说，我也犯不着惺惺作态假装自己义正词严回绝了对方，或是偏袒任何一方怀揣何等无聊的情怀偏好。听我这般坦陈胸臆，宰相不住频频肃然颔首，仿佛天下间他最钦佩的莫过于对升斗小民的妇人之仁嗤之以鼻的大格局巨擘。接着我探问朝廷眼下急需的现银底数究竟几何；一听闻那个数目足足占了我全部身家的四分之一，我脸色骤变，作凝重之态叹息道：鉴于彼邦眼下民穷财尽、焦头烂额的破败局面，要在眨眼间掏出如此巨资，恐怕难如登天。
“见我作势欲起身离席，宰相急得如热锅上的蚂蚁，苦苦哀求我切莫轻率决断。他赌咒发誓称只要先行承兑一部分现银解燃眉之急便可；现银乃是救命的稻草。只要凭我高深莫测的金融算度能为苏丹谋得一条生路，朝廷定当以最实打实的重酬厚礼结草衔环相报。

===Original===

“Upon hearing this I professed to be plunged into profound thought for about a quarter of an hour, and ended by slowly laying before him as an original and masterly plan the following proposal:
“The poor (he had admitted) were taxed beyond the limits of endurance, and were even upon the point of revolt; the rich were hiding their hoards, and many forms of portable wealth were leaving the country. Let him abandon these uncouth and rapacious methods of obtaining revenue, and ask the wealthier of the loving subjects of the sovereign to *lend* him at interest what they would certainly refuse to pay him outright. In this way a smaller annual sum by far than was now raised to meet the exigencies of the war would suffice to meet the obligations of the government. The capital so raised would be spent upon the campaign; the charge imposed upon the people would, it is true, be perpetual; but it would be so much smaller than the existing taxation as to be everywhere welcomed.
“The vizier sadly responded that though he would be very happy to undertake such a course he feared that the wealthy inhabitants would never lend (knowing, as they did, the embarrassment of the government) save upon ruinous terms.
“I had been waiting for this confession, and I at once suggested that I could act as go-between. I would, said I, stand guarantor. My great wealth would at once restore opinion, the loan would certainly be taken up, and I should only make the nominal charge of five gold pieces every year for each hundred I had thus guaranteed.
“The vizier was so astounded at my generosity that he almost fell backward, but recovering himself, he poured forth the most extravagant thanks, which were hardly marred by the look I detected in his eye, a look certainly betraying the belief that such an offer from a commercial man could hardly be made in good faith. To reassure him I adopted what is known in the financial world as the seventh, or frankly simple, tone. I told him without reserve the total of my wealth (which I put at a fifth of its real amount) and promised to bring it in cash to offices which he should permit me to establish in the city.

===Chinese===

“听闻此言，我故作凝神沉思了约莫一刻钟工夫，最后慢条斯理地将一份绝妙创见的救世良方摊在案前：
“朝廷业已承认，底层黎庶受到的重税已超越承受极限，甚至濒临揭竿而起的边缘；富商巨室们则隐匿地窖窖藏金银，各色细软浮财正源源不断外流离境。朝廷何不彻底摒弃这等粗暴酷烈的敛财旧法，转而恳请君王那些忠心耿耿的有钱顺民，按利息向朝廷‘放贷’——这笔钱若是勒令他们平白上缴，他们自是宁死不从的。如此一来，每年为筹措战事所需的岁入开销便大为锐减，足以平抑朝廷的亏空。这般募集来的本钱尽可投入疆场征伐；诚然，施加在万民头上的这笔利息负担将是千秋万代的无底洞，但分摊到每年的数字却远比眼下的苛捐杂税轻巧得多，定能受到朝野万民的一致拥戴。
“宰相愁眉苦脸地叹息道：这等良策他自是求之不得，但他深知城中富豪（深悉朝廷窘迫底细）绝不肯轻易借粮，除非朝廷答应杀鸡取卵的倾家荡产条款。
“我单等他吐露这句心腹之言，便立刻自告奋勇充当居间保人。我说，老夫愿以身家性命立下担保。凭老夫的万贯家资，定能一举稳定市面人心，这笔公债定会被哄抢一空；而老夫每年仅仅象征性地就所担保的每一百枚（100）金币，收取区区五枚（5）金币的微薄酬劳罢了。
“见我如此大公无私、仗义疏财，宰相惊愕得险些仰面跌倒；回过神来后，他对我感激涕零千恩万谢，唯独眼底掠过的一抹眼神教我尽收眼底——那眼神清清楚楚出卖了他心中的算盘：一个精明市侩的商贾开出这等慈悲条件，怎么可能是出于善心。为了教他安心，我施展出金融界俗称的第七号心法——即大巧若拙的纯朴语调。我毫不隐瞒地向他交底了自己的身家（实际上我只报了真实资产的五分之一），并保证随后便将现银运入他在城中恩准老夫开设的钱庄公堂。

===Original===

“Entering the next day with a million pieces of gold charged upon a train of very heavily laden camels, I set up my bank in the most crowded portion of the bazaar, published news of my intention to support the government, inviting the public inspection of the metal so lent, and at the same time proposing that any who desired regular interest of four pieces guaranteed by myself annually upon every hundred should come forward to take the loan off my hands. The hoards of gold still remaining in the country reappeared as though by magic﻿—so much more delightful is it to lend voluntarily at interest than to pay away under torture forever﻿—and at last there applied at my office for the favour of extending a loan ten times as many citizens as the situation required.
“My terms with the government were simple, and, I am sure, moderate. All that I asked was that the tax collectors should in future pay their receipts into my chest, from which I pledged myself to hand over to the government whatever surplus there might be after I had paid to the lenders their annual interest, four pieces, and kept for myself a fifth piece, which formed my tiny and not unearned commission.
“In this way I rapidly repaid myself and also took one piece on every hundred *others* had subscribed. The learned men of the place, who had never before imagined so simple and practical a plan, treated me with almost supernatural reverence. I was consulted upon every operation of war, my guarantee was eagerly sought for in other financial ventures, and I was able, I am glad to say, to secure other commissions without touching a penny of my treasure﻿—I had but to hold it forth as a proof of good faith.
“The enemy was repelled. But victory was not won. The war dragged on for a year and there was no decision. Gold grew scarce, and again the government was in despair.
“I easily relieved them. ‘Write,’ I said, ‘promises on paper to be repaid in gold.’ They did as I advised﻿—paying me (at my request) a trifle of half a million for the advice. I handled the affair﻿—on a merely nominal profit. I punctually met for another year every note that was paid in. But too many were presented, for the war seemed unending and entered a third year.
“Then did I conceive yet another stupendous thing. ‘Bid them,’ said I to the sultan, ‘take the notes as money. Cease to repay.

===Chinese===

“翌日，在由重载骆驼驮运的一百万枚（1000000）金币簇拥下，我昂然进城，在巴扎最熙熙攘攘的闹市开设了我的钱庄；我大张旗鼓地贴出榜文，宣称自己决意举资襄助朝廷，延请全城士庶公开展览核验这些用于借贷的真金白银；同时号召凡愿认领这批公债、坐享老夫亲自出面担保的每年每一百枚（100）金币稳赚四枚（4）利息的殷实之家，踊跃前来从老夫手中分包承接债权。散落在全邦民间的金银窖藏宛若受了神法召唤般纷纷破土重见天日——毕竟，心甘情愿放贷吃息，比起在皮鞭夹棍逼迫下被盘剥得一干二净，不知要惬意快活上多少万倍——最终，挤到我公堂门前争抢放贷恩准的市民，足足比实际所需多出了十倍（10）之巨！
“老夫同朝廷开列的条款极其简明扼要，且我敢断定，堪称仁至义尽。我唯一的诉求，便是勒令往后朝廷各路税吏衙役，必须将征得的所有税银全额缴入我的金库；而老夫则立誓保证，待我向认购公债的储户兑付完他们每年四枚金币的利息、并扣除老夫充当佣金的区区第五枚金币之后，将金库结余的一切盈余悉数解缴国库。
“凭此神机妙算，我不费吹灰之力迅速收回了自己的全部本钱，且还能从旁人认购的每一百枚（100）金币中稳稳净抽一枚干股佣金。彼邦的学者鸿儒这辈子从未见识过如此精妙绝伦且立竿见影的旷世奇谋，待老夫宛如对待降临人间的活神仙。朝廷每逢重大军机无不向老夫垂询，老夫的签字担保在其他商贸金融风潮中成了各方争抢的香饽饽；教人欣慰的是，老夫动都不必动库房里的半文私蓄，便能将各色大笔中介佣金尽收囊中——老夫只需将那些金箱摆在前厅充当信誉靠山便足矣。
“敌军被击退了，然而胜负未分。战事旷日持久拖延了整整一年，始终难解难分。金银日益吃紧，朝廷再次陷入了走投无路的绝境。
“我轻而易举便为他们解了围。‘下旨，’我说道，‘发行由朝廷亲笔担保、见票承诺以足色黄金偿付的纸质债票。’他们照单全收——并（应我的要求）慷慨解囊赏给我区区五十万（500000）金币作为献策的咨询劳务费。我接盘经办了这桩大案——仅抽取了象征性的微薄车马费。在接下来的一年里，我对每一张呈送前来的纸票皆如期兑付足额现银。然而由于战事看似永无休止、并拖入了第三年（3），市面上前来挤兑现银的纸票委实如潮水般汹涌。
“便是在那时，老夫脑海中参透了又一桩震古烁今的旷世绝谋。我对苏丹进言道：‘索性勒令天下万民将纸票直接充当钱币流通！自此终止现银兑付！

===Original===

Write, not “I will on delivery of this paper pay a piece of gold,” but, “this *is* a piece of gold.” ’
“He did as I told him. The next day the vizier came to me with the story of an insolent fellow to whom fifty such notes had been offered as payment for a camel for the war and who had sent back, not a camel, but another piece of paper on which was written ‘This *is* a camel.’
“ ‘Cut off his head!’ said I.
“It was done, and the warning sufficed. The paper was taken and the war proceeded.
“It was I that prepared the notes, and on each batch I exacted my necessary commission, my little commission, my due.
“It was not in my nature, dear nephews, how ever, in those days of hard and honest work, to lie idle. When I had put the sultan on his legs it occurred to me that the *enemy’s government* was also very probably in similar straits. I therefore visited the enemy’s capital by a roundabout route, and concluded with the vizier of that opulent but agitated state a similar bargain.
“The war thus replenished at its sources raged with redoubled ardour, for ten more years, and.﻿ ﻿…”
“But, uncle,” said the fourth nephew, who was an athlete and somewhat stupid, and who had heard of this double negotiation with round eyes, “surely they must have both been very angry with you!”
The excellent Mahmoud raised his left hand in protest. “Dear lad!” cried he, “how little you know the world! Angry? Why, each regarded me in the first place as a genius whose ways it was impossible to unravel, in the second place as a public necessity, in the third as a benefactor arrived at a miraculous moment; and as for the fact that I was aiding both sides, I have only to tell you that among the people of that region it is thought the proper part of all financiers to act in this fashion. I should have been treated with deserved contempt had I betrayed any scruples upon so simple a matter. Nay, I am sure that either party reposed the greater trust in me from the fact that my operations were thus universal.﻿ ﻿… But to proceed:
“The Mercy of Allah was never more apparent in my career than in the way these two sultans and their subjects fought like raging dogs upon the proceeds of those loans which the wealthy citizens upon either side had provided, and upon the mountains of paper which I spent half the day in signing.
“These loans increased ten, twenty, thirty-fold.

===Chinese===

票面上别再题写“凭此纸兑付足色金币一枚”，索性直接改写成：“此纸“即为”足色金币一枚”！’
“苏丹依计而行。翌日宰相神色慌张地跑来向我诉苦，说有个狂妄之徒，朝廷征收他一峰战驼充作军用，奉上五十张（50）此类纸币抵账，那厮竟把骆驼牵了回去，反倒递送回来另一张纸片，上面赫然写着：‘此纸“即为”骆驼一峰！’
“‘拉出去砍了！’我断然下令。
“刽子手咔嚓一刀，这一儆百的警示便立竿见影了。全天下乖乖接纳了纸币，战事得以继续如火如荼地推进。
“印制这批纸钞的全是老夫麾下的工坊，每印发一批，我自当克扣下属于老夫所必需的佣金、老夫那点微不足道的佣金、老夫应得的汗马酬劳。
“然而，我亲爱的侄儿们，在那些勤勉苦干的岁月里，我的天性绝容不得我安享清闲。当我扶持着苏丹重新站稳脚跟之际，我猛然省起：那敌邦朝廷，八成亦深陷在完全一模一样的财力泥潭中不可自拔啊！于是我抄小路迂回潜入敌邦国都，同那座同样豪富却焦头烂额的敌国宰相，依样画葫芦敲定了一模一样的一份放贷盟约！
“战事得此两头通吃的源头血脉注资，以翻倍的狂热重新燃起冲天烈焰，整整又厮杀了十年（10）之久，而且……”
“可是，伯父，”四侄是个头脑简单、四肢发达的跤手，听闻这等脚踏两只船的离奇买卖，瞪圆了铜铃大眼嚷道，“两边的主子要是知道了，岂不是都要把您给生吞活剥了！”
德高望重的马哈茂德抬起左手制止了他。“痴儿啊！”他叫道，“你对这大千世界究竟有多不开窍！生气？哈！他们双方首先皆把老夫奉为神机莫测的旷世奇才，其次视老夫为社稷所系的救命稻草，再者视老夫为危难之际神兵天降的万家生佛；至于老夫两头放贷通吃的实情，我只需告诉你们一句话：在彼方水土的百姓看来，全天下的金融巨擘理所当然都该这般行事！老夫若是在这等浅显的常识上表现出半点妇人之仁的道德顾忌，只会被两边天下名宿戳脊梁骨耻笑唾弃！恰恰相反，正是由于老夫的运作已然超脱于凡俗偏狭之上，两邦朝野反倒对我推崇备至、深信不疑！……且听我接着往下讲：
“在老夫的峥嵘岁月里，真主的仁慈（Mercy of Allah）从未像此刻这般昭彰显赫：两位苏丹及其臣民，手握由两邦富豪百姓倾囊凑出的放贷本钱，以及老夫每日耗费半天工夫画押签署的堆积如山的纸钞，如两条疯狗般咬得昏天黑地！
“这些放贷的规模翻了十倍（10）、二十倍（20）、乃至三十倍（30）！

===Original===

It was always I that guaranteed them; I had not to risk or expend one miserable dinar of my horde, and yet yearly my commission came rolling in, in larger and larger amounts, until at last the arduous but glorious campaigns were terminated in the total exhaustion of one of the two combatants (at this distance of time I forget which), and his territory and capital were laid under an enormous indemnity (which I again financed without the tedium of myself producing any actual metal of my own). As the beaten state might have repudiated its obligations I was careful to meet the patriotic clamours of the victorious populace, and to see that the territory of the vanquished should be annexed. You appreciate the situation, my dear fellow?” said the aged Mahmoud conversationally to his eldest nephew.
“I think so, uncle,” said the lad doubtfully, screwing up his face.
“It is quite simple,” said the wealthy old man, clearing his throat. “The peoples of both states (now happily united) were taxed to their utmost capacity; the one strong and united government guaranteed a regular revenue; a proportion of this revenue was annually distributed as a *fixed* income to the wealthy few who had subscribed my loans; another portion, amounting by this time annually to considerably more than my original capital, was retained in my coffers; and the mechanism of this was the more simple from the fact that all the public revenues passed through my own hands as state banker before any surplus was handed over to the crown.”
The old man ceased. His benevolent lips were murmuring a prayer.
At this moment the hideous call for prayer from the minaret would no longer be denied, and the seven boys, plunged in profound thought, retired slowly to the poverty-stricken home of the physician, their father. They found him tired out with having sat up all night at the sickbed of a howling dervish, who in his last dying whisper (and that a hoarse one), had confessed his total inability to pay the customary fee.

===Chinese===

而自始至终充当总担保人的，永远是老夫！老夫从自个儿的库房里连一文可怜巴巴的第纳尔都未曾动用或冒险，然而每年滚滚而来的提成佣金却呈泰山压顶之势越积越厚；直到最后，在一场漫长艰苦却名垂青史的鏖战中，交战双方中的一方（相隔经年，老夫早已记不清究竟是哪一方了）彻底筋疲力尽全面崩盘，其国土与王都皆被强加了天文数字的战争赔款——而这笔赔款，老夫再次出面替他们承保放贷，免去了老夫自掏腰包掏出任何真金白银的琐碎麻烦。鉴于战败国很可能会赖账赖债，我体贴入微地顺应了战胜国万民的爱国狂热，一举督促将战败国的版图全盘吞并！我亲爱的孩子，你可参透这其中的大玄机了？”上了年纪的马哈茂德以闲聊的口吻向长侄探问道。
“侄儿大概琢磨出来了，伯父，”男孩皱着眉头，满腹狐疑地应道。
“事情再浅显不过了，”腰缠万贯的老人清了清嗓子，“如今两邦万民（已然欣喜若狂地融为一统）皆被压榨到了竭泽而渔的税负极点；一个空前强盛的大一统王朝为岁入提供了铁打的担保；这笔岁入里的固定份额，作为稳赚不赔的年息分配给当初认购老夫公债的少数富豪；而另一笔到此时每年的进项已远远超过老夫当初全部初始本钱的巨额财富，则永无休止地流入老夫的私家金库；而维系这一庞大机器运转的手法更是易如反掌：盖因作为国家唯一钱庄总督，朝廷的所有赋税在将任何结余赏赐给皇室之前，无一例外皆得先行打老夫的手上过一道关隘！”
老人收声打住。他慈悲的双唇正念念有词地呢喃着祷词。
正在此时，宣礼塔上那凄厉刺耳的呼拜祈祷声已然不容推卸，七个（7）陷入沉思的小男孩缓缓退出了房间，步履蹒跚地返回贫困潦倒的郎中父亲家中。他们发现父亲因通宵守候在一位狂呼乱叫的托钵僧病榻前而疲惫不堪；那位托钵僧在临终气绝的最后一丝沙哑微弱的耳语中，向郎中坦白自己压根掏不出平日问诊的几个药钱。

## X *Al-Hisān*, or “The Horse” / 第十章 马
===Original===

When the nephews of Mahmoud once again attended their uncle at the hour of public executions he gazed at them in his benevolent fashion, again stroking his long beard, the better to expose the jewels upon his fingers, and continued the tale of his fortunes.
“You left me, my dear children, at the end of my last recital in a very deplorable condition. You will remember that through the superior business ability of a merchant renowned for his organizing power, grasp of detail, sense of affairs, etc., etc., etc., I had been reduced in property to a few camels and their attendants, and that even this poor remainder of my fortune I had lost through a miscalculation of the camel market on the eve of war.
“Your filial affection will also recall the bitter mood in which I hesitated whether to precipitate myself from a minaret or to hang myself from a beam.
“Advantages and disadvantages appeared equally balanced between these two courses; and though my long training in commerce had led me to make rapid decisions (as being the most certain way of forestalling competitors), yet I confess that in this debate I stood uncertain for nearly half an hour.
“It was well I did so; for in that half-hour was manifested in a triumphant manner the Mercy of Allah to them that fear Him.
“As I stood there, among strangers, without one single coin left in the world and utterly devoid of credit, with no knowledge even of how I should get food upon the following day, I heard cries and a confusion of horses’ hoofs, and saw galloping down the street towards me a very finely-bred grey horse, with flowing mane and a loose bridle. It bore a noble saddle of Indian workmanship, but no rider; while, some hundreds yards behind, impotently ran and gesticulated a corpulent man who, from his dress, seemed of some wealth and consequence. My first instinct was to catch the runaway like any beggar and restore him to his master in hope of some small reward; a few pence that might buy me food that evening and lodging for one night.
“But the beneficent Creator soon put other thoughts into the mind of his servant. I had caught the horse indeed. Its panting owner had slackened his pace and was coming towards me in a more dignified manner﻿—when it struck me that the animal (which was restive) could be better controlled were I in the saddle.
“I am, my dear nephews, as I have told you three times, no horseman.

===Chinese===

当马哈茂德（Mahmoud）的侄儿们在公开行刑的时辰再次前来拜谒他们的叔父时，老人一如既往慈眉善目地凝视着他们，一边再次捋了捋长髯，以便更显眼地炫耀指间佩戴的硕大宝石，随后接着讲述他的生财之道。
“孩子们，上一回老夫讲到最后，想必你们还记得我沦落到了何等悲惨可怜的境地。你们当记得，由于一位以擅长统筹全局、明察秋毫、精明强干闻名的大客商展现出的超凡商业才干，我的全副身家被盘剥得只剩下几峰骆驼和几个脚夫；而就连这最后一点可怜巴巴的家当，也因我在大战前夕对骆驼市行情的误判而赔了个精光。
“凭着诸位的孝思，想必你们还记得我当时痛苦万分、在究竟该从宣礼塔上纵身跃下一了百了、还是找根房梁悬梁自尽之间徘徊不定的惨状。
“这两条死路看似难分高下、利弊相当；尽管多年的商海历练教我养成了当机立断的魄力（毕竟这是抢先挫败竞争对手的最稳妥法门），然而我得坦白，在那场求死的纠结中，我竟犹豫不决了将近半个时辰。
“幸亏我犹豫了；因为正是在那半个时辰里，真主的仁慈（Mercy of Allah）以绝妙显赫的方式，降临到了敬畏真主之人的头上！
“正当我孑然伫立在外邦异乡、手里连半文铜板都不剩、信用扫地、甚至不知明日的一口干粮打何处来的绝境中时，忽听街头传来一阵喧嚷与凌乱急促的马蹄声。迎面朝我疾驰而来的，是一匹血统极纯正的青骢骏马，鬃毛飞扬，缰绳松脱。马背上配着一盘做工考究的印度精工雕花马鞍，背上却不见骑手；在后方数百码处，一个身形肥硕、看穿戴颇有身家体面的富商正气喘吁吁、徒劳无功地手舞足蹈狂奔追赶。我最初的本能，本想像寻常乞丐那般一把攥住奔马，将它奉还给失主，以求换取些许微薄的打赏——区区几文赏钱，好歹能换来今晚的一顿饱饭和一夜宿费。
“然而仁慈的造物主旋即向他的仆人脑海中灌注了截然不同的神启。我确实一把勒住了奔马。那位气喘吁吁的失主见状放慢了脚步，正摆出一副威严尊贵的架势朝我踱步走来——恰在此刻，我猛然省起：这匹性烈暴躁的畜生，若是由我跨上马鞍，岂不是能驾驭得稳妥得多？
“我亲爱的侄儿们，正如我先前三次向你们坦白过的那样，老夫压根不习骑术。

===Original===

My more habitual steed is the donkey and though I have, since my attainment to high rank, taken part in ceremonial processions, and even in the hunting to which His Majesty so kindly invites me, yet I must confess to you that whenever I have to ride *now* I take care to be provided with an animal not only trained in the most exact manner, but also previously soothed with drugs.
“I had, however, taken to the saddle when necessity drove me, as you have seen. And on this occasion, although the beast was infinitely more mettlesome than any I had yet dared to face, I took the risk. Courage was granted to me from on High. I scrambled into the saddle, but found that my control of the creature was no better than it had been when I stood at his head.
“I cannot swear that, in the bewilderment of the moment, I kept a sufficiently tight rein. I will not even swear that the value of the opportunity was lost on me. I certainly remember delivering several violent kicks with either heel into the ribs of the unquiet brute. There followed a few minutes in which (under my direction, I must admit) he seemed to be galloping farther and farther away from his original master, and at full speed down the main street of the town. I heard cries arising behind me on every side, and upon attempting to look round (a difficult feat for one so unused to the saddle) I was aware of a now considerable mob, in the midst of which I saw the distant figure of the horse’s wealthy owner frantically exclaiming and gesticulating.
“Nothing, my dear nephews, is more foolish than to treat generously, or even rationally, an excited crowd of human beings. All historians and philosophers will tell you that man in this state is but a wild beast, to be fled or mastered according to our abilities.
“As I had not ability to master them, then it was clearly my duty to flee them. Moreover, even as I urged the horse to further efforts, I confusedly appreciated what difficulty I should have in explaining my position, were I to attempt to return. We thundered through the open gate into the country outside, and by that time I had no course but frankly to take the track across the plain and shake off my pursuers forever.

===Chinese===

我平素更惯常骑乘的坐骑向来是毛驴；即便自我位极人臣之后，也曾参与过各种仪仗盛典、乃至陛下极体贴恩准老夫随扈的皇家围猎，但我不得不向你们交底：如今每逢我不得不骑马出行，我都格外注重挑选一匹不仅受过最严苛驯服、且事先喂过镇静药物的温顺牲口。
“然而，正如你们所见，在走投无路穷困交加的关头，我也曾硬着头皮跨上过马鞍。而眼下这一回，尽管这头牲口比我生平胆敢面对的任何烈马都要桀骜不驯得多，我还是横下心来铤而走险了。勇气从天而降加持于我。我手忙脚乱地爬上马鞍，却赫然发现自己在马背上驾驭这畜生的本事，比起先前站在马头前牵马时好不到哪儿去。
“在仓皇失措的慌乱关头，我不敢赌咒发誓说自己把缰绳勒得足够紧；我甚至不敢发誓说自己没看出这千载难逢的暴富良机。我清清楚楚记得，自个儿的双脚脚跟狠狠朝着这暴躁畜生的肋巴骨上猛踹了几脚。接下来的几分钟里（我不得不承认，全是在我的暗中督促之下），它似乎正风驰电掣般沿着市镇的主街一路狂奔，离它原本的东家越来越远。我听见身后四面八方传来了排山倒海的呼喊声；当我试图回头张望时（对于一个生疏骑术之人而言委实是桩高难度壮举），我赫然瞥见身后已然聚集了一大群嘈杂的人群，而在人群正当中，那匹骏马的原主人在远处正歇斯底里地顿足叫骂、手舞足蹈。
“我亲爱的侄儿们，天下间最愚不可及的蠢事，莫过于企图以宽仁乃至理智去应付一群情绪激亢的暴民。所有史家与哲人皆会告诫你们：人类一旦陷入这种狂热状态，便不过是茹毛饮血的野兽，全凭各人的手段，要么避之不及，要么铁腕镇压。
“既然老夫没有镇压他们的通天本事，那么拔腿逃之夭夭自当是老夫的神圣天职。况且，即便在我策马狂奔之际，我心中亦混沌地参透：倘若我此刻掉头折返，该如何向众人洗清自个儿的干系？我们伴随着雷霆般的马蹄声呼啸穿过敞开的城门，冲入了城外的辽阔原野；到了那个地步，老夫别无他途，唯有坦荡自若地顺着横穿平原的大道纵马疾驰，将身后的追兵彻底甩脱得无影无踪。

===Original===

“Admire, my dear nephews, the steps by which Providence, when It desires to succour one of Its favourites, will lead him through one consequence after another until at last he stands secure in the possession of some considerable sum of money! Here was I, not ten minutes before, contemplating death as the only issue from my poverty, and now mounted on a fine steed, seated in a saddle of price, and free to try any new adventure.
“I kept my handsome mount at the gallop until the gates were far behind me and all echo of the confused cries of my pursuers was lost. I checked him to a sharp trot until we had passed the first low rise of rolling land which hid my movements from the city. I then judged it reasonable to proceed at a pace less trying to the poor animal who had so befriended me. I noted from his freshness that he could but recently have left the stable. I did not hesitate, though with intervals of repose, to continue all day long to put a greater and greater distance between myself and that unfortunate misunderstanding which I had left behind me.
“By evening my many hours’ acquaintanceship with my horse had increased my pride in his possession, and I turned my mind away from all morbid considerations of his former owner. My only anxiety was for the night. Judge therefore of my satisfaction when, a full hour before the setting of the sun, I found myself, on emerging from a considerable wood, facing the walls of a new city, the gates of which stood about a league away from the spot whence I had first caught sight of it.
“I lingered in this pleasant pasture at the edge of the wood, loosening my horse’s girths, unbridling his bit, and letting him graze at large on the delicious herbage.
“I reclined myself, for repose, upon that same grass, and mused upon the distant prospect of domes and minarets under the mellow light, my thoughts naturally turning to conjecture what sums I might acquire in cash from the citizens within those walls during my enjoyment of their hospitality.
“The sun was barely set when I rode into the town; noting on the walls the usual proclamation against the eating of dates and receiving, as was due to one riding so well-accoutred and so fine a horse, the respectful looks of the passersby, and the humble but prolonged gaze of the guard at the gate.

===Chinese===

诸位且好生赞叹吧，我亲爱的侄儿们，天意在属意搭救它的宠儿时，其神机妙算究竟是何等层层推进、环环相扣，直至最终教那人稳稳当当坐拥万贯家资！就在不到十分钟（10）前，老夫还在视自尽为摆脱赤贫的唯一生路，而此刻我却高踞在一匹纯血良驹之上，身下垫着价值连城的华美宝鞍，且海阔凭鱼跃，尽可随心所欲开启任何崭新的冒险了！
“我策动胯下的神骏一路狂奔，直到城门远远落在了身后，追兵嘈杂混乱的呼喊声彻底消弭在虚空之中。我勒紧缰绳让它转为快步小跑，直到翻过第一道起伏的漫坡矮岗，将我的行踪彻底隔绝在城池的视线之外。此时我才断定，该以一种不至于过分折磨这匹有恩于我的可怜畜生的速度前行了。从它的神骏矫健我看出，它此前想必刚从马厩里牵出不久。尽管中途几度驻足歇脚，我毫不迟疑地终日策马狂奔，将自己与身后那桩不幸的误会拉得越来越远。
“及至傍晚，与马儿朝夕相处数个时辰之后，我对坐拥这匹骏马的自豪感与日俱增，脑海中彻底摒弃了关于它原主人的任何病态顾虑。我唯一的操心之处只在于今夜该何处栖身。因此，当太阳落山整整一个时辰前，我穿出一片繁茂的森林、赫然面对一座崭新城池的巍峨城墙时——城门距离我初次窥见它的林缘大约只有一联盟路程——你们尽可想象老夫心中奔涌的快慰与释然！
“我在林缘这片令人心旷神怡的草甸上流连小憩，松开了马肚带，卸下了马嚼子，任由马儿在这片肥美的青草地上信步啃食。
“我自个儿也惬意地仰卧在青草丛中休憩，沐浴在柔和的余晖下，遥望着远处掩映的座座穹顶与清真寺尖塔，脑中本能地暗自忖度：在城中百姓热情款待老夫的期间，我究竟能从这高墙之内的顺民手中搜刮捞取多少现银呢？
“夕阳刚刚隐入地平线之际，我策马踱进了市镇；城墙上依稀可见张贴着严禁食用椰枣的例行告示；而正如对待一位骑乘着这等配齐华贵鞍鞯的神骏良驹的大人物所应有的礼遇那般，迎面而来的行人们纷纷投来敬畏的目光，城门守卫亦投来了谦卑而意味深长的凝视。

===Original===

As I noted their attitude I could not but thank heaven for one more mercy which was now revealed to me. Had I happened to find this horse after some days of misfortune my own outward appearance would have ill consorted with his. How manifest was the dispensation of Providence whereby I came upon him within an hour after losing my other property, and, therefore while I was still in my decent merchant’s dress, cleanly, well-shaven, and groomed!
“There was in the central square of this town a runnel specially disposed for watering beasts of burden, and my horse (we had forded but one stream in all that day’s journey) eagerly approached it. I fondly patted his neck and thought with pleasure of how noble a friend I had acquired; for as you must have read, there is a sort of affinity between man and the horse which readily makes them intimate after even a short acquaintance: especially if the man be of a business turn of mind and the horse of considerable value.
“From this mood into which I had fallen while my handsome mount was taking his simple refreshment, you may guess the perturbation caused me when I heard at my side an eager voice deliberately pitched in a low key so that it might be heard by none but myself. That voice was full of passionate necessity, and was asking me whether it would be possible *now*﻿—*here*﻿—*at once*, for me to dispose of my mount to a man whose life depended on it.
“I turned and made out in the dusk under the shadow of his cowl (part of which he had pulled over his features to make a sort of veil) a young man whose agitation made me yearn instinctively to take some advantage of him.
“ ‘Sir,’ he whispered hurriedly, ‘my request is not only impertinent but extraordinary. I know that you will not understand it. I can only implore Heaven for a miracle. My time is very short. I know not how far my pursuers may be. My life is dear to me and still dearer is my honour. The night is falling. Here is my opportunity, which, if I do not take, all is over with me.’
“He thereupon passed up to me a leather bag upon opening which I could see in the fading light a quantity of gold pieces, and he accompanied the gesture with so imploring a look as explained the vastness of his offer.
“Had I passed through any series of adventures less astonishing than those of the last day and night, I would not have listened for a moment to a first proposal.

===Chinese===

目睹他们的敬畏姿态，我禁不住再次感谢上苍此刻向我昭示的又一重浩荡洪福。倘若我是落魄流浪数日之后碰巧撞见这匹宝马，我自身的寒酸尊容势必同它的神骏风采格格不入。天意的造化是何等神妙昭彰啊：它竟让我在倾家荡产后不到一个时辰内便撞见了它，因此老夫身上依然穿着商贾体面体统的锦袍，干干净净，剃刮整洁，仪容齐整！
“在这座城池的中央广场上，辟有一条专门供驮畜饮水的清泉水槽；我那匹坐骑（终日奔波中我们仅涉过一条小溪）急不可耐地凑上前去畅饮。我怜爱地轻抚着它的颈鬃，心中欣喜地思忖着自己结识了一位何等高贵的朋友；因为正如你们书中所读到的，人与马之间天然存在着某种灵犀相通，即便结识短暂亦能迅速情同莫逆——尤其是当那人满脑子生财算度、而那匹马又价值连城时更是如此。
“就在我沉浸在英俊坐骑享用清泉的闲适心境中时，你们尽可想象老夫听闻身侧传来一阵刻意压低嗓门的急促低语时是何等惊诧！那声音分明只求落入老夫一人的耳中，语调里充溢着生死攸关的绝望与迫切，正询问老夫能否在‘此时’、‘此地’、‘立刻’将这匹坐骑作价转让给一个命悬一线的逃难之人！
“我转过头去，在暮色阴影中，顺着兜帽的遮蔽（他将兜帽的一角拉扯在面颊前充当面纱），辨认出一个神情极度慌张仓皇的年轻后生；他的魂飞魄散教老夫本能地涌起一股趁火打劫狠狠敲上一笔的强烈冲动。
“‘大人，’他惊惶失措地耳语道，‘小人的非分之求不仅唐突冒昧，更是匪夷所思。小人深知您无法参透个中原委。小人唯有祈求上苍降下奇迹！小人的死期迫在眉睫。我不知身后的追兵距离此地究竟还有多远。小人的性命固然金贵，可小人的清白声誉更是重于性命！夜幕已然合拢。这是小人唯一的生路，若是错失良机，小人便万劫不复了！’
“说罢，他急不可耐地将一只皮钱袋塞进我手里；在残存的天光下解开袋口，我隐隐瞥见里面装满了沉甸甸的金币；而他随之投来的那道近乎哀求的绝望眼神，更是将他开出这等天价的缘由诠释得淋漓尽致。
“倘若老夫先前经历的离奇风浪稍稍逊色于过去这一昼夜的奇遇，老夫断不会对买主的初次开价便轻易首肯。

===Original===

I would have attempted, as was indeed my duty, to raise his price, to obtain immediately some of his apparel as well as his purse; and if possible a written promise of further payment as well. For he was distraught with fear and men in that condition are easily squeezed. But the rate at which I had been living, the perpetual succession, first of unfortunate and then of fortunate accidents, showed the manifest finger of God in all that had so far favoured me since the morning, and strangely convinced me. Without another word I took the bag of gold and dismounted.
“The young man, with a new expression such as I had never yet seen upon anyone’s face, said not a word, no, not even of gratitude to his benefactor; turned the horse’s head down the main street of the town, wisely refraining from too rapid an exit lest his passage should be remembered, and went at no more than a sharp trot through the gate into the falling darkness without. The last I saw of him he appeared, a dark figure rapidly dwindling against the darkening sky, framed in the tiled horseshoe of the Bab-El-Soued.﻿ ﻿… But even as I gazed a troop of mounted horsemen thundered past me and passed through that same gate into the night.
“For my part I thought no more of him, but turned back to the centre of the town. There I was, with three times the price of my horse in my pocket, and thus with solid ground on which to stand for the future.
“My first care was to make an excellent meal, my next to discover a good lodging for the night. In both I was fortunate. But before reciting my last evening prayers I took the precaution of informing a passing patrol that I had had a horse stolen from me; for, in business, no opportunity should be neglected. I then recommended myself to the Divine protection and fell into a sweet repose.
“Next morning, after I had humbly and devoutly recited my early prayers, I thought I would, before proceeding to any lucrative task, divert myself a little so that I might later approach serious business with a more open mind.
“It is my custom, when I am in need of recreation from the cares of commerce, to frequent the criminal courts and to attend the sentences passed upon those brought before them, as well as to be a spectator of the ensuing executions.

===Chinese===

依循天职本分，我本该坐地起价，不仅要将他的要价再往上翻上一番，还要当场扒下他的衣袍连同钱袋一并据为己有，甚至逼他立下一张日后追加赔付的借据欠条。因为他已被恐惧吓破了胆，落入这等境地的人最容易被榨出油水。然而老夫近来的际遇节奏委实太过跌宕起伏，先是一连串倒霉透顶的横祸，紧接着又是接二连三的飞来横财；自清晨以来一路眷顾老夫的种种神迹，处处显露出真主的神奇指引，令我油然深信不疑。我二话不说，一把收下金币皮袋翻身下马。
“年轻人脸上浮现出一种老夫平生从未见过的奇特神色，他连半个字都未曾吐露，甚至未曾向他的大恩人道一声谢；他拨转马头，顺着城镇主街纵马而去，却极精明地克制着速度免得引起闲杂人等侧目，只以急促的小跑穿过城门，遁入了城外漆黑的夜色之中。老夫目送他的最后一瞥，是他那迅速在黯淡天际下缩小的漆黑身影，定格在巴卜·苏埃德（Bab-El-Soued）彩绘瓷砖的马蹄形拱门门洞里……然而就在老夫凝神注视之际，一队全副武装的轻骑兵策马轰然从我身旁疾驰而过，呼啸穿过那道城门扎进了茫茫黑夜。
“至于老夫，我压根不再去理会他的死活，径直踱步折回市中心。此时此刻，老夫怀里揣着相当于三倍马价的巨款，稳稳奠定了开创未来的坚实根基。
“老夫的第一要务自当是饱餐一顿珍馐盛宴，次要任务则是寻觅一处舒适的客栈下榻。两桩美事皆圆满如愿。然而在念诵入夜最后一篇祷词之前，我机警地向巡夜的差役报官，声称自己刚刚被人偷走了一匹心爱的宝马；因为在商海之中，任何天赐良机皆不可等闲错过。随后我将自己托付给神圣的护佑，安然坠入了甜美的梦乡。
“次日清晨，当我虔诚谦卑地诵读完早祷祷文之后，我想在投身任何生财营生之前，先好生寻欢作乐一番，以便随后能以更超脱的心境去打理严肃的经商大业。
“每当需要从纷繁的商海劳顿中解脱消遣时，老夫的例行雅兴向来是流连于审理重犯的刑律公堂，围观那些被押解出庭的囚徒领受死刑宣判，并作为座上客津津有味地赏鉴随后的血腥公开行刑。

===Original===

No pastime affords greater relief from the dull, everyday round of buying and selling; while the contrast between one’s own pleasant position and that of the pauper who is to be beheaded, adds a zest which I recommend to all men of affairs.
“I strolled, therefore, to the court in which I had heard that certain criminals were to be that morning briefly examined and presumably dispatched.
“Great was my surprise upon entering to find that I had come just in time to hear the last evidence given and sentence pronounced upon the same young man who had so imprudently bought my horse the night before! Did I say ‘imprudently’?﻿—Well! The designs of Providence are hidden from us, and it is not for me to judge another!﻿ ﻿… While I pitied him, therefore, I had nothing to reproach myself with, for I had fulfilled in the most honourable fashion the only contract with which I was concerned in the matter. The pursuers had arrested him before he had left the city more than a mile. He stood accused of eating dates: a practice (you will remember) forbidden throughout all those dominions. He had been seen in the act by the Sultan’s officers a week before and his name and description had been sent round to every city. Indeed a troop was hot upon his trail at the moment he had come up the night before imploring for my mount. Sentence was pronounced, and the unfortunate young man was led out to execution.
“My natural love of such sights would have led me to follow him, when one more act of Heaven (I dare not ascribe the inspiration to my poor unaided soul) suddenly put an exceedingly valuable thought into my mind. I addressed the judge in a loud voice, complaining in the matter of my horse. At first he was disturbed and inclined to silence me, not understanding what plea I could have in this particular case; but I made bold to arrest his attention and told him that the evidence I had chanced to hear proved clearly that the horse on which the unfortunate young man had tried to escape was one stolen from me but a few hours before. This I was prepared to prove. The officers of the court were examined and admitted my description to be exact as to the horse, and, what was a clinching piece of evidence, as to the details of the saddle, the workmanship of which they had noted.
“I informed the judge further that I had ridden into the town the evening before.

===Chinese===

再没有哪种消遣，能比这更能让人从平淡乏味的日常买卖中彻底解脱了；而对比自己身处优裕体面的优越境地与即将被推上断头台的赤贫死囚的悲惨下场，更平添了一份别样的刺激——我谨向全天下的商界同仁隆重推荐这桩雅兴。
“于是我信步溜达进公堂；听说当天上午正好有几名死囚要接受速审并就地正法。
“踏入公堂的那一刻，老夫委实大吃一惊：我恰好赶上听取最后的呈堂证供，并目睹法官向一名年轻后生宣判斩刑——而那后生，恰恰便是昨夜冒冒失失向我买下宝马的那位仁兄！我说他‘冒失’？——哎！天意的运筹玄妙莫测，岂是老夫这等肉体凡胎所能妄加论断的！……因此，尽管我心怀慈悲怜悯他，老夫心头却无半点自责之念，因为在这桩买卖中，我已极其正大光明地履行了唯独属于老夫的那份契约。追兵在离城还不足一英里处便将他捉拿归案。他被指控偷吃椰枣——想必诸位还记得，这等行径在彼邦全境可是杀无赦的死罪。一周前苏丹的密探亲眼撞见他偷吃，他的相貌特征与海捕文书早已飞传各城。事实上，昨夜他跑来向我哭求战马的当儿，一队骑兵早已死死咬在他身后了。判决落定，倒霉的青年当即被押解出堂候斩。
“出于对公开处决的酷爱，老夫本该尾随出去看个热闹；恰在此时，上苍降下的又一重神迹（老夫断不敢将这等灵光归功于自个儿微不足道的肉身凡胎），忽然将一个价值连城的绝妙念头注入我的脑海。我当堂放开嗓门大声喊冤，就自个儿失窃的骏马向法官申辩。起初法官面露愠色，挥手喝令我闭嘴，参不透我在这桩案子里究竟有何诉求；但我壮起胆子成功吸引了他的注意，并告知大人：适才我碰巧耳闻的供词铁证如山，足以证明那倒霉囚徒企图骑乘逃窜的骏马，恰恰正是数个时辰前从老夫手里失窃的那匹宝驹！老夫愿当堂对质。公堂差役受到勘问，不得不招认老夫描摹的马匹特征分毫不差；尤为致命的铁证，是老夫对马鞍各处雕花做工的细致描摹，差役们当时亦曾留心过那些特征。
“老夫进一步向法官呈禀，自己正是昨夜策马进城的。

===Original===

I was prepared to bring witnesses from the guard at the gate who had seen me pass. And when these were summoned they agreed that I had entered riding a horse of the description I had just given. I could see that the judge inclined to the justice of my plea; the officers of the court naturally fell in with his mood; I made him, I think, the more gracious by my assurance that I would not dream of making too exact a claim. If the animal were but restored to me I should be satisfied, nor would I ask anything for dilapidation or loss of time. I was only too glad (I said) to have been of the most insignificant service to the court.
“The judge now smiled upon me with evident approval, and was further confirmed in his decision by remembering that even if I claimed any compensation it would not come out of his pocket but the public’s; and I have no doubt that this argument, though not explicitly put forward, was present in the minds of all the officers of the court as well. The judge therefore ordered that the animal should be restored to me, and was pleased to use the following words. They are not my own. I am not responsible for them. But I am glad that he used them.
“ ‘This honest merchant,’ said he, ‘who has given a very clear account of his movements, we are in some fashion beholden to, on account of the temporary loss which he has suffered in the filching of his mount by the criminal with whom we have just dealt. He was indirectly the cause of that criminal’s arrest. The least we can do, therefore, is to give him his property back with the least possible delay. I order that the animal with all his accoutrements, having first been properly fed and groomed, shall be restored to him.’
“I very humbly bowed and thanked the court for its just decision. But a new complication arose.
