“老夫实在不忍心看着曾受雇于他的那些水手船工活活饿死，便将他们悉数收编入老夫名下，派去为大桥铺设引桥通道、进一步加固桥梁、粉刷栏杆诸如此类；至于那些老旧渡船，老夫将它们全部打发运往下游水域，在彼方它们远比留在老夫凭借魄力与公益仁心催生出宏伟大桥的此地更有用武之地。老夫以废旧木料的行市从原主手中将船只全盘吃下，顺带赚取了一笔区区数千第纳尔（1000）的微薄差价。
“这真是一桩地名随岁月附丽于山川的生动例证：时至今日，当年老渡船往返停靠的河岸渡头，依旧被当地人唤作‘疯汉之冢’。盖因据闻那位老汉临终前的遗愿，正是恳求将自己埋骨在他往昔摇橹渡河的水岸身旁。
“到了这步田地，全面通盘整饬大桥本身及其财政大计的时机，已然水到渠成。”

===Original===

Through my goodness of heart and generous carelessness﻿—defects or amiable frailties against which I have always to be upon my guard﻿—the whole thing had got into a very unbusiness-like condition. The tolls were not more than customary payments, though I had raised them from time to time. There was no careful distinction between the different kinds of traffic. There were no regulations for the hours at which the bridge should be used, nor ready means of checking the accounts.
“The new bridge had caused the town to increase largely. Its governors and those of the adjoining districts were rightly concerned in its proper ordering.
“The authorities of the neighbourhood fully agreed with me that it was necessary to put the thing upon a more regular footing. I suggested to them that before going further it would be but a kindly and reasonable act to consult those who made regular use of the bridge upon a large scale, and especially the merchants of the place and of the more distant towns upon the farther bank who crossed and recrossed at stated intervals and with considerable trains of traffic. These, therefore, were courteously convened. They were regarded as representing the mass of humbler footfolk and between us all we drew up an excellent arrangement.
“First we made ourselves into a council. Next we voted ourselves full powers to do what we liked in managing the bridge.
“The merchants who were regular users of the bridge and who passed and repassed with their trains upon an average once a month, were to be free of toll on condition that they should pay an annual subscription to the upkeep of the structure. It came to an average, for each of their beasts of burden, to about one-quarter of the public toll, and for each of their servants to less than one-half.
“The common folk of the town and the villages, the herdsmen and all the humbler multitude which used the bridge in less lucrative fashion were to pay a toll double the original, which, after all, was only fair when one considered that they were compelled to use the bridge as there was now no other passage across the stream. I should add that the local authorities which sat with us upon this council, after drawing up the ordinances, passed a local bylaw full of common sense and the spirit of order.

===Chinese===

“全因老夫骨子里的仁慈心肠与豪迈疏阔——这些致命弱点与亲和软肋，恰恰是老夫毕生时刻警惕戒惧之物——以致整座大桥的打理完全沦落到了毫无商业规范的散漫境地。过桥钱不过是约定俗成的随喜打赏，尽管老夫时不时也会略微调高些价码。对于各色不同门类的往来客货车马，桥上从未做过严谨细致的分类计价；既没有规定桥梁开闭通行的固定时辰，亦缺乏随时核验账目的明晰账册。
“而这座横跨两岸的新桥，已然推动本城规模呈现出爆发式的扩张。本城的长官乃至相邻郡县的牧守要员们，理所当然对大桥的井然秩序给予了高度关注。
“当地官府衙门与老夫完全不谋而合，认定必须将大桥的营运纳入更为规范正统的体制之中。老夫向长官们提议：在采取进一步动作之前，若能屈尊垂询那些常年大规模使用大桥的主顾豪商，尤其是本城以及大河彼岸远方重镇那些定期押运大批货队往返穿梭的商界领袖，委实不失为一项体恤仁厚而通情达理的善政。于是官府以极尽礼遇的规格将他们召集一堂。这些商界巨贾被理所当然地视作广袤底层布衣行人的全权代表；在我们大伙的通力协作之下，一套无懈可击的完美章程应运而生。
“首先，我们自发组建为最高大桥管理评议会。紧接着，我们全体一致表决，赋予评议会掌控与处置大桥一切事务的至高全权。
“那些作为大桥常客、平均每月押运大批驼队货旅往返穿梭的大商贾们，一律豁免过桥税金，条件仅是他们每年按期缴纳一笔用于桥体养护的年度会费。折算下来，他们的每一头牲畜所分摊的开支仅为寻常市井税率的四分之一左右，而其仆役脚夫的分摊额更是不到常人税率的二分之一。
“至于本城与周遭各村镇的寻常布衣百姓、放牧樵夫以及一切将大桥用于微薄非盈利生计的芸芸众生，则须缴纳较原先翻倍的过桥税金；平心而论，考虑到他们如今除老夫之桥外在整条大河上再无其他任何渡河途径、不得不走此桥的事实，这笔收费实在公道合理至极。老夫还当补充一句：同我们一道列席评议会的本省官府要员们，在拟定上述法令之后，旋即颁布了一条通篇闪烁着理性智慧与秩序精神的本地行政法令。”

===Original===

In this bylaw they forbade the use of any boats whatsoever for the crossing of the water, under the excellent plea that men had in the past occasionally been drowned from these and that, anyhow, there was now a good bridge and no necessity for this old-fashioned and backward kind of travel.
“People were also forbidden to swim the river between sunset and sunrise upon the grounds of security and police control, and between sunrise and sunset upon the grounds of decency.
“After the new regulations had been passed the gates were strengthened, regular officers were appointed to take the toll and I was public-spirited enough to permit my own servants to be withdrawn and these officials to be named (and paid) by the new council, retaining to myself no more than the right of receiving the tolls and taking on of course the burden of upkeep as against the sums which I received from the regular merchants. I also reserved to myself the right, whenever the council or the local authorities thought it necessary to have the bridge strengthened or repaired or painted, or ornamented, or decorated upon feast days, or covered with an awning during the great heats, to take up the contract for all these services at a price to be agreed upon between myself and the council and the local authorities, at the head of whom was my dear old friend the sheik.
“When all these arrangements had been made, the thing was on a proper basis and formed, I am glad to say, for many other similar arrangements a precedent, in which the advantages of the public and a proper return on capital were both considered. My ‘Bridge council’ as it was called was copied in many another enterprise in those parts, to not a few of which I was admitted as a director.
“But one must march with the times. It could not be denied that this conservative and established way of recouping expenses and interest by tolls, excellent in its time, no doubt, had its drawbacks. There was something rather absurd in these progressive days (such was the phrase used to me by my friend the sheik of the place﻿—which was now growing under the influence of my bridge to be a very large town), there was something rather absurd in the spectacle of gates put up to block that very passage which had only been erected for the convenience of the community!

===Chinese===

“在这道行政法令中，官府明令严禁任何人以任何形式的舟楫小船横渡大河，其给出的绝妙理由是：昔日偶有黎民因乘船渡河不幸溺毙，况且如今既已筑成如此坚固便利的大桥，这等陈腐倒退的原始出行方式早已毫无存续之必要。
“法令还同时下达严旨：严禁任何人在日落至日出之间私自泅水渡河，违者以危害城防治安与触犯宵禁严惩；而在日出至日落之间泅水渡河亦在严禁之列，违者以有伤风化、败坏公序良俗论处。
“待这些崭新的章程法令雷厉风行推行开来之后，各处桥门关卡皆得以全面加固筑防，官府正式委任了正规税吏专司把守关卡收取过桥金。老夫出于公忠体国之热忱，主动撤回了老夫自家的私奴家丁，听凭这批税吏由新成立的评议会统一遴选任命（并支领薪俸）；而老夫自己，仅谦逊地保留了坐收全部过桥税款的特权，并以自常客商贾手中收取的年度会费为对冲，全盘承担起修缮维护桥体的神圣重任。老夫还特意保留了一项专有权利：每当评议会或官府要员认定有必要对大桥进行加固、翻修、粉刷、美化装饰、逢年过节搭设彩棚、或在炎炎酷暑期间加盖遮阳凉棚时，老夫独家享有承揽上述所有工程的特许承包权，工程价款则由老夫同评议会及以老夫至交好友谢赫为首的官府长官共同协商敲定。
“待所有这些架构布置停当，大桥的营运便彻底步入了正轨；老夫十分欣慰地告诉你们：这一模式不仅实现了公众福祉与资本合理回报的兼顾双赢，更为彼处日后层出不穷的同类公私合营架构树立了光辉的典范前例。老夫所创设的‘大桥评议会’在彼邦各行各业的诸多大型产业中被疯狂效仿照搬，而老夫本人亦顺理成章被推举为其中不少产业的董事会董事。
“然而人必须与时俱进。无可否认，这种依靠设立关卡收取过路税来收回投资成本与利息的保守传统模式，在特定的历史阶段固然功勋卓著，却也绝非全无弊端。在那个飞速跃进的时代（这正是老夫那位谢赫挚友时常挂在嘴边的口头禅——而在老夫大桥的强力拉动下，彼处已然繁衍蜕变为一座气象恢弘的超级大都市），在大伙千辛万苦专为便利大众而建起的大道正中央，竟然赫然矗立着一道道横档拦截通行的闸门铁锁，这一幕在开明人士眼中委实显得荒诞滑稽！”

===Original===

What would not posterity think of us if they heard that we built a bridge and then put up gates to interfere with its constant and easy use? It was a burden also upon the community that officials should have to be employed at either end checking payments, keeping books and all the rest of it.
“What was worse, there seemed to be some leakage. Officials could not always be trusted to make an exact return (for they were of the baser sort at a small salary). It was suspected that their relatives and friends had been allowed to cross free of toll, for we could not keep a big watch at night and there was I fear a good deal of illicit use of the bridge.
“All of this, quite apart from the bad example it gave and the feeling of disorder it created, was also a source of anxiety to those who were concerned with the finance of the enterprise. The feeling grew rapidly﻿—at least it grew very strongly in me and I made every effort to spread it in others﻿—that progress and sundry other virtues with which the Plain prided itself (as against the half-barbaric people of the mountains) demanded that all these anomalies should cease, and that the simple policy of ‘**The Free Bridge**’ should triumph.”
As the aged merchant described the last stage of his adventure his face took on an animated look; he spoke with decision; there was a freedom in his gesture which recalled his old oratorical triumphs when he had occasion as a younger man to combine the practice of commerce, investment and finance with the public speeches which had rendered him famous. He seemed, for the moment, not so much the merchant as the senator, the Free Bridgeite of the great old days, and his nephews could not but admire the lofty air, the direct glance, the eloquent vibration of voice which accompanied this mood.
“I for my part,” continued the old gentleman, now transformed by the recollection of his part in public life, “did not fear to speak openly in the council and (such was my love for my fellow-citizens) even in the marketplace. I was untiring in explaining the simple economic principles underlying the policy of The Free Bridge.

===Chinese===

倘若后世子孙听闻我们修筑了一座大桥、却又在两端设立关卡闸门阻碍大伙畅通无阻地往返通行，后人该如何耻笑我们的愚昧？况且官府还得在桥梁两头常年豢养一批差役税吏，专司核对过桥款、登记账册诸如此类，这对全城纳税百姓而言委实也是一笔沉重的财政包袱。
“更要命的是，关卡账目似乎总在跑冒滴漏。指望那些领着微薄薪俸的底层微末税吏做到账目分毫不差，向来是靠不住的。坊间风传他们的三亲六戚都得以白白免票过桥；盖因我们在夜幕降临后无法维持庞大的值守哨探，老夫唯恐偷渡借道的非法行径早已屡禁不绝。
“凡此种种，不仅败坏了社会风气，制造出纷乱无序的观感，更是令那些事关本项产业财政命脉的要人寝食难安。一种强烈的思潮迅速蔓延开来——至少在老夫心中这一信念已然坚如磐石，且老夫不遗余力地向四下播撒此种理念：文明跃进以及平原之邦引以为豪的各色崇高美德（与山中那些半开化的茹毛饮血之徒相比），皆严正昭示着必须彻底终结上述所有乱象，必须让‘**义桥全免**’的英明国策大获全胜！”
当这位上了年纪的商人回忆描摹到他壮举的终局篇章时，他的面庞焕发出容光焕发的神采；他语调铿锵决断，举手投足间展现出一种大开大合的豪迈气派，令人不由联想起他年轻时横扫讲坛的雄辩风采——那时他总能将商贸实务、投资金融运作与令他名噪一时的公开演讲熔于一炉。在这一刹那，他仿佛不再是一个唯利是图的商贾，而是一位指点江山的元老院议员，一位叱咤风云的“义桥全免党”领袖；侄儿们目睹此情此景，无不为他那高屋建瓴的气度、坚毅锐利的目光以及伴随着这股豪情而激荡震颤的如洪钟般的嗓音所深深折服。
“至于老夫自己，”这位老先生在往昔参政荣光的记忆洗礼下恍若脱胎换骨，继续讲道，“老夫从不畏惧在评议会公堂之上畅所欲言，甚至（全凭老夫对这方土地黎民的一片赤子之心）敢于在人声鼎沸的露天市集登台布道。老夫不知疲倦地向万民宣讲贯穿于‘义桥全免’政策背后的简明经济学大道。”

===Original===

I was delighted to observe, as my efforts proceeded, two parties forming﻿—the Free Bridgeites, who had the tide with them and were in the spirit of their day, and another party which, for lack of a better name, I will call the Recalcitrants, who were but a hotchpotch of evil-minded malcontents, dolts, public enemies, and in general a body who had no argument save that things were very well as they were and it was a pity to change.
“I need scarcely tell you which of these competing interests won. Intelligence, business enterprise, public spirit, common sense, justice and eighteen or nineteen other things which for the moment escape me supported the glorious triumph of The Free Bridge. At last, when the moment was ripe for it to be voted upon, we swept our opponents out of existence at the polls, securing out of every 100 votes no less than fifty-three for our project.
“The sheik who, in the growing importance of the community was now confirmed in his office by his sovereign under the title of Excellent, delivered an unforgettable harangue, saying that the day when the tolls should be taken off the bridge and the gates thrown down would stand in the annals of his country next to its historic charter and its acceptance of the True Faith. Amid the deafening shouts of a vast concourse, composed, as I was amused to discover, of both parties indifferently, but all out for the occasion, this great official proceeded in state to the entrance of the bridge, cut symbolically the silken thread with which the gates upon either end had been tied and in loud tones declared the bridge open in the name of Allah and his Prophet. Women wept profusely and even strong men had difficulty in hiding their emotion; only the younger of the children and the animals accompanying the procession appeared indifferent. Of the four officials deputed for the watching of the tolls two were thrown into jail on the charge of malversation; the other two were, on my making an appeal for them, allowed to leave the country.
“The head of the opposing party who had done his best to defeat this great and necessary reform now, upon payment, openly admitted that he was converted; whatever sentimental attachment he might still cherish for his old views, he now clearly saw that they were no longer practical politics.

===Chinese===

“老夫欣喜地目睹，随着老夫布道的推进，坊间自然分化为泾渭分明的两大阵营：其一乃是‘义桥全免党’，他们顺应历史潮流，代表了时代的进步精神；而另一个阵营，在缺乏更恰当措辞的前提下，老夫且称之为‘顽固反动党’——这帮人不过是一群心怀叵测的怨怼之徒、蠢钝木石、全民公敌的乌合之众；大体而言，这帮顽固之徒根本拿不出像样的立论依据，除了死抱着‘眼下一切不是挺好么、瞎折腾改它作甚’这一套陈词滥调不放。
“老夫想必无需赘言，这两大针锋相对的利益阵营究竟谁笑到了最后。智慧、商业开拓精神、公忠体国之情操、常理良知、正义公道，外加老夫一时之间未能尽数的其他18或19样（18、19）崇高美德，共同铸就了‘义桥全免’运动的光辉胜利！终于，在公投对决的时机彻底成熟之际，我们在投票站以摧枯拉朽之势将对手扫进了历史的垃圾堆：在每一百张（100）选票之中，我们的伟大倡议足足斩获了不下53票（53）！
“那位谢赫长官如今随着城邦地位的急剧攀升，已获其宗主君王正式加封‘卓越者’显赫头衔；他老人家登台发表了一篇名垂青史的壮丽演讲，宣称废止大桥过桥税、彻底砸碎闸门铁锁的这一天，在彼邦的光辉史册上必将同当年建国颁布历史大宪章以及举国皈依正信的圣日并驾齐驱！在一片山呼海啸、震耳欲聋的万民狂欢欢呼声中——老夫颇为好笑地瞧见，两大阵营的百姓此刻全都不分彼此地汇聚在了一起，只顾尽情宣泄盛节的狂喜——这位尊贵的长官身着盛装、仪仗齐备地缓步迈向桥头，极具象征意义地当众剪断了系在桥头两端闸门上的五彩丝带，以如雷洪音庄严宣告：大桥自今日起奉真主及其使者之圣名，永远免费向全天下开放！现场的妇孺感动得泪如雨下，连铁骨铮铮的昂藏七尺男儿亦难掩热泪盈眶；唯独随行队列里的幼童以及牲畜浑浑噩噩、浑然无动于衷。至于原先专司把守关卡收取过路钱的4名税吏之中，有2人因贪污舞弊中饱私囊被当场打入死牢；另外2人在老夫的力保求情之下，获准流放驱逐出境。
“至于那位昔日曾使出浑身解数阻挠这场伟大改革的反动阵营头领，在老夫如数结清一笔体面的‘补偿费’之后，当众坦白宣称他已然大彻大悟、彻底归化；无论他内心深处对他那套旧理念还残留着怎样一缕依依不舍的怀旧感伤，他如今已然清醒地洞察到：那套玩意在现实政治之中早已行不通了。”

===Original===

“The gardens of the city were illuminated for three successive nights, cannon were fired and in view of the quite exceptional character of the occasion many criminals were pardoned, including the young brother of the head of the opposition who, under an assumed name, had languished in jail for several months.
“In all this enthusiasm it was easier to get through the practical details of the change, as the obstacle of petty detailed criticism proceeding from an ignorant public was removed.
“A new constitution was happily agreed upon in place of the old revenue from tolls. This old revenue had fluctuated between the annual amounts of 15,000 and 25,000 dinars. To replace it and to allow for all contingencies a fixed sum of 30,000 dinars was put aside as an annual charge upon the public rates to be allocated to the service of the bridge. This sum would, of course, in the natural course of things have been paid annually to myself. But I had other plans.
“After this decision to allocate 30,000 dinars had been arrived at by a unanimous vote I created a very favourable impression when I rose in my place and said that I would never occupy the privileged and, in my view, corrupt position of a citizen drawing a regular pension from my fellows. However great my services had been in the past, I was glad that they should be at the disposal of my country﻿—for so I called the place, having lived in it now two years and more. I could not bear to think that I was, as it were, sucking the very lifeblood of the community and drawing into my private coffers pence which had been contributed, for the most part, by the humblest of my dear countrymen.
“Agreement had already been shown with this announcement﻿—which came from the depth of my heart﻿—when the council was overjoyed to hear my conclusion. It was, if anything, even more sincere. ‘I will accept,’ said I, ‘*if you really insist upon it*, a sum of money down which might represent the capitalized value of the revenue, but I absolutely refuse upon any terms whatsoever to remain a mere drone supported by this active commercial community, skimming the cream off the taxes and feeling myself a burden where I should be an aid.’

===Chinese===

“全城的花园张灯结彩、连续大放光明足足三个夜晚，礼炮轰鸣不绝；鉴于这场破天荒的盛典气象，官府下旨大赦天下，释放了大批囚犯，其中便包括那位反对派党魁化名隐姓在牢房里苦熬了数月之久的胞弟。
“在这一片狂热欢腾的浪潮之中，操办后续改革的具体实务细节便轻巧得多了，盖因来自愚昧市井无知底层的那些吹毛求疵与小肚鸡肠的噪音全被一扫而空。
“大家皆大欢喜地达成了一项崭新的宪制安排，以彻底取缔往昔陈旧的过桥税款收益。往昔那笔过路税进项，常年在每年15,000至25,000第纳尔（15000，25000）之间上下浮动。为了弥补这一缺口并防范一切不可预知的意外开支，官府在地方财政公共税负中专门划拨出每年30,000第纳尔（30000）的固定专项年金，永久用于大桥的养护与运营。按常理与世间规矩，这笔每年30,000第纳尔的巨款理所当然应当逐年如数奉送给老夫本人。然而老夫心中自有乾坤。
“当全票一致通过划拨30,000第纳尔的决议之后，老夫适时离席起身发表了一番感人肺腑的陈词，博得了满堂喝彩与无上钦佩。老夫庄严表态：老夫平生绝不贪恋特权，且在老夫看来，一个坐享同胞公帑定额年金供养的公民无异于寄生硕鼠。无论老夫往昔立下了何等汗马功劳，老夫皆欣慰于将它们无私奉献给老夫的‘祖国’——在彼处客居了整整两年（2）有余之后，老夫早已将彼邦视作桑梓故土。老夫绝不忍心坐视自己成为吮吸全城命脉的寄生虫，将大半源自老夫最挚爱贫苦同胞省吃俭用缴纳的血汗铜板，源源不断揽入老夫个人的私囊金库之中！
“当老夫这番发自肺腑的宣言刚一落地，全场已是一片如雷掌声与赞叹；而当评议会诸公听闻老夫话锋一转的最终结论时，更是欣喜若狂、击节赞赏。老夫的结论甚至比前言更为真诚恳切：‘老夫愿意接受，’老夫说道，‘倘若诸公委实盛情难却、执意坚持的话，由官府一次性向老夫支付一笔买断总款，以彻底折算并清结该笔年金的资本化现值！但老夫无论如何也绝不答应在任何名目之下，沦为这片繁荣商业热土上一只白吃白喝的游手好闲之徒，一边刮取公共税收的油水脂膏，一边在原本应当救民水火之处反倒沦为同胞的沉重累赘！’”

===Original===

“Applause was almost unknown in the dignified debates of our assembly, but upon this occasion it could not be restrained; for some minutes together the grave but voluminous cheers of my colleagues assured me that I had done right and amply compensated me for any loss that I might suffer, supposing (which was, after all, improbable) the revenue from the bridge in the future would largely rise.
“Such is the frailty of human nature that perhaps the recognition of my good deed would have been less frank, or less simple, had the council themselves been compelled to find the money out of their own pockets. But there was no question of this. The burden must fall, as was only just, upon the whole body of citizens, since all used the bridge. My proposal met therefore with enthusiastic assent from every side, and one speaker in the ensuing debate (a friend who, in his humble way, was associated with other of my lesser enterprises) pointed out what I could not in decency have alluded to, that I also was a taxpayer, and a large one; so that any public payment was borne partly by myself. The sheik, in closing the discussion, after a few compliments which my natural modesty forbids me to repeat, said that clearly nothing was now left but to make a computation﻿—a mere matter of bookkeeping﻿—and that this detail might safely be left to a small committee of three, which was nominated upon the spot; their work was of course honorary, for they were men of high standing; but I saw to it that all their expenses and other disbursements should be met and I gave them much hospitality. The committee met at intervals during the ensuing three weeks. I appeared frequently before this committee in the capacity of witness, I produced all my books and had, I am glad to say, the restraint and good feeling to let things take their course and not to haggle as though this great public settlement had been a private commercial deal.

===Chinese===

“在我们评议会威严肃穆的议事大堂之上，喝彩欢呼向来被视作大不敬之举；然而在彼时彼刻，山呼海啸般的赞叹却再也按捺不住了！足足数分钟之内，同僚们庄重而浑厚的雷鸣欢呼声响彻穹顶，他们用最热烈的掌声向老夫保证老夫此举何等大公无私、英明盖世；而假定——尽管这微乎其微——未来大桥的公帑收益当真暴涨，这份荣誉亦足以彻底抚慰补偿老夫未来可能承受的任何潜在利益亏欠。
“人性本就如此脆弱自私：倘若需要评议会议员诸公自掏腰包筹措这笔巨款，他们对老夫善举的感激之情恐怕便绝无可能这般坦荡纯粹、豪爽无欺了。然而此番买断压根无需他们破费分文。正如天经地义的公理那般，这笔财政重担理应由全体城邦百姓共同分摊，盖因大桥乃是人人皆享的公器。因此，老夫的动议赢得了四面八方的狂热赞同；在随后的辩论中，一位发言者（也是老夫的一位好友，他在其微末的阶层上曾与老夫合伙经营过若干小买卖）更是当众指出了老夫出于谦逊而不便启齿的铁证：老夫本人亦是本城数一数二的纳税大户！因此，官府支出的任何公帑买断款，老夫自己本就承担了相当庞大的一部分！谢赫长官在作总结陈词时，在将老夫天生羞怯而不便复述的溢美之词泼洒了老夫一身之后，宣布接下来的事务已别无挂碍，不过是算一笔账罢了——纯属简单的记账公案——而这一细务大可稳妥委派给一个当场成立的三人（3）特别仲裁委员会；议员诸公皆是德高望重的贤达，其履职自当是分文不取的荣誉差事；然而老夫打起十二分小心，包揽了他们所有的车马差旅与杂项开支，并在老夫府邸对他们极尽地主之谊。在随后的三周（3）光阴里，该委员会定期闭门推演研讨。老夫多次以证人身份坦荡出席听证，呈验了老夫名下的全部账册；老夫十分欣慰地告诉你们：老夫始终保持着克制与崇高胸怀，听凭程序依法裁断，绝未斤斤计较讨价还价，仿佛这桩利国利民的旷世公决不过是一场市井小贩的私相授受一般。”

===Original===

It is enough to say that at the end of this proceeding the sum of 1,400,000 dinars was awarded to me by the arbitrators and that I, after protesting against what I called the excessive generosity of the state, then added to my popularity by erecting at my own charges a fine gate of entry at the city end of the bridge which absorbed half the odd 400,000; the other half I gave in a burst of generosity to the members of the committee: not of course in their public capacity but privately, as being my personal friends, and in reward for their untiring public spirit.
“I was left with a million.
“I was fully content.
“I desired no more.”
“But, uncle,” timidly interrupted the eldest of the nephews, “I am puzzled by one thing. Will you allow me to ask you a question upon it?”
“Certainly, my dear lad,” said the old man, stroking his beard and awaiting the query.
“Why, uncle,” said the boy, still hesitating somewhat, “it is this. I do not quite see how it came that you should have a million dinars. You came to this place with *half* a million, how then did it become *one* million?”
The folly of the question raised a titter from his brothers, who had always regarded their senior as the least brilliant of their clan. But their uncle was more lenient and checked their mirth (which was especially loud in the youngest), and said:
“My dear boy, do you see anything extraordinary in an accretion of fortune to a man who served the community so well?”
“No, not exactly that,” said the elder nephew, still hesitating, “far from it, dear uncle; but what I do not quite clearly see is where the other half-million came from.”
“Foolish lad!” answered his relative, now touched with annoyance. “It came from my untiring devotion to the public service, from my foresight in providing a magnificent bridge which for all those years no one had attempted; from the freely expressed desire of my fellow-citizens through their honoured representatives. It was, indeed, but a small recompense for all the good I had done and all the immeasurable advantage to this town which my energy had created.”
“Yes, dear uncle, but﻿ ﻿…” went on the blushing lad.
“Oh, don’t listen to him,” cried his brothers in chorus. “You will never make him understand! Our father has always said that he could not even do his arithmetic,” and the shrill laugh of the youngest was heard at the end of his protest.

===Chinese===

“这桩大案的收尾无需赘述：仲裁委员会最终当庭裁决，由官府一次性向老夫赔付总额高达1,400,000第纳尔（1400000）的巨额买断款！老夫在对所谓‘国库过分慷慨隆恩’表达了一番义正辞严的抗议推辞之后，为了进一步收揽民心，老夫旋即宣布：老夫自掏腰包，在大桥紧邻本城城门一侧出资营建一座气象万千的巍峨入城牌坊凯旋门！这座雄关门楼整整耗去了老夫买断款零头400,000第纳尔（400000）中的一半；至于那剩下的200,000第纳尔（200000），老夫在一阵慷慨博爱的冲动之下，悉数赠予了仲裁委员会的三位委员诸公——当然绝非馈赠给他们的公职身份，而是纯粹出于私交，作为赠予老夫至交密友的私人薄礼，以重奖他们公忠体国、夙夜匪懈的奉献精神！
“扣除上述种种，老夫手中净落下了足足100万（1000000）第纳尔。
“老夫心满意足。
“老夫别无他求。”
“可是，叔父，”七个侄儿中最年长的大侄子怯生生地插话打断道，“有一件事让侄儿百思不得其解。您老人家能容侄儿向您讨教个明白么？”
“那是自然，我的乖孩子，”老人捋了捋修长的胡须，和蔼地静候下文。
“哎呀，叔父，”大侄子依旧有些吞吞吐吐，“侄儿的意思是这样：侄儿实在瞧不明白，您手里怎的就平白多出了整整100万（1000000）第纳尔呢？您老人家当年赶抵那处地界时，手里满打满算不过带着50万（500000）第纳尔盘缠，它究竟是怎么摇身一变，变成了整整100万（1000000）第纳尔的呢？”
这句愚不可及的蠢问，顿时引得他的六个兄弟一阵嗤嗤窃笑；他们向来视长兄为整个家族里最不开窍、最愚钝的笨伯。然而他们的叔父却显得更为宽宏大量，他制止了侄儿们的哄笑（其中以最小的侄儿笑得最为刺耳），温和地说道：
“我亲爱的孩子，一个为全城百姓立下了如此不朽功勋的大善人，其家私财富有所增益，这难道有什么好大惊小怪的么？”
“不，倒不是这个意思，”大侄子依旧涨红着脸支支吾吾，“侄儿绝无此意，亲爱的叔父；但侄儿心里头就是想不明白，那凭空多出来的另外50万（500000）第纳尔，究竟是从哪里冒出来的呢？”
“糊涂蠢才！”叔父闻言脸色一沉，隐隐带了几分愠怒叱道，“那是源于老夫对公众福祉夙夜匪懈的无私奉献！那是源于老夫高瞻远瞩，凭一己之力为全城百姓营建了一座多少年来从未有人胆敢奢望的壮丽大桥！那是源于老夫的满城父老同胞通过他们受人爱戴的代表所自由表达的无上感激！与老夫所成就的无量善功、以及老夫的魄力为本城开创的无可估量的万世基业相比，这点银子不过是微不足道的一星半点补偿罢了！”
“话是这么说，亲爱的叔父，可是……”面红耳赤的少年还欲穷追不舍。
“哎呀，别理他啦！”兄弟几个异口同声地哄叫起来，“你同他说破天也是对牛弹琴！爹爹早就说过，他连三岁小儿的加减账都算不明白！”在一片起哄声中，最小的那个弟弟尖锐刻薄的讥笑声显得格外响亮。

===Original===

“Well, well,” said Mahmoud good-naturedly, “we will not quarrel about it.”
At that moment the intolerable shriek of the muezzin calling the faithful to prayer was heard from the neighbouring minaret and the somewhat strained situation was relieved.

===Chinese===

“好啦，好啦，”马哈茂德和颜悦色地打起了圆场，“咱们不必为此争执不下。”
就在彼时彼刻，邻近宣礼塔上传来了令人难以忍受的凄厉呼拜声，唤醒正信的信徒前去晚祷；这略显尴尬紧绷的场面顿时得以化解。

## XII *Al-Mahallat al-Jadida*, or “The New Quarter of the City” / 第十二章 新城
===Original===

“As I proceeded the next morning across the waste” (continued Mahmoud to his nephews on their next visit) “I turned over in my mind how best to employ the considerable sum which my honest mule bore so patiently upon his back. Here was a year’s sustenance for fifty labourers or more, and with so much money a man earns more. For, as it is written in the Holy Book, ‘The labourer is worthy of his hire, but whatever is over is not for him.’ And again, ‘Blessed are the poor.’
“There are not wanting occasions for the employment of poorer men and the gathering of the fruits of their labour into one’s own pouch. But there is one difficulty in all such matters, which is the point of judgment.
“You will recollect (my dear nephews) how often in the past a most careful investment of mine had gone wrong, and how often a mere hazard, not even of my own choosing, had brought me sudden fortune. How in the matter of the sheep I was beaten almost to death by the market-police, while in the matter of the horse I had sold, three times over, an animal that had cost me nothing and had fallen to me by the pure goodness of God. How in the matter of the dates I had been left penniless by a man of greater business sense, foresight, organizing ability, eye for the market, knowledge of men, and the rest of the virtues; while in the matter of the Holy Man I had﻿—as my weary mule proved﻿—done extremely well out of a quite unexpected accident. Even as this last example passed through my mind I remembered, for the first time, that the mule himself was a new accession of fortune, over and above the silver he carried. For I had not had the inconvenience of paying for him. He was a fine beast, and in spite of his fatigue still looked well bred. I estimated him at quite ten pieces of gold, and mentally added that sum on my tablets to the total value of my possessions.
“It was so musing that I found myself approaching a high tangle of reeds, through which a narrow path wound and was lost to view. On this path I engaged. The reeds on either side were so tall as to hide the farther landscape, and so closely set that one could see but a few yards into their mass.
“After perhaps an hour of such journeying my mule and I came out suddenly upon the firm bank of a broad and shallow river whose noise and coolness, swift current and clear stream were a delight after so many hours of arid travel.

===Chinese===

“翌日清晨，当老夫骑着骡子横穿荒原之时，”在侄儿们下一次前来请安时，马哈茂德（Mahmoud）接着讲道，“老夫心中不住地盘算着，该如何最妥当地运用我那头诚实老实的骡子在背上耐心驮着的这笔可观巨资。这笔钱足以充作50名（50）乃至更多苦力一整年的嚼谷，而一个精明人手握如此一笔巨款，理应赚取更多的钱财。盖因圣书有云：‘工人得工钱是应当的，但凡有富余皆不得归其所有。’又云：‘安贫乐道者福矣。’
“在人世间，雇佣贫寒之人并将他们的劳动果实尽数揽入自个儿腰包的契机向来是不缺的。然而在一切此类营生之中皆有一大难关，那便是精准的审时度势。
“我亲爱的侄儿们，想必你们还记得，老夫在往昔岁月里精心谋划的投资多少次曾化为泡影，而多少次纯属偶然、甚至非老夫自愿的飞来横祸，反倒为老夫带来了骤然横财。你们想必记得：当年在绵羊的营生上，老夫险些被行市衙役活活打死；而在贩马的生意里，老夫却将一匹老夫分文未掏、纯凭真主仁慈白白得来的牲口转手倒卖了足足三次。在贩卖椰枣的公案里，老夫被一个商业嗅觉更灵、远见更卓绝、组织力更强、行市眼光更毒辣、更洞察人性的能人算计得身无分文；而在圣人的买卖上，老夫却——正如老夫胯下疲惫的骡子所证明的那般——靠着一场纯粹始料未及的横祸大发横财。甚至当这桩先例在脑海中闪过时，老夫平生第一次恍然记起：除了骡背上驮着的白银之外，这头骡子本身亦是老夫财富的一桩崭新进项！因老夫分文未掏白捡了它。这是一头不可多得的健硕脚力，尽管长途跋涉有些疲惫，却依然显得神骏不凡。老夫在心里为它估价足有10枚金币（10），并暗自将这笔款子添补在老夫默记总资产的泥板账目之上。
“正当老夫这般凝神沉思之际，老夫发现自己正走近一片高耸密集的芦苇荡，一条狭窄的羊肠小道蜿蜒伸入其间、隐没不见。老夫便踏上了这条小道。小路两侧的芦苇高耸入云，将远处的旷野遮挡得严严实实，密密匝匝得教人向两侧望去不过数码远近。
“在如此跋涉了约莫一个时辰之后，老夫同老夫的骡子冷不丁穿出苇丛，踏上了一条宽阔而水浅的大河的坚实河岸；在荒漠中饱受酷日暴晒了这么多钟头之后，那潺潺水声、阵阵清凉、湍急的奔流与澄澈的河水，委实是世间无上的享受与慰藉。”

===Original===

There did I sit me down. There did I unload and hobble my patient companion. There did we drink of the good water, and there did the mule eat plentifully of cool grass. But I made no meal, for the oatcakes and cheese which I had taken from the common table of the poor in the Pilgrims’ camp were now exhausted.
“It was this circumstance which made me a little anxious for the day. I looked about me, stood up on the highest part of the bank, and then saw that the shore opposite had been artificially heightened to form a regular levee or embankment, beyond which the flat country was hidden. I determined to seek this point of advantage for a view. I reloaded my mule, carefully forded the stream in its various branches and climbed to the farther shore.
“At the summit of the embankment, which was evidently of recent construction, my effort was well rewarded, for I saw a sight that set at rest all anxiety for food and shelter.
“The embankment on which I stood swept round in a horseshoe shape﻿—not everywhere finished, but everywhere traced out. It thus enclosed a peninsula, bounded by the river: a space of marshy ground full of stagnant pools and water-grasses. Three or four furlongs away, cutting across the whole neck of the horseshoe of swamp and stretching from the river to the river again, ran the well-built stone wall of a strong town, the flat roofs and low domes of which (it had no tower or minaret) made a ridge of snow-white against the intense blue of the sky. Far away beyond were distant mountains, purple in the heat.
“Scattered over the swamp itself and on the unfinished sections of the embankment were groups of labourers working with spades and barrows, and, overseeing them, nearby to me, a young man of energetic carriage, well dressed in a brown garment, rich, but suitable to his work and girded. He had no sandals, for they would have impeded him on such ground; he bore an inlaid ebon staff with an ivory hand-grasp, and was occupied, when I first saw him, in shouting a new order to a distant group of diggers. His back was towards me. He had not seen my approach.
“As he turned, to proceed along the levee towards another group, he caught sight of my mule and myself and at once started to run, pouring out when he had reached us a mixed flood of greetings, warnings, and varied exclamations.
“ ‘We were to beware of the embankment!

===Chinese===

“老夫在那河岸边坐了下来。老夫卸下了行囊，绑住了老夫那任劳任怨脚力的蹄子。老夫同它饱饮了甘甜的泉水，骡子在水边大口吞嚼着清凉的嫩草。然而老夫自个儿却水米未进，因老夫从香客营地穷人公席上顺手捎带的几块燕麦饼和干酪此刻已然告罄。
“正是这一窘境令老夫对这一天的生计略感忧虑。老夫环顾四下，攀上河岸最高的一处土丘，旋即望见对岸已被人工筑高，筑成了一道齐整的大堤或防洪坝，防洪坝背后平展的原野被遮挡得严严实实。老夫决定登上那处制高点以极目远眺。老夫重新给骡子备好驮子，小心翼翼地涉水穿过大河错综分叉的支流，攀上了对岸的水坝顶端。
“在那道显然乃是近期修筑的大坝顶峰，老夫的辛劳得到了极为丰厚的回报；老夫眼前展现出一幅壮景，令老夫对饮食与落脚客栈的全部忧虑顿时烟消云散。
“老夫立足的大坝呈一道巨大的马蹄铁形状蜿蜒盘旋——虽说并非处处合龙告竣，但全线地基皆已勾勒分明。大坝如此合围，便将大河环抱的一片半岛圈禁其间：那是一片遍布死水洼与水生杂草的泥泞沼泽地。在3、4弗隆之外，整齐划一横切过马蹄形沼泽地颈部、从大河一端横贯至另一端的，乃是一座壁垒森严的重镇用坚固条石垒砌的巍峨城墙；城中那些平顶房舍与低矮圆顶（全城未见半座塔楼或宣礼塔），在湛蓝如洗的天幕映衬下，勾勒出一道白雪般的起伏屋脊。极目远方，耸立着在酷热暮霭中泛着紫意的巍峨群山。
“散布在沼泽地腹地以及大坝尚未完工的各个工段之上的，是一群群手执铁锹、推着独轮小车挥汗如雨的苦力；而在靠近老夫身侧不远处督工的，乃是一位举止干练利落的年轻后生，身着一袭质地上乘却极合乎工地下作的棕色紧身袍服，腰系布带。他光着双脚未着凉鞋，免得在如此泥泞的烂泥地里泥足深陷；他手中攥着一根镶嵌黑檀木、配有象牙手柄的督工手杖；老夫初见他时，他正扯着嗓子冲着远处一群挖掘苦力高声传达崭新的号令。他背对着老夫，全然未察觉到老夫的临近。
“当他转过身来、准备沿着大堤迈向另一群苦力时，他一眼瞥见了老夫与老夫的骡子，旋即飞奔而来；待他奔至跟前，嘴里连珠炮似地涌出一大串问候、告诫与七嘴八舌的惊呼。
“‘千万小心这道大坝！’”

===Original===

It was but just raised and was not yet stable! Was it not a fine work? Half-completed, as we saw it, it had taken but three months. Did I notice how thoroughly it had cut off the river? Had I not found it firm as I came up it from the bank? Would I be pleased to go carefully lest the edge should be injured?﻿ ﻿… and so on.
“It was clear from such a salutation with what sort of man I had to deal. Here was an enthusiast. It is a character of the utmost service to the man of affairs. He was of that sort which is labelled in our indexes as ‘The constructive wild man,’ and happy is the captain of industry who chances upon such a one. He was perhaps thirty years of age, strong, short in stature, very dark in complexion, with steady but eager eyes, and such an expression of grasp, resolution, and immediate decision as should lead to a fortune, not perhaps for himself but at least for anyone who knew how to use him; for he was as keen as a boy upon the matter occupying him, and therefore careless for the moment of all other things.
“I answered him with a mixture of sympathy, caution, and gravity, which I was glad to see impressed him. I praised his work vaguely but courteously enough. I asked the names of the river and the town, and also his own. All these he gave me; and then asked me whether I would not share the midday meal he was about to take. I said I should be overjoyed to do so: and so I was, for I saw the prospect of refreshment at the charges of another, an opportunity, which remember﻿—my dear nephews﻿—is never to be neglected by men of clear commercial judgment.
“He led me, followed by the mule, to a shady place where a few trees stood on a drier part of the enclosed plain. There we found excellent meats, and there we reclined for above an hour, during the whole of which he did not cease to overwhelm me with description, praise, and prospect of the great enterprise in which he was wholly absorbed.
“This horseshoe bend, he told me, outside his native town, had never been utilized. It was flooded in the spring when the snow melted on the distant hills; the rest of the year it was a mixture of dry cracked mud and marsh, breeding fever, full of insects and ill-airs at evening. He had been left an orphan and was apprenticed to a maker of mill-wheels, such as were used in the stream above the city.

===Chinese===

“‘这堤坝才刚刚填土筑起，地基尚未夯实！这难道不是一项惊天地泣鬼神的伟业么？正如您老所见，工程才干了一半，却仅仅耗时3个月！您瞧见它如何干净利落地将滔滔大河拦腰阻断了吗？您老方才从河滩爬上来时，难道没觉察到脚下这土质有多坚硬？还请您老步履轻抬，仔细莫要踩坏了坝肩斜坡！’……诸如此类，滔滔不绝。
“单凭这一通寒暄问候，老夫便已对自个儿面对的究竟是何方神圣心知肚明了。老夫面前矗立着的，乃是一个纯粹的理想狂热之徒。在精明的生意人眼里，此类角色委实是这世间不可多得的无上利器！在我们的商贾名册索引里，此类人物一律被贴上‘建设型狂人’的醒目标签；商界巨擘若是有幸在半道上撞见这么一位尤物，真可谓祖上积德。他年纪约莫30岁上下（30），身板结实粗壮，个头不高，面庞晒得黝黑，眼神坚定而炽烈，眉宇间流露出一股雷厉风行、杀伐决断的决绝气概——这等气质本该为他挣下泼天家财，哪怕并非造福他自个儿，亦足够让任何深谙驱使驾驭之道的高人发大财；盖因他对自己所专注的营生正如孩童般痴迷狂热，因而对身遭的其他一切人情世故皆浑然不顾。
“老夫以一种兼具同情、审慎与威严的口吻应对答复，老夫欣慰地看出这一姿态深深震慑住了他。老夫轻描淡写却极尽得体地赞颂了他的浩大工程。老夫探问了大河与城邦的名号，顺带打听了他的尊姓大名。他对老夫全盘托出，随后便热情洋溢地相邀老夫共享他正欲享用的午间便饭。老夫表示能获此款待受宠若惊：事实亦确乎如此，因老夫瞧见了由旁人自掏腰包管饭的实惠，我亲爱的侄儿们，切记这一条铁律：具备清醒商业嗅觉之人，平生绝不可放过任何白吃白喝的良机！
“他在前引路，老夫与骡子紧随其后，步入了一处位于平原高燥地带的幽僻树林。在那里我们大快朵颐了极其丰美的肉食冷餐；我们并肩席地而坐足足歇息了逾一个时辰，在此期间，他一刻未歇地向老夫灌输着关于他全身心沉醉其中的宏伟蓝图的详尽描摹、无限颂扬与壮丽前景。
“他向老夫倾诉：这片坐落在他桑梓故土城外的马蹄形大弯地界，千百年来从未被人开垦利用过。每逢春季冰雪消融，山洪暴发，此地便沦为一片汪洋泽国；在一年的其余岁月里，这里不过是一片干裂硬结的烂泥滩与恶臭沼泽，滋生瘟疫热病，每到傍晚蚊蝇肆虐、毒瘴弥漫。他少时父母双亡，沦为孤儿，被城郊河畔的磨坊水车作坊收为学徒。”

===Original===

One day the idea had occurred to him that such instruments could be used not only for the grinding of corn but for the raising of water from ditches and therefore for draining.
“With that his grand project had rushed into his mind. Why should not the marsh, which had so far been so serious a trouble, be turned to the profit of the city? An embankment would keep out floods, drains cut through the enclosed marsh would collect the water and dry the whole. These drains could be regularly pumped out by wheels which the outer stream would turn, and a large area of good land would be added to the crowded town. On this new buildings could be raised and gardens laid out, to the great profit of all the citizens. For the city was increasing in importance, people were flocking in, there was crowding and difficulty and high rents, yet no place over which to expand between the marsh and the hills.
“He had approached the council and headmen with this project. They had hesitated long. At last they had grudgingly advanced from the taxes a sum which he warned them was inadequate. Nevertheless he had set to work, and the results were now before me. The swamp was still swamp, the embankment not completed, of the drains not more than a sixth were dug, and the whole was a confusion of mud-heaps, apparent ruin and chaos, very unattractive to the eye and very unpromising, in its outward aspect at least, of any result. It had all the character of waste and folly﻿—yet a sum of money, small in comparison with many private resources and insignificant in the budget of the town, would suffice to crown the whole and to replace the wretched prospect of unfinished labour by a noble plain of rich gardens and new houses. But the headmen of the city were now disgusted and would vote no more. Rather did they threaten him with penalties for his loss of public pence.
“I had, during this torrent of talk, interjected here and there a question and no more. I had spoken guardedly and yet with no disrespect for his enthusiasm. I had, as we say among the merchants, ‘sounded him.’ I asked him in conclusion what sum he thought necessary for the completion of his enterprise. He named 300 gold pieces, about one-quarter of what lay concealed in my sacks upon the ground, which sacks (I had casually informed him) were filled with coarse grain from the hills.

===Chinese===

“忽有一日，他灵光乍现：此类水车器具不仅能用于磨坊碾麦磨粉，亦能用于从沟渠中汲水排涝，因而堪充排灌排干沼泽的大器！
“顺着这一念头，他脑海中的惊天宏图如决堤之水般奔涌而出：为何不把这片长久以来令全城头疼不已的烂泥塘，彻底扭转为造福城邦的聚宝盆？只需筑起一道巍峨长堤便能抵御外河洪峰泛滥；在围闭的沼泽腹地纵横开凿排水沟渠，汇聚积水，便能将全境彻底排干！这些沟渠中的积水，可由外河奔流驱动的巨型水轮昼夜不停抽排入河，如此一来，便能为拥挤狭窄的城邦平白增添一大片肥沃平畴！在这片崭新的沃土之上，既能平地起高楼，又能开辟连绵花园农庄，令全城百姓皆大获其利！盖因本城的地位正急剧攀升，四方流民商旅蜂拥而至，城中人满为患、房租地价高得令人咂舌，偏偏在恶臭沼泽与崇山峻岭之间，全城再无半分立锥之地可供向外拓张。
“他曾怀揣这项伟大宏图面见城邦元老评议会与各坊里正长老。长老们犹豫迟疑了许久，最终才极为勉强刻薄地从公帑税收中拨付了一笔碎银子——他早已当面告诫过他们这笔银子杯水车薪。尽管如此，他依然毅然决然破土动工，其成果如今便历历在目展现在老夫眼前：沼泽终究还是烂泥沼泽，长堤尚未全线合龙，排灌水渠开凿出来的尚不足六分之一，放眼望去通体是一片狼藉泥丘、断壁残垣般的破败与混沌，既污浊不堪，单从其外观卖相而言，更是瞧不出半点能够成功的指望。整座工程通体散发着挥霍公帑与异想天开的败局气息——然而，只需一笔相比于许多富商私产微不足道、在全城财政预算中更是沧海一粟的真金白银，便足以力挽狂澜加冕全盘大业，用一片坐落着富丽庭院与崭新华厦的壮丽平原，去替换眼前这片半途而废的凄凉泥潭！然而本城的头面人物如今早已大倒胃口，断然拒绝再拨付半文铜板；反倒扬言要以挥霍公帑之罪严惩于他。
“在他那滔滔不绝的倾诉狂潮中，老夫仅时不时轻描淡写插上一两句问话，绝不多嘴。老夫言辞极其审慎，却又丝毫不失对他热忱的敬意。老夫正在对他进行我们商海中所谓的‘探底盘问’。最后，老夫问他究竟需要多少银子才能彻底打通全盘大业。他报出了一个数字：300枚金币（300）——约莫仅占老夫泥地麻袋里私藏家底的四分之一（老夫曾轻描淡写地向他吹嘘，说那些麻袋里装的全是从山里运来的粗粮粗豆）。”

===Original===

“Upon hearing this sum given, a sum so well within my means, an interior light broke upon me. I did not pray for guidance, as is my custom in any business dealing of doubt. I was directly and immediately inspired. To this I owe the whole of my present position; for it was the foundation of all that followed. I had suffered vicissitude. It is the lot of man. But henceforward my soul was to be filled with increasing and ever-increasing wealth until I should be able to call myself, as I do now, by far the richest man in all the caliphate and perhaps in the world. This, my dear nephews, was the turning-point!”
The old man’s eyes were full of tears, his voice trembled; never had the awestruck boys imagined that their uncle, in his greatness and serenity, could be so moved.
“Oh, my children!” he continued in broken accents, “never forget in your own lives this master precept; that of all those whom Allah presents to us for exploitation, none, none is so lucrative as the creative enthusiast; the man who can make and produce and yet be managed! the genius devoid of guile! You may know him, that rare jewel, by his eyes.”
The old man recovered himself with dignity, wiped his eyes on a piece of priceless embroidered silk from Samarkand, threw it out of the window, and, in his more usual tone, pursued the recital of his fortunes.
“The young man never dreamt that such a chance and dusty traveller as I, with my one mule, could help him. He had merely burst out with his story to me as he would have done to any human being that would hear him. I had the more advantage of him from his ignorance of my real wealth.
“I told him soberly at the end of his tale that it interested me greatly, that his idea was evidently sound, but that the stupidity, ignorance, and suspicion of town councillors were common not only to his city, but to all others﻿—a thing which I, who had travelled widely, could judge. I assured him, out of a vast experience (which he accepted with the utmost simplicity), that he could hope for no more from such a source. I then fell into a sort of bemused condition, as though I were ruminating what could next be done.
“The young man, his hopes now turned into a new channel, and, after so brief an acquaintance (for such is the nature of these enthusiasts) already beginning to look to me for aid, watched my face most anxiously. I continued silent.

===Chinese===

“一听闻这笔数目——一笔完全在老夫财力承受范围之内的数目——老夫心头骤然灵光大盛！老夫甚至无需像往常涉足存疑买卖那般向真主祈求启示指引；老夫当场便被神明之光彻底照亮了！老夫今日之所以能坐拥泼天富贵，全盘归功于那一瞬间的顿悟；盖因那正是奠定老夫后续一切霸业的无上基石！老夫在往昔虽历经沧桑沉浮——这本是凡夫俗子的寻常命数；然而自那一刻起，老夫的灵魂便注定要被滚滚翻腾、永无止境的泼天财富彻底充盈，直至老夫能像今日这般昂首自封：老夫乃是全哈里发帝国、乃至全天下首屈一指的不世巨富！我亲爱的侄儿们，这正是老夫命运的惊天转折点啊！”
说到此处，老人的眼中泛起了热泪，嗓音激动得微微发颤；这群吓得屏气敛息的孩子们何曾想象过，他们那位平日里威严肃穆、波澜不惊的叔父，竟会动情至此！
“啊，我的孩子们！”他用哽咽而激荡的语调继续叮嘱道，“在你们自个儿的一生中，务必将这条至高无上的黄金铁律刻骨铭心铭记在心：在真主恩赐给我们用以生财剥削的芸芸众生之中，没有任何一种人，绝对没有任何一种人，能比一个富有开创精神的狂热信徒更为值钱、更能生财！他既能开天辟地创造财富，又极其容易被肆意摆弄操控！一个毫无城府机心的绝世天才！想要认出这等举世罕见的无价瑰宝，只需端详他的双眸便知。”
老人威严地平复了心绪，抽出一块出自撒马尔罕、绣满精绝纹饰的无价丝帕擦拭了眼角，顺手将丝帕扔出窗外，随即恢复了往常平静沉稳的语调，继续讲述他的发家传奇。
“那个年轻后生做梦也未曾料到，像老夫这般风尘仆仆、孤身牵着一头骡子的落魄过客，竟能助他一臂之力。他不过是一时宣泄，正如他撞见任何一个乐意驻足倾听的凡夫俗子那般向老夫倒苦水罢了。而他对其真正实力的浑然不知，恰恰给了老夫无与伦比的先手胜算。
“待他倾诉完毕，老夫极其沉稳庄重地告知他：老夫对他的宏图大略极感兴趣，他的构想显然无懈可击；然而城邦市政长老们的愚蠢、无知与猜忌，非但不仅限于本城，更是全天下所有官府衙门的通病——这一点，对于老夫这般阅历广博、踏遍万水千山之人而言，早已司空见惯。老夫以过来人的渊博阅历向他信誓旦旦地担保（而他以极尽天真的赤子之心全盘笑纳了）：指望从那帮官老爷手里再抠出半文钱纯属痴人说梦。言毕，老夫故意陷入了一种若有所思的深沉凝滞，仿佛正替他苦思冥想下一步该如何力挽狂澜。
“这位年轻后生如今心头的希望被引向了一条崭新的航道；且仅仅萍水相逢片刻工夫（狂热信徒骨子里皆有这等毛病），他便已然不由自主地将求助的目光投向了老夫，万分焦灼地端详着老夫的脸色。老夫依旧保持着高深莫测的沉默。”

===Original===

“At last he could bear it no longer. He asked me impetuously what I should advise﻿—where could he turn? What could be done? It would be a tragedy﻿—a murder﻿—for his great scheme to fail merely because its obvious advantage could not be put before anyone who had the provision of 300 gold pieces necessary for paying the labourers till the plan was achieved. He sprang to his feet. He walked feverishly up and down. He betrayed all the symptoms of his case.
“I answered him with great deliberation and firmness. I said, first of all, that I had nothing. It was a pity, for I thoroughly understood his idea. I admired it. I believed in it. Indeed, it was obviously sound. If I had had the wherewithal (said I) I would at once have made the advance. If I had had even a portion I would have put that portion at his disposal if only to show my sincere appreciation of his genius. For it was the neglect of men like himself (I continued) that hindered the progress of the world. But so it was! I had but my trade as an itinerant merchant in grain to support myself and a very large family which I had left at home in the hills. I had nothing laid by.﻿ ﻿… However, my annual tour through this and neighbouring provinces brought me into contact (these were my very words) with many notables possessed of ample reserves. In this very town﻿—now that he had told me its name﻿—I remembered two or three correspondents with whom I had done business in the past, though I had never seen them. These I would approach. And if he would give me an appointment that evening after sunset I would tell him if I had been able to effect anything.
“He overwhelmed me with thanks, led me to an excellent house of call in the town, and, leaving me there with an appointed hour for our meeting in the evening, he returned to his labours with a lighter heart.
“I, for my part, retired to an inner room which I had hired, there disposed of my baggage (having seen to the stabling and feeding of my honest mule), and instantly fell upon my knees to thank Allah with all the fervour I could muster for His abounding grace. Indeed, my heart overflowed with gratitude when I considered the quite exceptional opportunity. I felt about this young man as does a caravan, when, after a weary march through the desert, there gleams a pure lake not half an hour away.

===Chinese===

“终于，他再也按捺不住了。他急不可耐地向老夫讨教：老夫究竟有何高见？他该向何处求援？究竟该如何力挽狂澜？倘若仅仅因为没人能预先筹措出300枚金币（300）用以支付苦力工钱直至全盘告竣，就眼睁睁看着这项利国利民的伟大宏图胎死腹中，这简直就是一场人世间的旷世惨剧、一场谋杀啊！他霍然弹身而起，心急如焚地在草地上来回踱步，将其病入膏肓的狂热病状暴露无遗。
“老夫以极尽沉着与坚定的口吻作答。老夫首先坦言：老夫自个儿手里分文皆无。这委实令人扼腕叹息，因老夫对他的构想心领神会、钦佩备至、深信不疑；平心而论，这项蓝图显然切实可行。老夫甚至说道：倘若老夫手头宽裕，老夫必定当场倾囊相助！倘若老夫哪怕有那么一丁点闲钱，老夫亦甘愿将那点微薄碎银全盘奉上任由他支配，哪怕纯粹为了聊表老夫对人不世出天才的由衷赞赏！老夫继续感叹道：正是由于世俗平庸对如他这般俊杰的冷落与扼杀，才导致整个大千世界的文明跃进止步不前！然而造化弄人啊！老夫不过是个走村串寨的行脚粮商，全凭这点微薄买卖养家糊口，家中高堂弱小一大帮子全指望老夫寄钱度日，老夫箱底委实没有半文积蓄！……然而话又说回来，老夫常年在彼省及四邻各省巡回穿梭，因而同许多坐拥万贯家财的头面巨贾颇有私交（这正是老夫的原话）。恰好在本城——如今老夫既已知晓了本城名号——老夫记起曾同城里的两三位往来商行掌柜有过生意往来，尽管老夫同他们素未谋面。老夫愿意亲自登门替他游说探口风。倘若他乐意在今晚日落之后同老夫定下一处碰头之所，老夫届时便能如实相告老夫究竟能否替他运筹出半分眉目。
“他闻言对我千恩万谢、感恩戴德，当即将老夫领入城中一家极体面舒适的官马驿栈；与老夫敲定了今晚会面的时辰之后，他怀揣着一颗轻快了许多的心，重新折返回泥潭继续他的苦工。
“至于老夫自个儿，老夫径直退入老夫包下的一间内室，在妥善安顿了行李行囊并打发小厮给老夫那头忠诚的骡子备好草料饮水之后，老夫当即扑通跪倒在地，使出浑身解数以最炽烈的情感向真主叩谢无量鸿恩！当老夫审视眼前这一破天荒的横财良机时，老夫心头委实被无尽的感激所充盈。老夫看待这个年轻后生，宛如一支在荒漠中跋涉得口干舌燥的驼队，冷不丁望见半个时辰脚程外正波光粼粼闪烁着一泓甘冽清泉一般。”

===Original===

How short a space of time now lay between this moment and the beginnings of splendid negotiations!
“Having so prayed with the deepest sincerity and humility, I first took out exactly *two* hundred pieces of gold from my sacks, tied them into a kerchief about my person, and then lay down upon a mat to sleep, first warning the servants of the place to wake me when the young man should return. I slept soundly for many hours.
“When they woke me it was already dark. I rose at once, lit the lamp, and received my young friend into the room. All was silent. We were alone under the one light of that subdued flame. The hour was propitious for what I had undertaken.
“I told him that I had spent the interval, since he left me, in seeking my wealthy correspondents and in making myself acquainted with their views upon the town’s circumstances and upon the opportunities for investment. I said that I had briefly and very cautiously mentioned the works I saw going on in the swamp as I had approached their city, and that I had discovered at once, by their pitying contempt for his enterprise, that there was no chance at all of interesting them in its progress. They called it﻿—as did all their fellow-citizens﻿—a folly. They bitterly regretted the public money that had already been advanced. They would certainly advance no more. They talked freely of bringing him to trial for wasting the public revenue. As for any private investment of their own fortunes, I clearly saw that it was out of the question.
“Here I paused to let this information sink in, and was pleased to observe the growing dejection of his features. But before he could voice his despair﻿—though he had expected as much﻿—I relieved him by another strain. I told him I had raised a certain sum. Partly upon the security of my known stores of grain (of which I was carrying samples﻿—pointing to the bags on the floor that contained my money), partly on my own personal security as an honest merchant, poor but of regular and punctual habit in payment, I had secured a loan which I had told them was for the general purpose of increasing my business, but which in point of fact I intended to put at his disposal﻿—such confidence had I in his scheme. I feared it would be insufficient, but it would be a beginning﻿—later we might find further means.
“His honest, eager face changed as I spoke.

===Chinese===

“在这动人的一刻与即将拉开帷幕的辉煌博弈之间，不过仅隔着短短数个时辰的光阴啊！
“老夫怀着最深沉的虔诚与谦卑献上祷告之后，老夫首先从随身麻袋里精准数出了200枚金币（200），用一块贴身头巾结结实实裹好缠在老夫腰间；随后老夫便往凉席上一躺沉沉睡去，临睡前特意吩咐驿栈的小厮待那年轻后生折返时务必将老夫唤醒。老夫酣然高卧了数个时辰。
“当小厮将老夫唤醒时，窗外天色已然全黑。老夫翻身下榻，掌起油灯，将老夫那位年轻的挚友迎入屋内。四下万籁俱寂，在那盏幽微油灯的一豆灯火之下，唯有我二人相对而坐。对于老夫谋划的大计而言，此情此时委实再吉庆不过了。
“老夫告知他：自从他离去之后，老夫将这大半日光阴全用在四处拜访老夫那些腰缠万贯的旧交掌柜身上，向他们打探探询本城的经济虚实与投资商机。老夫推说老夫曾极其简短审慎地提及了老夫进城时在城外沼泽瞧见的排涝工程；然而老夫当场便从他们对这项营生的怜悯与鄙夷之中洞察到：想要诱使他们对这项工程投资哪怕一文钱，绝无半分指望。他们同城里的寻常市井百姓一般无二，皆将其斥为疯子的胡闹。他们对官府先前预支的公帑痛心疾首，咬定绝不可能再追加半文垫资；甚至纷纷扬言要将他绳之以法、以挥霍官帑罪论处。至于要他们自掏腰包搞私人入股投资，老夫看得一清二楚，那完全是痴心妄想。
“说到此处，老夫故意顿住话头，好让这番残酷的实情在他心头彻底发酵沉淀；老夫欣慰地瞥见他的眉宇间肉眼可见地笼罩上一层深深的绝望。然而不等他开口哀叹——尽管他原本便预料到会是这般结局——老夫话锋骤然一转，瞬间将他从深渊中拉了上来。老夫告知他：老夫自个儿设法筹措到了一笔银子。一部分是凭借老夫在深山存有大批存粮的信誉担保（老夫指了指地上装满真金白银的麻袋，声称那是老夫随身携带的粮样），另一部分则是凭借老夫身为老实本分、虽说清贫但向来按期结账的本分商人的身家性命担保；老夫硬生生争取到了一笔商业借贷——老夫曾对债主宣称此款乃用于老夫扩大粮贸周转，但实则老夫打算全盘交由他支配调遣——老夫对他的宏图大略就是这般深信不疑！老夫唯恐这笔钱尚不足以大功告成，但好歹能解燃眉之急作为破局之用；待日后大局稍定，我们或许能寻得其他后着。
“听闻老夫这番肺腑之言，他那张诚挚炽烈的面庞瞬间由阴转晴。”

===Original===

It was delightful to feel that I could give so much joy, however brief, to so candid a soul. He had, however, a certain scruple. He said it was not his business, but he would rather there had been no ambiguity, and the money advanced for a purpose known to the lenders.
“I praised the nobility of such hesitation, but I pointed out that the risk was mine: that I had only spoken of ‘a general purpose of increasing my business,’ no false phrase; that I was so certain of *our* success that the loan was at any rate secure. In any case, business (of which he had little acquaintance) was always conducted on such lines (I assured him) and my backers being also business men would be the last to split hairs on points of honour when I paid back their loan. This soothed him and he was now quite prepared for what followed.
“I asked him again what sum he required? He told me he employed a hundred labourers, that their wages came to twenty gold pieces a week, and that he estimated fifteen weeks as the very least period in which the whole ground could be cleared and dried and set out. In all, he repeated, *three* hundred pieces of gold would be required: as he had said. I noted to myself privately once more that it was one-quarter of my hoard, and then, when he had completed his calculation, I thus addressed him:
“ ‘It is as I feared! The sum I have obtained is hardly sufficient. I have raised but *two* hundred pieces of gold!’ His face fell again. ‘But that will take us pretty far,’ I went on. ‘We may with care *nearly* complete our work, and the rest of it should be easy to find.’
“It has always surprised me how exact such men are in judgment and yet how little they use their talent to their own advantage. He was anxious. He was certain that beginning on too small a sum was dangerous. But I persuaded him; for no more (said I) could possibly be obtained.
“What made all smooth was my proposal for articles between us. These generously proposed that, though I had found the money, yet of the resultant profit we should take equal shares. ‘I propose that your own salary during the work,’ said I, ‘should be small; indeed, no more than your bare maintenance. For we have no margin, nay, less than we need. But if you are not agreeable, pray name your terms.’ He could find no words for my generosity! Of course he would live on the least possible sum and work to an extreme!

===Chinese===

“能给这样一个纯真无邪的灵魂带去如此巨大的狂喜——哪怕这狂喜注定转瞬即逝——委实是一件令人心旷神怡的美事。然而他心头终究掠过了一丝道德疑虑。他坦言这本非他该多管的闲事，但他宁愿账目清清白白，这笔银子最好能向出借人挑明其真正用途。
“老夫对这等崇高的顾虑大加赞赏，但老夫条分缕析向他指出：所有商业风险皆由老夫一人独揽；老夫对外声称的‘用于老夫扩大买卖的常规周转’绝非虚妄之词；况且老夫对‘我们’的功成名就坚信不疑，借款本息归还自然固若金汤。再者说来，商海之道（而他对此几乎一窍不通）向来皆是依循此类惯例运作（老夫信誓旦旦向他保证），而老夫的债主同为商界老手，只要老夫如期连本带利奉还欠款，他们绝不可能在道义细枝末节上吹毛求疵。这番开导彻底抚平了他内心的波澜，他如今已然完全做好了迎接下文的准备。
“老夫再次探问他究竟短少多少银子？他告知老夫：他麾下雇佣着100名劳工（100），每周的工钱开销高达20枚金币（20），他预估至少需要苦干整整15周（15），方能将整片荒泽彻底清理、排干并划线分块。正如他先前所言，总共需要足足300枚金币（300）。老夫在心底再次暗自盘算：这恰好是老夫私囊储蓄的四分之一；待他算清了细账，老夫便语重心长地对他说道：
“‘正如老夫所料啊！老夫好不容易周转来的款子终究略有短缺。老夫满打满算只筹措到了200枚金币（200）！’他的脸色顿时又垮了下来。‘然而这笔钱已然足够支撑我们走得很远很远了，’老夫话锋一转鼓励他，‘只要我们精打细算、厉行节约，工程几乎就能彻底大工告竣，至于剩下的收尾款项，届时筹措起来必定易如反掌！’
“此类狂热信徒在专业技艺上何其精准睿智，却在谋求自身利益上何其一窍不通、任人宰割，这一悖论委实令老夫终生惊叹。他隐隐透着忧虑，深知开局本钱短缺极其凶险。然而在老夫的三寸不烂之舌面前他毫无招架之力；盖因老夫咬定：天上地下绝不可能再多筹措出半文钱了！
“而彻底打消他一切疑虑的，乃是老夫顺势抛出的一份合伙章程契约。老夫极其慷慨地提议：尽管全盘本金皆由老夫一人筹措，但日后大业告竣所产生的全部红利，老夫与他各占一半！‘老夫提议在工程营建期间你个人的薪俸，’老夫说道，‘不妨先定得微薄些；实则只需维持你个人的粗茶淡饭即可。因我们手头没有半分享乐的余量，甚至比实际所需还要紧巴。但倘若你觉得不妥，大可由你自个儿开出价码。’面对老夫如此‘宽宏大量’，他感动得语无伦次！他当场赌咒发誓：他自愿以世间最低廉的开销度日，并以非人的精力拼死苦干！”

===Original===

He had no right (said he) to claim a half! It was achievement, not fortune, that he desired.
“I insisted. He gratefully yielded. We drew up the document in duplicate. He was especially gratified to find that I had left the whole direction to him. ‘I know nothing of such things,’ I said. ‘I am only the business man. You are the creator, the artist: I am but the base mercantile instrument, and I shall be proud to share in your triumph.’ As I said this I put into my eyes the expression of inspired admiration which we of the commercial world very properly assume when we are dealing with this kind of fodder.
“The next morning, in the cool, before the sun had power, our deeds were attested. I warned him to be utterly silent upon the source of this capital. I said I would be responsible for a rumour that a small saving of his own was engaged. He saw my point, and, though still scrupulous, consented. The work went forward.
“My next step I had already planned. I had privately set aside for it a fixed sum, the equal of what I had given my partner. I hired a pleasant little house and garden in the city, with a fountain of clear water in its shaded court. I purchased a stock of good clothes and even one or two not over-violent jewels﻿—and I began to entertain.
“I bought﻿—at a price which gave me pause﻿—a really wonderful cook; I learnt the games of hazard to which the wealthier of the place were devoted. The headmen of the various quarters of the city, the principal councillors and magistrates learned one from another of the excellence of my table and the interest of my play. I became their intimate. From time to time I spoke of my friendship with the enthusiast and of my regret at his wasting his poor savings upon the dreadful mess outside the walls. They agreed﻿—and all the while that fervid young man redoubled his ardour, himself worked side by side with his men, planned, urged them on, and effected prodigies of labour. Indeed, I feared for his health﻿—a natural anxiety for one in my situation﻿—but by the Mercy of Allah it remained perfect.
“He now, however, came to me more and more frequently and in a greater and greater anxiety. There were but fifty gold pieces remaining, but forty, but twenty!﻿ ﻿… It was a matter of a few days!﻿ ﻿… Already he had had to keep back wages, to devise half-shifts, even to discharge men!﻿ ﻿… Could I not, oh!

===Chinese===

“他甚至推脱说他根本没资格坐享一半的干股！他毕生所求者乃是建功立业，绝非金银财宝！
“在老夫的百般坚持之下，他满怀感激地顺从了。我们一式两份签署了立约文书。当获悉老夫将整个工地的全部指挥大权悉数委任于他时，他更是感激涕零。‘老夫对此类土木营造一窍不通，’老夫说道，‘老夫不过是个满身铜臭的账房商人。你才是天字第一号的创作者、艺术家；老夫充其量不过是一具卑微的市井工具，能躬逢其盛分享你的万世伟业，老夫深感无上荣光！’在吐露这番言辞之时，老夫双眸中恰到好处地流露出商界巨擘在打理此类‘垫脚饲料’时惯常摆出的那种神明般的崇拜神情。
“翌日清晨，趁着旭日初升、酷热未至的清凉时分，我们的契约在官府中人见证下正式画押盖印。老夫严正告诫他：关于这笔本钱的真正出处必须守口如瓶。老夫推说老夫对外自会放出风声，谎称这是他自个儿多年积攒的体己私房钱。他深以为然，尽管骨子里的清高使他略感别扭，最终依然点头应允。工地的大工自此轰轰烈烈铺将开来。
“老夫的第二步棋早已谋划停当。老夫私下里为此划拨出了一笔专款，金额恰好同老夫赏赐给合伙人的数目一般无二。老夫在城中租下了一处带花园的幽美宅院，幽凉的庭院正中央喷涌着一泓甘冽清泉。老夫采办了一批上等绸缎华服，甚至购置了一两件不显张扬却极具底蕴的宝玉佩饰——随后老夫便大宴宾朋。
“老夫以令老夫自个儿都暗自咂舌的高价，雇佣了一位手艺神乎其神的绝顶名厨；老夫还苦练了城中豪绅富商们嗜之如命的各色骨牌赌博技艺。城中各坊的里正长老、元老院的核心议员与推事长官们，口耳相传老夫府上珍馐美味之绝伦与老夫牌局之雅趣。老夫很快便同他们打得火热、称兄道弟。老夫时不时会在酒席间提及老夫同那位狂热工匠的私交，并对他竟将自个儿微薄的体己血汗钱白白糟蹋在城外那片恶臭烂泥滩上表达由衷的同情与惋惜。议员诸公深表同感——而在此期间，那位狂热的年轻后生更是使出了浑身解数，同苦力民夫并肩在烂泥里摸爬滚打，昼夜督工测绘，创造了一个又一个不可思议的工程奇迹。平心而论，老夫真切地担忧他的身子骨会被活活累垮——处于老夫这般处境之人抱有这等忧虑乃是人之常情——然而承蒙真主的仁慈（Mercy of Allah），他的身板硬朗如铁。
“然而好景不长，他跑来找老夫的步调愈发频繁，脸上的惶恐焦灼亦与日俱增。账上的金币只剩下50枚（50）了，紧接着缩水至40枚（40），随后仅剩20枚（20）！……满打满算只能支撑数日光景了！……他已经被迫开始拖欠部分民夫工钱、推行半日轮班、甚至开始辞退苦力！……老夫难道就不能，啊！”

===Original===

could I not raise some further sum?﻿ ﻿… As he had said, the work needed another month at least, and its present state was appalling, no visible security for a loan, all mud and confusion!﻿ ﻿… I could only reply that I would do my best, but that I was not sanguine, and my long face increased his fears.
“Perturbed as he was he had the generosity to regret *my* loss in the unfortunate enterprise.
“I showed a strong indifference and told him that I was used to the rubs of this sad world and that my trust was in Allah!
“At last, as the day when his funds would be exhausted was at hand, I gave a feast of special importance to the treasurer and the chief magistrates of the city, and there led the talk on to the works still continuing. I heard the usual grumble that the sum originally advanced out of the taxes was sunk in a morass, that the young man had, apparently, funds of his own for continuing the nonsense and that yet the city could recover nothing from him. I approved a threat that, out of sheer annoyance, they might stop the whole thing and put him in gaol till he should pay, or at any rate suffer for, his unsettled debt to his fellow-townsmen.
“Then it was that I struck, for the hour was ripe! I pleaded for him as a friend. I let them feel my influence, I waited for the suggestion﻿—and I was not disappointed. The treasurer after a little solemn hesitation said to me: ‘Sir, since you know this young fellow and seem to be in his councils, can you not discover what remains to him and perhaps make him reimburse the gold pieces he owes to our town-fund? We should be beholden to you.’ I answered that my intimacy with the enthusiast hardly went so far, but that I would do my best. Only I begged them for the interval of a week.
“The day after the morrow of that feast the young man came to me in an excessive perturbation. The mud of his travail was still on his hands, and I was concerned to see him limping from the effect (as he told me) of a heavy barrow which had overset as he wheeled it and crushed his big toe. But he neglected the pain in his extreme mental distress and told me that, having paid that week’s wages, his gold pieces were now reduced to *ten*. Even if he himself ate but dry bread in the next few days and sold his poor belongings he could not meet the next account, which was but seven days distant.

===Chinese===

“老夫难道就当真不能再设法周转出一笔银子么？……正如他先前所言，工程至少还需要苦干整整一个月，而工地当下的现状令人触目惊心，根本拿不出任何看得见摸得着的抵押物去借贷，放眼望去全是一片烂泥与混沌！……老夫只能愁眉不展地回复说老夫自当竭尽所能，但老夫心中委实毫无底气；老夫那张拉得老长的苦瓜脸，更是加剧了他内心的灭顶恐慌。
“尽管他自个儿已是焦头烂额，他却依然展现出一股可笑的慷慨博爱，反倒替老夫在这桩倒霉买卖里的巨大‘亏损’扼腕叹息。
“老夫表现出一副泰然自若、看淡风云的豪迈神态，告知他老夫早已习惯了红尘俗世的颠簸坎坷，老夫的一切指望全凭真主造化！
“终于，在他手中银根彻底断绝的大限前夕，老夫专程大摆筵席，盛情款待了本城的度支财务官与首席推事长官，并在酒过三巡后顺水推舟将话题引向了城外那桩仍在苟延残喘的烂尾工程。老夫耳边照例充斥着官老爷们的抱怨牢骚：当年从公帑税款中拨出的本钱全砸进了烂泥坑里打了水漂，那后生不知从哪儿弄来了一笔私房钱在继续胡闹，偏偏官府拿他毫无办法、收不回半分欠账。当官老爷们愤愤不平地威胁说索性动用铁腕彻底叫停整个工地、并将那后生打入死牢抵债直至追回欠银时，老夫适时附和首肯。
“就在这一刹那，老夫雷霆出手，因时机已然彻底熟透了！老夫以至交好友的姿态替那后生苦苦求情。老夫让他们暗暗掂量出老夫的千钧分量，老夫静候着他们自个儿把梯子递过来——而老夫果然未曾失望。度支财务官在一番故作严肃的迟疑沉吟之后，对老夫说道：‘先生，既然您同这后生相交莫逆，且似乎深得他的信任，您能否替官府探探他的底细，设法逼他把欠我们公库的金币如数奉还？倘若事成，全城衙门上下必对您感激不尽！’老夫答复说老夫同那后生的交情恐未深厚至此，但老夫愿勉为其难全力一试。老夫唯独恳请长官诸公宽限老夫一周（7）的周旋工夫。
“盛宴过后的翌日清晨，那年轻后生便失魂落魄地跌撞闯入老夫房中。他手上尚残留着工地的泥浆污渍；老夫颇为心疼地瞧见他一瘸一拐地走着，据他自述乃是推车时一辆沉重的翻斗车侧翻砸碎了他的大脚趾。然而在灭顶的绝望摧残下他早已顾不上肉体的剧痛；他面色惨白地告知老夫：在结清了本周的苦力工钱之后，他手中的金币只剩下可怜巴巴的10枚（10）了。哪怕在接下来的数日里他自个儿嚼干硬的面包充饥、当尽身上所有的破衣烂衫，他也无论如何撑不过距今仅剩7天（7）的下一期发薪大限了。”

===Original===

“I cast my eyes on the ground and delayed a while, the more to impress him. I then told him in grave and subdued tones that I had very bad news. I glanced up a moment to note the wildness in his eyes upon this blow, then cast them down at the ground and continued: ‘I have private advice﻿—perhaps I should not have divulged it to you, for it was given in confidence, but my concern and affection for you have proved too strong﻿—I have private advice that the council intend this very week to demand most formally the repayment of 100 pieces of gold which they say is due from you for advances long ago made, and failing payment to cast you into their dungeon.’
“The young man leapt suddenly to his feet, gave a loud shriek, and made to beat his head against the wall. It was with difficulty that I held his hands and restrained him.
“ ‘Oh! Curse my birth!’ he cried in a paroxysm of despair. ‘And curse my generation! My day has come!’ He raved. He incoherently demanded miracles and alternately denied the Justice of Allah! Grown more subdued but still distraught, he moaned of his affections. He told me﻿—what I had hitherto not known and what interested me very little﻿—that he had a little sister, orphaned as he was, who, if he were put away, would starve or become the prey of strangers. ‘What could be done? What refuge discovered? Curse the day when his fatal dream had struck him! Curse his works! Curse the river! Curse the marsh! Curse the city!’
“And so on﻿—the usual reaction of enthusiasts. It was most distressing. I still held his writhing hands firmly till he was calm enough to hear me, and then said:
“ ‘Listen to me. I have considered your case. I think I can rescue you. I have myself saved a little from my trading operations of the last two months in this town. My credit also is somewhat extended. *I* will find what you need. For I respect genius and I do not judge by common standards﻿—’
“I was proceeding when he burst out into extravagant gratitude; called me his benefactor, kissed my hand again and again, and irrelevantly alluded again to that little sister of his who really had nothing to do with the case. I checked him and continued:
“ ‘I will do more. You do not know your own value﻿—I mean your own moral and intellectual worth﻿—nor what admiration they excite in men who judge as I do that the rest is dross.

===Chinese===

“老夫垂下双目凝视着地面，故意拖延了片刻，好教他心中更受震慑。随后老夫以极其沉痛肃穆的低沉语调告知他：老夫带来了一个天大的坏消息。老夫冷不丁抬眼飞快瞥了他一眼，留意到这一记晴天霹雳在他双眸中激起的狂乱惊恐，旋即重新垂下眼帘继续说道：‘老夫得到了绝密消息——老夫本不该向你泄露半字，因那是旁人私下向老夫透露的机密，但老夫对你的一片爱护关切之情委实压过了理智——老夫获悉：城邦评议会打算就在本周（7），正式向你强索追讨你早年欠下的100枚金币（100）公帑垫资，倘若届时分文拿不出，便要当场将你锁拿打入不见天日的死牢！’
“那年轻后生猛然弹身跃起，爆发出一声撕心裂肺的凄厉尖叫，一头便欲往坚硬的石墙上撞去自尽！老夫好不容易才死死按住他的双手，将他强行拦了下来。
“‘啊！我诅咒我的降生！’他在绝望的癫狂痉挛中放声痛哭，‘我诅咒我的整个世代！我的大限已到啊！’他胡言乱语、语无伦次地奢求神迹降临，旋即又咬牙切齿地痛骂天理公道何在！待他稍稍平复却依旧神魂颠倒之际，他开始痛哭流涕地倾诉起他的骨肉至亲：他向老夫袒露——此前老夫对此一无所知、且老夫压根毫无兴趣的细务——他膝下尚有一个同他一般沦为孤儿的幼妹；倘若他被官府下狱处决，那可怜的女娃必定要活活饿死、或是沦为恶人践踏的猎物！‘这该如何是好？天地之间何处可供栖身避难？我诅咒那该死的痴梦袭上心头的破日子！我诅咒我的浩大工程！我诅咒这条滔滔大河！我诅咒这片恶臭烂泥滩！我诅咒这整座该死的城邦！’
“诸如此类，滔滔不绝——这本是此类狂热之徒大难临头时的寻常神经质反应。现场情形委实惨不忍睹。老夫依旧死死按住他拼命挣扎扭动的双手，直到他彻底冷静下来能够听进人话，老夫方才语重心长地开口说道：
“‘好生听老夫一言。老夫已然通盘替你权衡过了。老夫自忖有能耐救你出水火。老夫自个儿靠着过去两个月（2）在本城奔波粮贸的小买卖，勉强攒下了一点体己微薄积蓄；老夫的市井信用如今也略微宽展了些。这笔银子，由老夫替你兜底垫付！因老夫向来敬重惊世奇才，老夫看人从不用世俗凡夫俗子的鄙陋尺规……’
“老夫话音未落，他已然爆发出一阵近乎癫狂的感恩戴德之情；他连声高呼老夫乃是他的再生恩主，按住老夫的手亲了又亲，嘴里还东拉西扯喋喋不休地提及他那个同本案八竿子打不着的幼妹。老夫当即止住他的话头，继续严正说道：
“‘老夫愿为你做得更多。你根本不明白自个儿的身价有多贵重——老夫是指你身上蕴含的崇高精神与旷世才智——亦不明白在那些像老夫这般视金钱如粪土的饱学之士眼中，你的才华能激起何等无上的钦佩。’

===Original===

Our enterprise is clearly lost’﻿—he nodded his agreement. ‘My investment you may say is gone: or, at any rate, I must take it for what it is worth as a thing half-derelict and gravely threatened by the authorities. But *you* shall not go under. *Your* high talents shall not be wasted so long as I have credit for commerce.﻿ ﻿… Come. Our original agreement is useless now. It is waste paper. Well, we’ll tear it up.’
“ ‘Oh, sir!’ said he. ‘And what of *you*? All this trust in me and my work, all this fund of money laid out by you! All this kindness and support without which I﻿—﻿—’
“ ‘Say no more,’ I interrupted, ‘my mind is made up. I have here a draft of my intention, which I hope will jump with yours.’ So saying I pulled out duplicate papers on which were written very simple terms. The original (and now worthless) agreement for an imaginary and unobtainable profit was cancelled. I promised in the new draft to take over the wretched unfinished works (they were worth nothing), to indemnify the poor fellow against any public claims, and to guarantee him an ample salary for one year from the date of signing. I now added more. I bound myself, in the event of his death or prolonged imprisonment within this year, to pay to his heirs the sum of 100 pieces of gold. This, I pointed out to him, would provide for his little sister (of whom I had now heard enough), while to myself I excused the extravagance by considering his really robust health and the power of my protection to save him from incarceration.
“I would not have believed that a human being could feel so strongly. He fell at my feet, calling me his providence, his all, his bulwark and refuge. He said he did not know there was such goodness among men. I bade him not exaggerate. I reminded him how noble minds had in all ages loved to support the arts, and cited Yussouf-abd-Arham, Sulim-ben-Achab, Muswuf, Wawoo, Mah and other worthies. At last our business was completed, the new deeds signed and attested by my servants, and the gallant fellow, for whose ingenuous character I still retain a profound respect, was put to sleep in happy exhaustion upon soft carpets in my guestroom. I there left him to dream of his little sister and his mud-heaps, while I betook myself to a short casting-up of accounts followed by nightly prayers to the All-Merciful who guides His servant into pleasant places.

===Chinese===

“‘我们的合伙大业显然已彻底败亡，’——他神情沮丧地点头附和。‘老夫投入的真金白银你尽可说它已然打了水漂；抑或不论怎么说，老夫只能咽下这枚苦果，接盘这一堆被官府虎视眈眈、近乎彻底荒废的烂摊子。然而唯独你绝不能沉沦覆灭！只要老夫在商海中尚有一分信用借贷能力，你身上的绝世才干便绝不可白白被尘土埋没！……来吧。我们原先拟定的合伙契约如今已沦为废纸一张，毫无用处。既然是废纸，我们索性便将它当场撕得粉碎！’
“‘啊！老爷！’他惊呼道，‘那您老人家自个儿可该怎么办？您对我与工程的一片赤诚信任，您掏出的这如山真金白银！您对我的这般深恩厚德，若非有您相助，我早已——’
“‘休要多言，’老夫断然打断了他的话，‘老夫意已决。老夫手头拟好了一份崭新的意向契约草案，但愿能合乎你的心意。’言毕，老夫抽出一式两份的崭新文书，上面开列着极其清爽明了的条款：原先那份约定瓜分虚妄缥缈、根本无从兑现的红利的（且如今已沦为废纸的）旧契约当场作废注销；老夫在崭新草案中立誓：老夫全盘接盘城外所有凄凉未竣的工程遗迹（反正它们在世人眼里分文不值），由老夫一力承担并清偿这位可怜后生面临的所有官府追索罚款，并担保自契约画押之日起整整一年之内，按期向他支领丰厚体面的薪饷工钱。在此基础之上，老夫更是加码立约：倘若在这一年期限之内，他不幸身故或是遭遇官府长期羁押，老夫立字为据承诺向他的合法继承人一次性赔付100枚金币（100）巨款！老夫向他挑明：这笔抚恤金足以周全照拂他那个可怜的幼妹（老夫耳边听他念叨这女娃早已听得生茧了）；而在老夫心底，老夫之所以敢于如此‘挥金如土’，全因老夫看中了他那副当真硬朗结实的身板骨骼，以及老夫在衙门里翻云覆雨的通天庇护手腕足以力保他免遭牢狱之灾。
“若非亲眼所见，老夫无论如何亦不敢相信世间凡人竟能动情至此。他扑通一声跪倒在老夫脚边，呼号着说老夫乃是他的真主天意、他的一切、他的中流砥柱与避难堡垒；他说他做梦亦未曾想到红尘凡间竟有如此大仁大义的活菩萨！老夫劝他切莫过分溢美过誉。老夫谆谆告诫他千百年来圣贤俊杰向来热爱护持各色高雅艺术，并随口引经据典罗列了优素福·阿卜杜·阿尔哈姆、苏利姆·本·阿夏卜、穆斯武夫、瓦武、马赫以及诸般先贤名流。这桩大事最终圆满收官，崭新的立约文书由老夫的家仆奴仆画押见证加盖印信；而这位英勇执拗的后生——平心而论，老夫对其纯真如白纸般的秉性至今依然怀有一份深沉的敬意——随后在老夫贵宾客房里铺设的柔软地毯上，在一阵狂喜交加的虚脱中沉沉睡去。老夫任由他在梦乡中去梦见他的幼妹与他的烂泥堆，老夫自个儿则退入账房，在飞快清盘核算了账目之后，便向普慈特慈的真主献上了每夜必行的诚挚祷告——全能的真主向来乐意将祂的谦卑仆从引向流淌着奶与蜜的丰美宝地。”

===Original===

“Next morning I sent the enthusiast back to his labours (with a little cash to carry on with) and very carefully thought out my plans.
“First, at the next dinner I gave, I told my guests (who were, as usual, among the chief men of the city) that I proposed a deal in corn with the factors of Tambulistan: the place did not, as a fact, exist, but the name was well chosen and attractive. They were distant (I said) but in correspondence with me; and as they had a glut and I had news of a dearth in my own country I had taken the opportunity of a deal through third parties. I saw a profit of some 100 pieces of gold. Not more (said I). I was a modest man and only did business in a small way. My guests gallantly denied this and smiled in polite interest, but no more, till, a little later in the meal, I said that the transaction had been just a little heavy for me and that I had looked round for someone who would help in settlement and would share the profit. I told them this profit was fairly certain, that no ready cash was required more than I had myself provided, but that, as a bill or two would do no harm, I had obtained one from a friend at home. All this in the way of general talk.
“As might be expected, the chief magistrate of the town (my most honoured guest) approached me after dinner and told me privately that he would be happy to oblige. I told him there was no sort of necessity, I could draw what I would on my letters from home, or at any rate enough to meet the case. That if he *really* cared to share my little adventure I expected about ten percent profit on the sum engaged﻿—but indeed it was too small for me to trouble him with it. I accepted his pledge of 100 pieces of gold﻿—but I stoutly refused any paper, ‘Your name is enough,’ I said, ‘I shall gratefully use it. My people will trust my word.’
“I gave out next morning that I was going into the hills to meet a messenger. I did indeed pass through the city gate and proceeded till I was well out of sight; but as there was no sense in fatiguing myself I slept through the heat in a wood, watched through the warm night, and returned travel-stained in the evening of the second day.
“On the morning of the third day I met the chief magistrate in the bazaar: I stopped him, chatted, and then and there paid him eleven pieces of gold.

===Chinese===

“翌日清晨，老夫将这位狂热信徒打发回工地继续他的苦工（随手赏赐了他一小笔碎银子充作日常开销），老夫自个儿则极尽周密地盘算推演起全盘大计。
“首先，在老夫随后的下一次盛大晚宴上，老夫向满座贵客（照例皆是城邦元老院推事长官等头面巨擘）透露：老夫正筹划同一批来自坦布利斯坦的经纪行掌柜做一笔粮食大宗买卖——实则世间根本不存在这么个地界，但这名号老夫斟酌得极妙、听上去颇显神秘异域之威。老夫推说彼处山高水远，但与老夫常年鱼雁往来互通有无；鉴于彼邦粮食大获丰收、积压成灾，而老夫又恰好获悉老夫故土大闹荒歉，老夫便趁机居间斡旋做成了一笔三方倒买倒卖的过手生意。老夫预估这笔买卖纯利约莫在100枚金币（100）上下。老夫声称：‘绝不会更多了；老夫是个安分守己的本分人，平生向来只做些微末小买卖。’在座的官老爷们对此客套地予以否认，脸上浮现出彬彬有礼的附和微笑，却并未深究；直至酒过数巡之后，老夫看似无意地长叹一声：声称这笔倒卖的体量对老夫单薄的身家而言略显沉重，老夫正四处寻觅一位可靠的合伙人协助交割垫资、并与之平分这笔横财纯利。老夫向长官们保证这笔利润板上钉钉十拿九稳，且压根无需出借人自掏腰包垫付现银（老夫自个儿垫付的现钱已然足矣），唯独若能有一两张体面的期票汇票背书作为担保自是锦上添花，而老夫恰好已从故土的一位老友手里求得了一张。凡此种种，皆以闲聊闲扯的随兴口吻轻描淡写带过。
“果不其然，晚宴散席之后，本城的首席推事长官（老夫座上最尊贵的上宾）悄然踱步上前，私下向老夫耳语：声称他极乐意拔刀相助效犬马之劳。老夫故作推辞：宣称实则压根毫无必要，老夫凭借故土汇来的信汇银票尽可支取足够的现银，无论如何亦足够应付大局；但倘若长官‘当真’有雅兴躬逢其盛入股老夫这项微末小冒险，老夫预估投资回报率约莫在10%左右——但这点碎银子委实太微不足道，老夫实在不忍拿它叨扰长官清修。老夫勉为其难笑纳了他以100枚金币（100）为限的口头认购担保——但老夫坚决拒绝签署任何字据文书：‘长官的威名金字招牌便足矣！’老夫说道，‘能得长官威名庇护老夫已感激涕零；老夫的伙计信得过老夫的信誉！’
“翌日清晨，老夫对外放风宣称老夫须进山翻越关隘去接应信使。老夫当真昂首阔步穿出城门，直到彻底走出行人视线之外；然而老夫绝无自讨苦吃折磨身子骨的雅兴，便钻入一处密林在大树荫凉下酣然避暑，在温热的夜幕中静观星斗，直至第三日（3）傍晚方才风尘仆仆、装出一副鞍马劳顿的模样折返回城。
“到了第三天（3）清晨，老夫在热闹喧嚣的巴扎大集上‘偶遇’了首席长官：老夫一把拉住他攀谈寒暄，随后当场不容分说塞给他11枚金币（11）！”

===Original===

‘I met my messenger from Tambulistan’ (said I) ‘we exchanged parcels, and I find I have netted just over the ten percent. These eleven pieces are your proportion upon your kind investment of 100.’ He protested of course that it had been no investment, merely a few friendly words of support; but he took the money, and I could see that he was pleased. He was curiously pleased. Indeed, he was so pleased that, though a discreet man, he could not forbear mentioning the matter to his wife. Rich men love small windfalls.
“In a few hours, therefore, the head mufti, the chief of the guard, and two very important councillors had in their various ways touched casually upon the wheat trade during short conversations in which each had separately engaged me under the shades of the bazaar. A little later, as I took the air by the riverside at evening, the principal ascetic of the district, who had come in to buy his few lentils for the week, spoke to me briefly of the same matter. I gave each of them a different answer, alluding to various cargoes of wheat, caravans of wheat and tumbrils of wheat﻿—all in technical terms; to risks, to covering sums, to transfers from one district to another. In each case I refused anything but a pledge to stand in with a transaction somewhat beyond my unaided powers; in each case I ridiculed the smallness of the little adventure; in each case I paid, after the lapse of a few days, to one a single piece of gold as his profit, to another two, to another three. And each was very pleased.
“As the days went by I varied the procedure. Sometimes I regretted the unexpectedly small profit obtained. Once I deliberately announced a loss and sternly gathered reluctant contributions from my associates; but immediately afterwards I did another fine stroke in imaginary wheat and paid a fat dividend to my friends﻿—indeed, that particular affair cost me twenty-five pieces. But it was worth it. I got rid in less than a month of 200 pieces of gold in this fashion. It was a cruel trial, but proved, in the event, most fruitful. For though I would never advise in large investments, yet by this simple method, my reputation for judgment in that which men most value﻿—which is money﻿—was assured.
“I had set 250 pieces aside for this experiment, and my total stock was running low when I steered my ship to port.

===Chinese===

“‘老夫在山中接应上了自坦布利斯坦赶来的信使，’老夫对他低语，‘我们顺利交割了货单包裹，老夫一盘账发现净赚了略超10%的纯利！这11枚金币（11），便是长官您当日慷慨出资担保100枚金币（100）理应分得的红利干股！’长官嘴上照例推脱说这哪算什么投资入股、不过是朋友间的拔刀相助罢了；但他那双手却极其诚实地将金币紧紧攥入袖中，老夫瞧得出他简直心花怒放！他那份由衷的惬意简直无以言表。实则他兴奋得难以自持，以致尽管他平生向来以城府深沉著称，当晚回家后亦忍不住将这桩天上掉馅饼的美事同自家夫人全盘炫耀了一番。巨富豪门最嗜好此类不劳而获的意外横财。
“不出数个时辰，到了晌午巴扎市集的凉棚阴凉之下，大穆夫提圣法官、城防禁军统领、以及两位位高权重的核心元老院议员，便各自在不同的时段、以各自精巧的措辞，在同老夫的简短‘偶遇’寒暄中极其随意地扯到了粮食贸易的行市上来。到了傍晚时分，当老夫在河畔散步纳凉之时，本省德高望重、进城采买本周扁豆干粮的首席苦修隐士，亦就同一桩营生同老夫简短搭讪了几句。老夫对每个人皆给出了截然不同的玄妙答复，满嘴飙着‘各色散装粮船’、‘大宗运粮驼队’、‘翻斗大车粮运’等专业商贸行话黑话，神乎其神地大谈各类对冲风险、平仓准备金、跨省划转调拨诸如此类。对于每一位达官贵人，老夫皆断然回绝其大规模入股的企图，仅勉为其难应承容许他们在一桩略微超出老夫独揽财力的小买卖里插上一脚；老夫大肆嘲弄这等小打小闹的微薄利润；而在相隔数日之后，老夫如期兑现信诺：向一人奉上一枚金币的分红，向另一人奉上两枚，向第三人奉上三枚。人人皆喜笑颜开、心满意足！
“随着岁月流逝，老夫在操盘手段上虚虚实实、变化多端。有时老夫愁眉苦脸地叹息利润意外微薄；有一回老夫甚至故意宣布粮食走私亏损破产，板着铁面极其冷酷地从合伙诸公手里强行收缴了极不情愿的摊派亏损金；然而紧随其后，老夫立刻在另一桩纯属子虚乌有的‘特大假想小麦倒卖’中上演绝地大反击，向诸位同僚密友派发了一笔极其丰厚肥美的惊人分红——实则单单那一桩自导自演的戏码，老夫自掏腰包足足倒贴了25枚金币（25）！但这笔本钱下得太值了！在短短不足一个月的光阴里，老夫依循此法前前后后‘倒贴糟蹋’了整整200枚金币（200）！这委实是一场令人肉疼的残酷淬炼，然而事后的走向却证明这是一笔无与伦比的丰产奇迹！盖因老夫从不在大宗投资上替人出谋划策，然而单凭这套质朴无华的攻心术，老夫在人世间最珍视之物——那便是真金白银——上的金字招牌与绝顶预判神威，已然彻底牢不可破！
“老夫私下里为这场攻心试验划拨了整整250枚金币（250）的总预算；而当老夫驾驭的大船终于平稳靠港之时，老夫随身携带的全部金币私囊已然接近彻底见底。”

===Original===

“First, out of my remainder, I brought to the city council fifty pieces of gold saying that I had with difficulty screwed them from my young friend, who was still digging away in the outskirts, but that he had faithfully promised the second fifty in two months’ time. Next I created a sort of stupor in the now large circle of my friends by saying publicly and boldly that I was beginning to see something in this plan of draining the marsh. I reminded them that the engineer had always been my friend, that I had always seen something in him, that in spite of his obvious lack of business sense I could not help admiring his talent in his own line.
“The place was by this time dried and levelled, the embankment was all fairly sloped and paved, the cuttings, heaps, and rubbish had disappeared. Then it was that I took a party of these my important friends out to view the place at evening from the city walls and quietly told them that it was mine.
“There it lay before them: a magnificent plain, reclaimed and ordered, firm land pegged out in rows and with neat placards of new streets, all named.
“You know, my dear nephews, the admiration excited in all men of affairs for one who has forestalled them. I rose in the estimation of my neighbours to a height beyond compare. They already had a most deep reverence for my keen perceptions in commerce which had been proved in so many little tips﻿—insignificant in quantity, but, oh! so universally accurate. Now, indeed, upon learning this great stroke (or, as they called it in the local language, ‘Koo’) they were lost in surprise and respect.
“After sunset I counted my money. I had left precisely fifty pieces of gold: a close aim, a narrow edge of venture. But, as the proverb says, ‘The bold hunter slays the lion, the timid is slain of the cub.’
“It was late in that same night that the chief magistrate knocked at my door with the greatest precaution, bearing a hooded lantern, and walking on tiptoe. He begged me as an old friend (but in a whisper) to sell him so much land in the new quarter as might suffice for a good house and garden, suitable for his son or even for himself. I told him that I would have no sordid dealings with so excellent a friend. I could not do less than *give* him such a site.

===Chinese===

“首先，从老夫箱底残留的最后一点银两中，老夫亲自前往城邦评议会奉上了50枚金币（50），声称这是老夫费尽九牛二虎之力、好不容易才从老夫那位仍在城外沼泽挥汗苦干的年轻工程师朋友手里挤出来的退款；并信誓旦旦替他保证两个月（2）之后定能如数补齐剩下的另外50枚金币。紧接着，老夫在如今已然庞大无比的显贵密友圈子里投下了一枚重磅炸弹：老夫公开且斩钉截铁地放风宣布，老夫在这套排干城外恶臭沼泽地的宏大蓝图里，已然瞧出了泼天的旷世商机！老夫向同僚诸公深情回忆：声称那位工程师小友自始至终皆是老夫的忘年挚友，老夫老早便在他身上看出了非凡过人的潜质；尽管他在商业嗅觉上一窍不通，但他在自个儿土木营造上的惊世天才委实令老夫钦佩得五体投地！
“彼时彼刻，城外的恶臭泥沼已然彻底被彻底排干整平，外围巍峨长堤通体平整倾斜并铺上了齐整坚固的铺路石板，往昔狼藉杂乱的泥丘、弃土与瓦砾废墟早已荡然无存。就在彼时，老夫在傍晚时分专程陪同这一大帮位高权重的显贵同僚登上巍峨的城墙俯瞰全景，随后轻描淡写地悄然告诉他们：城外这整片辽阔平原，如今尽归老夫一人所有！
“一幅壮丽的画卷横陈在他们眼前：一座广袤无垠的沃土平原被彻底开垦驯服、井然有序，坚实的地块被白线齐整划分为行行方阵，主次干道两旁整齐竖立着崭新的街道名牌与公示招牌。
“我亲爱的侄儿们，你们尽可想象：商海中人对于一个抢在所有人前头捷足先登的先行者，会怀有怎样五体投地的崇拜与狂热！老夫在满城权贵心目中的威望瞬间飙升到了无以复加的崇高绝顶！他们先前早已因老夫在无数次小打小闹的内幕 tips 里展现出的神机妙算而对老夫敬若神明——那些 tips 获利金额虽说微不足道，但苍天在上，每一次都是百分之百的绝对神准！而如今，当获悉老夫竟悄无声息布下了如此惊天动地的神级大局（在当地土话里被唤作‘酷’，意即绝杀妙手）时，显贵诸公彻底陷入了无以复加的惊骇与崇敬之中！
“日落黄昏之后，老夫闭门盘点了随身盘缠：老夫手里恰好只剩下50枚金币（50）整——这真是一场命悬一线的极限下注、一场行走在悬崖边缘的惊天博弈！然而俗话说得好：‘胆壮的猎夫缚苍龙，怯懦的懦夫毙幼崽！’
“就在同一天深夜，首席推事长官提着一盏蒙着黑布的暗光气死风灯，蹑手蹑脚、做贼心虚般打起十二分小心敲响了老夫的宅门。他以多年老友的口吻（却压低到了极致的耳语）苦苦哀求老夫：恳请老夫无论如何在新城地盘上割爱卖给他一块地皮，面积只需足够为他的爱子、抑或为他自个儿晚年营建一处带后花园的体面豪宅即可。老夫义正辞严地回复他说老夫断然不屑于同如此可敬的莫逆之交进行铜臭市井的肮脏交易；老夫所能做的一切，唯有将那块风水宝地当场无偿‘赠予’于他！”

===Original===

“I brought out a plan (on which the engineer had already drawn out streets and public squares) and marked upon the main thoroughfare the plot I would assign to him. He departed with praise and blessings. Hardly had he gone when a yet more furtive step approached my door. It was the mufti. He offered me a hundred pieces of gold for such a site. I generously gave him for *fifty* a larger one than he had ventured to beg. I marked it somewhat farther down the same main thoroughfare. He went away belauding my name and soul.
“It was near midnight when another footfall halted at my door: a councillor’s. For fifty pieces he also had a site worth double, and in the same street. He had not gone an hour, it was the darkest of the night, when a much fainter shuffling of bare feet could be just perceived followed by a light trying of the latch. The door was opened a crack and the strong emaciated face of the ascetic twisted round the edge and peered in. I beckoned him. He put his finger to his lips, cautiously secured the fastening of the lock﻿—and then, bending forward, whispered in my ear.﻿ ﻿… I was a little surprised at the magnitude of his offer, but of course I accepted it at once. Such men have great influence with the faithful. He proposed to *let* his property or perhaps to hold for a rise. He would continue of course to live in his humble cell outside the city, in the wastes. He departed quickly and like a ghost. At dawn came yet another councillor, more bold than the rest, who made a plain business proposition for block fifty-three and was at once gratified.
“So for days the procession continued, each man coming singly and watching whether he were observed. Half the council had sites for little or nothing, and the other half had sites at rates really very reasonable.
“And all the while, to the mass of buyers who importuned me and clamoured about me, I said that only very limited sales could be made, and those of leases only, and even so not till a later date.
“Meanwhile the whole town council was converted. The councillors had quite lost their old aversion to the scheme.

===Chinese===

“老夫当场铺开一张详尽的新城规划蓝图（上面早已由工程师绘制齐备了纵横交错的街道与公共集市广场），在大道正中央用红笔圈定了一块最肥沃显赫的地块指派赏赐给他。长官千恩万谢、满口颂扬祝福着退了出去。他前脚刚迈出大门，一阵更为鬼祟蹑脚的细碎脚步声已然逼近了老夫的门扉：来者乃是大穆夫提圣法官！他一进门便慷慨开价100枚金币（100）求购一处宅基地；老夫豪情大发，仅象征性收了他50枚（50）金币，便赏赐给他一块远比他胆敢奢求的还要宽阔庞大得多的风水宝地，位置就圈定在同一条中央大道的下首地段。他嘴里将老夫的英名与灵魂颂扬上了九重云霄，心满意足而去。
“待时近子夜时分，又一阵蹑手蹑脚的步履声在老夫门外戛然而止：这回是一位核心议员诸公！同样只需50枚（50）金币，他便在同一条繁华大道上笑纳了一块市值翻倍的绝佳地皮。他离去不足一个时辰，正值夜色最漆黑深沉的黎明前夕，一阵极其微弱的赤脚窸窣拖沓声幽幽传来，紧随其后的是门闩被极轻微拨弄的试探声响。房门被小心翼翼推开了一条窄缝，苦修隐士那张枯槁干瘪却坚毅肃穆的面庞从门缝里探了进来，目光如鹰隼般环视屋内。老夫向他招了招手。他将食指竖在唇边示意噤声，打起万分小心倒扣上门闩锁扣——随后身子前倾，将嘴唇死死贴在老夫耳畔吐露了机密……老夫对他开出的骇人出资巨款略感惊愕，但老夫旋即毫不犹豫全盘笑纳了！盖因在这等信徒心目中，苦修神僧的无上威望足以号令万民。他谋划将这块地皮‘对外放租’、抑或是待价而沽坐等暴涨；至于他自个儿，当然依旧乐此不疲隐居在城外荒漠那间简陋凄苦的土坯陋室之中。他去得疾如幽灵，倏忽便消失在夜色里。待到东方泛起鱼肚白的黎明破晓，又一位远比同僚更为豪横大胆的议员破门而入，当场以干脆利落的商业条款向老夫全款认购了53号地块（53），老夫自是当场顺水推舟成全了他。
“在随后的漫长数日里，这般鬼祟的长队接踵而至，显贵们皆孤身一人单刀赴会，且生怕被身遭的耳目撞见。评议会整整一半的元老议员以白捡般的微末白菜价坐拥了黄金地皮，而剩下的另外一半议员则以当真极其公道优惠的折扣价瓜分了其余地块。
“与此同时，面对城中闻风而动、将老夫府邸围得水泄不通的汹涌民间买房人潮，老夫冷冷向他们表态：新城土地仅能限量极少额度发售，且仅限长期租约租赁权，即便如此亦须延宕至日后的大吉之日方可放盘。
“在此期间，整个城邦市政评议会早已全盘归化皈依；议员诸公往昔对城外工程的全部嫌恶与仇视，早已烟消云散得无影无踪。”

===Original===

They earmarked enthusiastically and by a unanimous vote a special tax for the laying out of the new quarter, its planting with trees, the bringing of conduits to it so that fountains of sweet water might appear in private houses and in public places, and, though the levy was no light one, it was paid cheerfully enough by all the councillors, who were now curiously proud of their town’s aggrandizement; even among the mass of poor ratepayers there were no executions, but only one mild case of torture, and perhaps a dozen bastinadoes.
“The public money so spent was very well worth while. The improvement in my property was immense; and when a fine road, bordered with trees, was laid down all along the embankment I obtained very heavy compensation from the city for the use of the ground and the cutting off of my approaches to the river.
“I, on my side, was not niggardly. I promised 100 gold pieces to the building of a new mosque in the centre of the place, on condition that ninety-nine others should do the same, and I started a hall of public recreation, the price of admission to which barely repaid the expenses of upkeep and cleaning, taxes, heat and light, interest on debentures (which I had myself very handsomely subscribed), service, literature, and secretarial expenses, decoration, approaches, annual depreciation, and at the most a profit of six to eight percent. I also provided kitchens where the poorer citizens could purchase food at very little more than its value. These were of great service to the police, who had here a central place whence the movements of my less fortunate neighbours could be traced. I presented also public fountains with solid pewter mugs, attached to the stonework by strong chains lest they should be stolen, and I even went so far as to provide, free of all cost, public plans of the new quarter showing where unleased sites still remained and the terms on which they might be acquired.
“I made it a rule that any man building a house on my land should promise to give it up to me for nothing after twenty years; but as many people were too poor to build their own houses I established a fund whence they could borrow the money at the ordinary rates of interest and the few dues, fees, deductions, etc., inevitable to such transactions. In every way did I develop and benefit this my creation of a new town.

===Chinese===

“议员诸公全票一致通过、狂热欣慰地在财政预算中专款划拨了一笔特别税金，用于新城的通盘市政大营建：沿街栽植绿柳林荫，自远山引流开凿引水渠，好教甘甜清冽的泉水喷涌于各处私家华宅与公共广场的喷泉之中；尽管这笔摊派税赋委实沉重不菲，却被全体议员兴高采烈地全额缴纳，盖因他们如今对自个儿城邦的开疆拓土产生了一种异乎寻常的自豪感；甚至在广袤贫苦的底层纳税布衣之中，催缴税款亦未曾闹出人命斩首，满打满算不过发生了一桩轻微的严刑拷打、以及约莫12次杖刑（12）小惩大诫罢了。
“如此砸下的公帑巨款委实物超所值！老夫名下产业的市值呈现出爆炸式的井喷暴涨；而当官府沿着巍峨长堤修筑起一条绿树成荫、富丽堂皇的滨河景观大道之时，老夫更以该道路侵占老夫私有土地、且阻断了老夫私家滨河通道为由，从城邦公库手里狠狠讹诈勒索了一大笔极其沉重的巨额赔偿金！
