Above its vast horseshoe gate, ablaze with azure tiles, was inscribed in gold the sentence, ‘Wealth is of God alone.’
“I was popularly known as ‘Melek-al-Tawajin,’ or the Pipkin King, but officially decorated with the local title of ‘Warzan Dahur,’ which was the highest they knew and signifies ‘Leader in battle.’ I was entitled to wear a sword with a silver hilt in a jewelled scabbard, an ornament of which I was justly proud, but the blade of which I very sensibly kept blunt lest my servant should cut himself when he polished it, or even I should inadvertently do myself a mischief when I pulled it out with a flourish to display it to my guests, or saluted with it on parade. I had become a most intimate companion of the court and was the most trusted counsellor of the king, to whose wives also I often lent small sums of money; nor did I ask to be repaid.
“In such a situation I mused upon my condition, and felt within me strange promptings for a new and larger life. I was now well advanced in manhood, I was filled with desires for action and device which the narrow field of that happy but restricted place could not fulfil. I longed for adventurous action in a larger world.
“The output and consumption of pipkins was at an exact unchangeable level; the revenue a fixed amount. The profit of the trade I held came to some 20,000 dinars in the year, the full purchase of which should be, say, 200,000 dinars.
“I prayed earnestly for guidance, and one night as I so prayed an idea was revealed to me by the Most High.
“I approached the king and told him how, all my life, I had nourished the secret belief that a trade necessary to the whole community should not, in justice, be controlled by a private individual, but should rather be the full property of the state, of which His Majesty was the sole guardian.
“The king listened to me with rapt attention as I unfolded with an inspired eloquence my faith that no one man should intercept profits which were due to the work of all. ‘It is your majesty,’ I cried, ‘who alone should have control over what concerns the body corporate of your people.’

===Chinese===

“至此，老夫已然牢牢扼住了全境陶锅产业的绝对命脉，全邦所需的所有陶锅器皿悉数出自老夫一人之手，举国再无半个竞争敌手！老夫为自个儿营建的巍峨府邸乃是全境最富丽堂皇的宫殿，然而老夫谦卑地补充一句：整座宅邸通体铭刻着无数庄严肃穆的圣典箴言。在宅邸正前方那道贴满湛蓝宝蓝琉璃瓷砖、光芒万丈的巨型马蹄形巍峨正门上方，赫然用赤金泥金铭刻着至理名言：‘唯真主独赐泼天富贵！’
“在市井坊间，万民皆顶礼膜拜尊称老夫为‘陶锅之王’；而在朝廷官府邸报上，老夫更荣获了本邦至高无上的显赫封号‘瓦尔赞·达胡尔’——此乃彼邦所知最尊贵的头衔，意为‘破阵统帅’！老夫获准在腰间佩挂一柄镶嵌纯银剑柄、配有宝石剑鞘的宝剑；老夫对这项恩典由衷深感自豪，然而老夫极其明智地命人将剑刃死死磨钝，免得老夫的小厮在擦拭保养时割伤皮肉，甚至免得老夫自个儿在宴请宾客拔剑助兴、或是校场阅兵行礼时不慎伤着自个儿。老夫已然沦为王室宫廷最推心置腹的核心内臣，是国王陛下最倚重的首席参赞，老夫甚至时常向深宫内苑的王妃娘娘们随手借支小额碎银周转；且从不指望她们奉还。
“高踞于这般人生巅峰之上，老夫俯瞰着自身的功业，灵魂深处却冷不丁涌现出一股对更崇高、更辽阔天地的神秘悸动！老夫彼时已步入壮年鼎盛之境，老夫胸中充盈着对惊天伟业与旷世棋局的无尽渴望，而这座幽闭快乐却极其狭窄局促的世外桃源，早已无法安放老夫磅礴的野心。老夫渴望在更浩瀚的大千世界里翻江倒海！
“全邦陶锅的年产销体量已然触及了不可逾越的绝对天花板；每年的公帑与商业进项已然沦为一笔死数字。老夫名下垄断的陶锅产业每年创造的纯利约莫在20,000第纳尔（20000）上下，折算其资本化买断总价，约莫当在200,000第纳尔（200000）巨款！
“老夫向真主至诚祈求指引；就在某夜老夫这般虔诚祈祷之际，至高无上的主宰向老夫揭示了一道惊天妙计！
“老夫当即求见国王陛下，向陛下深情剖白：老夫平生自始至终深藏着一个崇高的信仰——凡事关举国黎民切身民生福祉的命脉产业，依循公道正义，断不可由市井私人一手遮天独揽暴利，而理应全盘归属于国家公有，而陛下乃是国家至高利益的唯一神圣守护者！
“国王陛下屏气凝神、全神贯注聆听着老夫的演说，老夫以神明启示般的无上雄辩，向陛下痛陈普天下绝无任何一人有资格中饱私囊霸占本属于全民劳作的劳动果实！‘陛下啊！’老夫振臂高呼，‘唯有陛下您老人家，才配全权掌控这关乎举国黎民社稷的至高公器！’”

===Original===

He and he alone should superintend the purchase of pipkins, should regulate their sales, should receive all sums paid for them, and should use that revenue as he might think best for himself and the commonwealth. ‘While I was struggling in the dust and confusion of commercial life,’ I concluded, ‘I had no leisure to work out my scheme in its entirety, nor even to appreciate its serene equity﻿—but now﻿ ﻿… now, I see, I understand, I know!’
“Carried away by the fire of my conviction, my royal master could no longer brook delay. He bade me put the idea in its main lines before him at once, and assured me it should at once be put into execution.
“I thereupon pulled out a paper showing that since I was fully agreeable to take no more than the cash value of the trade *plus* goodwill and *plus* certain probable gains which I might reasonably expect in the future, I would be amply compensated if I were to hand all over to the commonwealth for the merely nominal sum of half a million dinars﻿—500,000. ‘A sum which,’ I continued, ‘is of little moment to Your Majesty; especially as it will be met by the taxation of your willing and loyal subjects.’
“The matter was at once concluded. My great act of renunciation was everywhere acclaimed with transports of public joy. Every honour was heaped upon me. The king himself pronounced my panegyric at the farewell banquet given in my honour, and an inscription was ordered to be encrusted in the most gorgeous tiles on the chief gate of the city: ‘On the tenth day of the month Shaaban in the three hundred and third year from the Flight of the Prophet, by the act of Mahmoud the Magnificent all citizens became in the matter of **Pipkins** his common heirs.’ ”
The merchant had been so moved by these old memories that he had difficulty in proceeding. He was silent for a few moments, and then ended in a more subdued tone.
“The sum of 500,000 dinars, well packed, will load without discomfort some dozen camels. These and their drivers were provided me by a grateful nation. I passed out of the town at sunrise, attended by a vast concourse of the populace who pressed round me in a delirium of grateful cries, and so took my way eastward across the mountains and left this happy vale forever.”

===Chinese===

“唯有陛下、且唯有陛下独尊一人，方配全权督造陶锅的采办、平抑调控其市场销路、坐收全境缴纳的全部购锅现银、并全权依循陛下自身与举国社稷的至高利益自由调拨支配这笔巨额财源！‘当年老夫在市井铜臭商海的泥泞与纷乱中苦苦挣扎搏杀之时，’老夫动情地总结道，‘老夫尚无闲暇通盘推演这一宏伟蓝图的真谛，甚至未能勘破其背后宁静而神圣的无上公理——然而如今……如今老夫看透了，老夫参悟了，老夫全明白了！’
“被老夫信念中喷涌而出的熊熊烈火所深深感染，老夫的君王再也无法按捺哪怕一刻的延宕！他当场勒令老夫将全盘架构要点立刻呈阅御览，并拍案保证定要以雷霆之势付诸施行！
“老夫旋即从袖中抽出一卷备好的文书，向陛下挑明：鉴于老夫完全乐意仅以全盘产业的即期资产现值、外加商誉溢价、外加老夫在未来岁月里理所应当合情合理预期斩获的潜在红利作为对冲，老夫深感倘若能以区区五十万（500,000）第纳尔（500000）的象征性名义总价将整座陶业帝国全盘奉献归还给国家社稷，老夫便已受宠若惊心满意足了！‘这笔碎银子，’老夫继续进言道，‘对于陛下而言不过是九牛一毛；尤其是这笔开销完全可通过向陛下忠贞不渝、心甘情愿的子民加征摊派税款来轻松冲抵！’
“这桩旷世大案当场便一锤定音！老夫这项大公无私、毁家纾难的退隐壮举，在全境每一个角落激起了全民狂喜的滔天巨浪！朝野上下将世间所有的至高殊荣尽数倾泻在老夫一身。在为老夫践行的盛大国宴之上，国王陛下亲自登台宣读了赞颂老夫万世功勋的御制颂词；朝廷下旨用全境最璀璨夺目的五彩琉璃宝石瓷砖，在王城主城门的城门楼上深深嵌刻下一道万古长青的纪念金字铭文：‘在大希吉拉历303年（303）舍尔邦月第十日（10），承蒙卓越者马哈茂德之慷慨善举，举国黎民在陶锅产业上皆顺理成章化为其共同遗产继承人！’
商人讲到此处，被往昔波澜壮阔的岁月记忆深深震颤，以致一时之间竟激动得语塞凝噎。他闭目沉思良久，随后用一种平复下来的低沉语调收束了全篇：
“整整500,000第纳尔（500000）沉甸甸的金币，用麻袋打包妥帖之后，足以毫不费力地压满整整12峰（12）精壮骆驼的背脊。这支驼队与其脚夫马夫，全是由感恩戴德的举国黎民无偿奉送给老夫的临别厚礼。在旭日东升的破晓时分，老夫在大批涌上前来的万民那狂热绝伦的感激欢呼声簇拥之下缓步穿出城门，迎着朝阳向东翻越崇山峻岭，永远告别了这处幸福祥和的人间仙境。”

===Original===

At that moment the detestable falsetto of the muezzin was heard from the neighbouring minaret, and the boys, all dazed at the recital of such triumphs, left the presence of their uncle as though it had been that of a God.

===Chinese===

就在彼时彼刻，邻近宣礼塔上传来了令人憎恶的假声呼拜尖叫；被这般通天壮举震撼得目瞪口呆的少年们，悄然退出了叔父的内堂，仿佛适才他们所朝觐的并非肉体凡胎，而是一位无所不能的活神仙。

## XI *Al-Wali*, or “The Holy One” / 第十一章 圣人
===Original===

When the hour of public executions had arrived (they were more numerous than usual) his young nephews respectfully assembled at the feet of the aged millionaire and received the further account of his fortunes.
“You might imagine, my children,” he began “that having this small capital so happily furnished me by Providence in the short space of a single day, I would again venture upon a commercial undertaking. That would have been indeed my natural course; but you must remember that I could not, without great danger, enter the city I had just left, lest my able transactions should lead me into contact with those at whose expense they had been conducted. Further, I was in a strange country with no knowledge of my way and with nothing to guide me save the happy circumstance that I was still within the boundaries of our holy religion. Most of those I should meet would thus be True Believers, whose frailties I could better understand than those of the Kafir, and of whom therefore I could (under the all-powerful guidance of Heaven) more easily take advantage.
“Devoutly remembering the signal mercies shown to me by Allah in this last short day, I determined to follow the same course as I had when my good fortune came to me﻿—to lie passive under the Mighty Hand directing me and to trust to luck.
“I took some sleep in the night beside my fire, but hardly had I awakened at dawn when I was aware of a group approaching me through the forest track. They were a party of a dozen or so, half of them on foot, half of them mounted; of no great consequence if one might judge by their clothing, which, my dear nephews, is in most occasions in life the signal by which we may know whether to revere men or to despise them. Both beasts and humans in this group were travel-stained as having come from some great distance.
“As I saw them before they saw me, I naturally took the precaution of creeping up behind them through the trees in order to overhear the object of their journey. It appeared that they were bound on pilgrimage to the shrine of a Most Holy Man, to obtain his oracle in a matter which concerned their miserable village.
“My mind was at once made up. I ran back by a circuitous course through the trees, came to a place ahead of their progress, and there, spreading my little mat upon the sward, I prostrated myself in prayer.

===Chinese===

当闹市公开行刑的时辰再次降临（今日处决的死囚格外繁多），年少的侄儿们毕恭毕敬地肃立围坐在白发苍苍的百万富翁伯父膝下，倾听他继续讲述发家传奇。
“我亲爱的孩子们，你们或许以为，”老人开口讲道，“在短短一日光阴里侥幸笑纳了上苍眷顾赏赐的这笔微薄本钱之后，老夫定会迫不及待再次重操旧业大搞经商实务。依常理而言这本是人之常情；然而你们务必谨记：彼时彼刻老夫倘若再踏入刚刚逃离的那座重镇，无异于自寻死路！因老夫那些干练利落的高超运作，随时可能让老夫同那些充当了老夫买单冤大头的苦主们狭路相逢！况且老夫孑然一身流落异乡，人生地不熟举目无亲，所能依恃的唯一幸事，乃是老夫依然身处在这门神圣正信宗教的庇佑疆域之内。老夫沿途邂逅的大多是正信的穆斯林信徒，与异教徒卡菲尔相比，老夫对正信同胞的劣根软肋自是参悟得透彻得多，因而在全能上苍的英明指引下，算计并剥削起他们来亦自然轻巧得多。
“老夫满怀虔诚地在心底默念着真主在适才这短短一日里向老夫倾洒的赫赫神恩，毅然决然决定奉行当初带给老夫无上横运的同一套哲学——那便是在冥冥中指引老夫的全能巨手之下顺应天命、随遇而安，一切全凭老天爷赏饭吃！
“当夜老夫在林间篝火旁沉沉睡去；然而拂晓天明老夫刚一睁眼，便敏锐察觉到林间小径上有一支人马正朝着老夫的方向缓缓逼近。那是一支约莫12人左右（12）的队伍，半数徒步，半数骑着牲口；单从其褴褛的衣着判断，这帮人显然皆是无足轻重的草民——我亲爱的侄儿们，切记在人世间的大多数场合下，衣衫服饰向来是判定究竟该对其顶礼膜拜还是该嗤之以鼻的至高风向标！这支人马中的人畜皆满身风尘仆仆，显然刚从极其遥远的山外长途跋涉而来。
“老夫抢先发现了他们而他们尚未瞥见老夫，老夫自然打起十二分审慎，悄无声息贴着林木树影摸到他们身后，暗中偷听他们此行的究竟目的。原来他们乃是前往某位至圣神僧的圣陵朝圣祈福的信徒队伍，专程为了向圣人讨求一则事关他们那个凄凉穷山村生计的神谕。
“老夫当场心中大定！老夫顺着树林兜了个大圈子一路飞奔，赶在他们前头绕到了他们必经的小径正前方；老夫当场在青翠的草坪上铺开随身携带的小礼拜毯，五体投地陷入了无比虔诚的俯伏叩拜之中。”

===Original===

Indeed, I was thus able to kill two birds with one stone, for I had not yet said those morning prayers of the True Believer which I had never omitted in all my life, save when I happened to be flying from justice and therefore deprived of leisure.
“As I heard them coming up behind me I raised my head and voice at once, and fell into a perfect ecstasy of worship, which did not fail to impress the simple mountaineers. They halted respect fully until I saw fit to terminate my conversation with the Most High. I pretended to be so absorbed in my contemplation of divine things as not to notice them: for to keep them waiting secured religious as well as worldly respect. They approached me with deference and even awe. I told them I was bound for the shrine of a Most Holy Man whose name I gave them. They were overjoyed to discover that they had a companion filled with the same sentiments as themselves.
“ ‘We also,’ said their leader, ‘are engaged upon the same sacred mission. For we have been informed by a messenger (whom we dispatched a month ago from our village) that the Saint will graciously receive us and give us a reply upon a doubtful matter of wild hedge-pigs which has greatly excited our tribe, whether they be pork or no.’
“I let them convey by chance phrases the direction we had to travel and the distance of our goal. I was delighted to discover that our way did not lie through the city, and that we might hope to reach the Holy One before night.
“The journey was tedious, passing over burnt land with but a few wretched villages upon the track; but by the late afternoon we could see far off, coloured by the declining rays of the sun, a small, white-domed building, the tomb of a great saint long dead, by the side of which a large group of tents and a considerable assembly lying out in the open round them cooking their evening meal, beasts of burden, and all the movement of a camp, showed us that we were reaching the term of our day’s journey.
“When we reached the camp I joined the thickest part of the throng, separating from the group with which I had been marching. I made my evening prayers in as conspicuous a place as possible, prolonged them prodigiously, the better to impress my new neighbours, and then lay down, uncertain what the course of the next day should be.
“It was revealed to me in a dream.

===Chinese===

“平心而论，老夫此举委实是一箭双雕：因老夫今晨尚未完成身为真正信徒每日必行的晨祷呢——而这项神圣天职，老夫毕生除却大难临头东窗事发亡命逃亡无暇顾及之外，向来是从未废弛过的！
“当听闻他们的杂乱脚步声在老夫身后戛然而止之时，老夫当即猛然拔高了嗓门与头颅，整个人瞬间陷入了一种近乎癫狂的极度神圣迷醉之中！这一幕毫无悬念地震慑住了这帮质朴愚钝的山野村夫。他们毕恭毕敬地伫足肃立在旁，生怕惊扰了老夫同至高主宰的亲切倾谈。老夫故意装出一副全身心沉醉于天国冥想、对身遭凡尘俗物浑然无动于衷的模样；盖因让他们多等上一阵子，不仅能赢得宗教上的神圣敬畏，亦能确立老夫在红尘世俗里的高超地位。村夫们怀着诚惶诚恐、乃至奉若神明的畏惧心境缓缓凑上前去。老夫从容告知他们：老夫亦正欲奔赴那位至圣高僧的圣祠朝圣，并随口报出了圣人的名号。获悉眼前这位神圣的旅伴竟怀揣着同他们一般无二的崇高信仰，村夫们顿时欣喜若狂！
“‘我们大伙，’他们的头人答道，‘亦是在践行同一项神圣使命！因我们一个月前（1）自村中派出的探路信使捎回了圣讯：圣人恩准接见我们，并将就困扰我们全村日久的野豪猪究竟算不算猪肉、能不能食用的存疑公案赐予最终神谕！’
“老夫在不经意的漫谈中，不动声色套出了我们要去的方位与圣陵的里程。老夫欣慰地获悉此道无需途径任何大都市重镇，且按脚程我们在日落黄昏前便有望抵挡圣地。
“旅途单调乏味，穿越了一片焦灼的荒原，沿途仅有几处凄凉的土坯村落；然而到了傍晚时分，在夕阳斜照的绚烂霞光之中，远处耸立着一座白垩圆顶的小巧陵寝——那是多年前一位已故大圣人的长眠之所；圣陵四周扎着大片大片的朝圣营帐，大批露宿在旷野中的信徒正围坐生火埋锅造饭，驮畜嘶鸣，整片营地沸沸扬扬，昭示着我们终于抵达了今日脚程的终点。
“甫一踏入营盘，老夫便立刻与那帮同行的村夫分道扬镳，钻入了人群最密集喧闹的腹地。老夫特意挑选了一处最引人注目的开阔制高点献上了老夫的晚祷，并故意将礼拜的工夫拖延得异乎寻常的漫长冗长，好教周围新结识的邻居对老夫肃然起敬；随后老夫便往草地上一躺，心中尚在揣摩明日究竟该依循何计破局。
“然而就在梦乡之中，神谕骤然降临了！”

===Original===

“In that dream there appeared a bright and beneficent Being who with one hand was relieving of his superfluous wealth an unconscious pilgrim to his left, and with the other was conferring precisely the same favour on another to his right. Each of the two pilgrims had his face turned away from the Blessed Genius thus engaged and seemed unconscious of the process to which he was subjected. The Glorious Visitant, without interrupting its occupation or ceasing with mechanical regularity to dip its hands into the pouches of its unwitting neighbours, looked upon me with the most benign expression, winked, and disappeared.
“I awoke. It was yet dark. I pondered until dawn what the revelation might mean. With the rising of the sun inner as well as outer light was bestowed upon me. I interpreted in the following fashion the vision that had thus been vouchsafed to me and the event proved me to have divined the right reading by interpretation:
“In every place largely frequented by pilgrims you will, my dear nephews (if your commercial pursuits lead you to such spots in after life), discover two kinds of men. There are those who have already spent their all under the influence of the Spirit and are about to depart. These, being in a necessity to raise a viaticum for their return, will eagerly convert into cash at a vile price such wretched objects as remain to them. On the other hand, those arriving are flush of money and eager to acquire holy relics and memorials of the blessed days before them.
“With two such markets before one’s eyes and clamouring for exploitation, all that is required is a little judgment upon which is which, who is who, and what is what. Such a judgment is essential to any commercial success, but especially to success with people in a state of religious exaltation. For whereas this mood often conduces to folly, it sometimes so supernaturally brightens the intellectual faculties of devotees as to procure most cruel rebuffs for him who attempts to take advantage of it.
“I mixed with my fellow-worshippers. I picked out those whom I judged from their anxious expression, coupled with their preparations for a journey, to be eager sellers.

===Chinese===

“在那场梦境之中，一位神光璀璨、威严慈祥的神人凌空降临！祂探出左手，极其轻巧地从侍立在其左侧一位茫然无知的朝圣信徒腰包里，轻巧抽走了多余的浮财；与此同时，祂又探出右手，以分毫不差的神乎其神手法，向侍立在其右侧的另一位信徒施加了完全同等神圣的恩泽！那两位朝圣者皆背对着这位正在辛勤劳作的福星仙尊，对其自身正在笑纳的神圣洗礼浑然不觉。这位荣耀的仙尊甚至未曾停下手中的活计，亦未曾打乱其机械而规律地伸入周遭懵懂信众钱囊的节拍，只是扭过头来，以世间最慈祥和蔼的微笑端详了老夫一眼，冲老夫狡黠地眨了眨眼，旋即凭空消散无踪！
“老夫猛然惊醒。窗外天色尚黑。老夫在黑暗中苦苦参悟这道天降启示的深奥真意，直到东方破晓黎明初现，内心的灵光伴随着旭日东升骤然大放异彩！老夫以如下大智慧解读了这场神迹梦境，而随后的走向证明老夫的破译堪称绝顶神准：
“凡是在香客云集的大型朝圣狂热圣地，我亲爱的侄儿们（倘若你们日后的商业征途亦涉足此类宝地），你们定能清晰辨认出截然相反的两类凡人：其一乃是那些在神圣狂热的洗礼之下早已将随身盘缠挥霍一空的信徒，他们正收拾行囊准备返乡；由于急需筹措回程的路费盘缠，这帮人必定迫不及待愿以令人咂舌的白菜贱价，将手中残留的微末破烂物什当场变现换成铜板！而另一类，则是刚刚踏入圣地、怀揣巨资且急不可耐企图抢购圣物残片、以纪念未来吉日的神圣信徒！
“面对这两大近在眼前、嗷嗷待哺呼唤着高人前去剥削的绝顶市井行市，凡人所需的一切，不过是审时度势辨明哪类是哪类、谁人是谁人、何物是何物罢了！此等敏锐的分寸感乃是世间一切商业辉煌的立足之本，而在面对陷入宗教狂热癫狂的信徒之时更是至关重要的命脉所在！盖因狂热往往容易诱发痴愚，但有时它亦能不可思议地超自然磨砺信众的智识机锋，以致给那些企图趁火打劫的外行带来最无情残酷的碰壁与羞辱！
“老夫当即混入了祈祷的香客人潮之中。老夫凭着他们脸上浮现出的焦虑惶恐、外加收拾行装的举止，精准锁定了那批急于脱手换钱的返乡卖家。”

===Original===

From these I acquired at prices quite surprisingly small, all manner of objects﻿—sticks, chaplets, sandals, water-bottles, rags, and cords﻿—while I dexterously sheered off the few who seemed too much inclined to bargain, and whom not even a prolonged residence at the shrine had wholly purged of avarice. The remainder were quite sufficient for my needs.
“With a stock thus acquired at the expenditure of not a tenth of my capital, I next proceeded to mix with the more prosperous newcomers, pressing on them now one object and now another (sandals, lace, rags, bits of bone and leather), as somethings of peculiar sanctity, either as dedicated to the shrine of the Dead Holy Man or as having touched the person of the Living One. I discovered a very brisk market indeed, at prices varying from a hundred to a thousand times the original cost of the rubbish. To each bit of rag, bone, or whatnot I was careful to affix a pedigree written in various hands and proving it authentic.
“In these negotiations I was careful, at the least note of suspicion, to pretend a complete indifference; and to one or two more than usually guileful I even made the sacrifice of giving a yard of used cord or a hopelessly worn sandal, remarking as I did so that Sacred Things should not be made a matter of traffic.
“In this fashion I passed four days so absorbed in the interest of the occupation that I quite forgot (it was an error on my part) to present myself within the clamouring line of those who daily demanded the opportunity to fall down before the object of our pilgrimage and to offer him obeisance. It was a mistake which nearly cost me dear, for the Holy Man had his hired watchers among the crowd.
“On the evening of the fourth day, as I was privately counting my gains under a secluded bush where I hoped to be unobserved, I was disturbed by a smart tap upon the shoulder and a summons from an aged but tall and still strong man, armed with a formidable bludgeon. This person bade me follow him without comment, and told me I had been granted the singular favour of personal access to The Master.
“It was with mixed feelings that I accompanied my guide. We elbowed our way through the foremost ranks of worshippers to that inmost place wherein the Holy One communed with his Creator.

===Chinese===

“老夫从这帮落魄信众手里，以令人匪夷所思的极低贱价，大肆收购了各类乱七八糟的破烂玩意——木手杖、念珠、草鞋凉鞋、水葫芦皮水囊、破布条、乃至麻绳头；与此同时，老夫极其老练地甩掉了几个企图斤斤计较讨价还价的刁钻之徒——那帮人哪怕在圣地浸淫多日，骨子里的贪婪亦未曾被彻底净化。剩下的破烂货色已然足够满足老夫的买卖所需了！
“手握这批仅仅耗费了老夫随身资本不到十分之一（10%）采办来的庞大存货，老夫旋即转身钻入了腰缠万贯的崭新朝圣客队列之中；老夫向他们轮番推销兜售着各色法宝器物（凉鞋、蕾丝花边、破布、碎骨头片与熟皮子碎屑），信誓旦旦声称这些皆是沾染了无上神圣气息的绝顶圣物——要么是供奉在已故大圣人陵寝前的通灵供器，要么是亲手触碰过活圣人法体的开光神品！老夫当场迎来了一片火爆狂热的抢购狂潮，老夫开出的售价，往往是这堆破烂垃圾原始进价的整整100倍乃至1,000倍（100、1000）之巨！在每一片破布头、碎骨块或杂物之上，老夫皆极其贴心地附上了一纸由各色不同笔迹墨水亲笔题写的源流谱系保真文卷，证明其血统百分之百绝对正宗！
“在整场斡旋推销之中，只要嗅出哪怕一丝一毫的怀疑端倪，老夫便立刻装出一副视金钱如粪土的彻底超脱神态；面对那一两个格外狡黠多疑的刺头，老夫甚至不惜自掏腰包割爱做大善人，当场无偿赠予其一码旧麻绳或一只彻底磨穿的破草鞋，一边塞给他一边肃穆宣称：‘神圣法器岂容市井铜臭玷污成买卖！’
“老夫以这般出神入化的神技度过了整整4天（4）光阴，整个人彻底沉醉在这桩暴利行当的无上乐趣之中，以致老夫完全遗忘（这委实是老夫的一大疏忽过失）去排入那条每日为了争抢在圣人脚下俯伏叩拜、献上金银供养而喧嚷拥挤的漫长朝圣队伍。这项疏忽险些让老夫栽了大跟头，因活圣人在狂热的人潮之中早布下了重金豢养的暗探耳目！
“在第4天（4）的傍晚，正当老夫躲在一处自以为无人窥见的偏僻灌木丛背后、暗中清点老夫这几日斩获的如山暴利之时，老夫肩头冷不丁挨了一记结结实实的重拍！紧随其后的是一道冰冷的拘传令——只见一位年过花甲却身形高大魁梧、手执一柄骇人重头大木棒的彪形恶汉正居高临下俯视着老夫！那恶汉勒令老夫闭嘴休要声张，乖乖随他前行，并冷冷告知老夫：老夫已蒙受破天荒的无上天恩，获准单独面圣朝见‘尊师法驾’！
“老夫怀着悲喜交加、忐忑不安的心境尾随向导前行。我们在狂热朝圣信徒的最前列队列中硬生生挤开一条通道，径直踏入了那位活圣人闭关同造物主密谈通灵的核心圣所深处。”

===Original===

They envied me and gazed with awe upon one so privileged, but for my part my heart sank lower and lower, and I waited in something approaching panic the interview that had been so graciously afforded.
“I was introduced through a curtain into a low hut, wholly devoid of ornament, built of dried mud and lit only by two small smoky lamps that stood upon the floor. I dimly perceived before me in this half-darkness the figure of a very aged man, incredibly emaciated with prolonged fast and vigil. He was upon his knees with a chaplet in his hands. His eyes were cast upon the ground, which his long but sparse white beard almost touched. He seemed oblivious to all the external world, and was plunged in profound communion with his Maker.
“The attendant in low but angry whispers bade me prostrate myself, which I was not slow in doing; and in that posture I waited for a space of time so tedious that it seemed to me the greater part of one hour. But during all that time I dared not move; for though I had never visited this particular shrine, I had heard tales of what had happened to those who underrate the Unseen Powers. I was relieved at last by a distinct and hollow voice bidding me in measured tones to rise. I arose, and found that we were alone. The attendants had been dismissed by a gesture while my face was yet buried on the ground, and though I have no doubt that they were within earshot, I suffered the added awe of loneliness.
“The Holy One still maintained for a moment his impassive attitude of prayer, then slowly pivoted round on his knees, turned his luminous eyes upon me, and sternly asked me what profit I had made by my infamous trade during the last few days. I felt that all was known to him. I did not (I thank Heaven for its mercies!) attempt to deceive him and thus jeopardize my life and reason. I told him the full tale and awaited his sentence.
“There was a long pause, during which what little was left of my courage ebbed away. I felt prepared to hear some short sentence of doom and resigned myself to my fate. But I had happily miscalculated the serene wisdom which accompanies holiness.
“The Saint spoke next in a more benevolent and softer voice, bade me be seated cross-legged before him, and adopting the same attitude pronounced the following remarkable words:
“ ‘The Just, the Merciful (Whose name be exalted!)

===Chinese===

“周遭的万民无不对老夫投来嫉妒如狂与顶礼膜拜的艳羡目光，视老夫为享有至高神圣特权的宠儿；然而至于老夫自个儿，老夫的心却随着每迈出一步而直往下坠，在一阵近乎濒临灭顶之灾的绝望恐慌之中，战战兢兢静候着这场恩赐召见的下文。
“老夫被引逗着穿过一道厚重门帘，迈入了一间极其低矮简陋、毫无任何雕饰装潢的土坯泥屋；屋内黑灯瞎火，唯有地上搁置的两盏（2）冒着滚滚呛人黑烟的小油灯闪烁着幽微豆火。借着昏暗的火光，老夫依稀辨认出在幽暗的正前方，跪坐着一位行将就木的极老之人，因长年累月的绝食苦修与通宵守夜，整个人已然瘦骨嶙峋、干瘪枯槁到了令人难以置信的骷髅境地。他双膝跪地，手中摩挲着一串念珠；双目低垂死死凝视着地面，那颌下修长却稀疏杂乱的雪白长髯几乎贴着泥土。他整个人仿佛已彻底超脱尘世、对身遭的红尘万象浑然不觉，正深深沉浸在同造物主的神秘沟通之中。
“侍从以极低微却透着凶神恶煞的耳语叱令老夫立刻五体投地！老夫不敢有丝毫怠慢，当场扑通贴地跪伏；老夫以这般卑微的姿态趴在冰凉的泥地上煎熬了漫长岁月，那段光阴在老夫感受中比一整个时辰（1）还要难熬漫长！然而在那漫长的时光里老夫连大气都不敢喘上一口；盖因老夫虽从未踏足过此处圣地，却老早便听闻过那些胆敢藐视无形神明神威之徒的可怖下场。终于，一道清晰而空洞的苍老回声以极其从容节制的语调传来，命老夫平身。老夫直起身躯，惊异地发现屋内仅剩下老夫与圣人二人独处：侍从早趁着老夫把脸贴在泥地里之际，被圣人随手一挥打发了出去；尽管老夫毫不怀疑那帮打手此刻就潜伏在隔壁门帘听壁脚，然而这种死寂的独处反倒给老夫平添了一层无以复加的毛骨悚然。
“活圣人依旧保持着木雕泥塑般的祈祷身姿凝滞了片刻，随后在双膝上缓缓转过身来，将那对精光四射、炯炯有神如明灯般的双眸死死锁定在老夫脸上，板着铁面极其冷酷地质问老夫：在过去这几天里，老夫靠着那套见不得光的无耻勾当究竟捞了多少黑心银两！老夫瞬间心如明镜：老夫的底细早已被他摸得一清二楚了！老夫（由衷叩谢上苍的无量慈悲！）压根不敢在他面前耍弄半点机心花招以自绝生路；老夫当场竹筒倒豆子，如实招供了全盘进账账目，战战兢兢静候着他的发落。
“现场陷入了一阵令人窒息的漫长死寂；老夫心底残存的最后一丝胆魄在这死寂中一点点消耗殆尽。老夫已然闭目做好了引颈受戮的准备，任由命运宰割。然而老夫极其庆幸地算漏了伴随着神圣修为而来的那一抹洞明世事的旷世通透智慧！
“圣人再次开口之时，嗓音竟不可思议地蜕变得格外慈祥柔和；他命老夫在他身侧盘腿席地而坐，自个儿亦调整为同样的坐姿，随后当众吐露了如下一番惊世骇俗的无上哲言：
“‘普慈特慈的公道主宰（愿祂的圣名受万世赞颂！）’”

===Original===

, has given to different men different aptitudes. The fool attempts that of which he is incapable. The wise man recognizes his limitations.’
“In the silence that followed I turned these weighty phrases over in my mind and recognized excerpts from the Proverbs of Mar-Hakim, whose wisdom has been collected by the Persians, and has been famous since the times of the second Omar.
“After a short interval the voice continued:
“ ‘In mutual appreciation and in mutual benefits each acquires profit. The shortsighted forfeit advantage by too much grasping.’
“These words, which were chanted rather than said, I recognized to be from a totally different collection of popular sayings, formerly current in Arabia, and reduced to writing in the first century of Hegira by the learned of Rasht. It was clear that I was in the presence of a man unusually well-informed, and my conviction was confirmed when after yet another solemn pause the voice continued as though in conclusion:
“ ‘In acquiring money there is profit, but in letting it slip there is none at all.’
“This last jewel of wisdom I immediately recalled as part of yet another collection, to wit, the Sacred Books of the Jews; and from this further example of immense erudition my estimation of the Being possessed of it arose to the clouds.
“After such a preface I might have expected further general statements of a moral nature from my host, when suddenly I perceived a total change in the tones of his voice and a similar change in his attitude. He put off the preoccupation of religion. He took on the tone of familiar intercourse proper to temporal affairs. He smiled genially and entered upon a conversation such as one might enjoy in the bazaar of any city or in the private hospitality of any merchant.
“ ‘There are some,’ he said, ‘who would have blamed your conduct; and in a sense I do so; for I cannot excuse your passage of four whole days without a thought of heaven. But then, we are all agreed that the driving of a trade, especially if it be remarkably rapid and lucrative, is a very worthy occupation, and it is one of my regrets that my professional devotion to the Other World has curtailed my own activities in the same line.
“ ‘I am visited by thousands of respectful worshippers. The small amounts of alms which they graciously leave with my treasurer might easily be increased by various commercial activities.

===Chinese===

“‘……赋予了不同凡夫俗子截然不同的天赋异禀。蠢材妄图涉足自个儿力所不及的盲区；唯有大智慧者，方能洞悉并敬畏自身的局限！’
“在这段箴言过后的死寂之中，老夫在脑海中反复咀嚼着这几句千钧重言，旋即认出此乃摘自《马尔-哈基姆箴言录》里的至理名言——这套东方智慧早被波斯饱学之士结集编纂，自第二任哈里发欧麦尔时代起便名噪天下。
“片刻之后，那道苍老的声音再次悠悠响起：
“‘在彼此赏识与互利共赢之中，人人皆斩获实惠；鼠目寸光之辈，终将因贪得无厌而满盘皆输！’
“这番宛如咏叹调般的唱词，老夫旋即认出乃是出自另一部全然不同的市井民谚典籍，昔日盛行于阿拉伯半岛，在伊斯兰大希吉拉历第一世纪由拉什特的鸿儒学者整理付梓。老夫心中惊叹：眼前这位骷髅般的圣僧，委实是一位博古通今、学富五车的绝顶通儒！而老夫的这一判断，在又一段庄严肃穆的沉吟过后被彻底印证了——只见圣人以盖棺定论的总结语调吐露道：
“‘敛聚银钱自当有利可图，任由真金白银白白从指缝溜走则愚不可及！’
“这最后一句智慧的珍珠，老夫瞬间记起乃是出自犹太人的神圣经卷！目睹眼前这位圣者竟坐拥如此汪洋大海般跨越宗派的博大精深学养，老夫对他的敬畏崇拜之情瞬间直冲九重云霄！
“有了如此冠冕堂皇的高深序曲开场，老夫本以为这位东道主接下来定要向老夫大肆灌输一套劝人向善的道德文章；然而冷不丁之间，老夫惊愕地察觉到他的语调神情骤然发生了翻天覆地的剧变！他彻底褪去了宗教神圣的严肃面具，换上了一副专属于红尘俗世利益博弈的市井熟络腔调。他脸上浮现出一抹极其精明的市侩微笑，随即便同老夫扯起了一场你只有在各大都会的巴扎集市或富商私密后堂密室里才能听闻的露骨交易：
“‘坊间某些假道学，’圣人说道，‘或许会对我老人家的行径横加指责；就某种意义而言，老夫自个儿亦颇有微词；盖因你这厮竟敢在圣地整整厮混了4天（4）光阴，脑子里连半点天国神明的念头都不曾动过！然而话又说回来，我们大伙心底里皆心知肚明：经商做买卖，尤其是搞这种周转极快、利润极其暴利的买卖，委实是人世间最体面尊贵的无上营生！老夫平生唯一的憾事，便是老夫这身侍奉天国幽冥的专业神职差事，硬生生捆住了老夫在商海同台竞技的手脚！
“‘每日有成千上万虔诚的信众前来叩拜老夫。他们随喜赏赐给老夫账房账房先生的那点微薄布施碎银，倘若能通过各类高超的商业运作倒腾一番，本该轻松翻上数倍不止啊！’”

===Original===

“ ‘Indeed, I have from time to time attempted to establish such in this camp, but I regret to say with no success. I opened a canteen here but two years ago where refreshments were sold to the new arrivals at from three to five times their value. But the evil servant to whom I entrusted the management of this concern decamped with the whole of the profits. I obtained the satisfaction of having him put to death by a distant friend, but I was never able to recover his ill-gotten gains.
“ ‘At another time I entered into a contract with certain brigands who hold the passes of the mountains. It was clearly understood between us that they should hold up the worshippers returning from my shrine and that one-half of the ransoms they collected should be paid into my chests. But from that day to this I have not received a penny.
“ ‘On yet another occasion it occurred to me that I might fix a regular tribute to replace the voluntary alms which, though considerable, leave room for improvement. But the alarming falling off in receipts and the dwindling of my income made me withdraw the order within six months of its issue.
“ ‘All these experiences combined, my dear Mahmoud,’ said he familiarly (thus showing that he knew my true name and disturbing me not a little), ‘have convinced me that I have not what you men of affairs call “a business sense.” I may have a latent talent, I may even have a genius, for religion. When I tell you that I sometimes pass three days without changing my position of prayer and without taking food or drink, the whole performance watched by a great crowd of astonished faithful, you will agree that I am not without capabilities of my own. But I am reluctantly convinced that what the Giaours call “a good nose for a deal” is not one of them.’
“Here I began to interrupt him with the usual compliments, and would have assured him that any man of ability had but to train himself for affairs to do as well as another, when he genially stopped me with a gesture and said:
“ ‘No, my dear Mahmoud’ (again the use of my name disturbed me), ‘whatever else we are let us not be hypocrites. Let us frankly acknowledge our limitations. You, as I am now convinced, know how to sell and to buy, and have all the qualities for discovering the dearest and the cheapest markets.

===Chinese===

“‘实则，老夫在此处大营里曾屡次亲自试水下场操盘，然而老夫不得不痛心疾首地承认：每一次皆以惨败收场！两年前（2），老夫在此地专程设立了一座官办平价军营大食堂，向刚刚进山抵港的朝圣香客兜售各类高价热汤饮食，售价往往加价到了食材原始工本的3倍至5倍之巨！然而老夫委以重任掌管账目掌柜的那个该死恶奴，竟卷走了全盘净利润彻底潜逃人间蒸发！老夫虽说通过远方的一位藩镇老友设法将那逆贼追捕就地正法斩首，但被他卷走的黑心银子老夫却分文未能追回！
“‘还有一回，老夫同把守大山险要关隘的几路山贼草寇秘密签署了分肥盟约。我们在密室里白纸黑字约定得清清楚楚：由山贼负责半道拦截从老夫圣陵朝圣折返的香客肥羊，收缴的绑票赎金由老夫同他们五五平分！然而打从那一天起直到今日，那帮天杀的绿林强盗连半文铜板都没给老夫奉送上来过！
“‘在另一次试水之中，老夫琢磨着索性立下规矩明码标价征收定额进香人头税，以取代随喜赏赐的自愿布施——随喜布施虽说数目可观，但终究有极大的挖潜空间嘛！然而此令一出，前来朝圣的香客人潮断崖式暴跌，老夫的账房进项直线下坠，吓得老夫在政令颁布不足6个月（6）之内便仓皇将其撤回废止！
“‘凡此种种惨痛教训加在一起，我亲爱的马哈茂德啊，’圣人极其熟络亲昵地称呼道（他这随口叫破老夫真实姓名的神通，委实把老夫吓出了一身冷汗），‘已然彻底向老夫证明：老夫骨子里压根缺乏你们生意人所谓的“商业嗅觉”！老夫在装神弄鬼通灵侍奉天神上或许有些潜藏的天赋，甚至堪称旷世绝顶奇才——老夫不妨向你交个底：老夫有时能保持同一种叩拜跪姿整整3天3夜（3）水米未进，任由满场聚集的浩瀚信徒在台下吓得目瞪口呆肃然起敬！单凭这一手绝活，你便当承认老夫手头亦是有两把硬刷子的！然而老夫不得不极其遗憾地承认：异教徒所谓的“做买卖的狗鼻子”，老夫身上是半点没有啊！’
听到此处，老夫赶忙习惯性地搬出市井奉承客套打断他的话头，刚欲拍胸脯保证任何有大智慧之士只要稍加点拨历练定能成为商海巨擘，圣人却和蔼可亲地挥手打断了老夫的谄媚：
“‘行啦，我亲爱的马哈茂德，’（他又一次叫破老夫的名号，令老夫背脊发凉），‘咱们明人不说暗话，休要在此假惺惺演戏！咱们务必坦坦荡荡正视各自的短板。正如老夫此刻所确信的那样：你这厮精通低买高卖之道，浑身上下长满了精准搜寻普天下最暴利行市与最廉价源头的绝顶狗鼻子！’”

===Original===

Much as I have desired to attain to the same faculties I have failed, and at my age (which, though it is not the 110 years reported, is at any rate well over sixty) it is too late to change. I will therefore make you what is called, I believe, in your world, an offer.’
“(With what relief did those words fall on my ears! I did not realize for the moment how greatly it was to his advantage to have begun by frightening your unhappy uncle and what an opportunity this had given him for negotiations!)
“ ‘I will make you a proposition. Think carefully over it, and at the end of a reasonable time give me your decision. I offer you two alternatives. The one is that you should continue your trade subject to the supervision of my agents, and that when you have reached a total of 1,000 pieces of gold you shall be impaled and the money confiscated. The other is that you shall continue to use your evident talents for the furtherance of this trade, but that I shall be regarded as a sleeping partner in the same, with half the takings. The choice lies with you.﻿ ﻿… Pray, pray take your time. Undue haste has spoilt many an excellent business contract, and I would not have you ruin your chances. Do not,’ he continued, repressing my evident anxiety to accept his terms, ‘do not let your judgment be prejudiced by any feeling of obligation. Think the alternatives over carefully and then let me know your conclusion. Take your time.’
“Restraining too great an evidence of haste, I told him that my mind was already made up and that I would be honoured to accept his second offer.
“ ‘I think, Mahmoud,’ said he, rising, ‘that you have acted with wisdom, though perhaps with a little precipitation. Let us, then, regard the matter as settled. Every evening my servants will call upon you for one-half of your takings, and they meanwhile will protect you in every way.’
“I prostrated myself once more, kissed the ground at his feet, and left the hut in a very different mood from that in which I had entered it.
“I remained in the camp for the matter of about a month. I extended my operations; and every evening the servants of the Holy One attended me and I handed over half of my takings. During the whole of that period my capital continued to increase prodigiously.
“But no good endures forever. The time came when this even tenor was threatened in a very unexpected way.

===Chinese===

“‘老夫虽说做梦亦渴望坐拥这等通天神技，但老夫终究屡战屡败；且念及老夫当下的高龄（老夫虽说未曾活到坊间讹传的110岁（110），但满打满算无论如何亦早已年过60（60）花甲了），此时改弦更张学做生意为时已晚。因此，老夫意欲向你开出一份依老夫之见在你们商界称之为“商业要约”的提议！’
（这番话音刚一落地，在老夫耳中无异于天降仙乐！老夫一时之间甚至未能咂摸透：眼前这位老谋深算的圣僧在开局时将老夫吓得魂飞魄散，究竟给他自个儿在随后的商业谈判中平添了何等无坚不摧的先手筹码！）
“‘老夫给你开出两套任选方案，你且在心底仔细掂量权衡，在充裕的时限之后给老夫一个准话。其一：你继续在老夫的暗探眼皮底下操办你的假圣物暴利营生，待你累计斩获的真金白银达到整整1,000枚金币（1000）大关之日，由老夫下旨将你当场施以穿刺死刑插在木桩上暴尸，全部赃款没收充公！其二：你继续发挥你肉眼可见的经商奇才全力将这门买卖做大做强，而老夫自此充当这项产业的隐名合伙人，坐享全盘流水的一半红利干股！生与死、全由你自个儿决断！……切记，切莫仓皇做决断，慢慢思量！市井商界无数绝妙的契约皆毁于轻率仓促，老夫可不愿看着你白白断送了自个儿的大好前程！切莫，’看着老夫迫不及待刚欲抢答应承，他伸手按住了老夫，‘切莫让任何道德负担左右了你的理性商业研判！把这两条道好生在脑子里过上一遍，届时再告知老夫你的最终定案。慢慢想，不急！’
老夫死死压抑着心头如释重负的滔天狂喜，毕恭毕敬向他启奏：声称老夫心意已决，能笑纳圣僧老人家的第二套方案乃是老夫毕生至高无上的荣幸！
“‘老夫自忖，马哈茂德，’圣人飘然起身，含笑赞许道，‘你做出了极其睿智的决断，尽管略显仓促了些。既然如此，这桩大事便就此一锤定音！自今夜起，每日傍晚老夫的亲信管事皆会按期登门提走你当日进项的一半真金白银；而在此期间，老夫的人马将在方方面面为你提供万无一失的绝对铁壁庇护！’
老夫当场再次五体投地，狠狠亲吻了他脚下的泥地；待迈出那间土坯茅屋之时，老夫胸中的气象同进门前相比委实已是云泥之别！
老夫在那座朝圣营盘里继续盘桓了约莫一个月（1）光阴。老夫的商业版图疯狂拓张；每日黄昏圣人的侍从如期登门，老夫毫不迟疑按期奉上一半的营运流水。在整整那段岁月里，老夫名下沉淀截留的资本以骇人听闻的神速疯狂膨胀！
然而世间无不散之筵席，花无百日红。这般稳如泰山的聚宝盆，终究在某一天迎来了始料未及的致命风暴！”

===Original===

“The Holy One was visited by certain ambassadors from the Grand Something or Other residing in the court of the Caliph, who informed him that his position was duly recognized by the authorities, and that they bore with them an illuminated charter confirming it. The temporal advantages of His Holiness’s trade, however, were no less clearly evident to the Caliph than the religious ones, and His Holiness would therefore be good enough in future to hand over one-half of his receipts to the imperial treasury.
“Heaven knows with what bitterness the Holy Man agreed; which, having done, he sent for me again and told me that it was now necessary for him to ask me for three-fourths of my receipts. In vain did I point out to him that all great empires had fallen by the increase of taxation. He was adamant, and I therefore reluctantly agreed to the new arrangement; taking a solemn oath to observe it for at least one year. But I asked him whether at the expiration of that time, in case I should find the new bargain more than I could support, I might depart out of the city? To this he agreed, and confirmed it with an oath equally solemn.
“That night I put together all my accumulated wealth (which now filled not less than four large bags with gold and silver) and charging it upon the mule of a peculiarly devout and therefore unobservant and abstracted pilgrim, and drawing the innocent beast away in the darkness by the bridle, I left the camp as slowly and cautiously as I might.
“Emissaries were sent out to kill me within half an hour of my departure. As I heard their approach I turned my mule round towards the camp as though I were arriving, and as they passed me, I said I was a pilgrim who had lost his way in the night and asked if I were on the right road for the shrine. This simple ruse deceived them. They went their way and I was alone once more. Still, their passage sufficiently alarmed me. I gave up the road for a less frequented path and wandered all that night through an unfamiliar district, for my poor beast could go no faster than a walking pace, so heavy were the bags of treasure which he bore.

===Chinese===

“某日，活圣人的营帐迎来了一批来自哈里发皇廷枢密院核心内阁的特命全权钦差大臣；钦差长官宣称朝廷正式承认了圣僧在宗教界的合法地位，并当场颁赐了一轴用五彩泥金精心彩绘的皇家敕建特许状诏书！然而朝廷长官话锋一转：宣称圣人在红尘俗世中所斩获的滚滚世俗暴利，在哈里发王上眼中同样是一清二楚、毫不逊色于其神圣宗教功绩的聚宝盆；因此，自诏书颁布之日起，圣僧必须老老实实将全盘进项的一半真金白银，按期上缴归入朝廷国库大内公帑！
苍天在上，唯有天庭方知那位圣人是在何等剜心般的仇恨与怨怼之下勉强在敕令上画押应承的！待钦差前脚刚迈出大门，他后脚便火急火燎将老夫召入密室，黑着铁面斩钉截铁地告知老夫：鉴于朝廷横征暴敛，自今日起他必须从老夫的全部进项中强索抽走整整四分之三（75%）的巨额红利！
老夫向他苦苦进言，引经据典痛陈自古以来多少庞大帝国皆亡于无度加征苛捐杂税的无底深渊；然而圣人铁石心肠丝毫不为所动！老夫只得无可奈何吞下这枚苦果，当众发下毒誓承诺至少在整整一年（1）之内严格恪守崭新的分肥契约。然而老夫在盟誓之余探问了一句：待这一年大限届满之后，倘若老夫发觉这笔苛刻的重税实在难以维系，老夫是否能获准自行收拾包袱离开这座圣地？圣人满口应允，并以同样庄严的神圣誓言确立了这一约定。
就在同一天深夜，老夫将这些时日积聚的全部惊天财富悉数打包（足足装满了整整四大袋（4）沉甸甸的金币白银！），将其暗中驮在了一位格外虔诚、因而神思恍惚目空一切的朝圣信徒名下的一头健硕骡子背上；老夫在漆黑如墨的夜色中悄无声息牵起那头无辜牲口的缰绳，以极尽轻巧审慎的步伐，悄然溜出了这处圣地营盘！
然而不出老夫拔营半个时辰（0.5），圣人派出的追魂索命暗杀死士便已呼啸出动！当听闻身后荒原上传来急促的夺命马蹄声之际，老夫灵机一动，当场将骡头猛然调转向圣地营盘的方向，装出一副正欲赶赴圣地朝圣的模样；当那队黑衣死士纵马从老夫身侧呼啸掠过之际，老夫故作惶恐地高声打探：诓称自个儿乃是在夜色中迷路的孤苦香客，探问眼前的官道究竟是不是通往圣陵的正途？这套简明拙劣的市井小把戏当场瞒天过海！死士们骂骂咧咧纵马疾驰而去，原野上再次唯独剩下老夫孤身一人！
然而这场虚惊着实把老夫吓得魂飞魄散。老夫当机立断弃绝了平坦官道，钻入了一条人迹罕至的荒芜野径，在完全陌生的穷山恶水之间整整摸黑跋涉了一整夜；因那头可怜的牲口背上驮着的金银财宝实在太沉重了，整整一夜它唯能以蜗牛爬般的沉重步伐艰难蹒跚前行。”

===Original===

“By dawn I felt myself secure, and﻿—”
But here the old gentleman heard the first intolerable note of the muezzin and stopped short, motioning to his nephews that they should leave him, which they did with their customary humility, each wondering in his heart whether he might not later feel a vocation to the Religious Life.

===Chinese===

“直到黎明破晓时分，老夫方才心头大定，认定自个儿彻底逃出生天，并且——”
然而讲到此处，这位老先生耳畔响起了宣礼塔上传来的第一声令人无法容忍的刺耳唤礼尖啸；老人戛然而止，挥手示意侄儿们退下。少年们依循惯例毕恭毕敬鞠躬告退，每个人心底皆忍不住暗自嘀咕：自个儿日后长大成人，究竟要不要亦遁入空门去体验一番神圣的宗教生涯呢？

## VI *Al-Wukalá*, or “The Lawyers” / 第六章 律师
===Original===

When the nephews next entered their uncle’s presence at the hour of public executions, it was in a subdued manner, as to a funeral, for their thoughts were full of that great loss, the story of which was in progress. They sat upon the floor before him in due order, and Mahmoud began:
“Upon the dawn that followed that hopeless night, my hopes were again raised, only for my further bitterness and disappointment. I had risen before day and gone out of doors. A chance acquaintance ran across me as I paced aimlessly in the narrow streets of the city, watching the shadows shortening under the rising sun, listening to the clear voices of the water-sellers and to the cries of the mariners at their calling.
“This acquaintance was one learned in the law. Not that he was himself a scrivener, or pleader, still less a judge﻿—on the contrary, he was born wealthy, and those so circumstanced are (in Izmat) very much averse to the tedium of a profession. But in youth he had been compelled by his grandfather to read whole libraries of books upon the legal system of his beloved country, and had further been compelled to pay considerable sums to one of the most renowned pleaders of the day in whose office he had passed three miserable years. Seeing he was so educated, and knowing well my misfortunes, he kindly took me by the arm (I could not help suspecting a certain patronage) and said:
“ ‘Alas, my poor Mahmoud! How we do all feel for you! And how we respect the way in which you have borne inevitable misfortune! But though I praise you as much as any other for your conduct and resignation, do you not push it a little too far? We, the free inhabitants of this our beloved Izmat, have a most glorious privilege, which is, that not the king himself (the glory of Allah be upon him) has any privilege as against the humblest of his subjects, when it comes to the issue of law. Our judges, as you know, stand above all mortal frailty and are, as we devoutly and firmly believe, filled with the spirit of God Himself. Though His Majesty and his ministers be your opponents in a case, that case will be decided with serene indifference to the position or wealth or power of the parties. You believe this, do you not?’ he insisted earnestly, for doubts upon so final a doctrine of religion are horrible to the imperial race of Izmat.

===Chinese===

次日行刑斩首的时刻，侄儿们再次来到叔父跟前。这回大家神情肃穆低落，宛如去参加一场葬礼，因为少年的心头全被上一回讲到一半的那桩惨重破产变故给填满了。他们依序在叔父面前席地而坐，马哈茂德便开了腔：
“在那绝望之夜过后的破晓黎明，我的希望之火一度重燃，结果却只是让我尝到了更深沉的苦涩与失望。天未破晓我就起了床，踱步来到屋外。晨曦微露，我漫无目的地走在城中逼仄狭窄的小巷里，看着阴影在初升红日下逐渐缩短，耳中听着卖水人清脆嘹亮的叫卖声，以及水手们在码头各司其职的呼号，就在这时，我迎面撞见了一位熟人。
“这位熟人精通律法。倒不是说他自个儿是个代书书记官或诉讼辩护士，更谈不上是个法官——恰恰相反，他出身豪门巨富，而生在这样家境里的人（在伊兹马特【Izmat】）向来极度厌恶从事任何枯燥乏味的职业营生。然而他在少年时，曾被祖父逼着通读了整座书库有关他挚爱祖国法律体系的典籍，后来又被迫给当年最负盛名的一位大律师缴纳了数额不菲的束金，在那位大律师的事务所里熬过了三（3）年悲惨岁月。鉴于他受过如此深厚的法学熏陶，又对我遭逢的厄运心知肚明，他便十分热络地挽起我的臂膀（我忍不住疑心其中带着几分居高临下的施舍意味），开口说道：
“‘哎呀，我可怜的马哈茂德！我们大伙儿对你的遭遇是多么感同身受啊！你承受这不可抗拒的灾祸时展现出的隐忍风度，又是多么令人敬佩！不过，虽说我和旁人一样赞许你的品行与顺从，但你是不是未免顺从得有些过头了？我们这片挚爱的伊兹马特，每一位自由子民都享有一项至高无上的荣耀特权：一旦诉诸法律，即便是国王陛下本人（愿真主的荣耀与他同在），在面对哪怕最卑微贫贱的臣民时，也绝无半分特权可言。如你所知，我们的法官超然于一切凡夫俗子的弱点之上；正如我们虔诚而坚定地确信的那样，他们的胸膛里充盈着真主亲临的神圣灵光。即便在诉讼案子里同你对簿公堂的是国王陛下及其内阁重臣，法庭也必将以泰然自若的超脱态度进行裁决，绝不在意涉案各方的地位、财富或权势。你也是深信这一点的，不是吗？’他言之凿凿、言辞切切地追问道；要知道，在伊兹马特这支帝胄贵胄民族眼里，对这般至高无上的宗教信条产生丝毫怀疑，都是大逆不道的恐怖罪愆。”

===Original===

“ ‘Yes, I believe it,’ said I with a sigh, though I confess that my short acquaintance with misfortune had shaken me in many points of loyalty to my new country.
“ ‘Why, then,’ said he, ‘do you not come into court with your plaint? Our lawyers have the skill to discover a claim in anything, and you may be sure that all that can be said in your favour would be allowed in the freest manner, and if there is a single loophole, the whole or part of your former fortunes may be restored at whatever cost. For it is a maxim peculiar to our island law that if a case is decided in favour of the plaintiff, then the plaintiff has the decision in his favour.’
“I confess that my acquaintance with the manners and habits of the foreign people among whom I had had the wretched luck to be born had made me think it futile indeed to approach the august judges in a matter where a king was concerned, or to ask relief from state officials for what the state had done to me. He stoutly denied the idea I had that the judges were state officials. Said he: ‘Have you never read the famous oath which every judge makes on taking office? Do you not know how we elect him?’
“ ‘Yes,’ said I, in tones which betrayed no enthusiasm, ‘I know indeed how the judges are appointed and the oath they take. They are appointed﻿—(how often have I not seen the firman signed in council! How often have I not affixed my own poor name to it!)﻿—they are appointed, as the rule goes, “by His Sacred Majesty, that is, by the familiars, the wives, and the secretaries of the richest men, indifferently.” Such are the very words of the statute.’
“ ‘You answer rightly,’ said my friend, with a noble carriage of his head. ‘Surely so impartial a source of office must make you feel secure! On the odious mainland the king appoints his own judges: it is a tyranny to which we in Izmat have long refused to submit. *Our* monarch is the ruler of free men! He would disdain to influence an appointment. He leaves that to his gentry, and they, in turn, leave it to their women and other dependents. Thus we alone of all nations secure a bench of judges wholly independent! But there is more: Have you forgotten the oath they take, my friend﻿—the oath they take on appointment?’
“ ‘No,’ said I, still wearily. ‘I remember it well enough.

===Chinese===

“‘是的，我深信不疑，’我叹了口气应道；不过老实承认，这短短一段横遭横祸的经历，已然让我对这个新国家的忠诚在许多方面产生了动摇。
“‘既然如此，’他说道，‘你为何不上公堂呈递诉状呢？咱们这里的律师手段高超，任何鸡毛蒜皮里都能给你掘出起诉的由头来；你大可放宽心，凡是于你有利的说辞，公堂上都会给予最充分的申辩自由。只要能找出一处法律漏洞，哪怕付出天大代价，你的旧日家产全部或一部分都能物归原主。因为咱们海岛律法有一条独一无二的至理名言：倘若一桩诉讼裁决原告胜诉，那么胜诉判决就归原告所有。’
“我坦白承认，由于我命途多舛，投胎降生在外邦异族的风土人情之中，那种耳濡目染让我觉得，凡是牵涉到国王的纠纷，去找那些尊贵的法官根本是白费心机；国家加诸我身上的冤屈，指望国家命官来替我主持公道更是痴人说梦。但他坚决驳斥了我把法官视作国家命官的浅薄念头。他反问道：‘难道你从未读过每一位法官走马上任时所立下的那份著名誓词吗？难道你不知道咱们是怎么把他们遴选出来的？’
“‘读过，’我声音里听不出半点热情，答道，‘我着实清楚法官是如何任命的，也清楚他们立下的誓言。按规矩，他们的任命——（有多少回我曾在枢密院亲眼看着敕令签署啊！又有多少回我自个儿那卑微的名字也签署其上！）——按成文律法白纸黑字所言，乃是“由神圣的国王陛下任命，亦即由最富有的显贵豪门之心腹亲信、妻妾姬妾以及机要秘书们不拘一格共同酌定”。法规的原话正是如此。’
“‘你答得一点不错！’我这位朋友昂起高贵的头颅说道。‘源头上如此不偏不倚的官职来源，定然能叫你心安理得！在那些可鄙的大陆各邦，法官全凭国王自个儿乾纲独断；那是咱们伊兹马特人早就誓死不从的专制暴政。咱们的君王乃是自由臣民的统御者！他不屑于插手干预任何司法任命。他将这大权委诸他的贵绅缙绅，而贵绅缙绅又转手交托给他们的妇道人家及其他依附食客。正是借由此道，唯独咱们伊兹马特能在万国之中确保整座法官席位享有百分之百的彻底独立！然而还不止于此：我亲爱的朋友，难道你忘记了他们就任时所立下的誓词吗——那份就职誓词？’
“‘没忘，’我依旧疲惫不堪地说道，‘我记得清清楚楚。

===Original===

Indeed, I have it all by heart, for I have read it a hundred times:﻿—“I swear by the Almighty God and by the contents of this book that I will not depart from justice in anything, either for orders, or favours, or personal advantage, or any consideration whatsoever, save in the political interests of my class or family, of the Lawyers’ Guild to which I belong, or for such other considerations as may occur to me.” ’
“ ‘That is right!’ said my friend in triumphant tones. ‘Well, can you want a better guarantee?’
“ ‘No,’ said I, ‘I suppose not.’
“ ‘Well, then,’ he cried, rising, ‘let me take you to a friend of mine among the most able scriveners of the city, and be assured that whatever can be done for you will be done.’
“ ‘I have no great part of my fortune left,’ said I timidly, rising as he did, but unwilling to follow him.
“ ‘Fear nothing,’ he returned heartily. ‘Justice in Izmat is not bought and sold. There are, of course, certain necessary fees. But the law compels you to hire no pleader. You can appear yourself in court. That freedom is one of our great privileges; and, believe me, you will be heard as patiently and directed as honestly as though you were one of the greatest pleaders in the city.’
“Half persuaded by such insistence, I followed my friend to a house where, seated in the midst of commentaries upon the law, of metal boxes containing the shameful secrets of great families and the record of their indebtedness, sat an elderly man, whose face reminded me, I know not why, of a vulture.
“ ‘I have brought you, Kazib,’ said he, ‘a client. You will recognize him, I think.’
“ ‘I do, indeed,’ said the scrivener, rising gravely and bowing to me. ‘He is no less than my lord the councillor Mahmoud.’
“ ‘The title is superfluous now,’ said I a little sadly.
“The scrivener, however, continued to give it to me in his great courtesy, and when my friend had left us together, I poured out my story. As the more important details fell from my lips my host jotted them down upon a small tablet with a fine quill that he carried. When I had concluded he spoke as follows:
“ ‘Such a case as yours would appear first in the Court of Sweetmeats.’
“ ‘Of Sweetmeats?’ said I.
“ ‘It is an old term,’ said he. ‘We love these historic traditions.’
“ ‘Exactly,’ I answered humbly.
“ ‘Well, it would appear, I say, in the Court of Sweetmeats.’
“ ‘Yes,’ said I.

===Chinese===

我甚至能倒背如流，因为我少说读过了一百（100）遍：‘我向全能的主真诚起誓，凭依本书所载内容发愿：老夫在任何公案审理中决不背离公道正义，无论面临何种命令、恩惠、私利或任何思量考量，概不例外——除非这关涉到老夫所在阶级或宗族的政治利益、老夫隶属的律师公会之利益，亦或是老夫灵机一动所想到的其他考量。’
“‘正是如此！’我的朋友得意洋洋、语调昂扬地喊道。‘试问，世上还能有比这更固若金汤的保障吗？’
“‘确实没有，’我答道，‘我想大概是没有了。’
“‘既然如此，’他霍然站起身来喊道，‘且让我带你去找我的一位朋友，他是全城最老练精明的诉讼书记官之一；你放一万个心，凡是能替你周旋的，他定会全力以赴。’
“‘我手头的家当已经所剩无几了，’我胆战心惊地跟着站起身来，心里却十分不情愿随他前去。
“‘莫慌！’他爽朗地答道。‘在伊兹马特，公道与正义是绝不能拿来买卖交易的！当然，某些必不可少的规费终归要交。但是法律绝不强迫你雇请诉讼辩护士。你完全可以亲自出庭应诉。这份自由是咱们的一大特权；相信我，堂上听你陈述时的耐心，给你的指引点拨之诚恳，绝不会亚于对待全城名头最响的大辩护士。’
“在他这般苦口婆心的劝说下，我将信将疑地跟着他来到一处宅邸。只见屋里堆满了浩繁的律法注疏，四周摆满了大大小小的铁皮匣子，里面锁着各大世家望族的丑恶隐私与借贷欠条；在这些卷宗铁匣正当中，坐着一位上了年纪的老者，不知何故，他的面孔竟让我想起了一只秃鹫。
“‘喀齐卜（Kazib），’我的朋友说道，‘我给你带了位主顾来。我想你该认得他。’
“‘当然认得，’书记官神色严肃地起身向我躬身致意，‘这不正是枢密顾问官马哈茂德大人嘛。’
“‘如今这头衔大可不必再提了，’我略带伤感地说道。
“然而书记官出于极大的客套，仍旧一口一个大人地称呼我。待我那位朋友告辞离去、只留我二人独处后，我便将自个儿的遭遇竹筒倒豆子般和盘托出。每当我嘴里吐出至关重要的细节，主人便用随身携着的一支细羽毛笔，飞快地记在随身的一块小写字板上。待我叙述完毕，他慢条斯理地开口道：
“‘像你这样的官司，首先得进糖果法庭（Court of Sweetmeats）。’
“‘糖果法庭？’我愕然道。
“‘这是古称，’他答道，‘我们最钟情这些历史悠久的古雅传统。’
“‘原来如此，’我谦卑地应诺道。
“‘好吧，我是说，这案子首先会提交给糖果法庭。’
“‘明白了，’我说。”

===Original===

“ ‘After it had gone to the Court of Sweetmeats, it would almost certainly be transferred to the Court of Wrecks, Lighthouses and Divorce, or to the Department of Wills.’
“ ‘Indeed?’ said I.
“ ‘It is so,’ said the scrivener. ‘Whichever of these dealt with it, an appeal would lie, of course, to a superior court, which is generally known as the Court of Mules.’
“ ‘Why is﻿ ﻿…’ I began.
“ ‘Oh, sir!’ interrupted the scrivener, with some impatience, ‘these things are immaterial! We must use such historic names.﻿ ﻿… From this court again the appeal would lie, of course, to His Majesty in council, which is the supreme authority of the land.’
“ ‘What,’ said I, ‘to His Majesty in council﻿—the very authors of the injustice?’
“ ‘Of course,’ said the scrivener.
“ ‘But,’ said I, ‘if the verdict is in my favour, what reason should I have for appealing?’
“ ‘None,’ said he, simply. ‘But your opponents would.’
“ ‘My opponents are the king and his council,’ said I.
“ ‘In quite another aspect,’ said the scrivener, looking on the ground and falling silent. ‘Under these circumstances,’ said he, after a pause, ‘you will do very well to proceed.’
“ ‘But,’ said I, ‘in case these appeals﻿ ﻿…’
“He waved his hands. ‘We will not talk of appeals yet,’ he said quickly. ‘After all, you lose nothing by first instance. Have you your charter with you?’
“I said I had, and brought it out for him. He read it slowly, consulted a book for a moment, and then said:
“ ‘An excellent case!’ (you may judge, my dear nephews, how my heart leapt at these words!) ‘An excellent case!﻿ ﻿… You have your charter and its terms are clear. By it there is bound to be paid to you and your heirs forever the revenue from the Salt Tax, and the issue will lie, I think, upon whether the clause implies payment undiminished and perpetual, or whether the recent proclamations make a gumbo-rumbo of the original.’
“ ‘A what?’ said I.
“ ‘It is a legal term,’ said he, a little wearily, ‘and signifies a mixalum-malory or general contortation.﻿ ﻿… But let us not at this stage go into technicalities of that sort. We must first state our case.’
“ ‘Precisely,’ said I.
“ ‘My own fee,’ said he, ‘is fixed by statute, and I must ask you for ten dinars﻿—a nominal matter.’

===Chinese===

“‘从糖果法庭出来之后，这案子几乎铁定要转送到海难沉船、灯塔与离婚法庭（Court of Wrecks, Lighthouses and Divorce），要不然就是遗嘱检验司。’
“‘当真？’我问道。
“‘千真万确，’书记官答道。‘不论上述哪家接手，判决之后当然都可以向上级法庭提出上诉，那家上诉庭通称为骡子法庭（Court of Mules）。’
“‘为何叫……’我刚要开口。
“‘哎呀，老爷！’书记官带着几分不耐烦打断我，‘这些枝节根本无关紧要！咱们必须沿用这些历史沿袭的名号！……打这所法庭再往上，上诉自然要呈递给御前枢密院（His Majesty in council），那可是咱们这片土地上的最高裁判权力机构。’
“‘什么，’我脱口而出，‘呈递给国王及其枢密院——恰恰是炮制这桩不公冤案的正主？’
“‘那是自然，’书记官说道。
“‘可是，’我说，‘倘若初审判决我胜诉，我又何必去上诉呢？’
“‘你自然不必，’他轻描淡写地答道，‘但你的对手会上诉啊。’
“‘我的对手正是国王和他的枢密院大臣们啊，’我说。
“‘那完全是另一副面孔与法律人格了，’书记官垂首盯着地面，陷入了片刻沉默。顿了顿，他又说道：‘在眼下这般情势下，你启动诉讼是再明智不过的抉择。’
“‘可是，’我说，‘万一这些层层上诉……’
“他挥了挥手。‘眼下先别扯什么上诉，’他飞快地说道。‘无论如何，打初审你又吃不了亏。你的特许敕令带在身上吗？’
“我答复说带着，便取出来递给他。他一字一句慢条斯理地读了一遍，翻阅了一会儿案头的典籍，随后断言道：
“‘天衣无缝的胜诉铁案！’（我亲爱的侄儿们，你们尽可想象我听见这几个字时心里是何等狂喜！）‘无可挑剔的铁案！……你有特许状在手，条款字字清晰。按其规定，盐税（Salt Tax）的所有岁入收益必须永世不绝地支付给你及其后嗣；依我看，本案的争讼焦点在于，该条款究竟是指收益须毫无克扣且万世不竭地支付，还是说朝廷近期的那一纸诏书已然将原件搞成了一团乌七八糟的乱麻（gumbo-rumbo）。’
“‘搞成了什么？’我愣住了。
“‘这是一句法学行话专有名词，’他显得有些倦怠地解释道，‘意指将原本条文彻底曲解歪曲、搅成一锅稀巴烂的混沌状态。……不过现阶段咱们不必深究此类专业术语。咱们首先得把诉状由头确立起来。’
“‘诚然如此，’我说。
“‘至于老夫自个儿的代书劳务费，’他说道，‘乃是法定的，老夫只需收取十（10）个第纳尔——权当象征性的微薄劳金。’”

===Original===

With this, before I could stop him, he seized a large metal disc, wet a corner of a parchment, put the disc upon it, struck it with a hammer, and then held out his hand for the fee. Luckily I had my pouch with me, and so, very reluctantly, paid over this first drop of my disastrous leakage.
“ ‘Good!’ said the scrivener. ‘We must next ask the opinion of two eminent pleaders.’
“ ‘Why?’ said I.
“ ‘The law demands it,’ said the scrivener.
“ ‘But you have already given me yours, and told me it is worth my while to proceed.’
“ ‘My opinion,’ said the scrivener, shaking his head vigorously, ‘may serve to guide you, indeed; but it would be altogether irregular to go into court upon that alone. So, I will draw up a statement, as we call it, and have it put before two men of the first standing﻿—it is always better in these cases to use the highest talent. In the long run it is worth while.’
“I asked timidly how much this further step would cost, and was somewhat relieved to learn that fifty dinars to each of these eminent men would be sufficient. He asked me to return on the third day when he would give me the responses; and he particularly reminded me that I should upon that occasion not forget to bring with me at least 150 dinars.
“ ‘But why the other fifty?’ said I.
“ ‘Stamps and fees,’ said the scrivener shortly, and then, with infinite courtesy, dismissed me from his presence.
“On the third day I returned, bearing with me the 150 dinars from my little hoard, which I put upon the scrivener’s table to save all further difficulties in the matter. He poured the money meditatively into a little metal case, beautifully engraved, and dating, I should say, from the second century of the Flight of the Prophet; it was probably (to my practised eye) of Syrian workmanship.
“ ‘Here,’ said he, ‘are the responses.’
“ ‘Have you them written?’ said I.
“ ‘No,’ said he. ‘We must wait for that. The first pleader, by name the Most Noble Ghadder, is of the opinion that you have a case founded upon the great principle of our Common Law, of which you, perhaps, as a foreigner, have not heard﻿—it is the principle that “The subjects of the king can suffer no wrong.” But he warns you against relying upon the Statute passed in the first year of His Majesty’s father’s reign, called “A Statute for the Prevention of the Loss of Money by the Rich.”

===Chinese===

不等我出言阻拦，他抓过一枚巨大的金属圆印，舔湿了一角羊皮纸，将圆印按在上面，抡起锤子狠狠砸了一下，随后摊开手掌讨要劳金。幸好我随身携着钱袋，于是极不情愿地掏出了这笔大破财的第一滴血。
“‘很好！’书记官说道。‘下一步，咱们必须征询两（2）位顶尖诉讼辩护士的法律意见。’
“‘这是为何？’我问。
“‘法律明文规定，’书记官说。
“‘可是您刚才不都已经给出意见，并说值得打下去吗？’
“‘老夫的意见，’书记官猛烈地摇晃着脑袋说，‘诚然可以为你指引方向；但单凭这一纸意见就想跨进公堂，那可就彻底坏了规矩纲纪。因此，老夫将起草一份陈述状，递交给两（2）位德高望重的顶流大律师——打这种官司，向来是请最顶尖的法学权威才最稳妥。长远来看，这绝对物超所值。’
“我怯生生地打听这一步还得耗费几何，得知每位权威大律师只需区区五十（50）第纳尔酬劳时，我心头稍觉宽慰。他叮嘱我在第三（3）天再来听取答复；并且特意关照我，届时千万别忘了随身至少带上一百五十（150）第纳尔。
“‘那另外的五十（50）第纳尔又是作甚？’我问。
“‘印花税与公堂杂费，’书记官简明扼要地吐出几个字，随后便以无限周全的礼数将我打发出了大门。
“到了第三（3）天，我如约返回，从微薄的私蓄里掏出一百五十（150）第纳尔，拍在书记官的案几上，省得再起波折。他若有所思地将银钱倒进一只雕工极尽精美的金属小盒子里；依老夫内行的眼光看，那盒子约莫出自先知迁徙纪元第二（2）个世纪，多半是叙利亚匠人的手艺。
“‘拿去吧，’他说，‘这便是辩护士们的答复。’
“‘可有书面文书？’我问。
“‘还没有，’他说。‘那还得等等。第一位诉讼辩护士，乃是尊贵的加德尔（Ghadder）大人，他认为你的案子牢牢依托于咱们普通法（Common Law）的一项崇高法理原则；你是个外邦人，或许此前未曾耳闻——那便是“国王的臣民绝不受委屈”原则。但他告诫你，切莫指望依据先王陛下登基第一（1）年颁布的那部法令，亦即《防止富人钱财亏损法》。”

===Original===

For this has been the subject of so many contradictory decisions that it is a very poor ground. He says, further, that there are certain case precedents which are interesting, and two of which, at least, in his judgment, could be urged upon your side. In one of these it was decided that if a man had more than a certain income no order or proclamation should be regarded as capable of prejudicing him or reducing his wealth. The question would thus lie as to whether or no at the time when the first change took place you fell within these limits. The second pleader is of an exactly opposite opinion. He says﻿ ﻿…’
“ ‘It is enough!’ said I. ‘The first learned pleader shall be my guide. I am content to hear from him that I have a good case, which doubtless he will continue for me in court.’
“ ‘What?’ said the scrivener, in astonishment, ‘do you suppose that such men lend their valuable services in court for fifty dinars?’
“ ‘Evidently,’ said I, ‘as they have been so kind as to give opinions with such nicety!’
“The scrivener laughed as heartily as such men can, and begged me to be disabused. ‘These are but the formal stages. The hiring of pleaders is quite another matter. Let me proceed to the second response. The second pleader, the Most Noble Makhar﻿—who, it may interest you to know, is a negro by descent﻿—we have many such at our bar, they have marvellous abilities, that strange race﻿—is of an opinion, I say, exactly contrary to the other. He thinks your chance under the Common Law very doubtful. But he thinks you are secure under the Statute passed for the Prevention of Cruelty to the Important﻿—that is its general title﻿—dated from the first year of the reign of His Majesty’s late father. All the cases, he says, are against you; but the general principle of the statute stands.’
“When I had heard all this I said, ‘Oh!’
“The old scrivener gazed down at the floor between his feet, where he sat upon his rug, and I gazed down at mine, not knowing what next to say.
“ ‘My fee for this second interview,’ said he pleasantly, after what he thought was a sufficient interval, ‘is the same as for the first, ten dinars.’
“ ‘I have not brought them with me,’ I said, having only brought the 150 dinars previously asked for.
“ ‘It is nothing, my lord,’ he answered, waving his hand majestically (and again, I thought, as in the case of my friend, with a touch of patronage).

===Chinese===

“因为关于这部法令已有太多自相矛盾的判例，拿它当依据根本靠不住脚。他进一步指出，倒是有若干颇有趣味的判例先例，依他卓见，其中至少有两（2）桩能在公堂上为你所用。其中一桩判例认定：倘若一个人的岁入超过特定数额，任何政令或敕令皆不得被视作能够损害其利益或削减其财富。因此本案焦点便在于，初次政策变动之时，你是否属于此等收入阶层。而第二位辩护士的意见则截然相反。他认为……’
“‘够了！’我说。‘我就以第一位饱学辩护士的指引为准好了。得知他断定我胜券在握，老夫心满意足，想必公堂之上他也定会继续替我据理力争。’
“‘什么？’书记官大惊失色，‘难道你以为这等显赫的大人物，肯为了五十（50）第纳尔就屈尊出庭为你效劳？’
“‘照理理当如此呀，’我说，‘既然他们都如此好心、给出了这般精辟透彻的指点！’
“书记官发出了此类市侩文人所能发出的最畅快笑声，劝我趁早打消这等不切实际的幻想。‘这些不过是例行公事的文书过场罢了！真要聘请大律师出庭辩护，那完全是另一码天价开销。且听我把第二份答复念完。第二位辩护士，乃是尊贵的马哈尔（Makhar）大人——顺便提一句你兴许会感兴趣的事，他拥有黑人血统；咱们律师公会里多的是这等人才，这个奇特的种族天生具有神乎其神的辩才天赋——我说过，他的高见与前一位针锋相对。他认为你指望普通法纯属凶多吉少。但他认为，依先王陛下在位第一（1）年颁布的《防止虐待显要人物法》——这只是俗称统称——你稳操胜券。他说所有判例都对你不利；但该项法令的大原则依然巍然屹立。’
“听完这番宏论，我唯能吐出一声：‘啊！’
“老书记官盘坐在毛毯上，低头凝视着双脚之间的地面，我也垂头盯着自个儿的脚尖，不知接下来还能搭上什么话茬。
“‘老夫这第二（2）次面谈的劳务费，’待他觉得冷场的时间已经够久，便和颜悦色地开口道，‘和头一回一样，十（10）个第纳尔。’
“‘我身上没带这笔钱，’我说，因我只带了先前吩咐好的一百五十（150）第纳尔。
“‘无妨，大人，’他高贵地一挥手答道（正如我那位朋友一样，我再次察觉出了一丝居高临下的优越感）。”

===Original===

‘We all know and respect your position of late councillor, and I should be the last to press you.’
“I begged him to wait a moment until I should return. I hurried to my wretched lodgings and quickly came back with the sum which he required. He put it into the little metal box. I thought he was ready to dismiss me, and I was about to ask on what date the case might first be tried, when he said, to my surprise:
“ ‘But we must first have Pleader’s Opinion.’
“ ‘But, great Heavens!’ said I, ‘have we not got it?’
“ ‘Why, no,’ said he. ‘We have not yet a Pleader’s Opinion. We have, so far, only the Opinion of Pleaders.’
“ ‘And what in the name of Fatimeh and Katisha is the difference?’
“ ‘Surely,’ said he, ‘you have heard of the distinction? The Opinion of Pleaders is the verbal response to the scrivener, but the law requires that another response shall be added in writing, and this we call Pleader’s Opinion.’
“Once more I could find no other commentary but a cry.
“ ‘And at what cost?’ I moaned in a hollow voice.
“He turned to a written list of fees, then to a special memorandum of his own. He made a short calculation upon an abacus and answered, ‘three hundred dinars.’
“I kept my mouth from blasphemy and asked him when the sum would be required.
“ ‘It is a mere formality,’ said he, ‘this written opinion, but we must have a record.’
“ ‘Yes, yes,’ said I.
“ ‘And I will,’ said he, ‘take the opportunity of obtaining the same before you come again.’
“Once more I returned to my disgusting rooms, took money from my secret hoard and, returning, put into the scrivener’s hands a little parcel of 300 dinars. He dropped them thoughtfully through his fingers in little streams till they nearly filled his metal box.
“ ‘It is a pretty box, is it not?’ he said. ‘I took it for a bad debt from one of my clients, who most unfortunately died by his own hand in a fit of melancholy after the most distressing disappointments in his suits at law.’
“ ‘And as to the date?’ I said.
“ ‘The date?’ Once more he consulted another document, then clapped his hands for the slave who sat in his outer apartment, and having asked him a question in some incomprehensible jargon, received an answer no less mysterious. Then he turned to me and said: ‘It will come on at some date after the next New Moon but one.’
“ ‘Cannot I have a precise date?’

===Chinese===

“‘咱们大伙儿都深知并敬重您身为前枢密顾问官的尊崇地位，老夫最不愿做的事便是逼债催逼。’
“我央求他稍候片刻，容我取钱。我急匆匆跑回凄惨的寓所，飞快带回了他索要的数额。他将银币收进那只金属小盒里。我原以为他这就该端茶送客了，正打算询问官司定在哪一天开庭初审，不料他却出乎意料地开口道：
“‘不过，咱们首先还得拿到一份《辩护士意见书》（Pleader's Opinion）。’
“‘天老爷啊！’我失声叫道，‘难道咱们刚才拿到的不是吗？’
“‘哎呀，当然不是，’他说。‘咱们手里还没有《辩护士意见书》呢。到目前为止，咱们有的仅仅是“辩护士们的意见”（Opinion of Pleaders）。’
“‘凭法蒂玛（Fatimeh）和卡蒂莎（Katisha）的名义起誓，这两者之间到底有个鬼的区别？’
“‘想必您早该耳闻这层微妙玄机吧？’他说。‘所谓“辩护士们的意见”，不过是向书记官作出的口头答复；但律法严正要求，必须补充一份书面形式的正式答复，这在律法上才被称为《辩护士意见书》。’
“我再次无言以对，唯能从胸腔里挤出一声哀鸣。
“‘那这又要花多少银两？’我用空洞干瘪的嗓音呻吟着问道。
“他翻了翻案头的一份收费名目清单，又核对了他自个儿的一本密记手札，在算盘上噼里啪啦拨弄了一番，答道：‘三百（300）第纳尔。’
“我强忍着没破口咒骂亵渎神灵，只是问他这笔巨款何时得缴清。
“‘不过是走个例行公事的过场罢了，’他说，‘这份书面意见，不过是为了公堂备案备查。’
“‘行吧，行吧，’我说。
“‘那么老夫便，’他说道，‘趁你下次登门之前，替你将这份书面文书办妥。’
“我又一次折返回那间令人作呕的破败寓所，从秘密私蓄里抠出银钱，赶回来将一小包计三百（300）第纳尔的银币塞进书记官手里。他神态安详地任由银币从指缝间如溪流般滑落，直装得那只金属小盒快要溢出来。
“‘真是个精致的宝盒，不是吗？’他说。‘这是老夫从一位主顾手里抵充坏账收来的；那可怜人打官司吃尽了令人心碎的败仗，在极度抑郁成疾之下，极其不幸地悬梁自尽了。’
“‘那么究竟哪天开审？’我追问道。
“‘开审日期？’他又翻检了另一份公文，随后拍了拍手，唤来候在外间屋里的黑奴仆役，用某种外人根本听不懂的隐语黑话吩咐了几句，对方亦用同样玄虚莫测的暗语回了话。接着他转头对我说道：‘约莫排在下下个新月之后的某个日子吧。’
“‘难道就不能定个确切的日子吗？’”

===Original===

said I, for I was thinking anxiously of my diminishing capital and wondering how long I could maintain my poor life before my cash should be completely exhausted.
“ ‘That is obviously impossible,’ he answered with a touch of indignation, which he evidently thought merited. ‘No man can say how long the cases before yours will last, nor in what order it may please His Holiness the Judge to take them. It will probably come, by the way,’ said he, peering at another list, ‘before His Holiness Benshaitan.’
“With that I left him and waited my call to the court.
“The time dragged wearily enough. I ate most sparingly and bought no raiment, nor even any game to pass my time, but my little stock dwindled day by day. My hours were spent gazing upon the busy life of the port, or sometimes standing on the edge of the pier and staring out to sea, as though I could read in the distant mainland beyond the horizon some hopes of a better fortune and a life restored. Daily, as the time approached for my case to be heard, as the second New Moon grew with every evening brighter in the last of its crescent, I consulted officials of the court. I discovered it was customary to give each some small sum of five or six dinars before they would answer a question. It was upon the twelfth day, the moon being nearly full, that the case before mine (which turned upon whether a man without means should owe for a debt or should postpone payment, and had lasted five exciting days,) was concluded. I heard that the precedent created was of the first importance, but would be subject to appeal.
“The sun sank. It was with the morrow that my case was to be called.
“I rose on that eventful day long before dawn. I put on my raiment with the utmost care, after having cleansed it with my own hands to make it as presentable as possible, lest the poverty of my appearance should in some way prejudice me. I had already given notice that I would appear myself; for the fees asked by the pleaders were quite beyond the poor remnant of my purse. I must confess that I had been strongly dissuaded from such action, but I had no choice. I found a great crowd assembled, for my name was familiar to all through the position I had enjoyed in the past; and it is ever of an absorbing interest to watch the miseries of another.
“I took my seat at a place reserved for me immediately opposite the bench.

===Chinese===

我追问道，因为我正忧心忡忡地盘算着自个儿日渐干涸的盘缠，琢磨着在银两彻底耗尽之前，我这条贱命究竟还能支撑多久。
“‘那显见是不可能的，’他带着几分自以为理直气壮的义愤答道。‘谁也说不准排在你前头的那些案子得审多久，更料不到尊贵的法官老爷高兴按什么顺序提审。顺便提一句，’他眯起眼睛端详着另一张名册，‘你的案子很可能会由尊贵的本沙坦（Benshaitan）法官老爷亲审。’
“听完这话，我只得告辞离去，苦苦等待法庭传唤。
“日子熬得漫长而煎熬。我省吃俭用，不敢添置半件衣裳，更不敢花半文钱买任何玩意儿消磨时日，然而我的那点积蓄依旧一天天见底。我终日呆望着海港里熙熙攘攘的人流，或偶尔伫立在码头栈桥的边缘极目远眺大海，仿佛能从地平线尽头那片遥远的大陆上，窥见重振家业、重获新生的微茫希望。一天天过去，开审的日期渐行渐近，随着第二（2）个新月的残钩每晚愈发丰满明亮，我四处打探法庭的书吏差官。我发现这里有个规矩：每向差官打听一句话，按例得塞上五（5）到六（6）个第纳尔的碎银，否则人家根本懒得搭理。直到第十二（12）天，月轮近乎浑圆之际，排在我前头的那桩案子（争讼焦点在于一个身无分文的穷光蛋究竟该背负债务还是该无限期延期偿付，此案整整唇枪舌剑激辩了五【5】天之久）终于尘埃落定。我听说该案确立的判例至关重要，但注定还得提起漫长的上诉。
“残阳西沉。翌日便轮到我的官司开堂提审了。
“在那命运攸关的一天，我早在大清早破晓前便翻身而起。我亲自动手浆洗熨烫衣裳，极尽细致地穿戴整齐，力求收拾得体面一些，免得寒酸落魄的外表让法官对我先入为主心生鄙夷。我早已向公堂递交了声明，由老夫亲自出庭自辩；因各大辩护士开出的天价束金，早已远远超出了老夫羞涩的囊底。坦白讲，此前曾有人极力劝阻我莫走这步险棋，但我实乃别无选择。我赶到法庭时，发现门外早已黑压压聚满了围观民众，因我昔日身居高位显赫一时，全城无人不知我的名号；而旁观他人的凄惨落魄，向来是市井看客们百看不厌的无上乐事。
“我在正对着法官席位为我预留的席位上坐了下来。”

===Original===

I noted on my right the pleaders chosen by the council, and beyond, among the spectators, not a few of my former colleagues.
“The pleaders were arranged in the vestments proper to their great function, resembling the priests of a religion, and bearing upon their heads, I noticed, what they never showed outside, a strange headgear of mule skin with twisted hair and long, furry ears. The judge, I saw, was clothed in the most magnificent cloth of gold, inscribed with the sacred texts and loaded with furs of such rare animals as the sacred Rat, the white Jackal of Tibet, and the Skunk, and bore upon his head a crown, which he lifted three times as a salute to the court, while all fell prostrate before him, murmuring in a buzz of low, prayerful whispers their praise and incantation to him as the representative of God.
“These ceremonies concluded, there was a bustle of men rising and taking their seats upon the rugs of the court, the judge himself upon a sort of throne overlooking the whole, and the proceedings began.
“A short man stood in front of the judge’s throne, who rose and piped in a shrill voice, ‘Mahmoud claims against the king.’ He then sat down, and from sundry pushes and jerks which I received from my friends, including the scrivener, who was kind enough to accompany me to the court, I saw that I was expected to rise from my carpet and put my case.
“I said: ‘Your Holiness and Voice of God’ (for such is the formula required, as a kind friend had warned me: and if one word be omitted the culprit is not only forbidden to plead but is thrust into a dungeon of the most noisome kind). ‘Your Holiness,’ I opened, ‘and Voice of God. I had from the king and his councillors a charter. It was given me as against one million dinars paid to them in gold by me on such and such a date. I shall show the charter to you, and you will see there the promise that I am to receive, as against the payment I made, the revenue from the Salt Tax forever and ever, so long as the state shall endure and the tax be levied. This tax has been in great part remitted, and by special imposts the remaining part has been wiped out. I claim that this charter gives me the right to the original revenue from the state in full.’ And I then concluded with the magic formula with which my friends had very kindly provided me, ‘and that, Divine One, is my case.’

===Chinese===

我瞧见右手边坐着枢密院指派的众位辩护大律师，再往外看，在围观的看客群中，赫然还立着不少老夫昔日的枢密院同僚。
“只见辩护士们身披彰显其崇高司职的庄重法袍，活脱脱宛如某个宗教的祭司主教；我注意到，他们头上还戴着在公堂之外绝不轻易示人的奇特冠冕——那是用骡皮缝制、缀着卷曲发丝、耸立着两只毛茸茸长耳朵的怪异头饰。至于法官，只见他身披极其富丽堂皇的金织锦缎，上头绣满了神圣经文，还缀满了各色罕见异兽的珍贵皮毛，诸如通灵圣鼠皮、西藏白豺皮以及臭鼬皮；头顶上戴着一顶宝冠。他恭敬地将宝冠抬起三（3）次向公堂致意，台下所有人立刻全体伏地跪拜，嘴里发出嗡嗡低语，如念咒般虔诚祈祷，将这位代行神权的大老爷奉若神明在人间的化身。
“此等典礼告一段落，堂下一阵骚动，众人纷纷起身在公堂毛毯上落座，法官老爷则端坐在居高临下俯瞰全场的一座宝座之上，庭审正式拉开帷幕。
“一名矮个子差役肃立在法官宝座前，只见他霍然起身，用尖厉刺耳的嗓音高声唱喏：‘马哈茂德控告国王案！’随后便坐了下来。这时，身旁的朋友们——包括那位好心陪我出庭的书记官——纷纷在背后戳我、推我，我心领神会，明白该轮到我从地毯上起身陈述诉求了。
“我开口道：‘至圣至尊的神明法官老爷、上帝在世间的金口玉言！’（因好心朋友早就告诫过我，公堂上有这套死板套话；倘若漏掉半个字，不仅当场剥夺申辩权，还得被投入最阴森恶臭的死牢。）‘至圣至尊的老爷、上帝的金口玉言，’我朗声陈述道，‘老夫曾从国王及其枢密大臣们手中受领过一份特许状。那是老夫在某年某月某日以足足一百万（1,000,000）第纳尔真金白银如数奉上为代价换来的。老夫自当呈递特许状御览，上头白纸黑字写得明明白白：作为老夫支付该笔巨款的对价，老夫世世代代、永永远远享有盐税（Salt Tax）的一切岁入收益，与国同休，直至国祚终结、盐税废止。然而如今这项税赋大半被朝廷豁免免除，剩余部分又被巧立名目的特别摊派给冲抵殆尽。老夫诉求，依照此特许状，朝廷必须全额偿付老夫原初享有的全部盐税收益！’言罢，我按照朋友们好心教我的通关密咒作结道：‘以上陈词，天神在上，便是老夫的全部案由！’”

===Original===

This formality completed, I sat down.
“I flattered myself I had done well, for all had been told with perfect ease, and, after all, there was nothing more to be said. But before I took my place, cross-legged upon my carpet, I handed up to those who served the Bench my original of the charter, signed and sealed, the which I had consulted. The judge rose from the throne where he was seated, put the charter down carefully upon his throne, sat upon it, and ordered the case to proceed.
“In the chief of the pleaders upon the other side, I was pleased to see an old guest of mine. He nodded to me familiarly, rose, and opened his statement, beginning, as I did, with the ritual phrase. ‘Your Holiness and Voice of God,’ said he, ‘His Majesty and council have instructed me. I admit that this is a case of peculiar subtlety and difficulty, nor do I doubt that it will employ many of my colleagues, not only in this court but in superior courts, for many months to come. Indeed, to my certain knowledge, one of them has recently purchased a marvellous vehicle which travels rapidly of its own act without horses, a foreign invention, in which he would not have invested had he not foreseen the lengthy and lucrative nature of the case. But that is by the way. I only mention it in order to make Your Holiness understand that we have here to settle an issue which indeed could hardly have been brought before any judge less divine than yourself.’
“There followed for about a quarter of an hour a fine passage upon the majesty of the law and the peculiar gifts and virtues of the judges. But through the whole of it he insisted in every second sentence upon the gravity of the case and its difficulty. I was flattered and surprised. I had not thought that my opponents would make so much of me; but, on the other hand, I remembered that their payment was at the charge of the public and that every day added to the sum they received. He next touched upon the folly of the Salt Tax, its iniquity, its old-fashionedness, its absurdity, and after an hour of this paused a moment to pull down and smooth the long furry ears of his headgear.
“In the second hour he brought in the very words of the charter. He first recited them, ‘forever and forever, so long as the state shall endure and the salt tax be paid.’ He insisted, with repeated emphasis, upon the word ‘*and*.’

===Chinese===

走完这套过场，我便坐了下来。
“我心中暗自得意，自以为表现得可圈可点，因为一切陈词流利自如，毕竟该说的事理全都交代清楚了。不过在回到地毯上盘腿坐下之前，我毕恭毕敬地将那份加盖印信、签署画押的特许状原件呈递给法庭执事。只见法官从宝座上直起身来，小心翼翼地把特许状铺在自个儿屁股底下的宝座上，一屁股严严实实坐了上去，随后下令庭审继续。
“在对方辩护团的首席大律师阵营里，我惊喜地认出了一位曾经常在寒舍作客的故交。他冲我熟稔地颔首致意，霍然起身发表答辩，同我一样，起手式也是那套死板的套话咒词。‘至圣至尊的老爷、上帝的金口玉言，’他启奏道，‘国王陛下与枢密院已全权委托老夫出庭。老夫必须承认，此案精妙绝伦且错综复杂，老夫毫不怀疑，不仅在这座公堂上，即便是未来在上级法庭里，它也足以让老夫的众多同僚在此后数月乃至数年里案牍劳形、大展宏图。实不相瞒，据老夫所知，老夫的一位同仁近日便刚购置了一辆无需马匹便能自行疾驰的神奇车驾，那是一桩海外奇淫巧技之发明；若非他早就预见到了此案的旷日持久与油水丰厚，他是断断舍不得下这笔血本的。不过这只是题外话。老夫之所以提及此事，无非是想让法官老爷明鉴：咱们今日所要裁夺的这桩是非，普天之下除了如您这般圣明通神的法官之外，恐无凡人能够消受处理。’
“紧接着整整一刻钟的时间，他洋洋洒洒大肆赞美律法的庄严神圣，极尽谄媚之能事歌颂法官老爷的天纵之才与高风亮节。但在整篇宏论里，他几乎每隔两（2）句话就要强调一次此案的千头万绪与旷古之难。这让我受宠若惊又大为诧异。我万没料到对手竟会如此把我当成头等大事来办；但转念一想，我便恍然大悟：他们的天价律师费全由国库公款报销，公堂上每多耗上一天，进他们腰包的银子便多滚出一笔。随后，他话锋一转，开始痛斥盐税（Salt Tax）的荒谬绝伦、祸国殃民、陈腐不堪与滑天下之大稽，这般滔滔不绝痛骂了一个时辰后，他稍作停歇，抬手拽了拽头上那顶皮帽耸立的毛茸茸长耳朵，将其抚弄平整。
“到了第二（2）个时辰，他终于引述到了特许状的原始条文。他先是煞有介事地高声诵读道：‘永永远远，与国同休，只要国祚绵延，盐税得以征缴。’随后，他咬住那个连词‘以及（and）’，反复咬文嚼字、大做文章。”

===Original===

In the third and fourth hours he quoted 150 instances of cases in which this word had completely changed the character of a document, as, for instance, in the famous case known as Abraham’s Will, where the testator left all his property to his beloved wife, Fatimah, *and* the remainder to her mother. Next, he quoted the case known as the ‘Degree of Dignity,’ when it was ordered that all those apprehended for speaking disrespectfully of the Grand Mufti should be brought into his presence *and* decapitated. Again (what interested me very much, for it was connected with Money), the terms of the statute, now over one hundred years old, by which the councillors of the king receive one dinar per day *and* whatever other sum they see fit to vote themselves out of the taxes.
“It was the word ‘*and*,’ said he, that made the difference in all these cases. He might call witnesses to show that the word was inserted in the charter to render the phrase abortive, absurd, nonsensical and altogether of no effect. But, alternatively, supposing that the word ‘*and*’ but confirmed my case in the decision of His Holiness, then he pleaded that the charter, having been obtained by a stranger, not a subject of the king, was null and void. Supposing that it were upheld in spite of this, then, alternatively, that I, Mahmoud, was a subject of the king, a native born, and therefore subject to the king’s decisions in council. Finally, he concluded that in any case I must not win because, if I did, it would make His Majesty’s council and members thereof look like a fool severally and collectively, than which no more deplorable thing could happen to the state. Further, even if His Holiness should decide that it mattered not a rusty nail whether the council were made to look fools or no, there was, anyhow, no money to pay me. This established a default contumax and a discharge in alias of the second degree. I give his exact words, for I noted them at the time, and could guess vaguely that they must be of grave import. When he got so far I noticed a great commotion among his colleagues. Every man in court wore an expression of strained attention mixed with admiration, and the judge himself could not withhold from his august features something of the same tribute to this genius of debate.

===Chinese===

在第三（3）和第四（4）个时辰里，他一口气罗列了一百五十（150）个判例，用以证明这个词如何能彻底颠覆整份公文的法律效力；譬如人尽皆知的‘亚伯拉罕（Abraham）遗嘱案’，立遗嘱人在遗嘱中将名下全部产业留给他挚爱的妻子法蒂玛（Fatimah），‘以及（and）’将其余剩余财产留给岳母。接着，他又引述了著名的‘尊严等级案’，该判令规定凡是因对大穆夫提（Grand Mufti）出言不逊而被捕之人，一律押送至大穆夫提御前，‘并且（and）’斩首示众。再后来（这桩最合我的胃口，因它直接关乎金钱利益），他引述了一部已逾一百（100）年历史的陈年成文法令，该法规定国王的枢密顾问官每日领取一（1）第纳尔津贴，‘以及（and）’他们自个儿认为合适、能从国库税收里给自个儿表决拨付的任何其他巨款。
“他说，在所有这些判例中，正是这个‘以及（and）’字起到了翻云覆雨的决定性乾坤逆转。他本可以传唤证人出庭，以证明当初特许状里塞进这个字眼，纯粹是为了让整条文书化为泡影、沦为荒诞不经的废话而彻底失效。但是，退一步讲，即便圣明的法官老爷裁定这个‘以及’字反而印证了原告的诉求，那么被告方亦要抗辩：这份特许状乃是由一个外邦异乡人取得的，并非国王陛下的子民，因而自始至终无效作废。退一万步讲，即便即便如此该状依然有效，那么被告方还要再退一步抗辩：我，马哈茂德，乃是国王陛下的归化子民、本土土著，因而必须无条件服从国王枢密院的一切决议敕令。最后，他总结陈词道：无论从哪个角度看，原告都绝无胜诉之理；因为倘若原告胜诉，岂不是让国王陛下的枢密院及其全体大臣，在个别与集体层面上统统沦为蠢不可及的白痴？对整个国家而言，天底下绝无比这更可悲可耻的浩劫了。此外，即便圣明的法官老爷铁面无私地判定，枢密院沦为跳梁小丑哪怕连一根生锈的烂铁钉都不如，国家左右也是断断掏不出半文钱来赔付我的。这便构成了法理上的‘抗传藐视违约’（default contumax）以及‘二等化名免责脱责’（discharge in alias of the second degree）。老夫之所以能一字不差地复述这些专有名词，皆因老夫当时亲笔记录了下来，并隐隐觉察出它们定然重如千钧。当他讲到此处时，我注意到他的同僚阵营里爆发出一阵剧烈的骚动。公堂之上的每一个人脸上，都浮现出紧绷专注与五体投地的赞叹神色；甚至连高坐宝座的法官老爷，那威严的面容上也忍不住流露出一丝对这位辩论奇才的由衷激赏。

===Original===

“ ‘Note also, Your Holiness,’ continued the pleader, wagging his arched forefinger (which was long and pointed) very significantly in the air, ‘the contumax in advert to subvert﻿ ﻿… and the same regardant.’ He added in a sort of sneering tone: ‘I will not weary the court with that’ (I could see that the judge nodded), ‘but even the plaintiff, learned as he is in the law, will admit,’ and here he turned and addressed me with a very contemptuous expression, ‘that plevin would not obtain in the case of recognizance, or at any rate in the defection thereof would be docketed as an endorsement pursuant. An endorsement pursuant would stand void,’ he continued, with a renewed interest in his tone (he now excited a feverish attention in his audience), ‘for that is in the very foundation, I take it, of our law of terce and perinomy and has been upheld by a long succession of your Holiness’s predecessors from the origins of our Sacred Lawyers’ Guild.’
“Here again I thought I noticed the trace of an uncertain nod from the august figure upon the bench. ‘It comes, therefore,’ concluded this eloquent man, ‘in plain words, to this: we rely on the terms general, and the reference particular, each interconnected, and certainly maintain that guaranty lies overt.’ Here he stopped dead, and then added in simple and lower tones: ‘That is my case.’ Then he sat down. I am told it was one of the most marvellous efforts in all the history of the Lawyers’ Guild.
“Applause may be permitted even in the Mosque or the most sacred of Shrines, but not in the august presence of the law. Yet it was with difficulty that the enraptured pleaders present, the scriveners and their attendants, could forbear from open praise. A man whom I did not know and who sat next to me, cross-legged, upon his mat, one of the pleaders, I think (for he also wore the mule skin with long, furry ears upon his head), muttered to me that it was the finest opening he had heard since Achmet had opened for the Sheik-ul-Musrim in the Oyster Case, and that was saying a great deal.
“When this great pleader had sat down there was a complete silence in court, which lasted for some time and seemed to me a little embarrassing. At last I perceived that I was in some way the object of too much attention, and my friend the scrivener leant over with the suggestion that I should call my witnesses.

===Chinese===

“‘法官老爷还请留意，’大律师继续高谈阔论，在半空中极其玄奥地晃动着修长尖锐的弯曲食指，‘针对颠覆的反向藐视抗辩……以及附随监护权。’他带着某种轻蔑讥诮的口吻补充道：‘老夫就不拿这些细枝末节来劳烦法庭了’（我看见法官赞许地点了点头），‘但即便是原告本人，鉴于他也曾受过高深的法学熏陶，想必也得承认，’说到此处，他扭头望向我，脸上挂着极其鄙夷的神情，‘在具结保释之情形下，财产返还担保令绝难成立；即便成立，在此等不履行之过失下，亦必当被归档注记为附随背书。而附随背书依律自当归于无效！’他语调中重新迸发出狂热的兴致（此时全场听众已听得如痴如狂），‘因依老夫愚见，这乃是咱们寡妇产特权法与周边旁系继承法的立论根基所在，并由历代圣明法官老爷的前辈先贤们，自咱们神圣的律师公会肇造伊始便代代恪守奉行不悖！’
“讲到这里，我隐约又瞧见宝座上那位威严的大老爷带着几分含混地点了点头。‘因此，’这位口若悬河的大才子总结道，‘归结为一句大白话便是：吾等依托于概括性条款，并参照特定之援引，彼此环环相扣互为表里，因而坚决认定：保证责任业已昭然若揭！’说到此处他戛然而止，随后用平实而压低的嗓音补充道：‘以上便是老夫的答辩！’言毕，他端坐回毛毯上。旁人后来告诉我，这是全律师公会史册中最登峰造极、惊天地泣鬼神的神来之笔。
“即便在清真寺或至圣神祠之中尚且允许偶有喝彩喝道，但在庄严神圣的国法殿堂之上却决计严禁喧哗。然而在场的全体辩护士、书记官及其随从侍役们个个听得如痴如醉，几乎按捺不住要当庭失声赞叹。一位我不相识、盘腿坐在我邻近草席上的男子——想必也是个辩护士，因他头上同样顶着一顶拖着毛茸茸长耳朵的骡皮帽——凑过来压低声音对我嘀咕道：自从当年艾哈迈德（Achmet）在‘生蚝案’中为穆斯林谢赫（Sheik-ul-Musrim）发表开庭辩护以来，这绝对是他所听过最绝妙绝伦的开篇陈词，而这赞誉的分量可谓重若千钧。
“待这位大律师落座之后，公堂之上陷入了一片死一般的沉寂；那沉寂持续了许久，让我觉得颇有些尴尬不安。终于，我察觉到四面八方的目光全齐刷刷聚焦在我身上，而我身旁的那位书记官朋友则凑过身来，悄悄示意我该传唤自个儿的证人出庭了。”

===Original===

‘But I have none,’ whispered I over my shoulder in great trepidation. ‘I have my charter. That is enough, is it not?’
“The scrivener shrugged his shoulders as though in despair, and left me to my fate.
“Then was it that I heard the voice of the great judge booming into my ears. ‘What evidence is there for the plaintiff?’
“I rose trembling. ‘I have given you all I have, Your Holiness.’
“ ‘You have given me none,’ thundered that tremendous personage. ‘All you have done is to make an opening plea.’
“ ‘I thought,’ stammered I, ‘that I had stated all that I had to state.’
“The judge glanced round at his fellow lawyers with a look of despair, then leaning forward, with a sort of tenderness in his tone, he said: ‘Be good enough to mount the Sacred Stool reserved for the witnesses.’ With that a little block of wood was brought forward, and upon it I mounted, and so stood conscious and foolish before the court.
“His Holiness the Judge leaned back on his throne and surveyed me with the contempt I deserved, nor did he repress the little titter that ran through the assembly. An official squatted in front of the throne put a scroll into my hand, bade me put it to my forehead and repeat after him certain words, the sense of which I lost in my perturbation. But I did as I was bidden. After that I remained dumb. ‘Well,’ said His Holiness, sharply, after a long pause, ‘how much longer are we to wait?’
“ ‘Pray, your Godship, what would you have?’ said I.
“ ‘I would have your evidence,’ said the judge.
“ ‘I have no evidence to give,’ I tremblingly replied, ‘save what you have already heard.’
“ ‘I have heard none,’ said he, and again the titter went round the court.
“Moved to action, I repeated exactly what I had said before, that the words were what they were in the charter, the clause was what it was. I repeated it point by point.
“The judge turned to the pleader who had just sat down and said: ‘Now, brother Selim,’ whereupon my former friend and guest rose, looked me up and down in a very offensive manner from head to foot three or four times, and cried:
“ ‘You drunken scoundrel! Do you still maintain the abominable falsehood which you have had the insolence to lay before the court?’
“I said there was no falsehood, but the truth.
“ ‘The truth!’ he sneered.

===Chinese===

“‘但我压根就没有任何证人啊，’我扭过头，战战兢兢地低语道。‘我手里有特许状，这难道还不够吗？’
“书记官绝望地耸了耸肩，任由我自生自灭。
“就在此时，法官老爷那洪钟般的巨响猛然灌入我的耳膜：‘原告可有任何证据呈堂？’
“我浑身发抖地站起身来：‘老夫能呈交的都已呈交了，法官老爷。’
“‘你何曾呈交过半点证据！’那位显赫的大人物厉声咆哮道。‘你适才所作所为，不过是一篇开庭陈词罢了！’
“‘老夫原以为，’我结结巴巴地分辩道，‘老夫该交代的都已交代清楚了。’
“法官满脸绝望地环视了一眼周围的律师同僚，随后身子前倾，语气中带着某种假惺惺的慈悲说道：‘那就劳驾你登上专门留给证人的神圣木凳吧。’话音刚落，一截小木块便被搬到了堂前；我只得硬着头皮踩了上去，就这般众目睽睽之下傻乎乎地鹤立鸡群，沦为全场的笑柄。
“法官老爷重新靠回宝座，用一种看待理所应当的鄙夷眼神上下打量着我，任由台下爆发出一阵嗤嗤窃笑而不加制止。一名蹲坐在宝座前的差役塞给我一卷经卷，命我将其贴在脑门上，跟着他念诵某些咒文；在极度慌乱之中，我完全没听懂那些字句是何含意，但我依旧老老实实照念不误。念罢，我便呆若木鸡地木立当场。‘喂，’过了好半晌，法官老爷厉声喝问道，‘咱们还得等上多久？’
“‘敢问天神老爷，您究竟要老夫呈递何物？’我说。
“‘老夫要你的证词证据！’法官怒道。
“‘老夫实在拿不出什么证据了，’我颤声答道，‘除了您方才听到的那些原由之外。’
“‘老夫何曾听到过半个字证据！’他说，堂下看客中再次爆发出一阵阵窃笑。
“逼不得已，我只得把先前说过的话原原本本复述了一遍：特许状里的字句白纸黑字写得如何，条款就是如何。我一条一条逐一重复。
“法官转头望向刚刚落座的那位辩护大律师说道：‘好了，塞利姆（Selim）贤弟，’我昔日的挚友兼座上宾闻声而起，用极其挑衅刻薄的目光，自上而下、从头到脚将我恶狠狠打量了三、四（3、4）遍，随即厉声喝道：
“‘你这个醉生梦死的无耻之徒！到了这步田地，你居然还敢死守着你胆敢向法庭呈递的弥天大谎不放吗？’
“我答辩道那绝非谎言，句句皆是实情。
“‘实情！’他从鼻孔里冷笑一声。”

===Original===
